# Decisions record (v2)

**Status:** plan of record, 2026-10-11. It replaces the v1 record (the PSF-oriented plan, D-01..D-136), which stays in git history (the repository's first commit) and is **not binding**. Binding input: the owner's direction of 2026-10-11 (community showcase, Android and iOS only, Material 3 only and identical on both platforms, not a 1:1 port, Zen of Python, phases rather than deadlines). Plan: [`PLAN.md`](../PLAN.md). Scope per feature id: [`feature-matrix.md`](feature-matrix.md).

**How this record works**
- Every decision is binding for code, docs and agents.
- Numbering restarts at D-01 for v2. v1 decisions are cited only as "v1 D-nn"; no live doc cites a v1 number against this file. From now on, numbers are never reused or renumbered.
- To change a decision, open an issue labelled `needs-decision` with the options and a recommendation, and settle it as D-101 describes (reversible choices don't wait for the owner). Then, in a PR merged before or together with the code that depends on it, add a new D-number and mark the old one "superseded by D-nn", or, when only part of it changes, "amended by D-nn" with the changed words struck through and the rest still binding.
- Decisions marked **prov. O-n** are provisional: they apply until open decision O-n is settled under D-101 (end of this file).
- `F1`..`F55` refer to the verified Flet facts in section N. Spikes `S1`..`S9` are defined in PLAN §8.

---

## A. Purpose and scope

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-01 | We rebuild the PyCon US mobile app ([PyCon/pycon-us-mobile](https://github.com/PyCon/pycon-us-mobile): Ionic 8, Angular 17, Capacitor 7) in Python with Flet, as a **community showcase** for the Flet and Python communities. The official app's quality is our bar. The PSF is not a dependency and is not contacted now. | Owner's direction. |
| D-02 | Presenting the app at **PyCon US 2027** (Long Beach, CA, 2027-05-12..18) is the objective, not a deadline. Work is ordered in phases P0..P4 with exit criteria and soft markers. Quality is never traded for a date. | Owner's direction: "not a blocking constraint". |
| D-03 | **Not a 1:1 port.** The Ionic app is the functional reference. We keep its data semantics and improve flows, information architecture, visuals, accessibility and offline behaviour wherever we can. | Owner's direction. |
| D-04 | Each of the 263 catalog features gets a v2 disposition and a phase in `docs/feature-matrix.md`, which is the source of truth for scope. Dispositions are `keep`, `improve`, `redesign`, `mock`, `interim`, `later` and `drop`, plus `new` for our additions, and they can combine (e.g. `improve+interim`). Ids are an inventory for traceability, not a contract: there is no "one test per id" rule and no parity gate. | Parity gating served the PSF plan; a test per id is weak evidence of quality (both external reviews). |
| D-05 | The only real backend is the public feed `https://us.pycon.org/{year}/schedule/conference.json`. The active year is one constant, `config.YEAR`; the feed URL and all other year facts are data (`data/<year>/conference.toml`), never code. We use 2026 until the 2027 feed is published. | Public data needs no permission. |
| D-06 | Features that need the private PSF `/api/v1` (sign-in, favourites sync, role flags, the four staff tools) are built faithfully against our **local mock** (section F). The app never calls private PSF endpoints. | No permission to use them. |
| D-07 | The staff tools (lead retrieval, door check, swag pickup, Code of Conduct incident capture) stay in scope, in P3. They are the best showcase for the barcode-scanner extension and offline queueing. Attendee features never wait for them. | Showcase value, attendee-first ordering. |
| D-08 | Non-goals: web, PWA and desktop targets; Cupertino or adaptive UI; push notifications; analytics; a crash-reporting service; remote config, kill switches and OTA; public store listings; multi-event support; in-app chat or session feedback. | Each is PSF-only, off-platform, or scope the showcase does not need. |

## B. Platforms and UI

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-09 | Android and iOS only, phones first. Tablets get a proper layout: centred maximum widths (720 dp for reading, 840 dp for lists) and, from P2, a `NavigationRail` at 600 dp and wider. A desktop window is only a developer and test host. | Owner's direction; the UI is designed for phones and works on tablets. |
| D-10 | *(The back arrow's node amended by D-234.)* **Material 3 only, identical on both platforms.** Never `adaptive=True` and no Cupertino controls. We pin the Material defaults that change on iOS: the bundled font (D-26), left-aligned titles (`center_title=False`) and the Material back arrow. Flet has no theme-level back icon (no `Theme.platform`, F8), so `AppScaffold` sets `AppBar.leading=IconButton(ft.Icons.ARROW_BACK, tooltip="Back", on_click=<navigate to parent>)` on pushed views. Residual platform adaptations that Flet cannot pin today (overscroll physics: bounce on iOS, stretch on Android; the text-selection toolbar and handles in selectable Markdown and text fields) are accepted and listed in `ux.md`; pinning them is upstream candidate U19. | Otherwise Flutter Material adapts typography and app-bar details on iOS. No Flet scrollable exposes scroll physics. |
| D-11 | *(O-2 settled by D-148.)* The one intended platform difference is gesture behaviour (the accepted residual adaptations are in D-10): `Theme.page_transitions = PageTransitionsTheme(android=PREDICTIVE, ios=CUPERTINO)`. iOS keeps edge-swipe Back and Android 14+ keeps predictive Back. Visuals stay identical. | All three UX studies recommend it. iOS swipe-back exists only with the Cupertino transition, and the build template already enables predictive back (F9). |
| D-12 | *(Values recorded by D-106; overlay values amended by D-113.)* Device floors are Flet's template defaults: iOS 15 from Flet 1.1, and Flutter's default Android `minSdk` (confirmed in S5: 24). We target the current SDK with edge-to-edge, in portrait and landscape. System bars are never hidden, and content respects safe areas. Each colour scheme sets `Theme.system_overlay_style` (status- and navigation-bar icon brightness, ~~enforced navigation-bar contrast~~ transparent bars with navigation-bar contrast not enforced, D-113). Both are checked with 3-button and gesture navigation, in light and dark (#311). | Widest reasonable reach: v1's API 34 floor was a PSF policy, and Ionic #307 asked for older Android. Ionic's edge-to-edge layout left the Android navigation-bar icons the same colour as the background, so they were invisible (#311). |
| D-148 | **Per-platform transitions are confirmed** (settles O-2 as recommended; S4). `Theme.page_transitions = PageTransitionsTheme(android=PREDICTIVE, ios=CUPERTINO)` in every scheme. The iOS edge swipe is checked with a finger on the iPhone (#121); if it fails, this reopens. | On Android 14 (with the developer flag) and 16 (by default) an edge swipe on a detail ran Flutter's predictive page animation, a cancel kept the route and a commit popped through our handler. On iOS the pushed page slides in and out (screen recording); simctl cannot drag, so the swipe itself waits for a finger. |

## C. Information architecture and navigation

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-13 | **prov. O-1.** A bottom `NavigationBar` with five fixed destinations: **Now · Schedule · My schedule · Map · More**. Labels are always shown, and the bar is identical for every user: it never changes with sign-in or role. Speakers live in More › Programme and in search. | M3 allows 3-5 destinations. This gives the two hero features (Now & Next for me, My schedule) a tab each, and a stable bar fixes Ionic's shape-shifting tab bar. It resolves the UX studies' disagreement over a Speakers tab versus a My schedule tab. |
| D-14 | **prov. O-1.** *(Now's phases and queries recorded by D-179; the screen by D-190.)* The app opens on **Now**, a home dashboard. Before the conference it shows a countdown and key dates; during it, your next session, what is happening now and what is up next; afterwards, a wrap-up. First run shows Welcome once (P2). | It answers "what should I do next?" in every season. |
| D-15 | No navigation drawer. **More** is one flat, sectioned list: Staff tools (staff only, P3), Programme, Expo & sponsors, At the venue, About, You (settings and About this app). | M3 Expressive deprecates the modal drawer; flat is better than nested. |
| D-16 | Staff tools have no tab. They are the first More section and a card on Now, both shown from `staff_entries(flags)`. P3 adds the setting "Open the app on: Now / Lead scanner". It defaults to Lead scanner for lead-retrieval accounts and is applied only after the stored sign-in has loaded. | The bar stays stable, and sponsors keep landing on the scanner (#310) without Ionic's race. |
| D-17 | *(Bar amended by D-144; the Tracks hub's pages of their own sit under the hub, D-207.)* **Real back stack.** Every list → detail move and every cross-link pushes exactly one View through a recursive drill route, so Back pops one step (fixes #279). Switching tabs shows that tab's root~~, and tapping the active tab returns to its root~~; the bar is on tab roots only, and re-tapping the active tab scrolls its root to the top (D-144). Back on any root other than Now goes to Now; Back on Now exits. Non-Now root Views set `can_pop=False` and an async `on_confirm_pop` that navigates to `/now` and awaits `e.control.confirm_pop(False)` (F1, F10); S4 verifies it with predictive back. | Predictable Back. Router semantics are verified in source (F6); S4 confirms on devices. |
| D-18 | *(Lifetime amended by D-143; how often the Schedule jumps by D-204.)* State that must survive navigation lives outside screen state: filters in the `Prefs` store (persisted); the selected day, scroll offsets, the search query, scope and base, and the map sub-tab and zoom in `ViewMemory`, a plain, non-observable holder for the ~~app session~~ app process (D-143), so remembering a position re-renders nothing (architecture §6.3). Screens never move by themselves on return. The Schedule scrolls to the current slot once per ~~app session~~ ~~app process (D-143)~~ conference day in each app process (D-204) and when "Jump to now" is tapped, never on Back, refresh or a filter change (#313). | Fixes #313. `scroll_to` needs explicit triggers. |
| D-19 | *(Where `ViewMemory` is created amended by D-143; Search's back arrow amended by D-165; the screen recorded by D-193.)* **One search**: a global modal route `/search` over all days, opened from Now, Schedule, Map and More with a preset scope. Scopes are All, Sessions, Speakers and Rooms in P1; Sponsors and Places join in P2. Results push details on top of search, so Back returns to the results. The stock Router pops every view of a modal chain to the modal's base (F6), so `main()` sets our own `page.on_view_pop` before `page.render_views(App)` (the Router then hands every pop to it). It navigates to `parent_of(e.route)`, one pure, unit-tested function in `routes.py` that drops the last drill pair or page segment; for `/search` it returns the remembered base location, query included (`ViewMemory.search_base`, recorded by the opener; ~~`main()` creates the `ViewMemory`~~ `main.py` creates the `ViewMemory` once per process (D-143), and `main()` passes it on with `page.render_views(App, memory)`). The pushed views' back arrow (D-10) uses the same function, ~~and `AppScaffold(back_to=...)` points Search's arrow at the same base~~ which already returns that base for `/search`, so Search's arrow needs no parameter (D-165). The handler goes once U18 lands; S4 verifies search → result → Back. | One obvious way. It replaces four per-page searches and "Search all days". |
| D-20 | Only Schedule has filters: a bottom sheet of grouped filter chips that apply immediately, with a live "Show N sessions". Whenever a track is hidden, a summary under the day tabs says so, including the default: Open spaces are hidden by default, and that shows as a filter state. The filter button's dot appears only when filters differ from the default. Filters persist. My schedule, Now and Search ignore them. | Ionic leaked filters between screens and hid its default. |
| D-21 | *(Unknown ids amended by D-186.)* Deep links (P2) use a custom URL scheme and host, named with the app identity (O-4); links have the form `<scheme>://<host>/<route path>`, mirroring internal routes. Each deep-linking table sets both `scheme` and `host`: the build template registers nothing when either is missing. A link opens its root with the target pushed, so Back never exits. ~~Unknown ids land on the root with a SnackBar~~ An unknown id opens its detail's not-found state, pushed on the root like any target (D-186), and reminder taps use the same paths. Shared links use the public us.pycon.org page. Both `[tool.flet.android.deep_linking]` and `[tool.flet.ios.deep_linking]` are configured, so links work with or without flet-dev/flet#6953 (F16). | Without the PSF domain there are no universal links or App Links. |
| D-141 | *(How the Back key reaches our handler amended by D-208; a sixth kind, `changes/<year>`, added by D-255.)* **The drill route shape stands, and every pop goes through our `page.on_view_pop`** (S4; confirms section M, architecture §5.2 and D-19).<br>• **Shape:** ~~`ft.Route(path=":kind(session\|speaker\|room\|sponsor\|plan)/:ident", recursive=True, component=Drill)`~~ `ft.Route(path=":kind(session\|speaker\|room\|sponsor\|plan\|changes)/:ident", recursive=True, component=Drill)` (`changes` joins `DRILL_KINDS` with the Schedule changes screen, #435; D-255) is the last child of every tab root, More page and `/search`, and `Drill` returns the detail component. Each View level reads its own `kind` and `ident` (`use_route_params()`) and its own `use_view_path()`. `drill_href()` quotes ids with `quote(ident, safe="")` and `Drill` unquotes them. The `s~id` fallback is not needed.<br>• **Back:** `main()` sets `page.on_view_pop` before `page.render_views`; it receives the Android Back key and committed predictive gestures and navigates to `parent_of(e.route, memory.search_base)`, so search → result → detail → Back → Back lands on the results with the query from `ViewMemory`, and Back again on `search_base`. | On the Android 14 and 16 emulators and the iOS 26.5 simulator, a five-level stack gave each level its own params, and ids with `:`, `+`, a space, `~` and `ç` reached their level intact by tap, by deep entry and by `--route` (`docs/spikes/04-router-back.md`); the Router escapes each consumed prefix before matching the next level. Each Back was one `view-pop` and one level; without our handler the Router pops a whole modal chain to its base (F6). |
| D-142 | **Roots: D-17's Back rule works as written, and every root View shares one route key** (S4; ux.md §2.4).<br>• Non-Now roots set `can_pop=False` and an async `on_confirm_pop` that calls `page.navigate("/now")` and awaits `confirm_pop(False)`; Back on Now is left to the system.<br>• Every tab root's `View.route` is `"/"`; pushed Views keep their resolved path (`use_view_path()`). | On Android 14 and 16, the Back key and the predictive gesture on Schedule, My schedule, Map and More each fired `confirm_pop` and landed on Now (the gesture shows no page animation there); on Now the system's back-to-home animation ran and the activity finished (F29). With a key per root, a tab switch ran the page transition (about 400 ms of slide and fade), as for a detail; with one key it swaps without one. Both pop handlers work from the route tree or from `e.route` of pushed Views, so the shared key changes nothing else. |
| D-143 | **`ViewMemory` lives per process, and the start route returns a new session to where the user was** (amends D-18 and D-19; S4). `main.py` creates one `ViewMemory` at import and every `main()` passes that one to `App`. `AppScaffold` records the top View's `page.route` in `ViewMemory.last_location`; `Start` (`/`) navigates there when it is set, else to `/now` (`/welcome` on first run, P2). A last location under `/search` restores its `search_base` instead: a fresh Router would put the global modal over `/` (its last non-modal location), not over the opener. A deep link or a `--route` start still opens its own route. | On Android a new session starts in the same Python process after an activity recreation (F26) and after Back on Now and a relaunch (F29). With memory per session, a recreation on `/now/session/42/speaker/17` dropped the user on Now; with memory per process it rebuilt the three Views and Back popped from there. After Back on Now the last location is `/now`, so a relaunch looks like a fresh start with positions kept. |
| D-144 | **The bottom bar is on tab roots only** (amends D-17; settles ux.md UQ-1 and architecture §19 #6 through #149). Pushed Views show the back arrow and no bar. Re-tapping the active tab on a root fires `on_change` (S4), and `AppScaffold` hands it to the screen's `on_retap`, which scrolls to the top. A bar on every View returns only with U23 (D-149), in its own PR. | A `NavigationBar` lives on its `View`, so each pushed View's bar moved with its page: on iOS it slid across beside the root's bar, on Android two bars ghosted in every push and pop and stayed doubled through a predictive gesture. With roots only, the detail covers the root's bar (iOS, like `hidesBottomBarWhenPushed`) or the bar fades out (Android). "It behaves" outweighs the one-tap switch from a deep chain. |
| D-164 | **Location helpers live in `locations.py`; the route table in `routes.py`** (amends section M's `routes.py`; #74). `routes.py` imports every screen for `ROUTES`, and screens build links, so the helpers they use cannot live there without an import cycle.<br>• `locations.py`: `TAB_ROOTS`, `DRILL_KINDS`, `PAGES` (every page path without a parameter, the places Back walks up to; a test keeps it equal to the table's), `drill_href()`, `parent_of()`, `start_target(last_location, search_base)` (D-143's rule for `Start`), `query_of()`, `is_top_view(location, view_path)` and the `use_query()` hook. All but the hook are pure and unit-tested.<br>• `routes.py`: `ROUTES`, `DRILL`, `Drill`, `Start` and `NotFound`; `App` passes `not_found=NotFound`, which the Router wraps in a View of its own (F6), so it brings a `SafeArea` and a "Go to Now" button.<br>• The top View is the one `page.route` addresses: `is_top_view(page.route, use_view_path())` compares the route's path with the View's. Not `use_route_location()`: under a global modal the Router renders the Views below at the opener's location, so the opener's View would match too, and so would `Start` under a `/search` entered directly. `use_query()` reads the query once, when its View mounts, and only for the top View: a deep entry also builds the Views below, a modal keeps its opener's mounted, and the query is theirs in neither case. `stub_view()` (then `AppScaffold`) records only the top View in `last_location`.<br>• A page with a parameter nests under the page Back returns to: `/more/tracks/<slug>` is a child of `/more/tracks`, so the stack the Router builds is the one `parent_of()` walks (as siblings, the track's View would sit on More while Back went to Tracks).<br>• `Start` shows the boot purple and shares the tab roots' View key `"/"` (D-142), so Now replaces it without a transition or a flash. It navigates only when it is the top View: a direct `/search` entry builds the modal over `/`, and `Start` below it leaves the entry alone (Back from search goes to `search_base`). | One obvious place for each: screens import `locations` and never the table. Deriving `PAGES` from `ROUTES` at import would put the cycle back; a test is enough to keep two short lists equal. Reading the query only on the top View keeps a deep entry such as `/map/room/hall-a?highlight=42` from handing `highlight` to the Map root below it. |
| D-165 | *(Strip during a refresh amended by D-181; the strip's module by D-200.)* **`AppScaffold` and the status strip** (records architecture §5.5 and D-32's strip; #76). In `ui/scaffold.py` (`StatusStrip` and `strip_line()`: `ui/status_strip.py` since D-200):<br>• **`AppScaffold(*, title, body, actions=None)`** builds every screen's View: a surface `AppBar` whose title is left-aligned (`center_title=False`) and stays a header for screen readers (Flutter's `exclude_header_semantics=False`), the screen's actions, `automatically_imply_leading=False` with the Material back arrow on pushed Views to `parent_of(view_path, memory.search_base)` (D-10, D-19), D-142's shared root key and D-17's Back to Now on non-Now roots, and `body` (one control, usually an `expand=True` `ListView`) in a `SafeArea`. No `back_to`: `parent_of()` already sends `/search` to its opener. The status- and navigation-bar style is the theme's (D-113, D-151): Flet's `AppBar` applies `Theme.system_overlay_style`, so no screen sets one. The bottom bar joins in #75 (roots only, D-144); the tablet rail and the 720/840 dp widths (D-09) wait for #30: Flet has no max-width constraint, so both need the page width and its changes.<br>• **D-112's content line moves to `AppScaffold`:** it logs `Content shown: <source> feed, version <n>` once per feed version per session, from the first screen that renders it (`Services.shown_versions`), after a SharedPreferences round trip; so a session that starts on another route (D-143, `--route`) logs it too, and a tab switch does not log it again. A newer version re-runs the effect, and Flet cancels the run in progress (`hook.cancel()`; an unmount does not, as the effect has no cleanup), so the round trip and the line run under `asyncio.shield`: otherwise a network version published within the round trip dropped the cached version's line, and the cold-start mark moved to the network version (once in three launches on a deep route on the iOS 26.5 simulator; with the shield both lines were logged in every launch). `AppScaffold` places the body in `Shown(body)`, a leaf component that reads `Conference`, logs the line and returns the same `body`: a refresh re-renders that leaf only, so screens that do not show the feed (More, its pages, the stubs) do not re-render with it (Flet keeps a retained child as is, `object_patch.py` `src is dst`).<br>• **`StatusStrip`** is its own component, placed by the screens that show the feed's state (Now, Schedule, My schedule): hidden when `fresh`; otherwise an icon, one line from the pure `strip_line()` and a Refresh `TextButton` (disabled while ~~refreshing~~ a refresh the user asked for runs, D-181), with an indeterminate 2 dp `ProgressBar` while ~~refreshing~~ any refresh runs (`Conference.refreshing`, D-181), on `surface_container_high`, as one live region with the identifier `app.status`. Lines: "Updating the schedule…" (only for a refresh the user asked for, D-181), "Updated 9 hours ago" (`timefmt.ago_label()`, whole units in words, so screen readers say "hours", not "h"), "Offline · schedule from Tue, May 12, 10:42 AM" and, after an answer we could not use (D-146), "Couldn't update · schedule from …", in conference time and the device's 12/24-hour format. Without data (rare, NEW-03) a stale strip says "No schedule yet", and a failed refresh keeps its reason: "Offline · no schedule yet", "Couldn't update · no schedule yet". #82 adds the refresh triggers and re-renders the age every 60 s.<br>• `SectionHeader(text)` is a `TITLE_SMALL` heading in `primary` inside `Semantics(header=True)`; `search_button(memory, scope)` moves here from the stubs. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), gesture and 3-button navigation in light and dark and in landscape, the title was left-aligned, pushed Views showed the Material arrow, rows stopped above the navigation bar and clear of the side bar in landscape, the bars' icons contrasted, and a real offline start showed the offline strip; on the iOS 26.5 simulator the same held around the notch and the home indicator. One log line per version keeps D-112's cold-start mark wherever a session starts, without a duplicate on every tab switch. A per-strip component keeps the strip's own hooks out of the screens, and `body` as one control lets a screen choose a `ListView` or a `Column` with tabs (P1's Schedule) without a wrapper. |
| D-166 | **The bottom bar: its module, identifiers and re-tap** (records D-13, D-27 and D-144 for the bar; amends section M's `ui/` list; #75).<br>• **`ui/navigation.py`** holds `TABS` (`Tab(root, label, icon, selected_icon)` in `TAB_ROOTS`' order, with `Tab.identifier`), `navigation_bar(root, on_change)` and `use_scroll_to_top()`. `AppScaffold` puts the bar on tab roots only: a Material `NavigationBar` (never adaptive), labels `ALWAYS_SHOW`, `selected_index` the View's own root.<br>• **Identifiers:** `NavigationBarDestination` has no `Semantics` or identifier of its own, so its `icon` and `selected_icon` are each an unlabelled `Semantics(identifier="app.nav.<tab>", content=Icon(...))` (`app.nav.now`, `app.nav.schedule`, `app.nav.my_schedule`, `app.nav.map`, `app.nav.more`; section M). Flutter merges a child's identifier into the enclosing node when that node has none (`SemanticsConfiguration.absorb`), so the destination's one node carries it, beside its label. Flet's `Semantics.identifier` docstring says an identifier forces a dedicated node (like `container=True`); the Dart control does not (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/semantics.dart` reads only `container`), and the merge relies on that, so each pin bump re-checks it (development.md §2.2) and #163 tracks the upstream fix.<br>• **Taps:** `AppScaffold(on_retap=...)`. On a root, another destination navigates to its root (one shared View key, D-142); the active one awaits the root's `on_retap`, and without one does nothing. `use_scroll_to_top()` gives a root one list ref (`use_memo`) and an action that awaits `scroll_to(offset=0, duration=300)`, a jump (`duration=0`) under `Prefs.reduce_motion` (D-34), and nothing before the list is built. Each render's `ListView(ref=...)` points the ref at the new control, which takes over the mounted list's identity (F38), so the ref stays live although `Ref.current` is weak; keeping one ref also keeps a list that a memoised child (F34) did not rebuild reachable. A re-tap is the user's own request, so D-18's "screens never move by themselves" stands. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), `uiautomator dump` showed each destination as one clickable node with `resource-id` `app.nav.<tab>`, named "Schedule, Tab 2 of 5" (TalkBack's text), the active one `selected`, each 82 × 80 dp; adb journeys in gesture navigation passed for tab switches, the re-tap scroll, a push (no bar) and Back, the Back key and the predictive gesture (cancelled and committed) on details and roots, and Back on Now leaving the app; the bar and a barless push were checked again in 3-button navigation and in dark mode. A recording showed a tab switch as a one-frame swap and the root's one bar fading under a pushed page. On the iOS 26.5 simulator an in-app journey (15 checks, light and dark) saw the same switches, re-tap and push. At 115 % to 200 % OS text (Android) and the largest Larger Text (iOS), "My schedule" wraps to two lines without clipping, because Flutter clamps the bar's labels at 1.3x and the label does not fit 82 dp; whether to keep it is #164's decision. Merging the identifier needs no change in Flet; an identifier field on the destination would be the cleaner upstream fix (#163). |
| D-167 | **"My schedule" stays the tab label, and its wrap at large text is accepted** (settles #164 under D-101; D-13, D-33). From 115 % text on Android and the largest Larger Text on iOS the label wraps to two lines: legible, never clipped, but start-aligned and with its icon raised (Flutter clamps `NavigationBar` labels at 1.3x, and Flet's `NavigationBarDestination.label` is a plain string). Upstream ask: a label control (centring or `max_lines`), added to #163. The owner may still choose a one-word label (e.g. "Saved"). | Keeps one name for the tab, its screen and the star's copy; the defect is cosmetic and best fixed in Flet. |
| D-168 | **Screenshots are uploaded with the GitHub contents API** (amends D-162's upload route; #166). `gh api -X PUT repos/<owner>/<repo>/contents/<pr>/<file> -f branch=pr-assets -F content=@<base64>`, one commit per file, never force-pushed; AGENTS.md has the loop. Everything else in D-162 stands. | An isolated agent worktree can't add a second worktree, so D-162's commands failed there (#162); the API needs no checkout, and a byte-for-byte round trip was verified. |

A session listed in several rooms (a plenary, the lightning talks' streams, DATA-16) is on in each; when several are on in one room, the one that started last comes first, ties in schedule order. A module of its own, the rooms family beside `timeline.py` and `agenda.py` (D-185's one module per family); like them it holds the model's own `Session` objects.<br>• **Other locations are year data:** neither the model nor `conference.toml` tells a room from a place, and name matching is ruled out (ux.md §1.9). `content.toml` gains `other_locations`, the feed's names of the places that are not rooms (2026: Elsewhere, Lobby, Out Front of Hyatt Regency, the three DATA-32 found without a floor-plan pin), compared by room slug; `config.py` learns arrays of strings and refuses a non-string, a duplicate or an empty name. The count is every session listed under the room, poster details included, as the room detail lists them (ROOM-02) and as Ionic counted ("Expo Hall AB 35"), where "Now:" follows the timeline only: Expo Hall AB counts 35 while the Schedule shows 10 rows there. `LinkTile.subtitle` takes a control for that, and a `LinkTile` with a subtitle tops its icons and title 14 dp down (`title_alignment=TOP`, `min_vertical_padding=14`; its Copy button 2 dp down), so the 24 dp icons share the title line's centre at every height and a two-line row stays 72 dp: under D-182's `TITLE_HEIGHT` a 72 dp row centred its chevron, a taller one moved both icons and the title the other way, so the icons stood unevenly down the list and jumped when a "Now:" line came on the minute tick. Rows without a subtitle keep `TITLE_HEIGHT`. The row is one merged node, read "Room 103ABC, 26 sessions, Now: …"; identifier `map.room.<slug>`; a tap pushes `…/room/<slug>` (the room stub until #101). Each has the header words of ux.md §5.1 and its own sections: key dates and "Build your schedule"; your next session, Happening now (phase `day` only), Up next and the Schedule link; a Sprints row (the last evening too); "Look back at the schedule". The header is one ~~level-1~~ level-2 (D-211) heading node read as one phrase; "Times are Pacific Time (PDT)" (D-35) only in the conference season, the one that shows times; the brand indigo `#3B3EA9` in both schemes (light `primary`, dark `primary_container`), its season icon in ux.md §3.1's gold.<br>• **Your next session:** `next_for_me()` in a `Card(FILLED)`: the `SessionRow` with its star (identifiers `now.next.*`) and, once it is on, its elapsed bar, counted like its status from `display_start` (D-186: the Job Fair's from 9:30, though its slot is 10:00); under it session detail's `StatusLine` and "Show room" (`room_href()`, only for a session with a room) in a wrapping row, so at large text the button moves under the status whole; the status is a stop of its own (row, status, star, button: four). The card's session is not repeated in Happening now or in Up next, and a section it leaves empty is left out: when it is all that is on (the card says "Happening now"; an empty section would say nothing is) or all of the next slot (the card says "Starts in 20 min"), which is usual, since the next saved talk is mostly in the next slot. Not on `primary_container` as §5.1 sketched: the dark one is the header's indigo, and the row's `on_surface_variant` lines read 3.7:1 on it; M3's filled colour, `surface_container_highest`, is a surface the token and badge tests already cover. With no answer on a conference day, a `LinkTile` says why: nothing saved (to the Schedule), or nothing in the next 3 h (to My schedule; in the evening after a day, phase `after_day`, "Nothing more saved for today").<br>• **Elapsed bars:** `SessionRow(elapsed=)` takes `agenda.elapsed_percent(session, now)`, whole percent from `display_start` to `end`, rounded down by integer division (a float share made 29 % into 28), and draws a 3 dp `ProgressBar` in `Tokens.live` among the row's excluded texts, M3's current bar (`year_2023=False`): a gap after the fill, a track of `on_surface` at 16 % (about 1.4:1 on `surface` and on the card's `surface_container_highest`, where `surface_container_highest` itself vanished; the fill reads 3.7:1 or more on it) and a stop dot in `on_surface_variant` (4.5:1 or more) at the end, which marks 100 % as WCAG 1.4.11 asks; a track at 3:1 on the surface (`outline`) would have left the fill at about 1.4:1 on it in light, since `live` is a dark green; the row's phrase ends with "60 percent elapsed." (`elapsed_phrase()`). ux.md §5.1's `semantics_label="Elapsed"` and `semantics_value="60 percent"` cannot say that at the pin: Flet types `semantics_value` as a number and sends it as a double ("60.0"), and without it Flutter gives the bar the value "60" (`progress_indicator.dart`, material_ui 1.6.0), which a merged row reads bare (#210). Whole numbers keep the memo: a minute re-renders only the rows whose bar moved, which a test checks and which fails with `ft.memo` removed.<br>• **Time:** `use_minute()` in `Now` (D-188): every 60 s while it is mounted and in the foreground, at once on a resume (`Services.run_every`), from one `clock.now()` per render, so the fake clock drives it (D-187); a test pauses the loop, moves the clock and sees the resume recompute. The strip keeps `auto_refresh=True` (D-175).<br>• **"See the rest of today in Schedule"** ("Open the schedule" when the next slot is on a later day) is a navigation to `/schedule` and nothing more, on every conference day until the conference's last end (gated on the phase, not on a next slot, so the day's last slot keeps it): the Schedule (#86) opens on today during the conference and owns its jump (D-155); a hint for it, such as a slot key in `ViewMemory` with D-185's `current_slot_key()` fallback, is #89's.<br>• **`SectionHeader(leading=, spoken=)`:** a decorative mark before the text, Happening now's 8 dp `live` dot, the text expanding beside it so a long heading wraps; the heading still reads its text, or `spoken`, which `now_header.spoken()` makes of it ("·" a pause, a range's dash "to"), as for the header phrase, the key dates and the Sprints row.<br>• **Identifiers on buttons and notes are merged** (`MergeSemantics(Semantics(identifier=...))`): alone, the identifier's `Semantics` is a node of its own, an empty extra TalkBack stop around the button. | A screen that changes with the minute is only worth its place at the front door if it reads right at every moment of the year, so each season and phase is tested at fixed times of the 2026 feed (`tests/test_now.py`), and the bars' semantics avoid a field Flet cannot fill. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK, the clock set through About › 7 taps › Developer): `uiautomator dump` read the header as one node ("PyCon US 2026. Never the Schedule's filters (D-20): a saved open space shows.<br>• **What has ended folds away** by `clock.now()`, through the pure `agenda.fold_ended(agenda, now, zone)` → `FoldedAgenda(earlier_days, earlier_today, ahead, overlaps)`: the days before the conference-local today go whole into one `ExpansionTile` at the top, "Earlier days (6 sessions)"; today's sessions with `end <= now` (Now's half-open rule, D-179) into a second one under today's header, "Earlier today (4 sessions)" (not §5.6's "Show 4 earlier sessions": one tile pattern for both folds, whose own semantics say expanded or collapsed, where a button's label would have to). A slot whose sessions end apart splits, so a tutorial that is still on stays when the talk saved beside it folds. Both tiles start collapsed (the "Earlier today" open state holds the date it is open for, so the next day's tile starts collapsed), their titles `BODY_MEDIUM` in `on_surface_variant` under the `TITLE_SMALL` day header, open on a `surface_container_low` fill, and are `ui/fold.py`'s new `FoldTile`, which session detail's other rooms (D-186) now use too, so the app has one fold: an `ExpansionTile` without divider lines, animated by `expand_style(prefs)` over `prefs.motion_ms(EXPAND_MS)`, 200 ms (none under reduced motion, D-173; Flutter's tile reads only the style's `duration`, for both ways), and controlled (`expanded=` from the screen's state, which `on_change` sets): Flutter writes the new state back into the control, so an uncontrolled tile collapsed again at the next render, as F33 does to a text field. A minute tick re-folds while the screen shows: ~~`ui/scaffold.py`'s~~ `ui/status_strip.py`'s (D-200) `use_minute()` (D-188, on `Services.run_every`, D-175). When nothing is ahead, a compact notice heads the list, start-aligned above the "Earlier days" tile rather than the full-screen empty state, which pushed the tile to the middle of the screen: "All your saved sessions are over" (a level-2 heading in a live region: `header=True` alone is no heading, F40), with "See what's on today" (to Now) while today, in the conference zone, is on or before the feed's last day, and after it "<name> is over. Your saved sessions stay here." (the year data's name) with no button, since Now has nothing on today.<br>• **No scroll to today:** with the past folded, today's header is the second item, so ux.md §5.6's once-per-process `scroll_to(scroll_key="day-<iso>")` and D-154's `cache_extent` for this list would move the list by one tile; the list stays lazy and opens at its top. This is only the scroll to today: restoring the tab's offset from `ViewMemory` (ux.md §2.4) is a separate item, still open for this root (D-192 restores the Schedule's day lists).<br>• **A clash in words:** a session ahead that overlaps another gets a last line: `SessionRow(..., overlaps=(names…))` shows `WARNING_AMBER` beside "Overlaps with “<first>”", cut at three lines like a title, and, with more clashes, "and 2 more" as a line of its own, never cut (D-188's "Now:" line: an ellipsis inside one two-line text hid the second name and the count at default size and the first name at 200 %), both in `Tokens.warning`; `spoken_phrase()` ends by naming every clash, "Overlaps with “A”, “B” and “C”.", so the clash is heard in full (D-33); each row of a clash shows it. Only a clash that can still change a plan shows: `fold_ended()`'s `overlaps` keeps, for each session ahead, the clashes with sessions that have not ended, and folded rows get none, so the history carries no warning colour and "and N more" counts only what matters.<br>• **A removal goes at once, with Undo** (ux.md §4.4, §5.6): the star's tap removes the row, and "Removed from My schedule · Undo" (`use_favorite_toggle`, D-184) brings it back where it was; a clash line on the other row goes and comes back with it. Without the folds, scrolling to today would have been the first thing the screen did on every conference day; with them, today is on screen at once. |
| D-193 | *(Each type's first rows and its "Show all" row added by D-218; the field's text, Clear and count line are `ui/fields.py`'s, D-226.)* **Global search: a pinned pill, a debounce per keystroke, a fresh search per opening, and the results' memoised path** (records D-19 and D-46 for NEW-02, SCHED-05 and SPKR-02, and ux.md §4.3; amends §4.3's `AppBar` field and section M's `core/`, `ui/` and `screens/` lists; settles S4's focus question on #97 under D-101).<br>• **Modules:** `screens/search.py` (`Search`, `scope_of()`) and `screens/search_results.py` (`ResultList`, `DayGroup`, `room_row()`, `summary()`), so each stays under about 400 lines (D-66). `ui/fields.py`'s `SearchField` is the pill both search and the Speakers filter use (D-189's field, moved; its hint now wraps to two lines only while it shows). The room count line, `session_count()`, moves from `screens/map.py` to `core/rooms.py`, since the Map and search both show it.<br>• **The field:** under an `AppBar` titled "Search" (back arrow to `search_base`, D-19, D-165), a `SearchField` pinned above the results: controlled (D-157), `autofocus`, `KeyboardType.WEB_SEARCH`, autocorrect and suggestions off, named by its hint while empty and "Search" once it holds text, the hint by scope ("Search sessions, speakers, rooms" in All, "Search sessions", "Search speakers", "Search rooms"), so it agrees with the idle title and, shorter, is no longer cut at the largest text, a Clear button ("Clear search") that empties it at once and puts the focus back in it. Not ux.md's `AppBar(title=TextField)`: a field in the 56 dp toolbar cannot grow with the text (Flet cannot read the scale, U16), while the pill reflows, as the Speakers filter did at the largest text (D-189).<br>• **Debounce:** the field's text is state set at every keystroke; the searched query is a second state that an effect on the text sets after `DEBOUNCE_SECONDS` (0.25 s, D-46), so each keystroke restarts the wait (Flet cancels the running setup, F2), with a sync `cleanup=` so an unmount cancels it too (F2, F39). The keyboard's action key, a scope chip and Clear search at once. The action key reads Done, not Search, until Flet has `text_input_action` (U30, flet-dev/flet#6962). Results are `use_memo` on (index, query, scope); the index is built once per feed version (D-180).<br>• **Scopes:** All, Sessions, Speakers and Rooms as `Chip`s with their check (not colour alone, D-33), one row that scrolls sideways when wider than the screen; a chip keeps the query, searches at once and gives `selection_click()` (ux.md §4.5). One scope is always on, so the chips use `on_click` with `selected` set by the screen: `on_select` toggles `selected` on the Dart side and sends it before the event (`chip.dart`), so a tap on the selected chip, which changes no state and so patches nothing back, left no chip selected on screen. The start scope is `scope_of(?scope=, ViewMemory.search_scope)`, decided and recorded once in the `use_state` initialiser: the opener's, All for an unknown one (D-180), the remembered one for a View that mounts without a query (a deep entry below a result).<br>• **Query and Back:** a result pushes `/search/<kind>/<id>` (D-141); the Search View stays mounted below it (the Router keeps the stack), so Back shows the same field, results and scroll, with no new search. `ViewMemory` gains `search_query` and `search_scope` (D-18; architecture §6.3), written by the handlers, for a View rebuilt below a result. `search_button()` clears `search_query`: each opening starts an empty search, scoped to the opener; recent queries stay a *(could)*.<br>• **Results:** sessions by day (`DaySessions`, D-185) as `SessionRow`s with the star, notices as `NoticeRow`s (D-184), then `SpeakerRow`s (D-189), then rooms as `LinkTile`s with their count (the Map's row without "Now:", D-188), in `core.search`'s order. Headings carry a level (F40): when the results hold more than one type, "Sessions", "Speakers" and "Rooms" at level 2, without counts (the count line says them), and the days at 3; with one type, no type heading (the count line already says "7 sessions") and the days at 2. A type heading is a step above a day's on screen too: `SectionHeader(theme_style=TITLE_MEDIUM, color=ON_SURFACE)` against the days' `TITLE_SMALL` in `primary`, a `Divider` before every type but the first, and the first day right under it (`top=0`). `ResultList` is memoised and each day is a memoised item keyed by its date given only its saved ids (D-153), so a keystroke before the pause re-renders no row and a star one row. A polite live region under the chips says what was found ("12 sessions, 3 speakers, 1 room", `search.count`).<br>• **States:** before a word, "Find sessions, speakers and rooms" (by scope) with "Search covers all days and ignores your filters." (`search.idle`); a miss, "No results for “…”" with the same sentence (ux.md §4.6), and, in a narrower scope that All would answer, "Show all results" (`search.no_match`), which goes away when used and so, as Clear, puts the focus back in the field; without data, the strip and the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" with Try again (D-182).<br>• **Focus on Back (S4):** Flutter's route focus restoration stands. The field gets the focus back only if it had it when the result opened; after the keyboard's action key (which unfocuses) nothing comes back. No `blur()` exists (the Dart `TextField` reads a `blur` property the Python control lacks), and moving the focus elsewhere would be a hack for a standard behaviour: the first Android Back then closes the keyboard, as for any focused field. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps): Search opened from the Schedule with Sessions selected and the field focused; typing "typing" showed the idle hint until the pause, then "7 sessions" by day; a result pushed its detail and the back arrow returned to the same results with the query and the field's focus; All kept the query; "chen" in All showed sessions by day and speakers under their type headings; Speakers showed the two rows under "2 speakers" with no type heading; Rooms gave the miss with "Show all results", which switched to All; a speaker result pushed `/search/speaker/120` and Back returned; Back from search landed on the Schedule; Clear emptied the field and kept the focus in it; light and dark. At the largest Larger Text the first build's wrapped chips took three rows and, with the keyboard up, the header overflowed by 10 px; with one sideways row nothing overflowed, and with the hint wrapping only while it shows the field shrank to one line once it held text. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`): the field took the focus and the keyboard on open; `uiautomator dump` showed `search.field` as the `EditText`, each chip a `Button` named by its label with All `selected`, `search.count` read "2 sessions, 2 speakers", each session row one `Button` read as its phrase (`search.session.15`) with its star a `Switch` (`search.favorite.15`), and each speaker row a `Button`; after the keyboard's action key a result opened and the Back key returned to the results with "chen" kept, the field unfocused and no keyboard (`mInputShown=false`); dark at 200 % reflowed with the chips scrolling sideways. A keystroke, measured with a throwaway build that logged the debounced search and its patch (D-159's method; the host was loaded, load average 14 to 30 and `Feed processed in 128 ms` against S2's 66): one search per pause, never per keystroke; `core.search` a median 1.0 ms (0.3 to 6.2) over 17 queries and the index 3.3 ms once per version; handler to patch a median 95 ms, from 7 to 57 ms for queries that find a few rows up to 240 to 720 ms for one or two letters that find most of the feed ("p": 326 sessions), the first render of their rows (#216). A real Flet session under the real Router (`tests/test_search_screen.py`) sees no search before the pause, one after it, no row re-rendered by a keystroke before it, and the same View, query and results after Back; the debounce test fails with the search taken from the typed text. After the review (a rebuilt build on both devices, checked against `build/python-app`): "chen" and "grand" in All show "Sessions" (`TITLE_MEDIUM`, `on_surface`) with "Thursday, May 14" close under it in `primary`, and a divider before "Speakers" or "Rooms"; the Rooms scope lists "Grand Ballroom A" and "B" with "26 sessions"; a tap on the selected chip keeps it selected (with `on_select` it showed none); "Show all results" left the cursor in the field; the Sessions scope opens with "Search sessions" over "Find sessions". At the largest Larger Text even "Search sessions" takes two lines, now whole rather than cut ("Search sessions, s…"), and the Speakers filter looks as before. |

In 2026 the rooms list 348 rows: 308 lose the room's name, 16 keep a place elsewhere, and 24 generic breaks had none.<br>• **Tabs:** one per day the room has sessions (`by_day()`, conference-local), the Schedule's tab ("Fri 15", read "Friday, May 15", today's with its dot and ", today", 56 dp), `room.day_tab.<n>`; the room opens on the highlighted session's day, else today (ROOM-02's "today preselected"), else its first day. The picked day is component state set by `on_change`, so a re-render never moves the tab back. All days render at once: a room's day holds a few dozen rows at most (D-156's deferral is the Schedule's).<br>• **Now in this room:** `rooms.room_now(data, slug, now)`, the room's `RoomEntry.on_now` (Now & Next's rule, D-179: the Map, room detail and Now agree), shown by `NowInRoom`, a leaf component on `use_minute()` (D-175), so the fake clock drives it and the screen does not re-render each minute. It heads **today's list**, not the page above the tabs as ux.md §5.9 drew it: there, at the largest Larger Text, the header and a three-line title filled half the screen and left the slots a quarter; in the list it scrolls away with them, and "now" is always today. It is a pointer, not a `SessionRow`: the session's row, with its star, is below it.<br>• **Highlight:** `highlight_of()` takes `?highlight=` from `use_query()` (already unquoted) and keeps it only when it is one of `Room.session_ids`, compared as a string; another room's id or a hand-made one is ignored. The lit box is `Container(~~key=ScrollKey("session-<id>"),~~ bgcolor=...)` (its `ScrollKey` on an empty anchor inside it, D-242) around the whole time slot, its gutter's time too and edge to edge as an M3 selected list item, its divider above the box, as the Schedule's slots (§5.2), when the row has the slot to itself, and around the row alone when it shares it (Room 102C's two 2:00 PM sessions): a row-only tint started at the gutter's edge and left the slot's time unlit, beside the inset Break card; lit with `primary` at 16 % over the surface, not `primary_container`: dark `primary_container` (`#3B3EA9`) puts `on_surface_variant` at 3.7:1, while the tint keeps it at 5.0:1 (light, `#E0E0F1`) and 5.9:1 (dark, `#2E2E38`), close to `primary_container` in light (a test). After a round trip (D-156), the shown day's list, built in full (`cache_extent` 100,000, D-154; `build_controls_on_demand=False` would not register a far key, F32), scrolls the row to its top with `jump_to_key()`, 400 ms or a jump under reduced motion, confirmed by the scroll's end and asked up to 3 times (D-155). It stays lit 2 s once in place (after the scroll's end, or from the start when the list stayed still), then fades in 250 ms, or simply goes under reduced motion (D-173).<br>• **Once per View:** the effect runs at mount; Back finds the View mounted and the query dropped (architecture §5.2), so nothing re-runs. `ViewMemory.room_highlights` maps the View's path to the session it lit; the effect's cleanup removes it when the View leaves (Back), so the next room tile highlights again, while a View rebuilt in a new session (D-143; the old one never unmounts, F30) finds it and neither scrolls nor lights up, still opening on the session's day. | A room's rows under its own name said it twice and doubled a row's height at 200 % (#101's comment); one field for display and phrase keeps them in step. Both years have them, and every track has an icon of its own (Events `CELEBRATION_OUTLINED`, no longer Summits' `GROUPS_OUTLINED`; a test checks), since the hub shows them side by side. A row is built like `SpeakerRow` (D-189), one phrase and a button ("Charlas. Talks given in Spanish. Charla, 16 sessions.", `tracks.track.<slug>`): a 40 dp circle in the track's badge colours with its registry icon, so the hub is also the key to the badges' colours; the plural name; the legend; then the track's badge, "New track" and the count, in one wrapping row; a badge that repeats the name is not read twice ("Lightning talks. Five-minute talks, back to back. 4 sessions."). "New track" (`ui/badges.py`'s `NEW_TRACK`) is outlined in `primary` (`Tokens.new_track`, contrast-tested on every neutral surface), as Registration required is in `error`: a status mark, not one more tonal category. The count is what the page lists (`track_sessions()`: 25 posters, not the Posters row; every open space, whatever the filter). Every row pushes `/more/tracks/<slug>`, Keynotes, Lightning talks and Open spaces included: ~~their own pages (#104, #105) point their rows at themselves when they land~~ the route table opens their own pages at those slugs (D-207), and #106 adds a Sprints row; until then a row that opened a stub page would be a dead end. Without data the skeleton ("Loading tracks"), then "Can't load the tracks" with Try again; a feed without sessions, "No sessions announced yet".<br>• **Track page** (`screens/track.py`, `/more/tracks/<slug>`; `TrackPage` reads the route, `TrackDetail(slug)` is the screen): the intro holds the badges every row would repeat, the track's and, where every session is in Spanish (Charlas), "En Español" (`shared_badges()`), then "New track"; then the plural name as the page's level-1 heading in `HEADLINE_SMALL` (`track.title`), then the legend; the app bar's title stays empty, as session detail's (D-186). ux.md §5.10 put the name in the app bar, where at the largest Larger Text it read "Trailblazing Python Se…". Then a lazy list of days, each one memoised item under a level-2 day heading (F40) with `SessionRow`s and their stars (`track.session.<key>`, `track.favorite.<key>`), no paging, which leave out the intro's badges (`DayGroup`/`SessionRow`/`badge_specs()`'s `omit`) and keep "Registration required"; the Schedule's filter never applies (D-20). An unknown slug shows "This track isn't in the current schedule" (`LABEL_OFF_OUTLINED`, `track.not_found`) under the back arrow with "All tracks", never a redirect (D-186); a registry track without sessions in the feed says "No sessions in this track yet". "Add all to My schedule" is #103's.<br>• **One day item for search and the track pages:** search's memoised `DayGroup` moves to `ui/day_groups.py` with a `screen` argument for its rows' identifiers (D-153, D-193), so both lists re-render one day and one row on a star tap. | Slugs, legends and the "New" flag change with the year, so they are data (D-45), and a slug written out is the one obvious way to a clean URL (DATA-29). Rows that repeated the page's badge spent a pill line, and a screen reader's sentence, per row on what the intro said, which D-186 already avoids on session detail. One list that names each kind, shows its badge and counts its sessions answers both "what is a charla?" and "show me the charlas", which Ionic split between a static legend and nine menu links. `filter_projects(listed, query)` keeps a project when every folded word of the query is a substring of its `search_text` (EVT-06's AND, D-180's matching), and returns `listed` itself for a filter with no word, so the screen knows no filter applies; `cut_short(sprint)`.<br>• **Year data:** `conference.toml` gains the optional `sprints_url` (the site's Development Sprints page) and `sprint_submit_url` (the project form), checked as https URLs like `registration_url`; 2026 has both (Ionic's two pages, both answering on us.pycon.org on 2026-10-13), 2025 neither. `ConferenceInfo.first_sprint_day` is the day after `last_talk_day`, or None when `last_day` is the last talk day: the sprints' days are year data (the feed has no sessions for them, D-126), and Now's key dates use it too. `timefmt.day_range_label()` ("Mon, May 18 – Tue, May 19") moves there from `now_header.py`, which used it privately.<br>• **Screen** (`screens/sprints.py`; the card `screens/sprint_card.py`, so each stays under D-66's 400 lines): an overflow (`sprints.menu`, merged into its button by `named()`, D-190) with "Sprint info on us.pycon.org" and "Submit a sprint project", links read "…, Opens in browser", as session detail's (D-186), each only when the year has its URL; the Speakers list's pinned `SearchField` ("Filter projects") and live count (D-189), the field only when there are projects to filter; then the list: the intro and the days (`sprints.dates`), a level-2 "Projects" heading and a `Card(OUTLINED)` per project, a memoised component keyed by id (D-153), whose name is a level-3 heading (`sprints.project.<id>`, F40) so a screen reader goes card by card.<br>• **The logo** sits on a white 56 dp tile with a hairline `outline_variant` edge, in both schemes: logos are drawn for a white page (dark marks on a transparent ground vanish on the dark surface), as the sponsor spotlight's tile (ux.md §5.1). Without a logo, or when it cannot load (offline), the initials stand in, in the brand indigo (8.6:1 on white), inside a `CircleAvatar`, which does not scale its text (`MediaQuery.withNoTextScaling`), so two letters fit the tile at any text size; a `Text` grew past it.<br>• **"More":** Flet cannot say whether a text overflows its lines, so `cut_short()` decides by the text: a description over `PREVIEW_CHARS` (160, about four lines on a phone at 100 %), or whose Markdown has a line break or a link, shows its text cut at four lines with "More"; a shorter one is never cut, so it shows whole at any size. "More" (`sprints.more.<id>`, read "More about <name>" or "Less about <name>", the visible word first so voice control finds it (WCAG 2.5.3), `Semantics(expanded=)`) swaps the preview for the description's selectable Markdown (`feed_markdown(inset=0)`), whose links open by D-197's rule, and becomes "Less"; the preview takes the Markdown body's size and line height (`BODY_LARGE`, 1.5), so expanding continues the same text. 5 of 2026's 24 projects show whole.<br>• **"Project page"** (`sprints.project_page.<id>`, a link read "<name> project page, Opens in browser") opens `feed_link(url, site_url)` in the in-app browser: three 2026 projects write their site without a scheme (`documentdb.io`), which D-197's rule completes; a URL that names no web page leaves the card without the button.<br>• **States:** no projects: the intro, then "No sprint projects yet" with "Submit a project" (the form, when the year has one) and no field; a miss: "No projects match “…”" with "Clear filter", which puts the focus back in the field; without data, the skeleton ("Loading sprint projects"), then "Can't load the sprints" with Try again.
