On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`, an iPhone 17 of #106's own, light and dark): the page listed the 24 projects by name with their logos on the white tiles (in dark too, where a transparent logo stays legible), the days and the cut descriptions; at the largest Larger Text the field and the intro reflowed with nothing clipped (the cards at large text were checked on Android at 200 %, below). On the shared simulator, driven by taps: "More" on aio-libs showed its Markdown list, code span and links in the preview's size, a tap on "sprint board" opened GitHub in the in-app Safari view and its close returned to the page; "python web" kept five projects with "5 projects" under the field and the list where it was; "zzz" gave the no-match state, whose Clear filter brought every card back; the menu opened us.pycon.org's Development Sprints page in the in-app browser; the Tracks hub ended Community with the Sprints row, which pushed the page. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read the field as `sprints.filter`, `sprints.dates` "Sprints: Monday, May 18 to Tuesday, May 19", each name `sprints.project.<id>`, "More" a 48 dp `Button` "More about …", "Project page" a `Button` "… project page, Opens in browser", the hub row a `Button` `tracks.sprints` read as its phrase, and, once `named()` wrapped it (D-190; session detail's `session.overflow`, still bare, dumped as a `View` "More options" over an empty `Button`: #252), the menu one `Button` `sprints.menu` "More options" whose two items read "…, Opens in browser"; as one card node it had read "by Nick" and the text before the name, hence `semantic_container=False`; dark at 200 % text reflowed the cards, "More" and "Project page" on one line, and CUDA Python showed "CP" on its tile. Back from the page opened from the hub lands on More, not the hub (a sibling page, not its child): #245. |
| D-203 | *(The bar's title without data added by D-211.)* **Speaker detail: the name heads the page, the sessions come by day before the bio, their rows name only the co-speakers, and a placeholder bio is no bio** (records ux.md §5.7's detail for SPKR-03 and SESS-12; amends §5.7's track badges and app-bar title, D-184's speaker rows and section M's `core/` and `ui/` lists; settles #99's comment under D-101).<br>• **Intro** (`screens/speaker.py`'s `intro()`): centred, the photo over the initials (`ui/rows.py`'s `speaker_avatar()` gains `radius=` and `initials_style=`: 48, a 96 dp circle, with `HEADLINE_MEDIUM` initials, named in `on_primary_container` again since a theme style brings the text theme's colour; `CircleAvatar` draws them without the text scale, so they always fit), decorative (`Semantics(exclude_semantics=True)`: the initials would be read before the name), then the name in `HEADLINE_SMALL`, centred, `selectable`, the page's level-1 heading (`speaker.title`, F40), with the app bar's title empty, as session detail's and the track pages' (D-186, D-199). No track badges: each row shows its own, and a speaker's two sessions can be of two tracks.<br>• **Sessions before the bio:** `core/speakers.py`'s pure `speaker_sessions(data, speaker_id)` gives `DaySessions` (D-185), every session that links the speaker (DATA-15), poster details included, in `directory()`'s order (the day, then `schedule_order`), so the page's first session is the Speakers list's (D-189). Each day is `ui/day_groups.py`'s memoised `DayGroup` (D-199) at level 2, `screen="speaker"` (`speaker.session.<key>`, `speaker.favorite.<key>`), so a star tap re-renders one day and one row (D-153). No "Sessions" heading above them: the day headings say what follows, and in both feeds a speaker has one or two sessions, so a heading over a day heading over one row would cost a line and a screen-reader stop.<br>• **Rows name only the co-speakers** (#99's comment): `session_view()`, `session_views()` and `use_session_views()` take `of_speaker`, the speaker's name, as `in_room` takes a room (D-194): `SessionView.speakers` becomes "With Robert Masson" (the others, in the feed's order), or empty when the speaker is alone, and `spoken_phrase()` reads that one field, so the row and its phrase never disagree. "With", so the line does not read as the session's whole cast with the page's speaker missing. Names are compared, not ids: feed processing links authors by name (D-125), and in both feeds every linked speaker's name is among its session's authors (177 links in 2025, 182 in 2026).<br>• **The bio** under `SectionHeader("About", level=2)`, through `feed_markdown()` (`speaker.bio`; D-116, D-197), and only when `has_bio()`: a letter or a digit in its text (`html_md.to_text()`). The feed writes a lone "-" for a bio nobody wrote (2025: 12 speakers; 2026: 7, among them five panellists of "Beyond the Hype"), which showed as "About" over a dash; a word such as "TBD" stays, since the app cannot tell it from a bio.<br>• **States:** a directory speaker the feed links to no session (neither recorded feed has one), "No sessions in the current schedule" (`EVENT_BUSY_OUTLINED`, `speaker.empty`) where the days would be, with no button (the app refreshes on its own, D-175) and the bio still below, as a track page without sessions (D-199); an unknown id, "This speaker isn't in the current schedule" (`PERSON_OFF_OUTLINED`) with "All speakers", under the back arrow titled "Not found" (D-186, `speaker.not_found`); without data, `NoDataState` (the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" with Try again, `speaker.no_data`) under the bar titled "Speaker" (D-211).<br>• **Back** needs nothing new: a row pushes `drill_href(view_path, "session", id)` and session detail's `SpeakerTile` the speaker (D-197), so session → speaker → session → room is five Views that `parent_of()` pops one at a time, from every root and from search (D-17, D-141; a test walks it). | The page answers "who is this and when do they speak" in the order an attendee asks it (ux.md §5.7), with the rows, star and day groups every other list uses, and nothing it repeats: the name once, not on every row (#190's review: a repeated speakers line added noise and, at 200 % text, doubled the row's height), and no section that says "-". On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`; an iPhone 17 of #99's own, deep entries with `--route`): Catherine Nelson (`/now/session/110/speaker/112`) showed her photo and name, Thursday's tutorial "With Robert Masson" and Friday's panel "With Carol Willing, Jelle Zijlstra, Jodie Burchell, Laís Carvalho, Maike Scherer", then About; Justin Lee's one talk had no speakers line; Jelle Zijlstra's initials "JZ" on `primary_container`, and, once `has_bio()` was in, no "About -"; an unknown id the not-found state under "Not found"; light, dark and the largest Larger Text (about 310 %), where the name wrapped by words and the badges, titles and meta reflowed with nothing clipped apart from the titles' 3-line ellipsis (D-184), whose full text the row's phrase says; at the largest text the panel's 13-word title stopped at its third line too. On the shared simulator, driven by taps: Now › session 110 › Catherine Nelson › the tutorial › Room 101A pushed one View each; three left-edge swipes (the Cupertino slide, the page below showing through mid-swipe) and the back arrow popped room → session → speaker → session → Now, one step each; Schedule › "Python Performance Lab" › Arthur Pastel showed "With Adrien Cacciaguerra", a star tap filled the star and a second showed "Removed from My schedule" with Undo, and two back arrows returned to the Schedule at the same place. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`, gesture navigation with predictive back on): `uiautomator dump` read the name as `speaker.title`, each day heading as a node of its own, each row a `Button` (`speaker.session.106`: "Tutorial, Registration required. Going from Notebooks to Production Code. 9:00 AM to 12:30 PM, Room 101A. With Robert Masson."), each star a `Switch` and `speaker.bio` the bio, the photo no node; the same chain popped one step per Back key and per left-edge gesture, and Back on Now left the app. Dark at 200 % text reflowed with nothing clipped apart from the panel's title, cut at its third line by the rows' 3-line ellipsis (D-184), whose full text the row's phrase says. |
| D-205 | *(The hub's rows amended by D-207; their list's module by D-228.)* **The Open spaces and Lightning talks pages: day tabs from the feed, the pages' copy and links as year data, and the hub's rows open them** (records EVT-01, EVT-02 and EVT-03, D-15's Programme for both and D-31 for the open spaces' slots; amends ux.md §5.10's hub rows, §5.11, §4.6 and §3.6's list tiles, architecture §8.1's schema, and section M's `screens/`, `core/`, `ui/` `year_schema.py` and `year_pages.py` entries; #105).<br>• **Year data:** `content.toml` gains two optional tables. `[open_spaces]`: `intro` and `propose_url`; `[lightning_talks]`: `heading`, `intro` (paragraphs), `signup_url`, `info_url` and `[[lightning_talks.doubts]]` (`doubt`, `answer`). 2026's are said in our own words from the site's public pages (the sign-ups `/2026/signup/open-space/` and `/2026/signup/lightning-talk/`, which ask the attendee to sign in; the info page `/2026/events/lightning-talks/`), with no exclamation marks (ux.md §4.9). The loader (`year_schema.build()`) learns a table of its own, an `X \| None` field built as its dataclass, and refuses a URL that is not `https://` with a host and an empty text. The two tables' dataclasses and these checks (`check_pages()`) are in `year_pages.py`, which `config.py` imports: with them `config.py` passed D-66's ~400 lines (428), without them it has 359. A year without them (2025) shows the pages without the action, the intro, the card and the fold.<br>• **Open spaces** (`screens/open_spaces.py`, `/more/open-spaces`): the app bar's `ADD` "Propose an open space", in the in-app browser (the link rule, INFO-14), a link read "Propose an open space, Opens in browser" with its tooltip out of the semantics (D-184); `ui/links.py`'s `external()` marks such a button as a link and names it (`open_spaces.propose`, `lightning_talks.sign_up`). A tab per day the feed has open spaces on (`track_sessions()`), the Schedule's `day_tab()` (`open_spaces.day_tab.<n>`), so the labels are dates and never Ionic's "Sunday"; the page opens on today when it has open spaces, else the first day (`opening_day()`), and keeps the picked tab across renders. Each day is a memoised lazy list (D-153) given only its own saved ids: the year's intro line, then gutter slots (`TimeSlot`) of `SessionRow`s with stars that push the session, without the "Open space" badge (D-199). The slots come from `row_views.by_slot()`, room detail's grouping by time group start moved into `core` so both share it; it returns its slots by start whatever order the views come in, since the open spaces come by the start a row shows (`schedule_order`), which a display-time override can put before an earlier slot. The intro heads each day's list rather than sitting above the tabs, where at large text it would take the room the slots need (D-194's reason). No thumbnail yet: #242 decides. The Schedule's filter never applies (D-20).<br>• **Lightning talks** (`screens/lightning_talks.py`, `/more/lightning-talks`): a `Card(FILLED)` with the copy (its heading a level-2 heading, the paragraphs in `BODY_LARGE`) and `FilledButton("Sign up for a lightning talk", icon=OPEN_IN_NEW)`, its text read "…, Opens in browser" (D-186: on the text, as an icon's label is read first); "Think you can't do one?", a `FoldTile` that starts collapsed and is controlled, so a star tap below never folds it (D-191), each doubt one node; "More about lightning talks", an external `LinkTile` with the host as its subtitle; then the talks by day (`DayGroup`, D-199) with stars, without the "Lightning talks" badge. The info comes before the talks, so the page reads as what, how to sign up, then when; ux.md had the link last.<br>• **One entry each:** More's Programme row opens these pages at `/more/lightning-talks` and `/more/open-spaces`; the Tracks hub's row (~~`tracks.OWN_PAGES`, by track key, the feed's kinds~~ ~~`config.HUB_PAGES`~~ `year_tracks.HUB_PAGES`, D-207, D-228) opens the same pages at `/more/tracks/lightning-talks` and `/more/tracks/open-spaces`, routes listed under the hub before its `:slug` route, so the View stack holds the hub and Back returns to it (D-17), and the generic track page never shows for these two slugs. Lightning talks appear once in More (a test).<br>• **Polish:** the theme's `ListTileTheme(title_alignment=TITLE_HEIGHT)` keeps a tall tile's icons beside its title's first line, the rule `LinkTile` follows (D-188), for every `ListTile` without its own alignment and for fold headers, which have no setting of their own: at 200 % the wrapped "Think you can't do one?" had its icons beside the middle of its title. The fold's title is a row's title, as the link row under it, not `TITLE_MEDIUM`. The sign-up has 10 dp above and below its label (M3's 16/24 dp at the sides), so a label wrapped at 200 % keeps clear of the pill's edge; one line stays 40 dp. The open spaces' intro is one sentence that names the action ("… or tap + to host one."). The loader also refuses an empty `doubts`.<br>• **States:** without data the open spaces show the skeleton, then "Can't load the schedule" (`NoDataState`), with the action still there; the lightning copy stays above the same states; a feed with no open spaces says "No open spaces yet" with "Propose one", the same sign-up as `+` and so an external link like it (`OPEN_IN_NEW`, "Propose one, Opens in browser", `open_spaces.propose_one`; `EmptyState(link_id=...)`), one with no lightning talks "No lightning talks in the schedule yet". | A tab per day the feed has is the one way to never mislabel a day (Ionic's "Sunday"), and the Schedule's tab keeps the two pages alike. The pages' words and links change with the year and come from the site, so they are year data (D-45), in our own words rather than the site's copy. One entry per page, from More and from the hub, is what SHELL-10 asked of Ionic's two lightning-talks targets. In a real Flet session (`tests/test_open_spaces.py`) a star tap re-renders one day list and one row, and fails with `ft.memo` taken off the day list. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds, the bundle checked against `build/python-app`; an iPhone 17 of #105's own, light and dark): Open spaces showed "Fri 15", "Sat 16", "Sun 17", the intro, then gutter slots of rows with stars and no badge; Lightning talks the card, the folded tile, the link and the four talks under their days. At the largest Larger Text the one-sentence intro left the first slot on the first screen, the tabs scrolled sideways, and the card's heading and text reflowed with nothing clipped. On the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", driven by taps: the hub's Lightning talks row opened the page; "Think you can't do one?" opened, stayed open through a scroll, a star tap below it and a session pushed and popped; a star filled and a second tap showed "Removed from My schedule" with Undo; "More about lightning talks" and "+" opened us.pycon.org in the in-app Safari view (the open-space sign-up asks to sign in: "You must be registered for PyCon US to host an Open Space"), whose close returned to the page; "Sun 17" showed Sunday's open spaces, a row pushed its session and Back returned to Sunday. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read `open_spaces.propose` as a `Button` "Propose an open space / Opens in browser" (a link is a button to TalkBack, F41), each tab "Tab 1 of 3 / Friday, May 15", each row a `Button` read as its phrase ("Web Scraping. 10:00 to 11:00 AM, S-3.") with its star a `Switch`; a row pushed the session and the Back key returned; dark at 200 % text wrapped the card, the sign-up button's label (clear of the pill's edge), the fold's title (its icons by its first line, as the link row's) and the rows with nothing clipped. After the reviews (#248): on the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", the hub's Lightning talks row opened the page and its back arrow returned to the hub, as did the back arrow of `/more/tracks/open-spaces`; on the emulator, the Developer feed URL pointed at a local copy of the feed without open spaces showed "No open spaces yet" with "Propose one" (`OPEN_IN_NEW`), which `uiautomator` read as `open_spaces.propose_one`, a `Button` "Propose one / Opens in browser". |
| D-207 | *(The list's module amended by D-228.)* **The Tracks hub's pages of their own also live under the hub, so Back from any page it opens returns to it** (settles #245 as option 2, D-101; amends D-199's and D-205's hub rows, D-202's Sprints row, D-17's note, ux.md §5.10 and §5.11, architecture §5.2 and §5.3, and section M's More pages; #245).<br>• **Rule:** a More page that a hub row opens is also a static child of the hub, `/more/tracks/<segment>`, listed before `:slug`, which would match it too: `/more/tracks/sprints` (Sprints), `/more/tracks/lightning-talks` and `/more/tracks/open-spaces`; #104 adds `/more/tracks/keynotes`. The Router builds the stack from the location (More, Tracks, the page), and `parent_of()` walks it, so one Back returns to the hub (D-17, D-164). Each page keeps its More entry at its own location (`/more/sprints`, …), whose Back returns to More; the other cross-links (Now's Sprints row, the Schedule's "See the sprints") switch to the More tab and keep `/more/sprints`.<br>• **One list:** ~~`config.HUB_PAGES`~~ `year_tracks.HUB_PAGES` (D-228) maps each segment to the key of the track whose page it is (the feed's kinds, DATA-14), or `None` for the sprints, which are not a track (D-126). It sits beside the registry it constrains, so the year loader stays free of Flet and dependencies keep pointing down (architecture §1): `locations.py` imports it from ~~`config.py`~~ `year_tracks.py` (D-228). `PAGES` takes the pages' locations from it, and a test keeps both equal to the route table. Every hub row pushes `/more/tracks/<slug>` and the Sprints row `/more/tracks/sprints`; `tracks.OWN_PAGES` and `hub_target()` go, since the route table picks the screen.<br>• **Loader:** `load_year()` refuses a track slug that names one of these pages unless the page is that track's (`tracks[6].slug: 'sprints' is the segment of another page`), and a track with a page of its own whose slug is not the page's segment (`expected 'lightning-talks'`): its row pushes its slug, which must be its page. | One rule for every row of the hub, decided once, rather than three behaviours: from the hub, Back from Sprints showed More (#243 on the iOS 26.5 simulator), a sibling page, not the hub's child. Two locations per page cost one line each in the table and `HUB_PAGES`; option 3 (the track page linking on to the dedicated page) would add a step to every visit. On the shared iOS 26.5 "PyCon iPhone 17" (debug build checked against `build/python-app`), driven by taps: More › Tracks & session types › Sprints, then the back arrow, showed the hub at the same scroll, and so did an edge swipe; Open spaces (edge swipe) and Lightning talks (back arrow) returned to the hub too; More › Sprints › back arrow showed More. A `--route=/more/tracks/sprints` entry on a simulator of #245's own built More, Tracks, Sprints. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), `uiautomator dump` after each Back key: the hub's `tracks.*` rows after Sprints, Open spaces and Lightning talks, and `more.title` after More › Sprints. |
| D-208 | *(Its TalkBack note amended by D-225: the check, not the app.)* **Back with a dialog open closes the dialog only: every pushed View declares an `on_confirm_pop`, so Flet leaves Back to Flutter's Navigator** (fixes #235; amends how D-141's Back key reaches our handler; F46, U33).<br>• **The cause is in Flet:** at the pin, Flet takes the Android Back (key or committed gesture, and VoiceOver's escape on iOS, which Flutter also sends as `popRoute`) before Flutter's Navigator and, when the top View can pop and has no `on_confirm_pop`, pops that View itself, without looking for a dialog, picker, sheet or menu open over it; the open route goes with the View's page (F46). Our `page.on_view_pop` only followed a `view_pop` Flet had already acted on, and ignoring it there would have left Flutter without the View and Python with it.<br>• **Interim:** `AppScaffold` gives every pushed View, `can_pop` still true, an async `on_confirm_pop` that awaits `confirm_pop(True)` (`let_pop`). With a handler, Flet hands Back to `Navigator.maybePop()`, which pops the topmost route: the open dialog, picker, sheet or menu, else the View, whose pop reaches `on_view_pop` through `onDidRemovePage`, the path a committed predictive gesture and the iOS edge swipe already take (D-141, D-148). Flet calls the handler only for a pop that `can_pop` refuses, which a pushed View never does, so it lets any such pop through. Roots keep D-142: non-Now roots already have a handler, and Now is a one-View stack, where Flet always leaves Back to the Navigator. One rule for every View the app builds, and every modal route at once, Flutter's own menus included.<br>• **Not `can_pop=False` while a dialog is open:** every opener would have to report opening and closing (a `PopupMenuButton`'s menu, a picker's dismiss), a missed close would leave Back doing nothing on that View, and `can_pop=False` turns off the iOS edge swipe and the predictive animation (D-148).<br>• **Upstream (U33, #259):** #259 has the full analysis and the minimal repro (also in [flet-dev/flet#6964](https://github.com/flet-dev/flet/issues/6964)); the suggested fix: return `null` from Flet's system-pop handler while the Navigator's top route is not a page. When the pin has it, `let_pop` goes in its own PR (D-64). | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APKs, gesture navigation), a build of `main` reproduced #235: one Back key press with Developer options' "Clear the cached schedule?", the fake clock's date picker, its time picker or the Sprints page's menu open landed on More. With this change, `uiautomator dump` showed Back closing only the confirmation (the cache kept), the date picker, the time picker (the field left empty), "Reset the app?" and the menu, the View staying; a second Back popped one level; the Back gesture did the same as the key; two quick Back presses on Developer went to More and then Now, as two gestures do; on the Schedule root, Back with the filter sheet open closed the sheet alone, as on `main` (D-142's handler). A minimal app without the Router (two Views, an `AlertDialog` or a `DatePicker`) showed the same `view_pop` with the dialog's dismiss, which #259 carries (and flet-dev/flet#6964). On the shared iOS 26.5 simulator (the build checked against `build/python-app`), the edge swipe with the confirmation open closed neither it nor the View (the dialog's barrier takes the touch), and without a dialog it still popped Developer options to More. With TalkBack on (the same emulator), the Back key with the confirmation open closed only the confirmation, and TalkBack's focus went to the app bar's Back arrow rather than back to the row; closing with Cancel does no better, so that is #262, not this change (~~a defect~~ an artifact of the check, which opened the dialog with an `adb` tap: D-225). VoiceOver's escape is read from the engine source, not seen: the Simulator has no VoiceOver, so it waits for the iPhone (#261). Keeping the handler on the Views and off the openers is the one place that needs no bookkeeping, and the pin's own Navigator does the rest. |
| D-209 | *(The item's tile built by `ui/menus.py`'s `menu_item()` since D-233.)* **A track page's "Add all to My schedule": an overflow item that saves the whole track, then one SnackBar with Undo** (records SCHED-24 and ux.md §5.10's *(#103)* line; amends section M's `ui/` list; #103).<br>• **Where:** the track page's app bar (`screens/track.py`) gets an overflow, `PopupMenuButton(MORE_VERT, tooltip="More options")` (`track.menu`, merged into its node with `named()`), with one item, "Add all to My schedule" (`track.add_all`), its content a `ListTile` (the `PLAYLIST_ADD` icon as `leading`, the label as `title`, `min_height=48` and `horizontal_spacing=12` for the menu's row and gap), so the label wraps at 200 % text rather than running off the menu (F47), with the icon beside its first line by the theme's `TITLE_HEIGHT` (D-188). It is offered only where it can do something (ux.md §1, no dead control): on a page that lists sessions (not while the feed loads or after it failed, not on a track without sessions), not on the open spaces, whose slug is the Open spaces page's own route (`/more/tracks/open-spaces`, before the slug route, D-205), a page without an overflow, so no track page shows them, and not on the notices and breaks (`track.offers_add_all()`: the `logistics` group), whose rows have no star (D-31).<br>• **What it saves:** every session the page lists, `track_sessions()`'s list on every day (each poster, never the Posters row, D-199), not only what is on screen, in one `Favorites.add_many()` (D-172): one assignment, one notification, one write. No dialog first, since it is reversible (D-29). The selection click when it saved any, as a star's add gives (ux.md §4.5).<br>• **The SnackBar** (`ui/badges.py`'s hook `use_add_all()` and the pure `added_feedback()`; one SnackBar builder, `_show()`, for them and the star's `use_favorite_toggle()`, so floating, 4 s, and `persist` with a screen reader as D-184): "Added 16 sessions · Undo", or "Added 1 session · Undo", counting only `Change.added`, so the sessions saved before are neither counted nor removed by Undo; "All of them are already in My schedule" (`ALREADY_SAVED`) with no Undo when nothing was new, and nothing is written; "Couldn't save your change" with Undo when the write failed (ux.md §4.4). **Undo** is `Favorites.undo(change)`: the list is again what it was, sessions saved before included. It says nothing more, as a star's Undo of a removal says nothing; only a failed write speaks, "Couldn't save your change", with no Undo of the Undo (the menu offers the add again). On the iOS 26.5 simulator and the Android emulator it showed on the track page, whether the page was opened by deep link or pushed from the hub (#255; #197 tracks the pushed Views where a SnackBar was not seen). | The star's flow was the model: one store edit, one `Change`, one SnackBar. `_announce()` would answer the Undo with "Removed from My schedule · Undo" for the 16 sessions the user had just asked to drop again, a second message about the same tap; Material's Undo closes its SnackBar, and the stars on the page show the result. A count of the new ids only is what D-172's `Change` was shaped for, and an "Add all" on an empty or loading page, or on rows without stars, would be a dead control. An item in the overflow keeps the app bar to the back arrow and one icon, as session detail's overflow does (D-186), and leaves room for P2's "Add all to calendar". |
| D-217 | *(`KEYNOTE_TRACKS`' module amended by D-228.)* **Keynotes: their people and words are year data keyed by session id and checked against the bundled snapshot; a card per keynote and panel; session detail shows them** (records SESS-08 and SPKR-04; amends D-45's note, D-199's count, ux.md §5.5, §5.10 and §5.11, architecture §5.2 and §8, and section M's year modules, `core/`, `screens/` and routes; #104).<br>• **Year data** (`content.toml`'s `[[keynotes]]`, one entry per session): `session_id` (the feed's id as a string, D-42), `kind` (`keynote` or `panel`), an optional `title` (the talk's own: the feed names a keynote by its speaker) and `abstract` (Markdown), and `[[keynotes.speakers]]`: `name`, `role`, optional `photo_url` and `bio`. 2026: the four keynotes and the D&I and Steering Council panels, said in our own words, in US English, from the site's keynote-speakers page; the photos are the site's 165 px copies (`.max-165x165`), as its originals weigh up to 5.7 MB. The dataclasses and `check_keynotes()` (a known kind, one entry per session, someone on stage, no blank name or role, `https://` photos) are in `year_keynotes.py`, as the pages' are in `year_pages.py` (D-205): `config.py` had 416 lines.<br>• **Unknown session ids:** `load_year(check_sessions=True)` processes the year's bundled snapshot and refuses an entry whose session it lacks (`2026/content.toml: keynotes[0].session_id: '999' is not a session of the year's feed`), or whose session is on a track no keynote has (~~`config.KEYNOTE_TRACKS`~~ `year_tracks.KEYNOTE_TRACKS`, D-228, `Keynote` and `Plenary`: `'142' is a Talk session, not a keynote or a plenary`), since a typo that lands on a talk would put it on the page and replace its speakers on its detail; and a year with keynotes but no snapshot. A test loads every year this way, and `tools/update_snapshot.py` refuses a new feed that drops a keynote's session or moves it to another track, writing nothing. The app's own start does not ask: parsing the snapshot at every start would undo D-38's on-demand read, and at run time a keynote a newer feed drops simply has no card. A test also looks for every name and title of the year data in the package's modules: no keynote text in code.<br>• **The page** (`screens/keynotes.py`; `/more/keynotes`, and `/more/tracks/keynotes` under the hub, `HUB_PAGES["keynotes"] = "Keynote"`, D-207): `timeline.keynote_sessions()` lists every session of the keynotes' track and every session the year data names (the panels are plenaries in the feed), by `schedule_order`, memoised per feed. One memoised `Card(ELEVATED)` per session (`screens/keynote_card.py`, D-153), keyed by its id: a keynote's people, a 96 dp photo each (`speaker_avatar(radius=48)`, which now takes a keynote person too, decorative) over the name (`TITLE_LARGE`, a level-2 heading) and role (stacked: beside the photo, the largest text left the name a word a line); the talk's title; the bios cut at 4 lines with "Read more" / "Show less", read "Read more about <speaker>" (a panel's "about <title>"). A panel's: the feed's name (`LABEL_LARGE`, `primary`) over its own title (the heading), the abstract cut the same way, and its people as a wrapping row of 64 dp photos over their names, each name its own width so it breaks only between words. "Read more" shows for a text over 80 characters or with a paragraph break, about four lines at 200 % text, since Flet cannot say whether a text was cut (U16). Under a divider the time-and-room tile pushes the session from the page's View, beside its star. A keynote the year data lacks still gets a card, with its name. `screens/more_pages.py`, the last More stub, goes.<br>• **The hub** counts the Keynotes row as its page lists, the panels included (6 in 2026; D-199's rule), and its legend says so: "Featured talks and panels by invited speakers".<br>• **Session detail** (`screens/keynote_people.py`): a session with an entry lists the entry's people under Speakers ("Panelists" for a panel), a 48 dp photo beside the name and role, one node, then the whole bio, selectable, with no chevron (the feed links no speaker to them, so they have no page); About shows the talk's title over the abstract, which replaces the feed's description, a link to the site; without an abstract that link stays. | SESS-08's one data source, where Ionic kept two hard-coded copies matched by substring. Checking ids against the snapshot catches a typo or a dropped keynote on the Mac, in CI or in the snapshot tool, never on a phone, at no cost to the start. Counting the panels keeps D-199's promise that a row's count is its page's. On the shared iOS 26.5 "PyCon iPhone 17" (a simulator build whose bundle matched `build/python-app`), driven by taps: More › Keynotes showed the six cards in light, the photos loading over their initials; "Read more" on the Steering Council panel opened its abstract and turned into "Show less"; a card's tile opened the session, whose detail showed both Djangonaut Space speakers with their bios and About with the talk's title over the abstract, and Back returned to the page; Tracks & session types showed "Keynotes … Keynote, 6 sessions", its row opened the page and Back returned to the hub. On a simulator of #104's own, dark mode and the largest text size: beside a 96 dp photo the name broke a word a line, so the photo moved over the name. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, an arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): light, dark and 200 % text; `uiautomator dump` read the name as a `View` heading node, "Read more about Your AI Product Doesn't Have a Moat… Yet" and the tile "Friday, May 15 / 9:45 to 10:30 AM / Pacific Ballroom - Arena" as buttons and the star as a switch, and from the hub the Keynotes row then the Back key returned to the hub. |
