| D-218 | **Search renders each type's first rows, with a "Show all" row for the rest** (settles #216; amends D-193's results; adds `first_results()` to section M's `core/search.py`).<br>• **The cut:** `core/search.py`'s pure `first_results(results, *, sessions, speakers)` keeps the first `sessions` sessions in `search()`'s order (the last day shown cut short, never an empty day), the first `speakers` speakers and every room (at most 34 in a year's feed); the days it leaves whole, and the results themselves when nothing is cut, are the same objects, so D-153's memoised path holds. `screens/search_results.py` shows `FIRST_SESSIONS = 50` and `FIRST_SPEAKERS = 20`.<br>• **"Show all":** under a cut type, a `ListTile` row whose words say the full count, "Show all 336 sessions" ("Show all 147 speakers"), in `BODY_LARGE` and `primary` from the rows' 16 dp line, with `EXPAND_MORE` at its end, 56 dp; one merged node read as its words and a button (`search.show_all.sessions`, `search.show_all.speakers`). A tap renders the rest of that type in the row's place; the other type stays cut. The count line keeps the full counts, so every result is counted and one tap away.<br>• **State:** `ResultList` holds which types are shown whole together with the results they were asked of: a keystroke that changes nothing keeps them open, other results start cut again, and so does the old query typed again after them (an effect forgets the opened types once other results show).<br>• **Not chosen:** a row cache across queries (#216's other option): the first broad query of a session still builds every row, and taking ~300 rows away again (a clear, or a narrower query) cost as much as a narrow query's render; "Show 50 more" steps: several taps to reach the end for the same rows. | #216: rendering every row of a one-letter query was the slow part, not the search. A throwaway timing build (never committed) logged D-159's handler → patch for the debounced search on my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (release arm64 APK, 2026 feed, All scope, "p", "a" and "e" typed and deleted 12 times each, two passes each way; the host shared with other agents' builds and emulators, load average 9 to 50, `Feed processed in` 84 to 520 ms against S2's 66): before, a one-letter query's render took a median **608 ms** (339 to 1,556; "p" 586, "a" 664, "e" 575) and clearing it 228 ms; after, a median **102 ms** (59 to 1,922, the outlier during a load spike; "p" 96, "a" 97, "e" 104), the band of queries that find a few dozen rows in D-193 (7 to 120 ms), and clearing it 36 ms. "Show all" on request: the speakers' 127 more rows a median 203 ms (157 to 275), the sessions' 286 more 441 ms (308 to 725). On the host (`tests/test_search_screen.py`'s harness, busy M4) "p" went from 428-1,193 ms to 53-56 ms and "a" from 693-923 ms to 37-46 ms. D-159's 16 ms budget is a keystroke's and a star's: a keystroke before the pause still re-renders no row, which a test checks with the list cut and opened. On the Android 14 emulator (the committed code, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`) `uiautomator dump` read each row as one clickable `Button`, `search.show_all.sessions` described "Show all 336 sessions"; a tap put "CTF for fun and profit" and the rest where the row was, light, dark and 200 % text (the words on one line, the icon beside them). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (a debug build checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps) the Sessions scope ended its 50 rows with the row and a tap rendered the rest in place; on an iPhone 17 simulator of #216's own, a throwaway build (never committed) that seeded the query and cut at one or three rows showed it in dark, and at the largest Larger Text its words wrapped to two lines with `EXPAND_MORE` beside the first, nothing clipped. |
| D-254 | **The expo's shared and empty booths, and the job fair pane: a booth card keeps every exhibitor, a booth without one shows its label and an icon, the job fair pane is one list headed by a plan card, its tables follow the feed with the year's note, and the print is cropped to the floor** (settles #365, `P2-05`, under D-101; amends ux.md §3.9's `LocationCard`, §6.2 and §6.3, architecture §2's and §7.5's `core/` modules and §8.1's `map.toml`, section M's `core/` and `ui/` lists, and the matrix's EXPO-02, EXPO-03, MAP-04 and SPON-10).<br>• **Shared booths:** a booth lists every sponsor whose `booth_number` is its number. Its card is headed "Booth 726 · Community" (the level only when all its exhibitors share it) over "4 exhibitors", then one 48 dp row per exhibitor in folded-name order (D-46): logo on its white tile, name, chevron, a tap that pushes `…/sponsor/<slug>`, one node each ("PyBeach, Community sponsor"); no "Sponsor details", since each row is that. The map node reads "Booth 726, 4 exhibitors", and the card is the same whichever exhibitor's "Find booth" opened it. A card taller than half the pane (both heights from `on_size_change`, F51) scrolls its rows inside a box half the pane high, so the selected booth stays in sight at the largest text. A booth of one keeps ux.md §6.2's card.<br>• **Booths without an exhibitor:** `[[booths]]` gains an optional `label` (2026: 407 "Python Software Foundation", 606 "Attendee lounge"); the card shows the label, a `PLACE_OUTLINED` icon on `surface_container_highest` where a logo would be (no PSF or PyCon logo, D-36), and no sponsor action or roles line; its node reads "Booth 407, Python Software Foundation". The feed wins: a label names only a booth without an exhibitor, and a test over the bundled snapshot fails when a labelled booth also has one.<br>• **The expo List** has one row per exhibitor (a booth without one is a row by its label), by booth number in natural order with a shared booth's exhibitors together, or by name, so every name has one place in either order; a row selects its booth with its card. This replaces #404's "shared booths as one row", whose name order had no single name to sort by.<br>• **The job fair pane is one scrolling list** (#365's option b): `job_fair_card()`, `LocationCard`'s plan-only form, pushing `…/plan/job-fair` (the floor viewer without pins); the year's note; a level-2 "Tables" heading; then one row per sponsor with a table number, tables in natural order, a shared table's sponsors together by name, "T2 · Bloomberg · 15 open roles" (the count only with a listing), read "Table T2, Bloomberg, 15 open roles", pushing the sponsor; without tables, the card and "No job fair tables yet". The same card is Job listings' (SPON-10) and Job Fair sessions' (SESS-10). Not chosen: option a, a fixed-height viewer over the list.<br>• **Feed against print:** the rows follow the feed, which a refresh can correct during the conference, while the image changes only with a build. The `job-fair` plan in `map.toml` gains an optional `note`, shown under the pane's card as a paragraph that wraps with no line limit, and not in the viewer, where it would cover the plan at the largest text and its "this list" has no list; 2026's: "The printed plan shows some tables with other sponsors. This list follows the conference's sponsor data." The image is cropped to the floor (tables, the food and beverage area, the entrance), without the print's header and its PyCon US 2026 logo (D-36); the viewer's app bar names the plan.<br>• **Table markers are adopted, as their own issue** (#464, under #23): a ratio rectangle per printed table T1 to T20 on the cropped image, `plan/job-fair?table=T8` selecting it as `?booth=` selects a booth, and a "Show table T8 on the plan" button on each pane row and behind sponsor detail's "Job fair table T8". They are tagged on the cropped image, so they follow #407. | Ionic seeded a label per booth and overwrote it with each sponsor of that number, so booth 726 showed one of its four community groups, and 407 and 606 got static PSF and PyCon logos (`expo-hall-map.component.ts`, `STATIC_BOOTH_LOGOS` and `loadSponsors()`). The 2026 snapshot has 105 sponsors and 61 booth numbers: 726 (SoCal Python, Inland Empire Python Users Group, PyBeach, San Diego Python), 727 (PyCon Africa, PyCon Senegambia, Python Mozambique Community), 728 (Data Con LA, SCaLE) and 734 (Django Software Foundation, Djangonaut Space), all Community; and 19 sponsors at 16 job fair tables, two each at T7 (Red Hat, Chainguard), T10 (Posit, marimo) and T11 (Kraken Tech, Black Python Devs). Ionic's printed plan (`job-fair.jpg`, 1700 × 2164) shows Temporal at T7, Chainguard at T8 (feed: Reuven Lerner), marimo at T12 (feed: Tetrix) and Black Python Devs at T16 (feed: PyCon Africa), and SentinelOne, Reuven Lerner, PyCon Africa and Tetrix at T13 and T17 to T19, which the feed leaves empty. Neither source can be shown to be right, so the list follows the one that can change without a build and says so, as data (D-45). A split pane fails the way #331 shows the Schedule's header failing at the largest text, and a zoom layer in a scrolling page fights its pans (MAP-01); a pushed viewer gets the whole screen. A row per exhibitor gives the List and search one name per row and a screen reader one sentence per sponsor; an icon for a place is the Rooms list's (D-188). No device check: this is a decision for the build issues (#401, #403, #404, #407, #464), which carry its checks. |

## D. Design system

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-22 | Colour: `ft.Theme(color_scheme_seed="#3B3EA9", surfaces=ft.ThemeSurfaces.NEUTRAL, color_scheme=...)` with brand overrides. Light: primary `#3B3EA9`, secondary `#680579`, tertiary `#A1009A` (Ionic's magenta `#DD04D2` darkened to 7:1 on white). Dark uses tuned light tones. Both platforms are identical. Ionic's default blue `#3880ff` and its iOS `#000` / Android `#121212` dark split are dropped. | The PyCon palette in M3, with the magenta derived for contrast (D-25). Flet builds `fromSeed`, neutralises surfaces, then applies the overrides (F9). |
| D-23 | Four colour schemes: light and dark (P0), plus high-contrast light and dark (P2). The high-contrast schemes are hand-authored overrides with 7:1 for text. Settings has Appearance (System, the default / Light / Dark) and a High contrast switch. The switch defaults to the OS flag where the OS reports it (iOS, Android 34+), re-read on resume. | Accessibility. Flet has no contrast-level option, so HC is explicit overrides. Two settings are simpler than Ionic's four-way picker. |
| D-24 | A small frozen `Tokens` dataclass, read through `use_tokens()`, holds what M3 lacks: `live`, `warning`, `success` and `favorite`, plus the track palette. | M3 has no success or warning roles. |
| D-25 | Track badges are tonal pairs per track (container fill plus text colour, in light and dark), every pair at least 4.5:1, outlined in high contrast. Badges always carry text. The "En Español" badge is the tonal pair `#FFD7F3` / `#5E0058` (10.1:1) in light, inverted in dark (Ionic's `#DD04D2` is 4.18:1). A unit test checks every text/background pair in every scheme. | A quiet, content-first M3 look; colour is never the only signal. |
| D-26 | **prov. O-3.** *(Font route amended by D-105.)* Bundle **Roboto** (400/500/700) as `Theme.font_family`. `page.fonts` maps a family to one static file, so the three weights are declared ~~as a `flutter.fonts` entry (family `Roboto`, three assets with `weight: 400/500/700`) through `[tool.flet.flutter.pubspec]` (F18)~~ in a local font-only Flutter package added through `[tool.flet.flutter.pubspec]` (F18, D-105). Packaged builds get all three; `flet run` falls back to the system font (accepted). ~~S5 verifies that the font assets resolve in the generated Flutter project and that the three weights render on iOS.~~ S5 verified that the three weights render on iOS and Android. A multi-weight `page.fonts` is upstream candidate U20. Text uses only the M3 type scale (`Text(theme_style=...)`), with no fixed heights around text. Layouts reflow at 200 % OS text, and long text is selectable. | Without a bundled font, iOS renders SF Pro (D-10). The M3 scale is tuned for Roboto, and its digits are tabular; `TextStyle` has no `font_features` (F8). |
| D-27 | *(The tooltip's node amended by D-234.)* Material Icons only: outlined when unselected or decorative, filled when selected. Brand logos are bundled SVGs. Every icon-only button has a tooltip, which is also its accessible name, merged into the button's node (D-234). | One icon family; accessibility. |
| D-28 | Chrome: a surface-coloured `AppBar` whose title is always visible, and no gradient heroes on content screens. Brand colour is reserved for identity moments: Welcome, the Now status header and About PyCon US. | Heroes cost 150-200 px before any content, and hidden titles left screen readers without a page name. |
| D-29 | Feedback: a floating `SnackBar` (one action, Undo when reversible) for transient results; a `Banner` for persistent conditions that need action; an `AlertDialog` only before destructive actions. Results the user waits for appear in place. No top toasts and no blocking loaders. | Material-native; Flet's `SnackBar` is bottom-only (F11). |
| D-30 | Favourites have one control: a 48 dp star on every session row and in session detail, labelled "Add to / Remove from My schedule". Adding fills the star, with a haptic. Removing shows "Removed from My schedule · Undo". Swipe-to-favourite is dropped. | Discoverable and accessible, with Undo where a row can vanish. |
| D-210 | **A SnackBar shown from a pushed View is seen on that View: D-29's SnackBars serve every screen, and the "until #197" notes go** (settles #197; amends the notes on SnackBars in D-187, D-197, D-198 and D-206, ux.md §4.7, §5.5, §5.12 and §5.13, and the feature matrix's SESS-06 note; records F48).<br>• **No defect at the pin, in Flet or in our Views:** `page.show_dialog(SnackBar(...))` goes through the page's one `ScaffoldMessenger`, above the `Navigator`, which gives the bar to every root `Scaffold`, one with no `Scaffold` of the same messenger above it; every View, pushed or not, is a route with a `Scaffold` of its own, so the View on top draws it (F48). `AppScaffold` (D-165) and the View stack (D-141, D-144) need no change, and there is nothing to report upstream.<br>• **What needs nothing:** `report_errors`' "Something went wrong" (D-163), the Undo SnackBars (D-30, D-184), "Couldn't open the link" (D-182, D-197) and "Couldn't open the share sheet" (D-206) show on the View the user is on, session detail included.<br>• **What stays:** About this app's unlock and the Developer screen's outcomes are said in place (D-187, D-198), now for D-29's own reason, a result the user waits for, and no longer as a way around #197.<br>• **Guard:** `tests/ui/test_snackbar_on_pushed_view.py` stars and unstars a session on session detail and finds "Removed from My schedule" and its Undo: finders skip a covered View (testing-and-quality.md §5.1), so a bar drawn only on the root under it would fail the flow. | #197 was not seen again, at the same pin, with main or with #88's own commit. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds, each bundle checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps): session detail opened by a tap and by `--route` showed "Removed from My schedule · Undo" on itself; throwaway builds (never committed) showed a SnackBar on About this app with a `Prefs` save before it and after it (the save re-renders `App`, D-150) and on Developer after Apply and Reset; #88's commit `6032197`, rebuilt as it was, showed Reset's "Back to real time and the year's feed" on the Developer page, entered by `--route` and by a tap from More. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) session detail showed it as well. A Flutter widget test of Flet's shape (one messenger above a `Navigator` of `Scaffold` pages, material_ui 1.6.0) found the bar on the top page, also after that page's `Scaffold` was rebuilt under a new key and after a theme change. The first try on the simulator missed it although the log showed it built and timed out 4.9 s later: on a host at load 14 the simulator's screenshots trailed the app by seconds, the likely source of #88's 2-frames-per-second recording; two of this issue's own false alarms had plain causes, a tap on Undo sent seconds after the 4 s bar had gone, and a UI-flow tap that `pump(1 s)` did not delay, which reached the bar 5 dp into its entrance (testing-and-quality.md §5.2). A throwaway Dart probe (never committed) found the bar's two copies, the covered root's and the top View's, neither in a hero flight, the top one hit at its centre. The new flow passed on the iOS 26.5 simulator (an iPhone 17 of #197's own) and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`), each in light and dark: the bar, its Undo, the session saved again and removed again, all on session detail. |
| D-31 | Schedule layout: day tabs show dates, and you can swipe between days. Each time slot is one list item, with its time in a left gutter beside its rows; within a slot, rows are ordered by room. Breaks and notices are compact rows without a star. | Works without sticky headers (F8), gives `scroll_to` slot-sized targets, and room order matches the printed grid. |
| D-32 | *("While refreshing" amended by D-181.)* Data state is always honest. The app renders the best local copy at once. A status strip appears only ~~while refreshing~~ while a refresh the user asked for runs (D-181), when data is stale (over 6 h on conference days, over 24 h otherwise) or when offline, and it offers Refresh. Skeletons appear only when there is no data at all, and there is never a full-screen error while data exists. Pull-to-refresh arrives once U1 is in our pinned Flet. | Offline-first; Flet has no `RefreshIndicator` (F8). |
| D-33 | *(Headings' levels amended by D-211.)* Accessibility baseline on every screen: every control has a name, role and state; each row reads as one phrase, with the star as its own stop; headings are marked (with a level, D-211); targets are at least 48 dp; contrast meets AA (AAA for text in high contrast); text works at 200 % without clipping; reduced motion is honoured; colour is never the only signal. Key controls get `Semantics(identifier=...)` for UI tests. | A showcase must be accessible; the APIs exist (F13, F24). |
| D-34 | Motion: M3 durations (150-400 ms) and no ambient or repeating motion: a pin pulses at most twice. All animation is off when the OS asks for reduced motion, which is read at start and on resume. *(How, and what "all animation" covers: D-173.)* | WCAG 2.2.2. Flet reports a snapshot, not changes (F13). |
| D-35 | Copy and time: US English, sentence case, verbs on buttons, no "successfully". Years, dates, names and URLs come from data. Times are always in conference time, with a "Pacific Time" note when the device's zone differs, and 12/24-hour format follows the device setting. | Consistency; Ionic hard-coded the year in about ten places. The device's 24-hour setting is readable (F23). |
| D-36 | Identity and attribution: we say "Built with Python and Flet" proudly, and "Not an official PSF app" appears wherever the app names itself (Welcome, About this app). PyCon and PSF logos are not our app icon or branding. The name, icon and bundle id are open decision O-4. | "PyCon" is a PSF trademark, and Apache-2.0 grants no trademark rights. |
| D-37 | Sponsors: no rotating strip. Instead, a sponsor spotlight card on Now (one `mobile_banner` sponsor per app open), a tiered Sponsors screen, and credits from year data wherever a sponsor provides something (Wi-Fi, the Quiet room). | Ionic's strip took about 50 dp on every screen and rotated every 30 s with no pause control (WCAG 2.2.2). |
| D-150 | **Screens read `Tokens` from a context** (records how `use_tokens()` works for D-24; #71). `theme.py` defines `TokensCtx = ft.create_context(LIGHT_TOKENS)`, and `use_tokens()` returns `ft.use_context(TokensCtx)`. `App` provides `build_tokens(dark=prefs.dark, high_contrast=prefs.high_contrast_on)` next to the store providers, so the tokens always match the theme `apply_theme()` shows (`Prefs.dark` follows the OS brightness under Appearance System, architecture §4.3). `Tokens` is a frozen value, not an observable, so reading it subscribes to nothing (F3): a scheme change re-renders `App`, which re-renders the components below it. A component wrapped in `ft.memo` takes the tokens it needs as a prop instead. | The same shape as `ServicesCtx`: one obvious way, explicit providers, no extra store. Deriving the tokens inside `use_tokens()` from `use_prefs()` would hide the brightness rule in a hook and subscribe every token reader to all of `Prefs`. |
| D-153 | **The Schedule memoises the whole path, not only the rows** (settles ux.md §3.9 and architecture §6.4; S2). A day list is a component wrapped in `ft.memo` whose props are its day, its query and **only its day's favourites** (`frozenset`, the favourites ∩ the day's ids); each slot item is a memoised component keyed by its slot key, given its slot view, its matching rows (a tuple) and only the slot's favourites; each `SessionRow` and `NoticeRow` is a memoised component keyed by session id, given its frozen row view and `is_favorite`. Props are values or stable callables: row views are built once per feed version (`use_memo` on `Conference.version`), and `on_toggle(session_id)` comes from `use_callback(..., [])`, the row binding its own id. A star tap re-renders one day list, one slot and one row. | On the busiest day (111 rows, 320 in five days) a star tap cost 145 ms of Python with plain rows, 158 ms with unmemoised row components and **38 ms with memoised rows alone**, because the parents still rebuilt and diffed every slot (F34); with the path memoised, **3 ms** and the star changed in the same frame as a change Flutter draws itself. A keystroke went 120 → 34 → 3 ms, a day switch 139 → 40 → 2 ms (`docs/spikes/02-day-list.md`). |
| D-154 | *(My schedule's list amended by D-191.)* **Day lists are lazy; today's list builds every slot** (replaces ux.md §5.2's and §5.6's `build_controls_on_demand=False`; S2, F32). Each day's list is a `ListView` with the default `build_controls_on_demand=True`. Today's list, the one Jump to now targets (D-155), also gets `cache_extent=FULL_LIST_EXTENT` (`ui/`, 100,000 logical px, more than three times the busiest day at 200 % text: about 26,000, estimated from its 4:30 PM slot, the 21st of 26, at 21,234 px and the day's scroll extent of 12,314 px at 100 %), so Flutter builds all its slots and registers every `ScrollKey`; outside the conference no list has it. `build_controls_on_demand=False` is never used to make items reachable, and a day is never a `Column`. ~~My schedule's list, which opens at today's header, gets the same `cache_extent`.~~ My schedule's list does not scroll itself and gets no `cache_extent` (D-191). | `scroll_to(scroll_key=...)` only reaches a built item (F7), and `build_controls_on_demand=False` still builds only near the viewport (F32): 0 of 3 jumps to 4:30 PM, at both text sizes. A fully built list reached it 3 of 3 but costs on the emulator: a day switch's worst frame a median 49 ms (up to 50-70 ms) instead of at most 24 ms, a filter's list change up to 82 ms instead of 16 ms. A `Column` also stalled 220-227 ms when a fling hit either end of the day (stretch overscroll of one huge child); the fully built `ListView` did not. So only the list that needs the jump pays for it: with today's list alone built in full, the jump landed 3 of 3 at both text sizes, switches to the other days stayed at 18-20 ms frames (33-50 ms only when switching to today), and flings had no frame over 29 ms. |
| D-155 | *(Sequencing settled by D-192; the target amended by D-204.)* **"Jump to now" is one keyed `scroll_to`** (records how D-18's scroll happens; S2). The first display of today and the "Now" FAB call `await today_list.scroll_to(scroll_key=ft.ScrollKey(slot_key), duration=400)` (0 under reduced motion, D-34) on today's fully built list (D-154), with ~~`slot_key` from `current_slot_key()`~~ the target from `now_place()` (D-204) (architecture §7.5); never a computed offset. From another day's tab the FAB switches to today first. S2 verified the jump only on the shown list, already built (a tap 1-3 s after launch); both flows above add sequencing that #86 settles and verifies on Android and iOS: the jump waits until the render that shows today's list has been applied (a round trip, as in D-156), handles a `TimeoutException` from `scroll_to`, and confirms the landing with `on_scroll`'s scroll end. | On a fully built list it landed on the slot every time, on Android at 100 % and 200 % and on the iOS simulator. The two flows differ from that case: `scroll_to` waits for the list's widget to register its method listener (`initState`) and raises `TimeoutException` after its timeout; a key whose item Flutter has not built yet makes it a silent no-op; and `TabBarView` builds only the shown page, so today's list is unmounted while another day shows (`scrollable_control.dart`, `Control.invokeMethod` in `control.dart`). A two-step jump on a lazy list (an offset estimated from row heights, then the key) worked at 100 % and failed at 200 %, where rows are twice as tall and the first step lands hours early. |
| D-156 | **The shown day renders first; the other days in a second render** (S2). On the Schedule's first render only the selected day's list is rendered; the `TabBarView` pages of the other days are empty `Container`s. An effect of that first render awaits one round trip to Flutter (a service call; Flutter has then applied the first patch, though no frame is guaranteed) and sets a `rest_ready` state; the second render fills the other days. Later renders keep all days. | Rendering all five days in the first render costs about 150 ms of cold start on the emulator: 970 ms to the cached content against 815 ms with the deferred pass (865 against 1,037 at 200 %), Python's first render 245 ms against 105 ms. Rendering a day only on its first visit starts as fast (849 ms) but slides in an empty page when swiping to a day not yet shown. On the iOS simulator's debug build the second pass took 112 ms of Python for 209 rows. The adopted design (lazy lists, today's built in full, D-154) reached the cached content at **886 ms** (856-958) in a later window, its first render 111 ms of Python; the 70 ms over 815 ms had accrued before `main()` ran (762 against 701 ms after the intent), where the list plays no part, and `column/deferred` itself read 884 ms in an earlier window. So S2's numbers from different windows agree to about ±70 ms, and only same-window comparisons, such as the 150 ms above, carry a decision. The content line does not wait for a frame (D-112), so Flutter building today's whole list in the first frame is not in it; #86's screen recording shows it. |
| D-158 | *(At large text amended by D-216: past a fifth of the width the time goes above the rows.)* **The time gutter grows with the text** (amends ux.md §3.9's 64 dp `TimeGutter`; S2). The gutter has no fixed width: a `Stack` holds an invisible "10:00" / "AM" (`opacity=0`) under the slot's time, so its width follows the text scale, is the same for every slot, and the rows keep one left edge. It is `Semantics(header=True, label="9:25 AM", exclude_semantics=True)`, so it reads once. Padding 16 dp left, 8 dp right. | At 200 % text the 64 dp gutter broke "7:30" into "7:3" / "0"; Flet cannot read the text scale factor (U16), so the width has to come from the layout. Without `exclude_semantics` the header read "7:30 AM, 7:30, AM". |
| D-173 | *(Note on #178 amended by D-183.)* **Reduced motion: one flag, one way to ask, one place for resume** (records D-34 for THEME-09 and sets its scope; #84).<br>• **The flag:** `Prefs.reduce_motion`, not persisted, is `reduce_motion or disable_animations` from `get_accessibility_features()` (iOS Reduce Motion; Android's Remove animations, i.e. `transition_animation_scale` 0). `bootstrap()` reads it before the gate opens, and `app.on_resume()` reads it again on every `RESUME`: the one place for what a return to the foreground does, which #82's refresh of a stale feed joins (D-40, D-175). A change is logged ("Reduced motion is on").<br>• **One way to ask:** `prefs.motion_ms(ms)` returns `ms`, or 0 under reduced motion. Every duration the app sets goes through it: `scroll_to` (the re-tap, D-166; jump to now, D-155), `animate_*`, `AnimatedSwitcher`, pulses and highlights.<br>• **Scheme changes keep Flutter's 200 ms crossfade, under reduced motion too:** `bootstrap()` sets `page.theme_animation_style` back to `None` when the gate opens (D-161), whatever the flag, and nothing changes it on resume. A crossfade changes colour and opacity, not position: WCAG 2.3.3 leaves those out of "motion animation", and iOS Reduce Motion itself replaces slides with dissolves. A hard swap was worse: on iOS the surfaces changed in one frame while Material's text-style tween (U26) still ran, so the list text was dark on dark for about two frames. On Android, `disable_animations` already runs the crossfade at 5 % (10 ms), like every other Flutter animation. So D-34's "all animation is off" means motion (movement, scrolling, scaling, pulses), not colour fades.<br>• **Page transitions stay the platform's** (D-148). Android's setting reaches Flutter as `disable_animations`, which already runs every `AnimationBehavior.normal` controller (routes, ripples, Material's implicit animations) at 5 % of its length, and the predictive gesture stays. On iOS, `PageTransitionTheme.NONE` would remove the edge swipe, which lives in the Cupertino builder, and would leave a popped page on screen for the route's 300 ms; the slide stays. Flutter's own implicit animations (Material's 200 ms text-style tween, radios, ripples) keep their length under iOS Reduce Motion: U26 (#179).<br>• **Progress indicators keep moving:** they show work running and stop when it ends. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), the three animation scales set to 0 with the app in the background, then a return, logged "Reduced motion is on" in the same process (one session, same pid), and back to 1, "Reduced motion is off". A change made while the app is in the background reached Flutter's `disable_animations` a few seconds after the return, after the `RESUME` read, in 6 of 8 tries (a change made in the foreground was current at the next resume), so on Android it applies at the following resume: #178, accepted until U17 (D-183). On the iOS 26.5 simulator, a throwaway `flet test` flow switched Appearance to Dark and back on More while recording: with motion on, the recording held crossfade frames (mean grey 152 and 39 between 249 and 19); with a first version that turned the crossfade off, after Reduce Motion was turned on with the app in the background (`defaults write com.apple.Accessibility ReduceMotionEnabled -bool true` and `notifyutil -p com.apple.accessibility.reduce.motion.status`, then a relaunch to the foreground), the surfaces swapped in one frame, in the same process, but the list text trailed by about 200 ms (Material's tween, U26) and was near-black on the dark surface for two frames. With the crossfade kept and Reduce Motion on (`get_accessibility_features` returned `reduce_motion: true`; the gate set `theme_animation_style` to null), two switches to Dark each held crossfade frames (mean grey 182 and 123, then 152 and 97, between 244 and 20), with the labels readable in every frame. An OS dark-mode switch on iOS crossfades the whole screen for about 450 ms whatever the app sets (seen with the app's crossfade off). The page-transition reasoning is read from the code, not timed on a device: Flutter's `animation_controller.dart` (`AnimationBehavior._enableAnimations`, false under `disableAnimations`, and the `0.05` scale in `_startSimulation`), Flet's `NoPageTransitionsBuilder` (`utils/theme.dart`) and `AnimatedTransitionPage`'s 300 ms route (`widgets/animated_transition_page.dart`). |
| D-174 | *(Why a label breaks only between words recorded by D-216.)* **Track badges: where the palette lives, how a badge is drawn, and Registration required outlined in `error`** (records D-24 and D-25; amends ux.md §3.4's palette and its Registration required fill; #80, THEME-08, SCHED-20).<br>• **Palette:** `Tokens` gains `tracks` (a read-only mapping from each key of the track registry, `"Open Space"`, `"Ai"`, `"Sponsor Presentation"`, to a frozen `BadgeColors(text, fill)`), `unknown_track` (the neutral grey of breaks and notices, for a key next year's feed adds), `spanish` (`#FFD7F3` / `#5E0058`, inverted in dark) and `registration`; `Tokens.track_badge(key)` applies the fallback. `tracks` stays out of the generated hash (`field(hash=False)`), so `Tokens` stays hashable.<br>• **Distinct fills:** ux.md §3.4's track fills are retuned onto one CIELAB lightness (87 light, 29 dark) and chroma (22), about 30° of hue apart, with Summit a quieter slate: any two fills, En Español and the Break grey included, differ by a CIE76 distance of 10 or more in each scheme, and every label reads at 6.4:1 or better on its fill. A test checks both.<br>• **Registration required** is outlined: label and a 1 dp border in `error`, no fill. So both schemes declare `error` / `on_error` with the seed's own values (`#BA1A1A` / `#FFFFFF`, `#FFB4AB` / `#690005`), and the contrast test checks the label on every surface of the neutral ramp (4.8:1 or better in light, the 4.76:1 on `surface_dim`; 6.6:1 or better in dark, on `surface_bright`).<br>• **Drawing:** `ui/badges.py`'s `TrackBadge(label, colors)` is a plain builder, like `LinkTile`: a `Container` with `LABEL_SMALL` text, 8 dp corners, 8/2 dp padding and a 1 dp border in its fill's colour (the text's when outlined), so every badge is the same height; no fixed size, and the label wraps inside the pill at large text. `session_badges(session, info, tokens)` gives a session's badges in Ionic's set and order (its track by `track_name()`, then "En Español", then "Registration required"; a charla shows both of the first two), and `badge_row()` lays them out in `Row(wrap=True, spacing=8, run_spacing=4)`, on ux.md §3.6's 4 dp grid.<br>• Until P1's real session detail (#90), the session stub shows the badge row under its title instead of the raw track key. | An outline marks a requirement as a different kind of badge from a category, as Ionic's danger outline did, and HC (P2) outlines every badge anyway. The first palette's fills were shades of a few hues: Security and Sponsor presentation were 2.0 apart in light, Keynote and Tutorial 2.1, Summit and the grey 2.6, and En Español sat 3.5 from Tutorial and 5.5 from the Keynote it follows on the Spanish keynote, so the second badge read as part of the first (#181's review; `error_container` was 16.4 from En Español). An even grid in a perceptual space spaces them by construction; 10 is clearly different at a glance, and 30° steps at chroma 22 give it while the fills stay pastel. A builder given its colours lets a memoised row (D-153), which receives the tokens as a prop (D-150), draw the scheme on screen. The seed's error values were computed with Flutter's `material_color_utilities` 0.13.0 (`SchemeTonalSpot` from `#3B3EA9`) and sampled on screen: the outline drew `#BA1A1A` on `#FFFFFF` and `#FFB4AB` on `#121212` on the iOS 26.5 simulator. On that simulator and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), light and dark, sessions of 14 kinds (10 on Android) showed their badges with Roboto labels and equal heights, the outlined badge beside the tonal ones; at 200 % text (Android) every pair still fit one line, and at the largest Larger Text (iOS) the second badge wrapped onto its own line, nothing clipped. After the retune, the same builds showed 16 kinds on iOS (a generic break with no place line among them) and 12 on Android, light and dark: each track in its own colour, Keynote and Charla clearly apart from the En Español beside them, 8 dp between badges, and a wrapped line 4 dp below at the largest text. |
| D-184 | *(A room's rows amended by D-194; the modules by D-200 and D-227; a speaker's rows by D-203; how long the Undo SnackBar stays by D-251.)* **The row family: memoised rows keyed by session id, one spoken phrase per row, a toggle star and one star handler per list** (records D-30, D-31, D-33 and D-153 for SCHED-03 and SCHED-04; amends ux.md §3.9's sketches and §4.5's SnackBar; #81).<br>• **Modules:** the session rows are `ui/session_rows.py` and their views `core/row_views.py`, not `ui/rows.py` and `core/queries.py` as section M first said: those hold `LinkTile` (D-182) and the schedule queries (D-178, D-179), and each would pass 400 lines with the rows (amends section M). D-200 moved `TimeGutter` to `ui/time_gutter.py`, `semantics_key()` to `ui/semantics.py` and the phrases (`spoken_phrase()`, `notice_phrase()`) to `core/row_views.py`.<br>• **Row views:** `core/row_views.py`'s frozen `SessionView` (`session`, `when` "9:25–10:10 AM", `spoken_when` "9:25 to 10:10 AM", `place`, `speakers`) from the pure `session_view()` / `session_views()`; a list owner gets them from `ui/session_rows.py`'s `use_session_views(conference, ids, use_24h=...)`, memoised on the feed version, the ids and the clock format. A row says no day, so a list that spans days (a speaker's~~, a room's~~) puts a `SectionHeader` per day above its rows, grouped by `row_views.by_day()`; a room's has a tab per day and its rows leave out the room's own name (D-194).<br>• **Rows:** `SessionRow(view, is_favorite, on_toggle, tokens, *, screen)` and `NoticeRow(view, *, screen)` are plain functions that return an `ft.memo` component keyed by `view.session.id`, so no caller can forget the key (`ft.component`'s typing leaves out the `key=` its wrapper takes: one `pyright: ignore` each). A row's text is `Semantics(label=<phrase>, identifier="<screen>.session.<key>", button=True, exclude_semantics=True)` inside a `MergeSemantics` with the tappable `Container`: one button node, read as `spoken_phrase()` ("Talk. Title. 9:25 to 10:10 AM, Room 104A. Ana Lima."), whose action is the container's tap; `Semantics.on_tap` is not used, since at the pin it never reaches Flutter (#187). An identifier's key is `semantics_key(session.id)`, lower case with `-` for other characters, so a break's synthetic id (spaces, colons) is selector-safe. Supporting lines use `on_surface_variant`; the meta's time ("AM" kept to it by a no-break space, and the "·") and its place are two texts in a wrapping `Row`, so at large text the place moves down whole; no fixed heights.<br>• **The star:** `ui/badges.py`'s `FavoriteButton(selected, on_click, *, color, identifier)`, a plain builder given `Tokens.favorite`: `MergeSemantics(Semantics(label="Save to My schedule", button=True, toggled=selected, identifier="<screen>.favorite.<key>", content=IconButton(STAR if selected else STAR_BORDER, icon_color=..., tooltip=Tooltip("Add to My schedule" / "Remove from My schedule", exclude_from_semantics=True))))`. The spoken name is stable and the state is said once, as the switch's on or off: the tooltip, which says what a tap does (D-30), stays out of the semantics (iOS reads a node's tooltip after its label), and the icon is swapped without `IconButton.selected` (its `Semantics(selected:)` would add "selected"). This amends D-30's label for screen readers; its visible tooltip is unchanged.<br>• **Notices:** a session whose track is in the `logistics` group of `conference.toml` (Break, Informational; `is_notice()`) is a `NoticeRow`: its track's registry icon, the title and meta on `surface_container` with 12 dp corners, padding 12 × 8 and margin 16 × 4 (its title starts 12 dp down, on the gutter's line), no star, tappable only with a description, its own merged node read as `notice_phrase()` ("Break. Coffee, 8:00 to 9:00 AM, Expo Hall AB."), a button when it opens, identifier `<screen>.notice.<key>`.<br>• **`TimeGutter(label)`** as D-158; a 24-hour label is one line over an invisible "00:00".<br>• **One handler per list:** `use_favorite_toggle(favorites)` returns `use_callback(toggle, [])`: the store's toggle, then `HapticFeedback.selection_click()` on an add (`Services.haptics`; a failed click is only logged), then the tap's one SnackBar from `feedback(change)`: none after a saved add, "Removed from My schedule" with Undo, or "Couldn't save your change" (Undo kept after a removal). Undo awaits `favorites.undo()` and reports its own failure the same way.<br>• **SnackBar persistence:** Flutter 3.47 keeps a SnackBar with an action on screen until it is used (`persist` defaults to true then), which would leave "Removed · Undo" over every later screen. So `persist` is true only for an Undo SnackBar while a screen reader is on (`Prefs.screen_reader`, Flutter's `accessible_navigation`, read at start and on resume with the other OS flags): 4 s otherwise (ux.md §4.5), and time to reach Undo for TalkBack and VoiceOver users; until the next favourites edit, which closes it (D-251).<br>• **Where rows are until their screens:** the Schedule stub's five sample sessions, the speaker stub's sessions and the room stub's sessions (by day, in gutter slots with notices) are rows, so the star works end to end; #86 builds the Schedule's memoised path, #94 My schedule, #99 and #101 the speaker and room details. | A row that owned its tap and its phrase in one merged node, and a star with its own name and state, is what S2's TalkBack tree lacked. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) with TalkBack on, `uiautomator dump` of the room stub showed one clickable `Button` node per row with the phrase as its description and `resource-id` `room.session.<key>`; the star as a separate clickable `Switch` node, checkable, 48 × 48 dp, named "Save to My schedule", checked after a tap and not `selected`; each gutter as one "5:00 PM" node; and each notice as its own node, `room.notice.break-expo-hall-ab-2026-05-15t08-00-00-07-00`. On the iOS 26.5 simulator, the debug build's semantics tree (`debugDumpSemanticsTreeInTraversalOrder` through the Dart VM service) gave each row one merged node with `isButton` and the phrase, and the star one node with `isButton`, `hasToggledState` / `isToggled`, the label "Save to My schedule", no tooltip and no `isSelected`; Flutter's iOS bridge (`SemanticsObject.mm`) appends a node's tooltip to its `accessibilityLabel` and maps `isSelected` to the Selected trait, which the earlier star had both of, so VoiceOver read its name twice and its state as "on" and "selected". In light and dark, and at 200 % text (Android) and Larger Text up to the largest (iOS 26.5 simulator), rows and notices reflowed without clipping, the place moving under the time whole ("5:00–7:00 PM ·" / "Expo Hall AB", where one text broke it as "· Expo" / "Hall AB"), and a notice's title on its gutter's line; at iOS's largest size, about 310 %, the wide gutter leaves the row so narrow that a long word can break inside itself and the time breaks after its dash. On the shared iOS simulator a star tap filled the star in `tertiary`, a second tap showed the floating "Removed from My schedule · Undo" for 4 s, Undo saved the session again (SharedPreferences held it), and the star was still filled after a relaunch. A unit test in a real Flet session sees a star tap re-render exactly one row body, and fails with `ft.memo` removed. Flutter's 3.47 default (persist with an action) was read in `scaffold.dart` and `snack_bar.dart`. |
| D-251 | **A favourites edit that says nothing closes the last favourites SnackBar: a screen reader is never left with "Removed from My schedule" about a saved session** (settles #334; amends D-184's SnackBar persistence and ux.md §4.4 and §4.5).<br>• **The bug:** with a screen reader on, D-184 keeps "Removed from My schedule · Undo" until it is used or replaced. Saving the session again, by its star on any screen, shows no SnackBar (§4.4), so nothing replaced it: TalkBack found it on session detail and back on Search, 95 s later, beside a filled star, and its Undo did nothing.<br>• **The rule:** `ui/badges.py`'s `_report()` reports every favourites edit, the star's (`use_favorite_toggle()`), its Undo, "Add all" (`use_add_all()`) and that Undo. An edit with a message shows its SnackBar, which replaces the last one, as Flet's `SnackBarControl` removes the current SnackBar before it shows one (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/snack_bar.dart`, line 109). An edit that says nothing (an add, an Undo that was saved) closes the last favourites SnackBar the page showed, while Flet still reports it `open`: `open = False` and `update()`, which `SnackBarControl` turns into `ScaffoldMessenger.removeCurrentSnackBar()` (lines 124-129). The page's last one is kept in a `WeakKeyDictionary` keyed by the page, so a star on session detail closes the SnackBar a star on Search showed; its value is a `weakref.ref` to the SnackBar, since the SnackBar holds its page (its Undo's closure, the dismiss handler `page.show_dialog()` wraps) and a strong value would keep a page whose session has ended, with its stores and services. The page's dialogs own the SnackBar while it shows (`base_page.py`'s `show_dialog()`), so the reference lives as long as there is something to close. Any edit closes it, not only one of the same session: the SnackBar does not say which session it removed. Edits overlap: Flet runs each event as its own task (`flet_socket_server.py`), so a second tap can finish while the first still awaits its write or its haptic click. Each handler takes a number from a per-page counter before its first await (`_begin()`, another `WeakKeyDictionary` keyed by the page), and only the page's newest edit reports: an older add or Undo that finishes late never closes a newer removal's SnackBar, and an older removal whose write answers after a newer add began shows nothing over the add's silence. The newest edit writes the whole set (`Favorites`), so its result is the one that counts; the SnackBar's `ref` alone could not mark the order, since it changes only when a SnackBar shows. D-184's `persist` is unchanged: a SnackBar still stays for a screen-reader user until it is used, replaced, or closed by the next edit.<br>• **Rejected:** `page.pop_dialog()`, which closes whatever dialog is on top, a filter sheet included (ux.md §4.5); closing before every show as well, since the close and the show, sent together, each remove the current SnackBar after the frame, in an order Flet does not promise, and the close could take the new SnackBar away; ending `persist` for screen readers, which would take Undo out of their reach again (D-184).<br>• **Tests:** `tests/test_badges.py` (a real Flet session with a screen reader on): saving the session again closes its "Removed" SnackBar and sends it, Undo closes it, saving another session closes it, a removal's SnackBar stays until the next edit, another SnackBar on the page stays open, one Flet already reports gone is not sent again, and a page with an Undo SnackBar is released when its session ends (it fails with a strong value), an older add held in its haptic click, or an older Undo held in its write, leaves the newer removal's SnackBar open, and an older removal held in its write shows no SnackBar once a newer add has saved the session again; `tests/test_track_add_all.py`: "Add all"'s Undo closes its SnackBar. | On my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (Android 14, TalkBack 14.2 driven by its keyboard shortcuts written to the emulator's keyboard device, read from its verbose speech log; release arm64 APK of this branch, light, default text size), on Search ("pathlib"): removing showed "Removed from My schedule · Undo", which TalkBack read (`TYPE_WINDOW_CONTENT_CHANGED`) and which stayed over 40 s, as D-184 wants; Alt+Enter on the star again saved the session and the SnackBar went at once, focus staying on the star; opening the row then showed session detail with the star on and no SnackBar. Undo, reached and activated with TalkBack, saved the session and the SnackBar went (TalkBack's focus moved to Back, as when a focused node goes). Removed on session detail, the SnackBar followed to Search on Back (D-210) and went when the star there saved the session again. |
| D-216 | *(Words wider than their line amended by D-229.)* **At large text a slot's time goes above its rows: the gutter may take at most a fifth of the width, measured by a probe on every screen** (amends D-158 and D-174 for the largest sizes, D-69's boot gate, ux.md §3.9's `TimeGutter` and `TrackBadge` and §5.2's day list, and section M's `ui/time_gutter.py`; #224, #214).<br>• **The rule:** `time_above(gutter_width, width)` is true when the gutter, padding included, would take more than `MAX_GUTTER_SHARE` (0.2) of the width. Then every list with slots (the Schedule, My schedule, room detail, Open spaces) shows each slot as `Column[TimeAbove, Column[rows]]`: "2:00 PM" on one line in the gutter's `TITLE_SMALL`, 16 dp in and 20 dp down (the rows keep their own 12 dp under it, so the time sits with the rows it heads, not midway between them and the divider or now line over it), the same heading read once (`header=True`, `label`, `exclude_semantics`), and the rows take the whole width. Otherwise the slot keeps D-158's gutter. `slot_layout(label, rows, *, above)` builds either (the Schedule's keyed box wraps it, D-155), `TimeSlot(..., above=)` adds the divider; `above` is a required argument, so no list can forget it, and it is a prop of the memoised day lists and slots (D-153), which re-render only when it flips.<br>• **The measurement:** Flet cannot read the text scale (U16), so `GutterProbe`, a component in `ui/time_gutter.py`, lays out an invisible copy of the widest gutter ("10:00" / "AM" at opacity 0, the gutter's padding) in a 0 dp high box as wide as the screen and takes both widths from `on_size_change` (F51). The box is the View's width, beside the body's `SafeArea`: only in landscape on a phone with a notch or a cutout is a slot narrower, by the side insets (62 dp a side of 874 on the iPhone 17), and there the largest gutter takes 18.0 % of the 750 dp left, still under a fifth, so the answer is the same; accepted rather than wrapping every screen's body in one more column. It writes the answer to `Prefs.time_above`, a device fact beside `reduce_motion`, not persisted and kept by `reset()`, and only once it has both widths, since every screen mounts a probe of its own; an observable notifies only on a change, so a resize with the same answer re-renders nothing. `AppScaffold` puts it under every body (`View(spacing=0)`, so it takes no space) and `BootView` holds it too, under the boot gate's `PrefsCtx`, and the gate waits for its first answer (`Prefs.time_measured`, amends D-69's gate), or at most `RING_DELAY` (1 s, then the ring shows and the lists keep the gutter until a screen's probe answers): the answer is in before the first screen renders, and it follows a text-size change and a rotation while the app runs.<br>• **Badges** (D-174) need no rule of their own: the badge row's `Wrap` already moves a whole badge to a line of its own, and Flutter breaks a label inside a word only when that word is wider than the whole row, which happened only beside a gutter at the largest sizes. With the rows at full width "Registration required" wraps between its words; a word wider than the row (a 320 dp phone at the largest size) would still break, which clipping would make worse (ux.md §3.4).<br>• **Not chosen:** a gutter capped at a fixed share, which broke "10:00" inside itself as the 64 dp gutter had (D-158); the time inline in every row's meta at every size, which drops the scannable gutter where it works; a share of a quarter, under which iOS's first two Accessibility sizes and Android's 200 % kept a gutter of 21 % to 23 % and titles of two or three words a line ("Running Large / Language / Models on Lap…"). | Words breaking inside themselves ("Lightnin" / "g…", "Registratio" / "n required") read as broken at the sizes people choose because they need them (D-33). A fifth separates the sizes cleanly: measured on an iPhone 17 simulator (402 dp, iOS 26.5) the gutter takes 15 % at the default size, 18 % at the largest standard size (72.6 dp), then 20.7 %, 23.3 %, 26.9 % and 33.7 % (83.0 to 135.3 dp) from the first to the fifth Accessibility size; on the Android 14 emulator (411 dp) 14.6 % at 100 %, 19.5 % at 150 % (80.3 dp), 21.0 % at 180 % (86.4 dp) and 22.0 % at 200 % (90.5 dp), and the same 200 % on a 360 dp width (`wm density 480`) 25.1 %; in landscape (874 dp) even the largest gutter takes 15 %. On those devices (release APK and simulator builds checked against `build/python-app`; a throwaway build, never committed, started the fake clock at Friday, May 15, 2:01 PM and logged the probe's widths): at the default sizes the Schedule, a speaker page and a room page look as before; at Android's 200 % and iOS's Accessibility sizes the slots put "2:00 PM" above their rows, titles break between words and "Registration required" wraps as "Registration" / "required"; a font scale changed while the app ran re-sent the widths and the slots returned to the gutter without a restart. A first build placed the probe over the body in a `Stack` (`left`, `top`, `right`), which drew Flutter's grey error box over every screen (F51); a second, with the probe only under the body, opened the Schedule straight from `--route` with the gutter and switched a moment later, after its jump to 2:00 PM had already run against the first layout, so the list stood one slot too far; with the probe in the boot view too it still did at the Accessibility sizes, because each screen's own probe heard the gutter's width before the screen's and answered "beside" for about 0.2 s, under the jump; a probe that answers only once it has both widths fixed that, and a gate that waits for the boot view's answer fixed the start on a loaded host, where the gate had opened before the first answer came (0.7 s after the session started). |
| D-229 | **At large text a time range breaks only after its dash, a talk's title on a card is `TITLE_MEDIUM`, and the filter summary's "Show all" keeps its inset** (settles #281 and #283; amends D-186's time tile, whose range "never breaks in two", D-217's panel title, D-216's words wider than their line, D-195's summary, and ux.md §3.6's text scaling, §5.2's summary, §5.5's time tile and §5.11's panel title).<br>• **Time ranges: one helper, no word joiner.** `timefmt.on_screen(label)` is how every line shows a time or a range: session detail's time tile, the Keynotes tile, `SessionRow`'s meta line, room detail's "Now" line and `TimeAbove`, which each made their own no-break copy before. Its spaces become no-break spaces, so "AM" keeps to its digits; Unicode then allows a break in "9:45–10:30 AM" only after the en dash (class BA), and Flutter breaks there only when the whole range is wider than its line. A range always starts its own line, so a word joiner at the dash (#281's suggestion) never keeps one whole that would not fit anyway: it only takes away the dash's break, and Flutter then breaks the run inside a time ("9:00 AM–12" / ":30 PM", seen in D-186). Shared text (`share_text()`) and spoken labels keep plain spaces.<br>• **Why a range cannot always stay whole.** Measured on an iPhone 17 simulator (402 dp, iOS 26.5): on the Keynotes tile, beside the 48 dp star, the text column is about 247 dp, and "9:45–10:30 AM" in `BODY_MEDIUM` stays on one line at 100 % and at iOS's 200 % (`accessibility-large`) but breaks after its dash from the fourth Accessibility size on (about 269 dp at the largest); in session detail's tile (`BODY_LARGE`, about 297 dp) "11:45 AM–12:15 PM" breaks after its dash at 200 % and at the largest size, where "11:45 AM–" alone is 245 dp and "12:15 PM" 211 dp. On the Android 14 emulator (411 dp) both ranges stay on one line at 200 %, since Android 14 scales large text less. Keeping them whole would need smaller type, which Flet cannot pick by the text scale (U16); "9:45–" / "10:30 AM" is the break style guides allow.<br>• **Words wider than their line.** Flutter has no hyphenation, so a word wider than its line breaks inside itself (D-216's badges). On a card, a talk's title is `keynote_card.TALK_TITLE`, `TITLE_MEDIUM`, a keynote's and now a panel's: in `TITLE_LARGE` "Community" took about 361 dp of the panel card's 338 dp column at iOS's largest size ("Comm" / "unity"); in `TITLE_MEDIUM` it takes 255 dp, and every 2026 keynote and panel title breaks between its words at every size. People's names keep `TITLE_LARGE`, as their words are short. Session detail's `HEADLINE_SMALL` title is not changed: about 80 of the 345 sessions of 2026 have a word of twelve letters or more, some without a hyphen ("Telecommunications", "Interoperabilidad"), which no heading size above body text would hold at the largest size on a phone; a heading that steps down at the Accessibility sizes, as iOS's own text styles do, waits for the scale (U16) and is a question of its own (#305).<br>• **The filter summary's "Show all"** (`schedule_filter.SHOW_ALL_INSET`, 8 dp): Material shrinks a text button's side padding as text grows (12 dp at 100 %, 8 at 200 %, 4 from 300 %), so a "Show all" on a run of its own started right of the words above it (#283). The button's horizontal padding is pinned at 8 dp, with no vertical padding (Material's 8 dp goes at large text too, and at 100 % the 40 dp minimum height holds the label), and the words start 8 dp into the wrapping row, which has no spacing of its own, where the 8 dp gap after the mark was; the row's right margin is 12 dp, so at 100 % the label still ends 20 dp from the edge. The 48 dp target, `schedule.show_all` and the label are unchanged. | Checked on a simulator build whose bundle matched `build/python-app` (an iPhone 17 simulator of #281's own and, for scrolling, the shared "PyCon iPhone 17") and an arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched it on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`), light and dark. The summary's left edges, measured on the screenshots: at iOS's largest size "space" and a wrapped "Show all" at 45.0 and 48.7 dp before, 45.0 and 44.7 dp after (the "S" and "s" glyphs' own side bearings); at Android's 200 % on a 360 dp width (`wm density 480`) 44.3 and 43.0 dp; at 100 % "Show all" ends 21.3 dp from the edge as before. The D&I panel's title wraps between its words at 100 %, 200 % and the largest size on iOS and at 200 % on Android. The ranges broke as above, after the dash only. |
| D-182 | **The link rule, the shared states and where a link opens** (records ux.md §3.9's `EmptyState`, `SkeletonList` and `LinkTile`'s external form, and §4.7 for INFO-14; #83).<br>• **One rule, decided by the target, in a module of its own:** `ui/links.py` (used by `ui/rows.py`'s `LinkTile` and, from #91, by `Markdown`'s `on_tap_link`; `rows.py` is where P1's rows go, D-66). Its pure `link_kind(target, *, app=False)` returns a `LinkKind`: a location starting with `/` is a page (pushed, `CHEVRON_RIGHT`); `http(s)` is a web page (`LaunchMode.IN_APP_BROWSER_VIEW`), or, with `app=True`, a social profile or a map (`EXTERNAL_APPLICATION`); `geo:` is an app; `tel:` and `mailto:` are a phone or an email (`EXTERNAL_APPLICATION`); any other scheme raises `ValueError`, and so does `app=True` on anything but a web address or a `geo:` link, so the flag never does nothing silently. The caller marks a profile or a map: a host cannot say it (Mastodon has many instances). `open_link(svc, target, app=...)` applies the rule for rows and for `Markdown`'s `on_tap_link` (#91); a launch that raises shows "Couldn't open the link", and the log names the kind and the error's class only (`Could not open the email link: RuntimeError`), never the target nor the error's text, which repeats it (Android's `ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND` names the intent, a timeout the call's arguments; a `mailto:` holds an address, D-72).<br>• **External rows:** one merged node, `Semantics(link=True)`, whose `OPEN_IN_NEW` icon has the `semantics_label` "Opens in browser" ("Opens in another app"), read after the title: the icon says it visually, the label says it to screen readers, whatever their hint setting. Not `Semantics.hint_text`, which never reaches Flutter at the pin: the Dart control reads `hint` (#187). Phone and email rows have no trailing icon (their title is the verb, "Call the Code of Conduct hotline") and a 48 dp Copy `IconButton` beside the merged node, a stop of its own (`<identifier>.copy`), which copies the number or the address (`contact_of()`) and says "Phone number copied" ("Email address copied"), except on Android 13 (API 33) and later, whose own overlay confirms every copy (`page.get_device_info()`; Android 12 and earlier, and a version that cannot be read, get the SnackBar); a failure says "Couldn't copy the phone number" ("…the email address") on every platform, logged like a launch. No `can_launch_url` probe until U6.<br>• **Icons beside the first line:** link rows use `ListTileTitleAlignment.TITLE_HEIGHT` (a 56 dp row's icons centred; a 72 dp row's leading icon, and past 72 dp both icons, 16 dp from the top), and Copy is top-aligned 4 dp down, level with the leading icon, so at 200 % text no icon drifts to the middle of a four-line title.<br>• **Services:** `Services` gains `launcher` (`ft.UrlLauncher()`) and `clipboard` (`ft.Clipboard()`), as architecture §4.2 planned. The in-app `LinkTile` stays a plain builder, cheap in long lists; its external form is a component (`_ExternalLinkTile`), because a tap needs `use_services()` and a builder called in a loop cannot call hooks (D-70).<br>• **`EmptyState(title, *, icon, body=None, action=None, on_action=None, disabled=False, identifier=None)`** (`ui/states.py`): a 48 dp outlined icon in `on_surface_variant` (decorative), the title in `TITLE_MEDIUM` as a heading node of its own (`Semantics(header=True, container=True)`: without `container`, Flutter merges the flag and the body's text into the live region, and the whole state reads as one heading), an optional `BODY_MEDIUM` line, both in one `Semantics(container=True, live_region=True)` carrying the identifier, and at most one `FilledTonalButton`, a stop of its own, `disabled` while its action runs; centred, no fixed height. `action` and `on_action` come as a pair (`ValueError` otherwise). Every empty state names a way out: the button, or its sentence where no button can help (ux.md §4.6's search miss and My schedule unavailable).<br>• **`SkeletonList(label="Loading schedule")`**, a component: five session-shaped placeholder rows in `surface_container_highest` under one `Semantics(label, container=True, exclude_semantics=True)`, in `ft.Shimmer(period=prefs.motion_ms(1500), highlight_color=surface_container_low)`; under reduced motion there is no `Shimmer` (D-173). Like a progress indicator it shows work running and goes with it, so it is not D-34's ambient motion. A screen without data shows it until the first fetch has failed (`problem is None`), then `EmptyState` "Can't load the schedule" with "Try again", its body by `problem` (ux.md §4.6), which stays during a retry with its button disabled (`disabled=refreshing`): swapping it for the skeleton would remove the focused button and lose a screen reader's place; the strip's bar shows the work. | A hint would be the natural slot, but the one Flet sends never arrives, and an icon label is read whatever the screen reader's hint setting. Copy beside the row, not only after a failure: on the iOS 26.5 simulator a `tel:` tap opened nothing and raised nothing, so a failure SnackBar alone would never show. Checked with a throwaway build (not committed) whose More list had a web, a profile, a `tel:` and a `mailto:` row and whose year had no snapshot and a feed URL that never answered: on the iOS 26.5 simulator a tap on the web row opened us.pycon.org in the in-app Safari view, the profile row handed off to Safari ("◀ Conf Companion"), and Copy put `+1-555-010-0199` and `coc@example.org` on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`) with "Phone number copied" / "Email address copied"; on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) the web row launched Chrome (its first-run screen), Copy showed the SnackBar, and `uiautomator dump` read the web row as one node "PyCon US website, us.pycon.org, Opens in browser" with `resource-id` `preview.web` and Copy as "Copy phone number" (`preview.phone.copy`; its button is the same tooltip-and-button pair as the app bar's Search). On both, the Schedule showed the skeleton for the 10 s the fetch waited, then "Can't load the schedule" with Try again; with motion on, two frames 0.8 s apart differed in 754 (iOS) and 808 (Android) rows of the skeleton, and under iOS Reduce Motion or Android's animation scales at 0 ("Reduced motion is on") in none. Light, dark and the largest text (iOS) or 200 % (Android): every state and row reflowed with nothing clipped, and the Copy icon lines up with the rows' `OPEN_IN_NEW` once its button sits 12 dp from the edge (8 dp put it 4 dp out). The review round (#189) rebuilt it with a feed host that accepts and never answers: at the largest text (iOS) and 200 % (Android) every link row's icons, Copy included, sat beside the title's first line, where M3's default had centred them against four-line blocks; a tap on Try again left the state in place with the button disabled (`uiautomator dump`: `enabled="false"`, same bounds) and the strip saying "Updating the schedule…"; the dump shows the title as a node of its own inside `schedule.no_data`, which now carries only the body; and on the Android 14 emulator Copy brought the system's clipboard overlay and no SnackBar. The log rule: in a probe on the previous code, Android's `ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND` text and Flet's timeout text put the address in the log twice through `exc_info`. |
| D-226 | *(The hook's text and `Ref` amended by D-243.)* **The list filter's text, Clear and count line are shared, and the count has one padding** (settles #249; amends D-189, D-193 and D-202 where they build the field's state, Clear and the count, and section M's `ui/fields.py`).<br>• **The hook:** `ui/fields.py`'s `use_field_query(initial="")`, beside `SearchField`, returns a frozen `FieldQuery`: `value`, the controlled text (D-157); `changed`, the field's `on_change`; `ref`, a fresh `Ref` each render, which holds this render's field, as the screens' copies did; `clear()`, which empties the text and then focuses the field, since a Clear goes away when used (D-182); and `focus()` alone, for search's "Show all results". A plain function that calls one `use_state`, so the screens call it once per render, before any early return (D-70). Speakers and Sprints pass its handlers to the field and their no-match "Clear filter" as they are; search wraps `changed` and `clear` in handlers of its own that also write `ViewMemory.search_query` and, for Clear, the searched query at once (D-193). No callback parameter: one screen keeps extra state, and its own handlers say so where they are read.<br>• **The count:** `found_count(line, *, identifier)`, the one copy of the three screens' `_found_count()`: a polite `Semantics(live_region=True)` line (`<screen>.count`) in `BODY_SMALL` and `on_surface_variant` (ux.md §9). The screen still words it ("5 speakers", "3 projects", search's `summary()`) and shows it only while the text finds something; a miss has its no-match state, which announces itself.<br>• **One padding:** 16 dp from the sides, 4 dp under the field or the chips and **8 dp over the list**, Sprints' padding: the line is pinned and the list scrolls under it, so a card's edge, an avatar or a row's ripple never touches the text. Speakers and search had no bottom padding, so their first heading or row now starts 8 dp lower while a filter applies; nothing else moves. Not chosen: no bottom padding on all three (a scrolled card touched the line, the reason Sprints had it), or 4 dp (under the 8 dp ux.md §3.6 keeps within a group).<br>• **Matching unchanged:** `filter_directory()`, `filter_projects()` and `search()` are as they were; what each screen counts and when it shows the line too. | The pinned filter was written three times nearly verbatim (the hint and Clear constants, `changed`, the per-render `Ref` with an async `clear()` that refocuses, and `_found_count()`), and the copies had drifted: only Sprints' count had a bottom padding (#249, raised by #243's second opinion). One hook and one builder put the D-182 refocus and the live region in one place, where #250's iOS live-region fix will land too. In real Flet sessions (`tests/test_fields.py`) the probe's field keeps every keystroke, Clear empties it and focuses that render's field, `focus()` keeps the text, and the count is a live region with its identifier, style and padding; `tests/test_speakers.py`, `tests/test_sprints.py` and `tests/test_search_screen.py` pass unchanged (the count's text, its live region, Clear refocusing). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (iPhone 17; debug builds checked against `build/python-app`), the filtered lists of a throwaway build that started each filter with a query ("mu", "python web", "chen"; never committed) and of the same patch over main differ only below the count line on Speakers and search, by 8 dp, at the default size and at the largest Larger Text, and not at all on Sprints; on the shared simulator, driven by taps, "m", Clear, then "u" left "u" in the field with "59 speakers", "Clear filter" and search's Clear kept the cursor in the field, and "python web" showed "5 projects". On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), `uiautomator dump` read `speakers.count`, `sprints.count` and `search.count` under their fields, and after each Clear the field was empty and focused, and the next key went into it. |
| D-183 | **A background change of Android's Remove animations applies at the following resume until U17** (settles #178 under D-101; amends D-173's note on #178).<br>• **No fix at the pin:** nothing in the pinned Flet reports a change of an accessibility flag. `get_accessibility_features()` is a snapshot (F13); `page.on_media_change` fires only when a `PageMediaData` field changes, none of them is an accessibility flag, and its builder (`packages/flet/lib/src/widgets/page_media.dart`) reads `MediaQuery.paddingOf()` and the like, so it does not even rebuild on one; no Page or service event follows `didChangeAccessibilityFeatures`.<br>• **`app.on_resume()` keeps its one read.** On Android a change made in the background can reach Flutter after that read; the app's own durations (`prefs.motion_ms()`) then follow at the following resume. In practice that is the scrolls (the re-tap, D-166; jump to now, D-155): Flutter runs `scroll_to` on an unbounded `AnimationController`, whose `AnimationBehavior.preserve` ignores `disableAnimations`, so until the next resume they keep their full length. Flutter's own animations follow the late value at once, because each `AnimationBehavior.normal` controller (routes, ripples, Material's implicit animations, and the app's `AnimatedSwitcher` and `animate_*`) reads `disableAnimations` when it starts, so they already run at 5 %. A change made in the foreground, and iOS Reduce Motion, apply at the first resume. The high-contrast default (D-23) is read the same way; its Android timing is not measured.<br>• **Not done:** a second read a few seconds after `RESUME`, and a read on the 60 s tick.<br>• **U17 is the fix** (#183): accessibility fields in `PageMediaData` make the existing `on_media_change` fire whenever Flutter's value changes, late or not (flet-gaps §5.4). The app needs it from P1 (THEME-09), no longer P2; it keeps its place in D-98's group 6, one XS PR with U16, and `Prefs` follows it in its own PR after the pin bump (D-64). | #178's measurements on the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK): the `RESUME` read returned the previous `disable_animations` in 6 of 8 tries, and the late value came 3 to 10+ s after the return; on the iOS 26.5 simulator Reduce Motion applied at the first resume, both ways. Flutter's `animation_controller.dart` (3.47.7, `AnimationBehavior._enableAnimations` and the `0.05` scale): an `AnimationBehavior.normal` controller, the default constructor's, reads `SemanticsBinding.instance.disableAnimations` at each start, so only the app's own motion lags, by one return. `AnimationController.unbounded` defaults to `AnimationBehavior.preserve`, which never reads it; `scroll_to` (`scrollable_control.dart`: `animateTo`, `Scrollable.ensureVisible`) runs as a `DrivenScrollActivity` on such a controller (`widgets/scroll_activity.dart`), so a scroll keeps its length under `disableAnimations` and only `motion_ms()` shortens it. A fixed delay is a guess that still misses some returns and adds a timer to cancel on pause and close (D-147); the tick is up to a minute late and calls the platform every minute for a rare change. The real fix is an XS change in Flet. |

A P2 deep link with an unknown id is "its root with the target pushed" as any link is, so Back lands on the root.<br>• **Layout** (`screens/session.py`): the app bar holds `FavoriteButton` (`session.favorite`, `use_favorite_toggle()`'s handler and Undo SnackBar, D-184) and, only when `Session.url` is set, an overflow (`session.overflow`, "More options") with "Open on <host>" (the host from the URL), opened in the in-app browser view and, as it leaves the app, a link read "Open on <host>, Opens in browser" (the words on the text: the leading icon's label was read first); sessions without a public page (plenaries, keynotes, summits, breaks, notices, posters) have no overflow (D-60), and Add to calendar joins it in P2. The app-bar title stays empty; the page's heading is the session's name in `HEADLINE_SMALL`, `selectable`, `Semantics(header=True, heading_level=1)` (`session.title`; on Android one `View` node holding the text, not an `EditText`, F40), 8 dp under the badge row, which leaves out a badge that repeats the title (a break's "Break"). The sections sit 24 dp apart (ux.md §3.6); a section's `SectionHeader` takes `level=2` and `top=0`, as the list's gap already stands above it. Speakers stay plain links until #91; Share and posters are #92's.<br>• **When and where** (`ui/when_where.py`, a module of its own so the screen stays under D-66's 400 lines and Now can reuse its status line): an outlined card whose time tile shows the day over the time range (a line each: on one line, at large text the range broke in two, "9:00 AM–12" / ":30 PM", even with no-break spaces and a word joiner) and reads as one phrase ("Saturday, May 16, 10:30 to 11:00 AM, Starts in 25 minutes", the range's dash spoken as "to", `session.when`), with "Times are Pacific Time (PDT)" (`timefmt.zone_note()`) when the device's offset at the session's start differs from the conference's (D-35). Then the room tiles from the pure `row_views.places()`: the session's one place (`display_location`) as a `LinkTile` to `room/<slug>?highlight=<id>` (`session.room`; a place the feed has no room for is shown and opens nothing), and its other rooms, each once, in an `ExpansionTile` (`session.other_rooms`: "+4 more rooms", or "In 5 rooms" for a break with no one place; each room `session.room.<slug>`), which opens in `prefs.motion_ms(200)`. `OtherRooms` is a component that holds `expanded` itself (as D-157 does for text fields): left to Flutter, the tile folded up again after a room's detail and Back. Breaks and notices get no star, as their rows have none (D-31).<br>• **Status** (`timefmt.time_status(start, end, now, zone)`, pure): counted from the start the tile shows (`display_start`: the Job Fair opens at 9:30 though the Schedule groups it at 10:00, DATA-11, so at 9:45 it is "Happening now", not "Starts in 15 min"); before it, on the session's own conference-local day only, "Starts in 25 min" / "Starts in 1 h 5 min" (minutes rounded up, so never "0 min"), spoken in words ("Starts in 25 minutes", D-165's reason); "Happening now" in `Tokens.live` from the start up to, not including, the end; "Ended" from the end on (D-179's half-open span). A notice (no duration) counts down but never says "Ended". `StatusLine` is a leaf component that ticks every `TICK_SECONDS` through `Services.run_every` with `stop_ticks` as its cleanup (D-175, F39), reading `clock.now()`, so the screen around it does not re-render and #88's fake clock drives it. The status and the zone note sit in the tile's title column, styled as supporting lines, not in a `subtitle`: an empty subtitle kept a two-line tile's height, a blank under the range, in the commonest case (another day, on Pacific time).<br>• **Registration** (DATA-09): a filled card, "Register separately" / "Your conference ticket doesn't sign you up for this session." (what to do, as the badge above already says "Registration required"; true of a paid tutorial and of a summit's free sign-up alike), then a `LinkTile` "Check your registration" to `conference.toml`'s new optional `registration_url` (2026: the registration dashboard, from us.pycon.org/2026/attend/information/); a year without one shows the card without the link.<br>• **Removed while open** (ux.md §4.1, brought forward from P2): the component remembers the session it last showed (`use_ref`), for that id only: the same View given another id in place (the Router reuses a level's component) forgets it. When a newer feed lacks the id, the content stays under an inline note on `tertiary_container`, "Removed from the latest schedule" (D-47's words), a live region (`session.removed`). A status component of its own keeps the minute tick out of the screen's render, as the strip's does (D-165). One tile for the place an attendee walks to, with the others folded, replaces Ionic's up to seven inline links (SESS-02). A countdown on other days repeats the date beside it. A throwaway build with the fake clock at Saturday 10:05 AM (not committed) showed "Starts in 25 min", then "Starts in 24 min" after the next tick without a touch, and "Happening now" in `live` on the 10:05 break. At the largest Larger Text (about 310 %) and at 200 % on Android everything reflowed without clipping; at the largest size only, "registration" in the link breaks inside the word ("registratio" / "n"), one word wider than the tile's text column between its two icons. After the reviews (a throwaway build with the fake clock at Friday, May 15, 6:00 PM, and a feed that drops 142 25 s after the start): on Pacific time (`SIMCTL_CHILD_TZ`, Android `cmd alarm set-timezone`) the talk's time tile was its two lines, with no status, no note and no blank; the removed note showed on `tertiary_container` in light and dark on both platforms; the break without a "Break" badge and the 24 dp sections as above; `uiautomator dump` read the menu item "Open on us.pycon.org / Opens in browser". | D-192 | *(The current slot, the first display's flag and the "Now" button amended by D-204; what TalkBack hears after a jump by D-241; where the key sits by D-242.)* **The Schedule screen: one-line day tabs, the current slot, the jump's sequencing and where positions live** (records SCHED-01, SCHED-02, D-18 and D-155 for the screen; settles S2's day-tab question; amends ux.md §5.2's two-line `DayLabel`, architecture §6.3's `ViewMemory` and §7.5's `current_slot_key`, and section M's `screens/` and `ui/` lists; #86).<br>• **Day tabs:** an `ft.Tabs` whose content is a `Column`: the `TabBar` first (U10), then the `StatusStrip`, then the `TabBarView`, one page per day of `days()`, sprint days included (seven in 2026).
