The bar is scrollable (`TabAlignment.START`); each label is one line, "Fri 15" (`timefmt.day_tab_label()`), in a `Tab(height=56)`; today's has a 6 dp `primary` dot beside it, centred on the label so it stays by the digits at the largest text (a `Badge` dot covered the last digit). The text's `semantics_label` is the full date, "Friday, May 15, today"; Flutter's tab bar puts its position first, so TalkBack reads "Tab 3 of 7, Friday, May 15, today" and the selection. Identifiers `schedule.day_tab.<index>` (section M).<br>• **The day it opens on:** `ViewMemory.schedule_day` when it is one of the days, else today (`clock.now()`, so #88's fake clock drives it) when today is a conference day, else the first day.<br>• **Lists:** D-153..D-156 as written, in `screens/schedule_days.py` (`DayList`, `SlotItem`, with `day_tab()` and `now_button()`); slots come from `timeline(data, day, prefs.excluded_tracks)`; today's list has `cache_extent=FULL_LIST_EXTENT` (`ui/scrolling.py`). A day without sessions shows "Monday is a sprint day" with "See the sprints" (`/more/sprints`); a day the filter empties shows "No sessions on Wednesday match your filters" with "Clear filters" (`excluded_tracks=()`).<br>• **The current slot:** ~~`current_slot_key(slots, now)` (`core/timeline.py`) is the last slot that has started while one of its sessions has not ended (`start <= now < end`, D-179's rule), else the next slot to start, else the day's last slot~~ the slot that is on, else the now line (`now_place()`, D-204); a slot of notices alone is over once it starts. Architecture's sketch, the first slot starting at or after `now`, would skip the slot that is on.<br>• **The jump** is `ui/scrolling.jump_to_key(list_ref, key, duration=prefs.motion_ms(400), ended=...)`: `scroll_to(scroll_key=...)`, then a wait for the list's `on_scroll` end (`duration` + 0.5 s), asked again up to 3 times when nothing scrolled (an item not built yet, or one already in place). It returns how it ended (`Jump`): `"landed"` on a scroll end; `"still"` after 3 tries without one, which on today's fully built list (D-154) means the slot is in place; `"failed"`, asking no more, when there is no list to scroll: the ref is unset; the list has left the page, which Flet reports as a `RuntimeError` saying the control "must be added to the page first" (logged at INFO), and which a "Now" tap's jump meets when the user switches tabs before it lands, since a handler, unlike an effect, is not cancelled on unmount; or Flutter's `TimeoutException` (a list that never registered its methods within 10 s), which reaches Python as a `RuntimeError` and is logged. Any other error propagates. **First display of today:** the mount effect awaits one round trip (`storage.get`, as D-156), renders the other days, then jumps, once per ~~process (`ViewMemory.jumped_to_now`)~~ conference day in each process (`ViewMemory.jumped_on`, D-204). Its dependency is whether a day can show (`[shown is None]`), so a Schedule opened before any data (NEW-03) runs it on the render that first has the data: the second render and the jump are not lost, and a later tab return does not jump. Both jump effects pass a no-op sync `cleanup=` (`_cancel_on_unmount`), so a bottom-tab switch cancels a jump still waiting (F2, F39, D-70 rule 3); without it the task scrolled a list that had left the page and reported the error on the next screen. The slot's divider sits outside ~~its `ScrollKey` box~~ the slot, above its `ScrollKey` anchor (D-242), so a jump puts the slot's time, not a second line, under the tab bar. **"Now" from another day** (the button reads "Today" there): it selects today, and an effect of that render waits for `Tabs.on_change` to report the switch (at most 1 s), which Flutter sends once the slide has finished, then jumps. After a jump the user asked for ("Now", "Today") lands, or finds the list already on the slot (`"still"`; not when it fails), the screen calls `announce_message("Friday, May 15, 2:00 PM")` when a screen reader is on and `supports_announcements` is true (`Prefs.announcements`, read with `screen_reader`): the switch made from Python and the scroll are silent otherwise. Flutter reports no announcements on Android (F13), so ~~TalkBack hears nothing yet (#223)~~ TalkBack hears it through a live region instead (D-241); an iOS live region would move VoiceOver's focus off the button.<br>• **The echo:** setting `selected_index` from Python comes back as `on_change`; the handler returns when the reported day is already the shown one, and setting equal state renders nothing.<br>• **Positions:** each day list's scroll end is kept in `ViewMemory.schedule_offsets[day]`. A detail pushed over the Schedule keeps it mounted and in place. A day list that shows again (a swipe back, a tab return, a new session) is a new list on the device, at the top: `TabBarView` keeps nothing of a page it no longer shows and Flet's scrollables have no `PageStorageKey`, so the screen puts the offset back with `scroll_to(offset=..., duration=0)` once the switch has settled; a swiped-back day therefore slides in at its top and then jumps to its place: U29.<br>• **"Now" button:** an extended FAB (`SCHEDULE`, tooltip "Jump to the current time", identifier `schedule.jump_now`) shown while today has slots, ~~; #89 owns when it shows beyond that and the one-time policy (D-18)~~ except while the place it goes to is on screen (D-204); the lists leave 88 dp under their last slot for it. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build with a fake clock at Friday 2:10 PM, check-only, never committed): the first display of Friday landed on the 2:00 PM slot at offset 3,823 px on the first try (`on_scroll` end), also when the Schedule was first opened from Now; "Today" from Saturday logged the echo of the switch, then landed on 2:00 PM on the first try; a detail push and Back kept Friday at 2:00 PM with no scroll event; a tab return to Schedule put Friday back at 3,823 px; a swipe from Saturday back to Friday logged its restore 15 ms after the change, and a recording showed Friday slide in at 7:30 AM and jump to 2:00 PM, the U29 interim. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK, the same fake clock) both jumps landed on the first try, and `uiautomator dump` read each tab as one clickable node, `schedule.day_tab.2` "Tab 3 of 7, Friday, May 15, today", selected. At iOS's largest Larger Text (about 310 %) a 46 dp tab cut the label's glyphs; at 56 dp the label and the indicator fit at every size, and at 100 % the bar is 10 dp taller than Flutter's default. A two-line label cannot fit a fixed-height bar at 200 % (S2), and Flet cannot read the text scale (U16). |
| D-204 | *(The button's refresh rule amended by D-224.)* **Jump to now: once per today, to the slot that is on or to the now line, a button that hides while it shows where now is, and a minute tick** (finishes SCHED-15 and SCHED-16; amends D-18's "once per app process", D-155's and D-192's target (`current_slot_key()` becomes `now_place()`), D-192's first-display flag and "Now" button, ux.md §5.2 items 4 and 5, its Interactions and Accessibility, architecture §6.3 and §7.5, and section M's `screens/` list; #89).<br>• **When it jumps by itself:** on the first display of each today in the app process (`ViewMemory.jumped_on`, the conference day it last jumped on, replaces `jumped_to_now`): when the Schedule opens on today, and also when it opened on another, remembered day and the user swipes or taps to today later (`Tabs.on_change`, which Flutter sends once the slide has settled, so today's fully built list is there, D-154). A "Now" or "Today" tap counts as that day's jump. Never on Back, a tab return, a refresh, a filter change or a minute tick, and never twice the same day; a process that lives into the next conference day, or a fake clock moved to another day, jumps once on that day's first display.<br>• **Where:** `core/timeline.now_place(slots, now)`, over the shown (filtered) slots, gives a `NowPlace`: `line`, how many slots have started, and `on`, the key of the last of them while one of its sessions has not ended (D-179's half-open rule; a slot of notices alone is over once it starts; a long session in an earlier slot does not hold it back). The jump goes to `on`, else to the now line (`NOW_KEY`, its own `ScrollKey`), which is then just above the next slot, above the first before the day starts, or last after it ends: the jump shows the line with what comes next, where D-192's next slot put the line just above the screen. `now_place()` replaces `current_slot_key()`.<br>• **The now line** (ux.md §5.2 item 4), on today's list only, before `slots[line]`: "Now · 10:42 AM" (`LABEL_MEDIUM`, `primary`) beside a 2 dp `primary` line to the right edge, 16 dp from the sides and 4 dp above and below, in place of the next slot's divider. A memoised component keyed by `NOW_KEY` (`screens/schedule_now.py`); a slot's divider is hidden rather than left out, so a moving line re-renders the two slots it passes and no row. One node read "Current time 10:42 AM", identifier `schedule.now_line`; not a live region, which would speak every minute.<br>• **The minute tick:** the Schedule calls `use_minute()`, so every 60 s while it is mounted and the app is in the foreground, and at once on a resume, today's dot, the button and the now line follow `clock.now()`, the fake clock included. The day shown is pinned at the first display, so a midnight while the Schedule is mounted moves the dot and turns the button into "Today", never the list. A minute re-renders the screen and today's day list (the line's time); its slots and rows are skipped (D-153).<br>• **The button:** after a jump that lands, or finds the list in place, the screen keeps a `Landing` (the day, the `NowPlace`, the feed version, the filter and the list's offset), and hides the button while today shows, now is at the same place over the same layout, and the list's last scroll end is within half a viewport of that offset (a plain distance: an offset does not say where a keyed item sits, so now may still show in the lower half when the button is back). It is also hidden while the first display's jump runs (`schedule_now.PENDING`), since a list already in place answers only after `jump_to_key()`'s tries (about 2.7 s), and kept while a screen reader is on, so a tap never removes the focused control. A tap aims at `clock.now()`'s place, as the render its landing causes does. A scroll away, a slot's start, a ~~refresh~~ refresh that publishes a new feed (D-224: one that brings the same feed publishes no version) or a filter change (both change the heights above the place) brings it back. A scroll of the user's own while a jump is waiting ends it as its landing, since a scroll end does not say who scrolled (the pinned `ListView.scroll_to()` returns nothing about where it got to); the next half-screen scroll corrects the button. On another day it always shows, as "Today". After the day's last slot it keeps "Now" and goes to the line at the list's end, which says the day is over: no label of its own.<br>• **What a screen reader hears** after a jump the user asked for: the slot's start, or the line's words ("Friday, May 15, current time 2:35 PM").<br>• **Tests** wait for what they expect (a poll with a deadline, as `tests/test_room.py` does), never for a fixed time, and the stand-ins' landing and settling waits are 5 s, so a loaded host only makes them slower (#237). | An app process often lives through the whole conference (iOS and Android keep a suspended app), so "once per process" jumped on its first day only; a jump to the next slot put the now line just off screen; a button that stays while its target shows is a dead control (ux.md §1, principle 8). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (an `ios-simulator` build, the fake clock at Friday 10:40 AM set through More › About this app › Developer): opening the Schedule logged one jump, to the 10:30 AM break, with the line "Now · 10:40 AM" under it and no button; a scroll to the morning brought the button back; a session pushed and Back, a tab switch to Map and back, "Show all" and the filter's Reset each logged no jump (the count stayed at one) and kept the list where it was; "Now" jumped again and hid the button; "Today" from Saturday switched and jumped. A minute later the line read 10:41 AM without a scroll. At 2:35 PM the first display landed on the line above 2:45 PM ("Jumped to now"). A first display elsewhere, then today, in one process (the fake clock moved through Developer after a day was picked): opened on the remembered Saturday with "Today", a swipe to Friday and, after another clock move, a tap on Sunday's tab each logged one "Jumped to slot-… (try 1)" and showed the slot that was on with the line under it; the same swipe on the Android emulator also landed on try 1. Before the day (7:00 AM) the list is already in place, so the jump answers "still" after its tries, about 3 s; screenshots taken through that time on both platforms show no button. After the day (9:30 PM) the line ends the list above the 88 dp clearance, nothing clipped. At iOS's largest Larger Text the line's words and the line fit beside each other. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) the same first jump, the button after a scroll away and "Now" landed on the first try, light, dark and at 200 %; `uiautomator dump` read the line as one node, `schedule.now_line` "Current time 10:40 AM". Under artificial load (five other builds and three emulators on the Mac) `tests/test_schedule.py` passed 20 times in a row (#237). |
| D-241 | **TalkBack hears where a jump the user asked for landed through a landing note, a polite live region that empties before and after it speaks** (settles #223; amends D-192's note on Android and ux.md §5.2's Accessibility).<br>• **Which path:** after a "Now" or "Today" jump lands (or finds the list in place), while a screen reader is on: `announce_message()` when the platform takes announcements (`Prefs.announcements`, iOS, unchanged); else the landing note (Android, where Flutter never reports announcements, F13). Never for the first display's jump, and never without a screen reader, so the note has no words then.<br>• **The note:** `screens/schedule_now.landing_note(words)`, a `Semantics(live_region=True, container=True, identifier="schedule.landing", label=words)` over a 1 dp square, placed first in a `Stack(fit=EXPAND)` around the `TabBarView`, so the list is above it and takes any touch there. It always renders, so the node exists before its label changes: the engine sends a live region's change only for a node it had in its previous update whose label changed, and a node with no size is dropped (F61). The words are Schedule state (`said`).<br>• **Said again:** every asked jump empties the note as it starts, so the same slot twice is a change twice. **No lasting stop:** an effect on `said` empties it `SAID_S` (5 s) after it was set; a new phrase restarts the wait (Flet cancels the previous run on a dependency change), and its no-op cleanup cancels it on unmount. Empty, the node has no label, action or state, so TalkBack's linear navigation passes it by; with words it is a stop for those seconds. The empty change says nothing: TalkBack drops an empty output and does not count it against its rate limit (F61). A focus-blocked node would need no timer (U41).<br>• **Rejected:** `announce_message()` on Android too, which works on API 34 but goes through `announceForAccessibility`, deprecated from API 36 and discouraged (F13); a label that keeps its words, a stray stop for good; a visible line, which would add a row to every Schedule for a screen reader's sake. | On my own `flet_probe_api34` emulator (Android 14, TalkBack 14.2 with its speech log on, release arm64 APK, fake clock Friday 2:10 PM): with focus on "Now" (a touch), Alt+Enter landed on 2:00 PM and TalkBack spoke "Friday, May 15, 2:00 PM" once (`windowContentChanged ... nodeLiveRegion=1`, `Speaking fragment text`), with no focus event after it and the green focus box still on the button; the fade 5 s later produced `ttsOutput= {}`. "Today" from Saturday switched and spoke the same, once. A second Alt+Enter 20 s after the first spoke nothing (`nodeNotFrequentAnnounced=false`); after a touch on the button, one 26 s after spoke again (F61). From Refresh, TalkBack's next item was the "2:00 PM" heading: the empty note is no stop. |
| D-197 | *(The link SnackBar's note amended by D-210.)* **Session detail's speakers and description, and what a link in a feed text opens** (records ux.md §5.5's speakers and About for SESS-06 and SESS-07; settles #91's open-space link under D-101; amends D-116's sheet with a rule style and smaller headings, the matrix's SESS-07 sketch and section M's `core/` and `ui/` lists).<br>• **Speakers** (`screens/session.py`, under `SectionHeader("Speakers", level=2, top=0)`): the pure `row_views.people(session, speakers)` lists the session's authors in the feed's order, each once: a directory speaker the session links is a `Speaker`, a name the directory has no one for is the name (the lightning talks' "Hosted by …", D-125, the only one in both feeds), and a speaker linked by id under another spelling (poster authors) comes after them. A speaker is `ui/rows.py`'s `SpeakerTile(speaker, target, *, identifier)`, built as an in-app `LinkTile`: a `ListTile` (`TITLE_HEIGHT`) whose leading is `speaker_avatar()` (the Speakers list's 48 dp `CircleAvatar`, the photo over the initials on `primary_container`, now shared with `SpeakerRow`), the name and `CHEVRON_RIGHT`, in a row of at least 64 dp (`min_height`: Material's 8 dp above and below the avatar; at the default 56 dp, six stacked avatars 8 dp apart read as one column), a tap pushing `…/speaker/<id>` (D-17), one merged button node named by the speaker (the initials under `exclude_semantics`), identifier `session.speaker.<key>`. No subtitle: Ionic's was the constant "Speaker", a cut bio says little, and the speaker's page, one tap away, has the bio and the sessions (the matrix had sketched a one-line bio). A pseudo-speaker is `BODY_LARGE` text in a `Semantics(container=True)` that nothing taps, identifier `session.name.<n>`: such names are numbered from 0 in the authors' order, since D-125 takes every "Hosted by …" record out of the directory and an author name may match no one, so a session can have two, and one shared identifier would leave a UI test unable to find either.<br>• **About:** `SectionHeader("About", level=2, top=0)`, then `ui/links.py`'s `feed_markdown(value, svc, *, identifier)`, the one builder of D-116's Markdown for every feed text (the speaker's bio in #99 too): `selectable`, `GITHUB_WEB`, `MARKDOWN_STYLE`, no auto-follow, 16 dp from the edges, in a container node (`session.description`); its `on_tap_link` awaits `open_feed_link(svc, href)`. Speakers come before About (ux.md §5.5); a session without speakers or without a description has no such section.<br>• **What a link in a feed text opens** (`feed_link(href, site_url)`, pure; settles the open-space slip of D-140): `http`, `https` and `mailto` links open as written (the HTML descriptions' links are absolute already, D-114); a path from the site's root (`/2026/…`) or a host without a scheme (`//host/…`) joins the year's site URL, which is what it means on the site; a web address written without its scheme, whose first segment is a host name with a dot and a top-level domain of letters (2026's `[pytest](pytest.org)`), gets `https://`, as a browser's address bar would: joined to the site URL it was `https://us.pycon.org/2026/pytest.org`, a page that does not exist; anything else (another scheme such as `javascript:`, `file:` or `tel:`, `#top`, `../slides`, a word) depends on a page the app does not know, so it opens nothing. The result goes through D-182's link rule (web pages in the in-app browser view, `mailto` to the mail app); a site path is never handed to `link_kind()`, which would push `/2026/…` as an app page. A tap that opens nothing says "Couldn't open the link", as a failed launch does, rather than nothing at all, and the log names no link (D-72); ~~on a pushed View such as session detail that SnackBar is drawn on the tab root underneath and not seen until #197 is fixed~~ that SnackBar shows on session detail itself (D-210) (neither recorded feed has such a link). An address `urlsplit` cannot read (`http://[`, `//[`) opens nothing too.<br>• **The sheet's rule and headings:** `MARKDOWN_STYLE.horizontal_rule_decoration` is a 1 dp `OUTLINE_VARIANT` top border, Material's divider: Flet's default, a 5 dp bar in the divider colour, read as a heavy block between a description's paragraphs (2026's 1142). Headings are 18/16/16/16 sp (h1..h4, weight 500; D-116 had 22/18/16/16): under the level-2 "About" (`TITLE_SMALL`, 14 sp), D-116's 18 sp "Abstract" read larger than the section holding it; at 16 sp an h2 is a row title's size, set apart from the body by its weight, and the primary colour marks the section header.<br>• **Screen readers** (F41): every link in a description is a node of its own named by its text, with its tap as the node's action. VoiceOver gets the link trait (Flutter's iOS bridge maps `isLink` to `UIAccessibilityTraitLink`); TalkBack calls it a button, since Flutter's Android bridge makes a link without a URL a `Button` and neither Flutter's text spans nor Flet's `Semantics` can carry one. Accepted for P1, settled on #91 under D-101 with the issue's criterion amended to match: the link is found, named and operable, with the link trait on iOS; TalkBack's link role waits for U31 (#230, `Semantics.link_url`) and #109's pass hears both screen readers on devices.<br>• **Not done:** a `LinkTile` list under the text (D-116's fallback), since the links work and are announced as actionable; a bio subtitle. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build checked against `build/python-app`, light and dark): the talk 142 showed its speaker row with the photo and the description, the panel 110 six rows in the feed's order with initials on `primary_container` for the five without a photo, the lightning talks 175 "Hosted by …" as plain text and their description's headings and eleven-item list, the talk 1142 its link, the 1 dp rule and the headings, the open space 9086 its two links, underlined in `primary`. On the shared simulator a tap on 9086's "pytest" opened `pytest.org` (redirected to docs.pytest.org) in the in-app Safari view, whose close returned to the session; a tap on Catherine Nelson pushed `/schedule/session/110/speaker/112`, and Back returned to the session. At the largest Larger Text the header and the card reflowed; the sections below were checked at 200 % on Android, since the own simulator cannot scroll. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) `uiautomator dump` showed each speaker as one clickable `Button` node, `resource-id` `session.speaker.<id>`, named by the name alone (no initials), 56 dp tall; the description's links as `android.widget.Button` nodes named "pytest", "pytest-fly", "on our event page"; a tap on "pytest" started `VIEW https://pytest.org/` in Chrome's custom tab; before the container, "Hosted by …" was read into the heading node ("Speakers / Hosted by …"), after it a node of its own. At 200 % text in dark, the rows, the text and the lists reflowed with nothing clipped. TalkBack itself could not be driven from adb (an injected tap activated the link instead of moving TalkBack's focus), so its words are inferred from the class it reads, `android.widget.Button`, and the bridge's source (`AccessibilityBridge.java`, `shouldBeTreatedAsButton`); the iOS simulator has no VoiceOver, so the link trait is read from `SemanticsObject.mm`. After the reviews (#231; new builds on both, checked against `build/python-app`): the iOS 26.5 simulator, light and dark, showed 110's six speaker rows 64 dp apart (192 px at 3x) with a clear gap between avatars, and 1142's "Abstract" at 16 sp under the 14 sp "About"; on the Android 14 emulator `uiautomator dump` gave every `session.speaker.<id>` 64 dp and "Hosted by …" its own node `session.hosts`, and dark at 200 % reflowed with nothing clipped. In pytest, `feed_link("//[", …)` raised `ValueError` from `urljoin` before the join moved inside the `try`. |
| D-206 | *(The share sheet SnackBar's note amended by D-210.)* **Session detail's Share, the Posters row's posters, and an open space's image and site link** (records ux.md §5.5's Share and its item 7 for NEW-06, SESS-09 and SESS-11; amends ux.md §5.5's and NEW-06's URL pattern, D-186's overflow for open spaces and section M's `core/`, `screens/` and `ui/` lists; #92).<br>• **Modules:** `screens/session_extras.py` (`ShareButton`, `Posters`, `open_space_extras()`, `site_title()`), so `screens/session.py` stays under D-66's 400 lines (367); `ui/share.py`, the share sheet: `centre()`, moved from `screens/developer_data.py` so no screen imports another, and `share_text()`; the pure `row_views.share_text()` and `timeline.posters()` in `core/`.<br>• **Share** (NEW-06): `IconButton(SHARE_OUTLINED, tooltip="Share session")` between the star and the overflow on every session, a break and a notice too (their time and place are worth sending), identifier `session.share` merged into the button (`named()`, D-190), disabled while the sheet is up, its identifier's `Semantics` with it (`named(..., disabled=)`, F42, D-195). It calls `Share.share_text(text, subject=<title>, share_position_origin=centre(page))` (`controls/services/share.py` at the pin; the origin anchors the sheet on a tablet, D-62). The text is three lines: the title; the conference's name, the day, the time in conference time with its zone's abbreviation, and the place ("PyCon US 2026 · Saturday, May 16, 10:30–11:00 AM PDT · Grand Ballroom A"); the public page. The zone is named because the reader may be anywhere (D-35); the time starts where the detail's does (the Job Fair's 9:30, DATA-11) and follows the device's 12/24-hour setting; the name says what the day belongs to when there is no link; a generic break has no place. **The page is the feed's own `Session.url`**, the link "Open on us.pycon.org" opens, not a pattern from the year data and the id as ux.md and NEW-06 sketched: the 2026 lightning talks (id 175) are at `presentation/177`, and keynotes, plenaries, summits, events and notices have numeric ids and no page, so a pattern would send dead links. Synthetic ids (collapsed breaks, the Posters row, poster details) have no `url`, so they share text without one (tests). A sheet that fails to open says "Couldn't open the share sheet", logged by the error's class (D-72); ~~on a pushed View that SnackBar is not seen until #197, as a failed link's (D-197)~~ it shows on session detail itself, as a failed link's does (D-210).<br>• **Posters** (SESS-09): `timeline.posters(data, session)` is the poster details (DATA-07) of a Posters row, the sessions of its kind outside the timeline at its start and location, in `schedule_order` as the Posters track page lists them (D-199), memoised per feed and row; `()` for any other session and before the feed publishes posters. The Posters detail shows them under a level-2 "Posters (25)" (read "25 posters") as the memoised `SessionRow`s with their stars (`session.session.<key>`, `session.favorite.<key>`), without the "Poster" badge (`omit`), the time (`SessionRow(show_time=False)`, which `spoken_phrase()` follows) and the place (`in_room`), which the page above says for all of them: a poster takes its row's time (DATA-07), so its own could never differ. A row pushes `…/session/<row>/session/poster-detail-<k>` (D-17), so Back pops to the Posters detail.<br>• **An open space** (SESS-11): its image in a 16:9 frame the content's width (`Container(aspect_ratio=16 / 9)`, 16 dp gutters, 12 dp corners, `surface_container_highest`), an `Image` with no size of its own, so its constraints scale it down to fit (`CONTAIN`) and a small one keeps its size, centred, rather than blur at the frame's; the frame's fill shows while it loads, then it fades in over `prefs.motion_ms(250)`. The frame is a `Stack`: under the image, the same image at `COVER` filling the frame (`left`…`bottom` 0, `exclude_from_semantics`, an empty `error_content`, and no fade, which would lay it out loose, F45) and a `Container(blur=24)` with a 30 % `surface` scrim, so a portrait flyer sits on its own colours instead of grey bars over half the frame (the first build's 5K Fun Run filled 49 % of its width); the image itself is not positioned, so the Stack gives it at most the frame and it still scales down and never up. `error_content` is a centred row of `HIDE_IMAGE_OUTLINED` and "Couldn't load the image" (`expand_loose`), so at large text the line grows sideways before it grows down in the fixed frame; its name is "Image for “<title>”" (`session.image`), since the feed describes no picture. No `placeholder_src`: Flet sizes it with the image's own width and height, which a scaled image has not (F44). Then its entry on the site's open-spaces page as an external `LinkTile` (`LANGUAGE`, "Open on us.pycon.org", `session.site`), by the link rule (D-182). That link is the overflow's one item, so an open space has no overflow (amends D-186) rather than the same link twice; P2's Add to calendar brings it back. | The feed already says where each session's page is, and only it knows the pages that exist. The posters are what an attendee opens the Posters row for, and the rows the rest of the app uses give them a star, a phrase and a push; the badge, time and place repeated 25 times under a page that names them were noise (the review of #246 took the time out too). Ionic cropped the image as a hero (`cover`, at most 220 px); posters of text and logos lose their edges that way, and a frame of fixed proportion keeps the page from jumping when the image arrives. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build checked against `build/python-app`; an iPhone 17 of #92's own, light and dark): the talk 142 showed the star, Share and More options; the Posters row its 25 posters under "Posters (25)" with stars and no badge or place; the 5K Fun Run (9119) its portrait image centred in the frame, then "Open on us.pycon.org" and no overflow; at the largest Larger Text the app bar's three icons kept their places and the header wrapped. On the shared simulator, driven by taps: a poster pushed its detail and Back returned to the Posters detail; Share opened the share sheet, titled by the subject, with Share greyed while it was up and enabled after; its Copy put the talk's three lines on the pasteboard (`simctl pbpaste`), the URL last, and the Posters row's two lines with no URL. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`): Share opened the system chooser ("Sharing text", the two lines of the Posters row); a poster's tap pushed its detail and the Back key returned; in airplane mode the open space's frame said "Couldn't load the image" under its icon, read as `session.image` "Couldn't load the image", and online "Image for “5K Fun Run”"; `uiautomator dump` read `session.share` as a `Button` "Share session", each poster a `Button` `session.session.poster-detail-<k>` with its phrase and its star a `Switch`, and `session.site` "Open on us.pycon.org / Opens in browser"; dark at 200 % text wrapped the posters' titles at three lines and the frame kept its proportion, nothing clipped. After #246's reviews (iOS 26.5 debug build and Android release APK, each checked against `build/python-app`): the poster rows show and say the title and the speakers only (`uiautomator dump`: "Beyond “LGTM”: … Talk to Each Other. Daaimah Tibrey."); the 5K Fun Run's frame shows its blurred copy beside the image in light and dark, read as one `session.image` node; a first build with the copy fading in left it the image's own width, white at the edges (F45); at the largest Larger Text the poster titles end in an ellipsis at three lines with the star on top, and the frame keeps its proportion; the Android chooser for the talk holds its three lines, the URL last, and previews the first two. |
| D-195 | *(The summary's insets amended by D-229; the chips' labels by D-249.)* **The Schedule's track filter: a hook and a sheet beside the screen, a chip per track the feed has, a summary listing badge names, a count over every day, one edit at a time, and nothing that moves the lists** (records D-20 and ux.md §5.3 for SCHED-09, SCHED-10 and STORE-09; amends ux.md §5.2's summary words, §5.3's group names and `draggable`, D-178's `FILTER_USERS` and section M's `screens/` list; #87).<br>• **Modules** (D-66; `schedule.py` is at 357 lines): `screens/schedule_filter.py` holds `use_track_filter(info, tracks, count)`, the Schedule's hook (the `TUNE` action, the summary, the sheet's open state and the edits), and the pure `hidden_tracks()`, `summary_text()` and `differs_from_default()`; `screens/schedule_sheet.py` holds `filter_sheet()`, `filter_groups()`, `count_label()` and `DEFAULT_HIDDEN` (the storage table's default). The Schedule stays the only caller of `timeline()` (D-20, D-178): it hands the hook the feed's tracks (`timeline(data, day, ())` on every day, memoised on the feed version) and the count; the hook edits `Prefs.excluded_tracks`, so `schedule_filter.py` joins `FILTER_USERS`; the sheet module is given the hidden keys and names neither.<br>• **The stored filter** is unchanged (`pycon.excluded_tracks`, D-160): an absent key reads as `['Open Space']`, an explicit `[]` is kept. Chips, Reset, "Show all" and "Clear filters" write through `Prefs.save` under one `asyncio.Lock` per Schedule, each edit computed from the filter the last one saved, so two quick taps both count (a slow-write test fails without the lock); a failed write keeps the change for the session and says "Couldn't save your change" (D-120). A chip tap gives `selection_click()` (ux.md §4.5) before the write.<br>• **Chips:** one per track the Schedule can show on any day (a chip for a track without sessions would change nothing, ux.md §1.8), under level-2 headings by `conference.toml`'s `group` in `TRACK_GROUPS`' order and the registry's: "Talks and keynotes", "Hands-on", "Community", "Breaks and notices" (ux.md's "Logistics" is organisers' jargon); a key the registry lacks follows its group's known ones, by name. Labels are `track_name()`'s short names, as on the badges (a chip and a badge name a track alike). `ft.Chip(on_select)`: a filter chip toggles, unlike search's scope chips (D-193); Flutter flips it and sends the new state first (`chip.dart`), so the re-render patches nothing. Identifiers `schedule.filter.<key>`; Android reads each as a `Button` with its name, `selected` or not. An unselected chip shows only its outline (M3's filter chip): Flutter's `RawChip` sits on a canvas `Material`, which paints `surface` under any chip colour, white on the light sheet even with `bgcolor=TRANSPARENT`, so the chip paints `SHEET_COLOR` (`surface_container_low`, M3's sheet default); the sheet keeps that default rather than being given it, since a colour given to a shown sheet stayed light when the device turned dark. The rows are 8 dp apart, as the chips in a row: at large text a chip outgrows its 48 dp target, and 4 dp let the rows touch.<br>• **The sheet:** `ft.use_dialog` from the hook; `BottomSheet(show_drag_handle=True, scrollable=True, use_safe_area=True)`, not `draggable` (Flutter's handle is always draggable, and the chips scroll without a drag competing); no slide under reduced motion (`AnimationStyle.no_animation()`, D-173). "Filter sessions" (level-1 heading) and Reset (disabled while the filter is the default; its identifier's `Semantics` is disabled with it, as is "Show 0 sessions"'s, since Flet's `Semantics` sets `enabled` from its own `disabled` and the merged node read as enabled on Android, F42) share a wrapping row on top and scroll with the chips and "What do these mean?" (`schedule.filter.tracks`, to `/more/tracks`; the Tracks hub is #102's); only "Show N sessions" (`FilledButton`, centred, its own 24 × 12 padding, `elevation=0`: given a style, Flet's `Button` sets every state's elevation to its own, 1 by default, and the shadow under the disabled button's 12 % fill made it dark grey on the light sheet, F43) is pinned, at the bottom: with the title pinned too, the chips had about a fifth of the screen at the largest text. The hook builds the sheet only while it is shown (D-153). The count is the rows the Schedule shows on all days with the filter, notices included (2026: 197 by default of 320); at 0 the button is disabled over "Select at least one type". The sheet takes the screen's height: Flet sizes a scrolling `Column` to at least the room it is given (`scrollable_control.dart`, #6450), and a `ListView` shrink-wraps only without a bound, so no Flet scrollable can be as tall as its content (U32, #238).<br>• **The summary** sits under the day tabs whenever a track the feed has is hidden, the default included: a decorative `FILTER_LIST`, "Hidden: Open space" (up to three names as a list, "Hidden: Talk, Open space", then "and 2 more": "Hiding: Talk and Open space" read as one talk and one open space), "Show all" (`excluded_tracks=()`), the words and the button in one wrapping row so at large text the button moves under the words whole. While nothing is hidden an empty `Container` keeps its place: when the line came or went, the day pages shifted in Flutter's `Column`, which rebuilt them and put every list back at its top. Neither the summary nor the count is a live region: on iOS a changing live region takes VoiceOver's focus (F13), out of the sheet at every tap.<br>• **The dot** (`Badge(small_size=8)` on the `TUNE` icon, not the 48 dp button, whose corner put it by the screen's edge) shows only when the hidden tracks differ from the default's over the feed's tracks (D-20: not "while any track is hidden", which the summary says); the button's node is labelled "Filter sessions" with the value "Changed", its tooltip kept out of the semantics (D-184).<br>• **No movement:** a filter change calls no `scroll_to` (#313), and the memoised path re-renders only the slot items whose rows or divider changed (D-153, D-178): a test hides charlas and counts exactly those slot bodies and no row body; it fails with the slot memo removed.<br>• **Tests:** `tests/sessions.py` can encode each message as a transport does (msgpack with Flet's encoder, `sessions.ENCODE`, on in `tests/test_schedule_filter.py`): `ft.use_dialog` diffs the page's dialog list against the snapshot that encoding records, so without it the sheet never reached the session's index. It stays off elsewhere: encoding every patch made a timed jump test miss its wait on a loaded host. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds, the bundle checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17" driven by taps): the default showed "Hidden: Open space" with no dot; the sheet opened with every 2026 track under its heading and "Show 197 sessions"; hiding Talk made it "Show 137 sessions" at once; "Show 137 sessions" closed the sheet over "Hidden: Talk, Open space" and the dot; a relaunch kept both; Reset brought back the default and "What do these mean?" opened the Tracks stub. A first build's "Show all", with the list scrolled to 12:30 PM, put the list back at 7:30 AM; with the placeholder, Reset from a scrolled list added the line and the list stayed where it was. Light, dark and the largest Larger Text: the title wraps by words with Reset under it, the title and the chips scroll above the pinned, centred two-line button, chip rows stay 8 dp apart, "Select at least one type" wraps centred above the disabled button, and the summary wraps with "Show all" under it (the first build broke "session" / "s" and left the button's label against its rounded end). On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` read the action as `schedule.filter` "Changed, Filter sessions" (before the label it read "Changed" alone), each chip a `Button` `schedule.filter.<key>` with `selected`, the headings, `schedule.filter.reset`, `schedule.filter.show` "Show 197 sessions", the summary `schedule.filter_summary` and `schedule.show_all`; light, dark and 200 % text reflowed with nothing clipped and no chip rows touching. After the review fixes: Reset read `enabled="false"` at the default and `enabled="true"` after a chip tap, and "What do these mean?" read as `schedule.filter.tracks`; a sheet left open while the simulator turned dark followed it. At iOS's largest Larger Text, beyond 200 %, "Lightning talks" faded at its chip's end: Flutter's chips keep a string label on one line (`chip.dart`, `maxLines: 1`); D-249 lets the label wrap. |
| D-249 | **A filter chip's label wraps: the chip draws no check mark or side padding of its own; its label, `chip_label()`, holds the check, the padding and a `Text` that may wrap** (settles #330; amends D-195's chips and ux.md §5.3; F69).<br>• **The bug:** at iOS's largest Larger Text, "Lightning talks" lost its last letter and "Sponsor presentation" four, faded at the chip's end: Flet sends a string `label` as a plain `Text`, and Flutter's `RawChip` gives its label one line, no wrapping and a fade. That failed P1's exit criterion 4 and ux.md §9.<br>• **Why not just a `Text` label:** `ft.Text(name, max_lines=3)` wraps, but the chip measures its label at its whole width and then lays it out beside the check mark and paddings no taller than that (F69). On the iOS 26.5 simulator "Sponsor presentation", too wide for either, wrapped whole; "Lightning talks", one line at the whole width and two beside the check, showed "Lightning" with "talks" cut off below it.<br>• **The chip:** `show_checkmark=False`, `padding=Padding.symmetric(vertical=8)` (M3's vertical 8 dp), `label_padding=Padding.all(0)`, so the chip lays the label out at the width it measured it at. `selected`, `on_select`, `bgcolor=SHEET_COLOR` and `named()`'s identifier are unchanged, so the chip still reads as a `Button` with its name, `selected` or not (D-195).<br>• **The label** (`screens/schedule_sheet.py`'s `chip_label(name, shown)`): a `Container` with M3's start and end padding (16 dp, or 8 dp before the check), painted `TRANSPARENT` so it takes a hit anywhere (the chip hit-tests its label at the label's centre, `chip.dart`'s `hitTest`, which for a short selected name such as "AI" falls between the check and the name), around a tight `Row` (8 dp apart) of the check while selected, `Icon(CHECK, size=18, color=PRIMARY, apply_text_scaling=True)` (M3's 18 dp chip icon in Flutter's selected check-mark colour, growing with the text as Flutter's own check mark does, D-215), and the name, `Text(max_lines=3, expand=True, expand_loose=True)`: loose in the row, so a chip stays as narrow as its name and wraps only when it must (`max_lines` because the chip's text style says one line; a track name is at most a word per line). The check now appears and goes with the re-render after a tap, not with Flutter's drawer animation, so it follows the chip's colour by a round trip.<br>• **Tests:** `tests/test_schedule_filter.py` checks the label's shape (a wrapping, loose `Text`; the check only while selected; the painted box), that no chip draws its own check mark or label padding, and that a tap takes the check away. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator at the largest Larger Text (`accessibility-extra-extra-extra-large`), light: "Lightning talks" and "Sponsor presentation" show whole on two lines beside the check, selected or not; taps on "AI", "Security" and "Sponsor presentation" toggled them; at the default size the sheet looks as before. On the Android 14 emulator at 200 %: nothing cut, as before; `uiautomator dump` read each chip as `Button` `schedule.filter.<key>`, named by the track and `selected` as before, and a tap on "AI" unselected it. |
| D-215 | **The status strip at large text: its icon scales with the text, and Refresh moves under a line that does not fit beside it** (settles #195; amends ux.md §3.9's `StatusStrip`).<br>• **Why the icon was not beside the first line: not a Flet gap.** `vertical_alignment=START` reaches Flutter at the pin (`RowControl` passes it as the `Row`'s `crossAxisAlignment`, `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/row.dart`, and an expanded child is only wrapped in `Expanded`, `base_controls.dart`'s `_expandable`), and on the iOS 26.5 simulator the icon did top the line. But it stayed 16 dp while a `BODY_SMALL` line (12/16) grew with the text, to about 50 dp at iOS's largest size, so it sat as a speck at the top of the first line's box, above its letters.<br>• **The icon scales with the text:** `Icon(size=16, apply_text_scaling=True)` (Flutter's `Icon.applyTextScaling`, which sizes the icon through `MediaQuery.textScalerOf`). 16 dp is one `BODY_SMALL` line at 100 %, so the icon is one line tall at every size, and topping the line puts it beside the first line. Scaling needs no text-scale factor, which Python cannot read until U16; a top padding sized to the first line would. Under Android 14's nonlinear font scaling a 16 dp icon grows a little less than a 12 sp line (at 2.0, about 28 dp beside a 32 dp line), a few dp that do not move it off the line.<br>• **Refresh moves under a line that does not fit beside it.** The strip's row is `Row(wrap=True, alignment=SPACE_BETWEEN, spacing=0, run_spacing=0)` over the line, a `tight` row whose text is `expand_loose`, and Refresh, in a column that stretches it to the width (a Wrap is only as wide as its runs). A line that fits beside the button shares its run, centred against the 48 dp button with the button at the end, as before. A longer one keeps the width, and the button takes a run of its own under it: at large text, and at 100 % for a long line, such as "Couldn't update · schedule from Sun, Oct 11, 5:56 PM" on an iPhone 17 or a 411 dp Android phone, or the Offline line on a 360 dp one (the strip is then 88 dp, not 56). Python cannot tell 100 % from large text until U16, so the drop is by width, not by text size. Before, the button kept about 40 % of the width at the largest size and the line wrapped two words per line, a strip about 260 dp tall (#202's comment on #195).<br>• **How a run of its own looks.** The line has 8 dp above and below it (centred against the 48 dp button, that changes nothing; alone, it keeps the line off the strip's top edge), and Refresh has 16 dp before it, the icon's 16 dp and its 12 dp gap less the button's 12 dp inset, so on a run of its own its label starts under the line's words at 100 %, as "Show all" does in the Schedule's filter bar (D-195). At larger text the icon grows and the indent does not, so the label starts between the icon and the words. The filter bar puts its icon before the wrap; the strip keeps it in the line's row, because an icon before the wrap would be level with the line only when the line shares the button's run. | Seen on an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5) of this PR's own and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`), release builds checked against `build/python-app`, the strip held at "Offline · schedule from Sun, Oct 11, 5:56 PM" by a Developer feed URL on a closed port, and at "Couldn't update · …" by one that answers 404. Before: the 16 dp icon at the top of a five-line block, Refresh beside it (iOS, largest text). After: at 100 % a line that fits beside Refresh is pixel for pixel what it was (the Offline line, iOS and Android, the screens' rows compared); "Couldn't update · …" at 100 % (both) and the Offline line at 360 dp (Android, `wm density 480`) put Refresh under the line, its label under the words; at 200 % (iOS `accessibility-large`, Android 2.0) and at iOS's largest size, the icon is level with the first line, the line takes two or three lines over the full width and Refresh sits under it, a 48 dp target; light and dark alike. |
| D-211 | **Every heading has a level: the page's title 1, its sections 2, a group or a slot inside a section 3** (amends D-33's "headings are marked", D-170's title node, D-190's header level, D-194's "Now in this room" level and D-203's page without data; amends ux.md §2.2's Appearance label, §3.9's `TimeGutter`, `SectionHeader` and `EmptyState`, §5.1's header, §5.2's and §5.6's gutter and day headings, §5.7's app-bar title without data, §5.9's "Now in this room", §5.12's app name and §9's checklist; #205).<br>• **A heading is `Semantics(header=True, heading_level=n)`.** Both screen readers take a heading from its level alone (F40, F49); the flag stays with it, as Flutter and Flet call a heading a header.<br>• **Level 1, the page's title.** `AppScaffold` wraps a non-empty app-bar title in `Semantics(header=True, heading_level=1)`, which on a tab root also carries `<tab>.title` (D-170), all in the title's one node: Flutter's `AppBar` flags its title a header with no level, so "Schedule" or "More" was no heading (F49). A detail page whose bar is empty (session, speaker, track) has its own level-1 title in the page (D-186, D-203, D-199), and its empty bar title is no heading; speaker detail without data, which has no name to show yet, is titled "Speaker" in the bar (D-203). The filter sheet's "Filter sessions" stays level 1: the sheet is a page of its own for a screen reader (D-195).<br>• **Level 2, a section.** `SectionHeader(text, level=2)` is the default: the day, letter, section and group headings of roots and details; callers that already pass `level=2` keep it. `EmptyState`'s title, which takes the place of the page's content under its title, as My schedule's all-over notice does (D-191). About this app's app name, under the bar's "About this app": a node of its own, as are the two lines under it, so it is read alone and first. Now's status header, the first heading under the bar's "Now" (D-190). The Lightning talks card's heading (D-205): a node of its own, as its paragraphs are, and the card is none (`semantic_container=False`), since a Flutter `Card` merges what it holds into one node and the whole card read as one heading.<br>• **Level 3, inside a section.** Search's days under its type headings (D-193), a sprint card's name under "Projects" (D-202), More's "Appearance" inside You (D-169), merged into its `ListTile`'s own node (`MergeSemantics`, as `named()` does, D-190), room detail's "Now in this room", a group inside the selected day beside its slots (D-194), and every `TimeGutter`. **The gutter stays a heading** (ux.md §5.2, §9): on a day of 30 slots it is the one way to move slot by slot without stopping on every row and star. A slot belongs to its day: a level-2 day heading on My schedule, or the selected day tab on the Schedule, room detail and the open spaces, which a screen reader reads first ("Tab 3 of 7, Friday, May 15", D-192); so one level, 3, everywhere, without a parameter.<br>• **A test holds it:** `tests/test_semantics.py` scans every module under `src/pycon_us/` with `ast` for a `Semantics` call that sets `header` without `heading_level`, a level without `header=True`, or a constant level outside Flutter's 1 to 6, after checking that the scan finds the headings it should. | #205 found `SectionHeader` without a level; #209's second review found `EmptyState`, More's Appearance, About's app name and the gutter; reading Flutter's `AppBar` for this issue found the bar's title. Neither screen reader speaks the level (Android's `AccessibilityNodeInfo.setHeading` takes a boolean, iOS's header trait has no level), so what the user hears is only whether a node is a heading; the levels still say what each heading heads, so the choice for a new one is mechanical and a reviewer can check it. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, a `flet build apk` checked against `build/python-app`), the tree read through `UiAutomation` (`AccessibilityNodeInfo.isHeading()`, what TalkBack's Headings navigation moves by; `uiautomator dump` has no such attribute) had these headings: More: "More", "Programme", "You", "Appearance"; Schedule: "Schedule" and each gutter time ("7:30 AM", "9:00 AM"); session 2: its title, "Speakers", "About", and no heading for the empty bar; speaker 2: "Pamela Fox", "Wednesday, May 13", "About"; About this app: "About this app", "Conf Companion", "This build"; My schedule: "My schedule" and the empty state's "Nothing saved yet"; Now: "Now" and its status header; Map: "Map", "Rooms". Each root's title kept its `<tab>.title` on the heading node. The first build showed two nodes wrong, fixed above: Appearance's heading was an unlabelled node around the tile's, and the app name took the two lines under it into one heading. TalkBack's own gestures could not be driven from adb: injected taps bypass touch exploration, and console key and touch events did not move its focus. The iOS simulator has no VoiceOver. After the review (#258), a throwaway Flutter 3.47.7 widget test built the same widgets with the platform set to Android and to iOS and printed the semantics nodes: the bar's title is one node with its text, level 1 and identifier under the `AppBar`'s own unlabelled header node, on both platforms and as on main (on iOS without `namesRoute`, which `AppBar` sets only off Apple platforms); a day heading inside an `ExpansionTile`'s children is a level-2 node of its own on both, so My schedule's folded day headings need no container (one added on iOS moved the heading under the tile's own node); the Lightning talks card as it was read its heading and both paragraphs as one level-2 node with the button under it, and as fixed reads the heading alone, then the paragraphs, then the button. These are framework checks, not device ones: the re-check on the emulator and the simulator could not run in that session. |
| D-225 | *(Back from a pushed View settled by D-242.)* **A dialog, picker, sheet or menu gives the screen reader's focus back to its opener by itself: Flutter's `Navigator` restores it, and the app adds no focus code** (settles #262; amends D-208's TalkBack note, ux.md §2.4 and §9, and testing-and-quality.md §7.3; records F54).<br>• **The cause of #262 was the check:** at the pin, Flutter's `Navigator` keeps, for each route, the node that last had the screen reader's focus, and gives that node the focus back when a route above it pops (F54). Every modal route the app opens is such a route on the Views' `Navigator`: Developer options' confirmations (`AlertDialog`), the fake clock's `DatePicker` and `TimePicker`, the Schedule's filter `BottomSheet`, and the overflow menus (`PopupMenuButton`) of session detail, a track page and Sprints; and each closes by a pop, whether by Cancel, an action, a tap outside or Back (Flet's `closeModalRoute()` pops a route that is on top; D-208). #256 opened "Clear the cached schedule?" with an `adb` tap, which reaches the app without passing through TalkBack, so the row never had TalkBack's focus: the route gave it back to what had it before, the app bar, or to nothing.<br>• **No shared helper:** a `focus()` on the opener after the dismiss would redo what Flutter already does, would race its refocus (300 ms after the route is disposed, on Android), and exists only on buttons: `ListTile` and `PopupMenuButton` have no `focus()` at the pin, so the Developer rows and the menus could not use it (D-66: no speculative abstraction).<br>• **What keeps it working:** the opener stays in the tree, as the same control, while its route is up. A control swapped for another, or removed, has no node to come back to. Every opener today stays.<br>• **How to check it** (testing-and-quality.md §7.3): put TalkBack's focus on the opener and activate it as a double tap does (by hand, or `ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_FOCUS` then `ACTION_CLICK` through `UiAutomation`), never with an `adb` tap, and read the focus a second after the close, not at once: for up to a few hundred milliseconds it is on no node (the refocus landed 247 to 710 ms after the close).<br>• **Not covered, and not routes:** another app's window on top (the in-app browser, the share sheet), after which TalkBack's focus is on no node (#302); ~~and Back from a pushed View, which should restore by the same code but did not (#303)~~ (Back from a pushed View restores by the same code once the opener keeps its node: D-242).<br>• **VoiceOver:** the same framework code posts the node to VoiceOver at once (`UIAccessibilityLayoutChangedNotification`); read from the source, not heard: the Simulator has no VoiceOver, so #261 checks it on the iPhone (D-102). | On my own Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, a release arm64 APK of `main` at `be97438` whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`, TalkBack 14.2), with a throwaway `UiAutomation` probe (never committed; #257 makes a repo tool) that puts TalkBack's focus on a node, activates the focused node as a double tap does, sends TalkBack's Back (`GLOBAL_ACTION_BACK`) and reads which node has the accessibility focus every 20 ms: "Clear cached feed" focused and activated opened the confirmation with the focus on its title; Cancel put it back on the row 549 ms after the tap (on no node from 205 ms), Back 247 ms after; the fake clock's button opened the date picker (focus on its barrier, "Dismiss"), and its Cancel, a day and OK then the time picker's OK, and the time picker's Back each put the focus back on the button; the Schedule's "Filter sessions" got it back after Back on the sheet and after Talk hidden and "Show 137 sessions", read then as "Changed, Filter sessions"; session detail's "More options" after Back on the menu (710 ms) and a track page's after "Add all to My schedule" (382 ms). Screenshots show TalkBack's green box on each opener. The same row opened by an `adb` tap instead gave the focus back to the app bar ("Developer options") when TalkBack had it there before, and to no node on a page TalkBack had not focused yet: #256's two observations. A throwaway build that awaited `IconButton.focus()` on Share 2.5 s after the share sheet closed did move TalkBack's focus there (0.5 s after, it did not): the lead for #302. Relying on the framework is the one obvious way; code that repeats it would have to be kept in step with it. |
| D-232 | **A text field's helper that must be read in full is a `Text` in Material's helper style, and Reset and Apply keep their own padding at large text** (settles #280; amends D-187's Developer fields and buttons, D-198's role of `developer_ui.py` and section M's screen modules, and ux.md §5.13).<br>• **Helpers:** Flutter's `helperMaxLines` has no "no limit": null cuts a string helper to one line with an ellipsis (`input_decorator.dart`, `_HelperError._buildHelper()`, Flutter 3.47.7), and three lines cut the fake clock's at the largest text, at "Empty for r…", the way back to real time. `option_field()` now passes the helper as `developer_ui.helper_text()`, a `Text` in `BODY_SMALL` and `on_surface_variant`, which Flutter draws as given (a `helper` widget gets no `helperStyle`), so it wraps to every line it needs; both fields take it. The value and the error keep their three lines: the errors are short, and the value is what the user typed.<br>• **Buttons:** Reset and Apply take `developer_ui.LABEL_PADDING`, 24 dp at the sides and 8 dp above and below. Material shrinks a button's side padding from 24 dp to 12 at 200 % and 6 at 300 % and over, and at the largest text the labels touched the rounded ends, as the Schedule sheet's Show did (D-195). 24 dp is Material's own at 100 %, and the label line and its 16 dp stay under the 40 dp minimum there, so at 100 % the buttons are unchanged; Show's 12 dp above and below suit a full-width button, while these two sit beside each other at Material's height. Apply sets `elevation=0`, as Show: with a style, Flet lifts every state of a `FilledButton` to its `elevation` (1 by default); `OutlinedButton` has none.<br>• **Modules:** `option_field()` moves from `developer.py` to `developer_ui.py`, beside the screen's other drawing, so `developer.py` stays under D-66's 400 lines; D-230's `ON_PURPOSE` entry for `developer.fake_clock` names that module now. | A widget helper is the only way Flutter shows a helper whole at any size; a larger `helper_max_lines` is only a guess at the narrowest phone and the largest text. Pinning the buttons' padding is the fix the Schedule sheet already uses, and keeping Material's 100 % look leaves nothing to change where the default worked. The other buttons that keep Flutter's shrinking padding are #309's. |
| D-233 | **Every overflow menu is `ui/menus.py`'s: `overflow_menu()` and `menu_item()`, whose label wraps at 200 % text** (settles #260; amends D-209's item, D-230's `ON_PURPOSE` and section M's `ui/` list; U36, #308).<br>• **The item:** `menu_item(icon, label, on_click, *, identifier, link=False)` returns a `PopupMenuItem` with no `icon`: its content is the identifier's `Semantics` around `ListTile(leading=Icon(icon), title=label, horizontal_spacing=12, min_height=48)`, D-209's tile (F47), so the label wraps inside the menu (at most 280 dp) and the theme's `TITLE_HEIGHT` keeps the icon beside its first line (D-188), in the menu's 48 dp row and 12 dp gap. With `link=True` the title is a `Semantics(link=True)` around a `Text` read "<label>, Opens in browser" (ux.md §4.7, D-186), the words before the note. The tile has no `on_click`: the item takes the tap and closes the menu.<br>• **The button:** `overflow_menu(identifier, items)` is the `PopupMenuButton(MORE_VERT, tooltip="More options")` merged with its identifier by `named()` (F50, D-214), which each of the three menus built on its own.<br>• **The menus:** session detail's "Open on us.pycon.org" (`session.overflow`; the item `session.open_site`), the Sprints page's "Sprint info on us.pycon.org" and "Submit a sprint project" (`sprints.menu`; `sprints.info`, `sprints.submit`) and a track page's "Add all to My schedule" (`track.menu`; `track.add_all`). The items' identifiers are new but `track.add_all`'s. Each stays bare inside its item, whose own `MergeSemantics` takes it in (D-230), so `ON_PURPOSE` lists `menu_item()`'s one site in place of `track.py`'s. P2's items (Add to calendar, Export to calendar, Reminder settings, Schedule changes) are `menu_item()`s too.<br>• **The guard:** `tests/test_menus.py` scans every module: only `ui/menus.py` builds a `PopupMenuItem`, and none passes `icon=`; its unit tests hold the item's shape.<br>• **Seen** (#260): at 200 % text on the Android 14 emulator each label wraps beside its icon inside the menu ("Open on" / "us.pycon.org"; "Sprint info on" / "us.pycon.org" and "Submit a sprint" / "project"; "Add all to My" / "schedule"), and `uiautomator dump` shows each item as one `MenuItem` carrying its identifier and its whole label, the links' ending "\nOpens in browser". At the largest accessibility text on the iOS 26.5 simulator they wrap onto three lines, and "us.pycon.org", wider than the menu by itself there, breaks inside the host ("us.pycon.o" / "rg"). A zero-width space after each dot of a host was tried and dropped: it moved that break to a dot, but at 200 % on Android, where the host had a line of its own, it split the host too ("Open on us.pycon." / "org"), since Flutter fills a line up to its last break. | Three menus built the same button and item three ways, and two had the clipped label that F47 found on the third; one function each makes the right shape the only one, and the scan keeps a new menu from bringing `icon=` back. A tile also gives Material 3's 12 dp gap and the first-line icon, which a `Flexible` in Flet's `Row` (U36, #308) alone would not, so it can stay after that fix. |
| D-240 | **The Schedule's zone note: under the status strip, from the device's offset as the app's Python reads it, with a placeholder when the zones match** (records ux.md §5.2's note for SCHED-18 and DATA-12 under D-35; settles #218; records F60).<br>• **The rule:** `timefmt.zone_note(now, info.zone, info.time_zone_name, now.astimezone().utcoffset())` at the minute's `clock.now()`: "Times are Pacific Time (PDT)" (PST in winter) when the device's offset differs from the conference's at that instant, nothing when they match. Now (D-190) and session detail (D-186) already follow the same rule; Settings is P2's.<br>• **The device's zone:** `datetime.astimezone()` in the packaged app is the device's (F60), so no `ft.Page` field and no upstream work is needed; the minute tick (`use_minute()`) also catches a zone change made while the Schedule shows.<br>• **The look:** a 16 dp `PUBLIC` icon scaled with the text and 10 dp before `BODY_SMALL` words in `on_surface_variant`, 16 dp from the sides and 8 dp above and below (`ui/status_strip.zone_note_line()`, `schedule.zone_note`); the words start 42 dp in, under the filter summary's, the line it sits under most often. Not a live region: it changes only with the device's zone.<br>• **The placeholder:** while the zones match, an empty `Container` keeps the note's place in the tabs' column, so the `TabBarView` keeps its index and the day pages are never rebuilt (#313). |
| D-242 | **Back from a pushed View gives the screen reader's focus back to the row, tile or button that pushed it: Flutter's `Navigator` does, as for a dialog, once no opener sits under a control with a `ScrollKey`; a keyed list item carries its key on an empty anchor** (settles #303; amends D-192's and D-194's keyed boxes, D-225's note on pushed Views, ux.md §2.4, §5.2, §5.9 and §9, and testing-and-quality.md §7.3; records F62, U42).<br>• **The cause is in Flet:** a pushed View is a page route on the Views' `Navigator`, so its pop runs the same refocus as a dialog's (F54); the root's route entry survives (Flet keys each page by its View's route, `_buildNavigator` in `controls/page.dart`), and on the More page "Keynotes" kept its node and got the focus back 1.77 s after Back. The Schedule's rows did not keep theirs: at the pin Flet gives a control with a `ScrollKey` a new `GlobalKey` at each build (F62), so the box around each slot, and every row in it, was mounted again with new semantics nodes whenever an ancestor rebuilt, which a push, a pop, a sheet and a star tap all do. The refocus went to the old id, a node no longer in the tree.<br>• **Interim (U42, #321):** `ui.scrolling.anchored(key, content)` puts the `ScrollKey` on an empty 0 dp `Container` above the item, in a `Column` that stretches the item to the list's width, as the box did. A keyed scroll lands where it did, since the anchor's top is the item's top (`Scrollable.ensureVisible`, alignment 0). Only the anchor is mounted again. It serves the Schedule's slots (the divider stays above the anchor), the room page's highlighted row (the tinted box holds the anchor and the row) and the Schedule's now line. Every `ScrollKey` in the app goes through it.<br>• **No focus code:** no opener's identifier in `ViewMemory`, no `focus()` after the pop. Flutter already refocuses the recorded node, 300 ms after the route is disposed on Android (F54); a second refocus would race it; and the rows are `Container`s, which have no `focus()` at the pin (F54). The same reasons as D-225's.<br>• **What keeps it working:** an opener keeps its node while its View is covered. A new `ScrollKey` goes through `anchored()`; the scans in `tests/test_schedule.py`, `tests/test_room.py` and `tests/test_scrolling.py` fail on a key around content.<br>• **VoiceOver:** the same framework code posts the node on iOS (F54); not heard, since the Simulator has no VoiceOver (#261, D-102). | On my own Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, TalkBack 14.2, release arm64 APKs whose `app.zip` held the branch's code) with #262's throwaway `UiAutomation` probe, which also printed each node's virtual view id (Flutter's semantics node id). `main` at `ed7c215`: `schedule.session.19` focused (node 119) and activated pushed session detail; TalkBack's Back left the focus on no node for 3 s, and the row was now node 429; closing the filter sheet made it 655, and a star tap on another row gave every row on screen a new id while the slots' headings and the list kept theirs; idle for two minutes, nothing changed. In Python every control kept its `_i` across a re-render, a minute tick and a star tap, and a Flutter widget test of the same tree without Flet kept the row's id across a push and pop: the remount is Flet's. With this change: the rows kept their ids across a star tap and a push and Back; Schedule › "All about decorators" › Back put TalkBack's focus back on the row 726 ms after Back (still there 4 s later), and 741 ms after on a second round; a room page's highlighted row › session › Back, 773 ms after; the Back key on another row left the focus on it. The room page's jump still put the lit row at the top of its list. |

## E. Data and offline

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-38 | The app bundles a feed snapshot per year (`data/<year>/conference.json`, updated by `tools/update_snapshot.py` before builds), so it works offline from the first launch. The network refresh runs in the background. | The cheapest, most visible improvement, and the best demo opener. |
| D-39 | *(Data path confirmed by D-108; a refresh that brings the published feed again publishes nothing, D-224.)* One `Conference` store refreshes with a single in-flight request. Only after a successful parse is the raw feed written to the durable app data directory (`FLET_APP_STORAGE_DATA`, set by packaged builds and by `flet run`), through a temp file and `os.replace`. A corrupt cache is deleted and the app continues from the snapshot. A refresh never deletes the cache first. | The cache directory can be purged by the OS (F14). This also fixes Ionic's bug where an offline refresh wiped the cache. |
| D-40 | *(Triggers, interval and ticks recorded by D-175.)* The feed refreshes on start, on resume when older than 15 min, every 15 min while Now or Schedule is visible, and on demand. Time-dependent views recompute every 60 s, and only while visible. | Fresh enough at the venue, with no work in the background. |
| D-41 | Feed processing keeps Ionic's rules, each with a named test: the placeholder filter (which exempts posters and breaks), collapsed Break and Lunch rows and Lunch spans, the Job Fair row (grouped at 10:00, labelled 9:30), poster details, speakers linked by name, seeded sprint rooms, a start time on every time group, and bad slots skipped and logged. Ionic's display quirks are fixed as `improve` rows, not in the parser. | The data semantics are the product. |
| D-42 | Ids are strings and stay stable, including Ionic's synthetic ones (`conf_key+9000`, `poster-detail-<k>`, `{kind}-{room}-{start}`). They are URL-quoted in routes. | Favourites, the change log and the mock `mobile_state` all key on them. |
| D-43 | Time: datetimes are timezone-aware, in the year's IANA zone via `zoneinfo` and bundled `tzdata`. There is one `clock.now()`: a test override first, then the Developer fake clock, then real time. Day buckets are conference-local dates. `core` functions take `now` as an argument. Tests run under two machine time zones. | Ionic had two definitions of "today" and used a fixed UTC offset. |
| D-44 | *(Converter scope, escaping and rendering recorded by D-114..D-116; where it runs, its API and its boundaries by D-130 and D-131.)* Feed HTML is converted to Markdown once per feed version by a converter built on stdlib `html.parser`. It makes links absolute and falls back to plain text for unknown tags. The result is shown with `ft.Markdown(selectable=True, on_tap_link=...)`, and links open in the in-app browser. Search and filters match text, never markup. | Flet has no HTML control (F8), and this avoids heavy wheels. S3 measures coverage. |
| D-45 | *(The keynotes' entries and their session check recorded by D-217.)* Year content is bundled data, not code, in three files under `data/<year>/`:<br>• `conference.toml`: name, dates, time zone, venue, URLs, feed URL, track names.<br>• `content.toml`: keynotes, Wi-Fi, safety contacts, venue hours, links, event reminders, sponsor credits.<br>• `map.toml`: floor plans, room pins, booths.<br>`tomllib` loads them into frozen dataclasses, and the loader validates with clear errors. A unit test loads every year. Safety contacts come only from a verified public source and are re-checked each year. A content fix needs a new build (accepted). Rolling over means adding `data/2027/` and setting `config.YEAR`. | No hosting or remote config. The loader is the schema: stdlib only, and the files can carry comments. |
| D-46 | *(Folding, matching and result order recorded by D-180; the Speakers list's letters and filter by D-189.)* Search ignores case and accents (NFKD plus casefold) and requires **all words** to match (AND). It covers session titles and description text, speaker names and rooms, plus sponsors and places from P2. It debounces for 250 ms and groups results by type. The Speakers list sorts by displayed name, folded the same way. | Results narrow as you type. Ionic's OR matching over raw HTML both missed and over-matched. |
| D-47 | *(Which refreshes are diffed, who hears of a batch and the screen's route amended by D-255.)* Change log (`PyCon/pycon-us-mobile#312`, P2): after each successful refresh, a pure `diff(old, new)` by session id compares start, end, room and title, and records added and removed sessions. Changes are stored as JSON in the data directory. They show as a "Changed" chip for 24 h, a "Schedule updated · Review" SnackBar and a Schedule changes screen with saved sessions first. The wording is "Removed from the latest schedule", never "cancelled". ~~There is no diff on first install~~ A refresh over the bundled snapshot (a first install) is diffed only when sessions are saved, and only for them (D-255), and a parser change cannot cause a false one: the cache keeps raw bytes, so both sides of a diff are parsed by the current code. | A real complaint about the 2026 app, solved without a database. The feed's `cancelled` flag is always false. |
| D-48 | Favourites are local-first (a `list[str]` in SharedPreferences). They need no account and survive sign-out. They are stored per conference year (`pycon.<year>.favorites`), and so are the feed cache and the change log. | Attendees should not need an account to plan their day. Ids are only guaranteed unique within one year's feed, so a 2026 favourite must never mark a 2027 session. |
| D-49 | Storage:<br>• SharedPreferences, keys prefixed `pycon.` and listed in one table in `services/storage.py` (dicts JSON-encoded): prefs, favourites, flags.<br>• Files in the durable data directory: the feed cache and the change log.<br>• `flet-secure-storage` (P3): mock credentials.<br>• sqlite (P3): the staff outbox only. One connection, used only from the asyncio loop thread (no `to_thread`), with WAL mode and `PRAGMA user_version` migrations. | One store per need, and explicit thread ownership. SharedPreferences accepts only simple types (F12). |
| D-120 | **Storage failures are logged and returned, not raised** (refines D-39, D-49 and D-71; #68). In `services/storage.py`:<br>• a failed SharedPreferences write (including a timeout or a client error) or feed-cache write returns `False`; the cache write removes its temp file and leaves the previous cache, and the caller still publishes the data it parsed;<br>• a store that saves what the user chose (settings, favourites) keeps the new value in memory and says so on `False` (a SnackBar such as "Couldn't save your change"), so nobody believes a change is saved when it will be lost on relaunch;<br>• an unreadable cache file reads as `None` (the app continues from the snapshot), and a preference that cannot be read, or whose stored value is not of its type, reads as its default;<br>• atomic writes take turns (one lock), because a refresh whose task was cancelled can still be writing in its thread;<br>• each case is logged with the key or file name, never the value.<br>The exception is a data directory that cannot be created: `data_dir()` raises, since that is a broken install. Without `FLET_APP_STORAGE_DATA` (pytest, a plain `python` run), the data directory is `.flet/storage/data` under the working directory, the layout `flet run` uses. | The data in memory is correct for the session, and the next refresh or save writes again, so a storage error is worth a log line (`console.log`, D-72), not `report_errors`' "Something went wrong" or a lost refresh. Packaged builds and `flet run` always set the variable (F14); the fallback keeps what tests and scripts write in the git-ignored `.flet/`, as `flet run` would. |
| D-121 | **2025's nested posters are posters** (settles DATA-27 for poster slots; #58). A `poster` slot's `sessions` list (2025: the 19 posters of "Posters Session", `conf_key` 154, which has no top-level `posters`) is decoded like the top-level `posters` list: `conf_key` → `Poster.id`, `name` → title, `description` (HTML) → description, `speakers` registered as poster authors; both lists share one id space, so a nested poster whose id is taken is skipped. So both years get `poster-detail-<conf_key>` sessions (DATA-07), and the slot itself folds into the Posters row as any poster slot does. `sessions` on any other kind stays unported (DATA-27 `drop`): it is logged as a warning and ignored. | They are real posters, with titles, abstracts and authors. Ionic never read them (poster slots collapse before its shared-session branch), so dropping them would hide 19 sessions and their speakers in the 2025 fixture; mapping them costs one small decoder. |
| D-122 | **Venue names in Ionic's rules are year data** (#58). Ionic prefixes a room named `Hall …` with `Expo ` (in collapsed rows, and from P0-12 in plenary-like names), because the 2026 feed writes `Lunch (Hall AB)` for the hall it otherwise calls `Expo Hall AB`. The prefix is `hall_prefix` in `content.toml`: `"Expo "` in 2026, `""` in 2025, whose feed calls its halls `Hall A`, `Hall B` and `Hall C` everywhere. `FeedSettings` carries it, with the year's `site_url` (which must end in `/`; open-space links and images resolve against it). | Rules that name a venue belong to the year (D-45). With Ionic's literal, 2025's halls would split in two rooms: `Hall A` for notices and `Expo Hall A` for lunches and events. |
| D-123 | **Where Ionic's filtering and collapse rules were unclear** (#58; DATA-04..07):<br>• **Accounting:** every record that does not become a session of its own is listed by rule in `ConferenceData.dropped` (`available-open-space`, `open-space-duplicate`, `placeholder`, `posters-plenary`, `blank`, `collapsed`, `unscheduled-open-space`) and counted in one INFO log line, so "no session lost" is checked exactly (D-77).<br>• **Open spaces** without a start, an end or a room are proposals: dropped as `unscheduled-open-space`, not skipped as broken. Their location is `room_display`, or `room` when that is empty (Ionic checked both but showed only `room_display`).<br>• **Collapsed ids** keep Ionic's form `<kind>-<room>-<start>`, the start written in ISO 8601 at the conference's offset. That is the feed's own string whenever the feed writes the conference offset, as both recorded feeds do, and stays stable if a feed ever writes UTC.<br>• **Poster details need a Posters row:** they take its time, day and place. Without one they are not created and a warning is logged (Ionic created them with no time, on "Sun", in `Expo Hall AB`).
