The posters themselves stay in `ConferenceData.posters`.<br>• **A session id already taken** (e.g. an open space's `conf_key + 9000` equal to a slot's `conf_key`) is skipped and recorded in `skipped`, never silently overwritten. | Each choice keeps Ionic's output on both recorded feeds (2026: 345 sessions, 170 speakers, the same 14 collapsed rows and day counts as the Ionic simulation in `docs/research/ionic/data-layer.md` §3.7) while making the edge cases explicit instead of accidental. |
| D-124 | **The lightning talks' location is year data** (#59; like D-122). Ionic shows every lightning-talks row at `Pacific Ballroom - Arena`, whatever rooms the slot lists, because 2026's talks are given in the Arena and streamed to five rooms. The place is `lightning_talks_location` in `content.toml`: the Arena in 2026, empty in 2025, whose lightning talks list their one room (Hall B) and so show it. The rooms the slot lists still get the session (DATA-16). | A venue's name is year data (D-45); Ionic's literal would put 2025's talks in a hall that year's venue did not have. |
| D-125 | **Where Ionic's name, speaker and room rules were unclear** (#59; DATA-08, DATA-15, DATA-16):<br>• **Tags in names:** only real tags (`</?letter…>`) are stripped, keeping their text, so 2025's `CANCELLED: <s>…</s>` reads `CANCELLED: Introduction to Sprints Workshop` while a title such as `DuckDB <3 Python` keeps its `<3`. Whether a cancelled row shows as such is a separate UX question (the feed's `cancelled` stays false).<br>• **Pseudo-speakers:** a speaker record whose name starts with `Hosted by` (the lightning talks' hosts, both years) is not a person: it stays out of the directory and shows as text from `Session.speaker_names`, which keeps every author. Ionic listed it as a speaker.<br>• **Linking by name:** each author name links the first directory speaker of that name; a session lists each person once. Poster details link their authors by id, as Ionic did, and keep every author's name as text too.<br>• **Room order:** a room's sessions are sorted by start, then name; Ionic sorted by start only and kept whatever order its arrays had. Rooms are sorted by `slugs.room_sort_key`: numbers by value, case and decomposable accents (`é`, not `ø`) ignored, close to Ionic's `localeCompare(…, {numeric: true, sensitivity: "base"})` for English room names. | Each keeps Ionic's output on the 2026 feed (the 28 rooms with sessions and their counts in `docs/research/ionic/data-layer.md` §3.7, 170 speakers less the pseudo-speaker) and makes the edge cases deterministic. |
| D-126 | **The Schedule's days are the conference's dates** (#60; DATA-17, SCHED-01). `queries.days(data, first_day, last_day)` returns every date from `conference.toml`'s `first_day` to its `last_day`, widened to any schedule day outside them, as an immutable tuple: a day's index is its position and never moves for a given feed and year. Sprint days, which the feed has no sessions for, get a day (SCHED-01 shows them with an empty state that links to Sprints). Filters play no part. | Ionic built its day list from the sessions and sorted it in place, so sprint days had no tab and indices could shift under another screen. Taking the dates from the year data is the one obvious source, and widening keeps any session reachable if the feed strays outside them. |
| D-130 | *(Model fields and `conference.content` handling recorded by D-140.)* **Feed markup is converted in `feed.process()`, once per feed version, never at render time** (records D-44's "once per feed version" and architecture §7.3 rule 16; #67). `core/html_md.py` has two public functions:<br>• `to_markdown(value, site_url)`: the D-114/D-115 Markdown; a value with no tag is plain text.<br>• `to_text(value)`: the text-only form for search (D-46): the visible text with blocks, list items and line breaks as spaces, link targets, `script` and `style` left out, whitespace collapsed, not yet folded.<br>Processing converts each HTML field (descriptions, bios, sprint and job texts, the `conference.content` blocks) after Ionic's rules, so the placeholder rule still reads the raw description as Ionic does. The model keeps the raw HTML (SPON-09's roles and MAP-09's sections read it) beside the Markdown, and `search_text` is built from `to_text`. Screens and queries never convert. The wiring is #134 (after #58 and #59, which change the same constructors); its time counts in `feed processed in N ms` (#60). | Converting on render would repeat the work on every rebuild of a detail screen, and search over raw HTML is Ionic's bug (D-46). The 2026 feed's 464 non-empty HTML values convert in about 20 ms on the host (M4), well inside D-109's budget. |
| D-131 | **Converter boundaries beyond D-114** (#67; none occurs in the 2025 or 2026 feeds, each has a named test in `tests/test_html_md.py`):<br>• A code span's edge spaces stay outside it, and an empty span adds nothing: the renderer trims one space at each edge, which joined the words around it.<br>• Content written directly in a `ul` or `ol` becomes an item, so its text shows (S3's prototype dropped it).<br>• An `a` without `href`, inside another `a`, or with an `href` that cannot be parsed (`http://[`), is text: a missing `href` would join to the site URL, links cannot nest, and conversion runs inside feed processing, where a field must never raise.<br>• Emphasis or code that directly follows an element of the same kind (`<em>a</em><em>b</em>`, `<b>` then `<strong>`) continues it: rendered apart, the markers touch (`*a**b*`, `` `a``b` ``) and show literally.<br>• Consecutive `br`s give one hard break, and breaks at a paragraph's edges are dropped: a blank line would end the paragraph and split emphasis or a link across it.<br>• A bare URL needs something after `://`; curly closing quotes end it like straight ones.<br>• A block element inside an inline one (a `p` inside an `a`) is set off by spaces, so words never join.<br>• Emphasis flanking (D-114) treats neighbouring markers of the same character as one delimiter run, as CommonMark does (`a<strong><em>x</em></strong>b` stays bold italic), and is checked again after a pair is dropped. | Found while writing the production converter and by hypothesis properties over plain text and random tag mixes (testing-and-quality §3.4). Each keeps the visible text intact or avoids markers shown literally; all are reversible. |
| D-135 | **The bundled snapshot** (records D-38's details; #69, NEW-03).<br>• `src/pycon_us/data/<year>/conference.json` holds the public feed's bytes unchanged, so the current parser reads them (D-47). Only the active year bundles one. `conference.toml`'s optional `snapshot_captured_at` (an aware date and time) says when it was fetched; a year has the key exactly when it has the file (a unit test).<br>• `YearData.read_snapshot()` returns the bytes and that time on demand, never at `load_year()`. It reads relative to the package (`config.DATA_DIR`), which works in packaged builds, where `src/` is copied, not installed (F15): an arm64 APK on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` read and processed it (728 sessions, no skipped record).<br>• It is a separate file from `tests/fixtures/feeds/conference-<year>.json`, even while the bytes are equal (as on 2026-10-12; the repository then stores one blob).<br>• `tools/update_snapshot.py` fetches the year's `feed_url` and refuses a feed that `feed.process` rejects, that has a record the parser skips, or whose days fall outside the conference dates: the same bar as the test of the committed snapshot. Then it writes both files with `write_atomic` (only the `snapshot_captured_at` line of `conference.toml` changes; a new year gets it after `feed_url`) and prints each count before and after. | The fixture and the snapshot have different jobs: the fixture is frozen test data whose exact counts the tests assert, while the snapshot is refreshed before every build, so one shared file would make each refresh rewrite the tests. A skipped record is tolerated at run time (D-41), but the snapshot is our own build input, so it must parse cleanly, and the tool never writes one that CI would reject. The capture time is the data's age for bundled data (D-32): a file's modification time does not survive a checkout or `flet build`. |
| D-140 | *(The fold amended by D-180; the sprints' text by D-202.)* **Where converted markup lives in the model** (records D-130's wiring; #134, DATA-23..25). `feed.process()` converts last, after Ionic's rules and the grouping, inside the timed span of `Feed processed in N ms`:<br>• `Session.description_md`, `Speaker.bio_md`, and `description_md` on `Poster`, `Sprint` and `JobListing`, each `to_markdown(raw, site_url)`; the raw field stays beside it. An open space's `description_md` is its description unchanged: it is Markdown already (DATA-25), and converting it would escape its markup. Its links stay as the feed writes them, not joined to the site URL: the one relative destination in either feed is 2026's `[pytest](pytest.org)`, an author's slip that `urljoin` would turn into a wrong `https://us.pycon.org/2026/pytest.org`; SESS-06's link handler (#91) decides what a tap on a relative link does (D-197: it opens `https://pytest.org`).<br>• `Session.search_text` = `queries.fold(name + to_text(description) + speaker_names)`, joined by spaces: the target shape of architecture §7.4. An open space's text comes from its Markdown instead: each inline link `[text](destination)` keeps its text only and the `*` and backtick markers are removed before `to_text`, so a search never matches a link destination or a marker (D-46); `_` stays, since these descriptions use it inside identifiers (`ic_ocr_cataloger`), never for emphasis. `fold` (~~NFKD without combining marks, then `casefold`~~ D-180's fold) is the one D-46 fold; a query folds the same way. ~~Sprints and job listings get their text when their search lands (P2).~~ Sprint projects get `description_text` and `search_text` for the Sprints page's filter (D-202); job listings get their text when their search lands (P2).<br>• `ConferenceData.content` holds the feed's `conference.content` HTML blocks by key, as the feed gives them (MAP-09 reads `venues-hours`' sections), and `content_md` their Markdown. `conference` and `content` are optional; either one that is not an object raises `FeedError`, as an optional section that is not a list does, and a block that is not a string is a skipped record.<br>• Each distinct value converts once per `process()` (a poster's description is also its poster detail's). On the host, conversion takes about 30 ms of the 2026 feed's 40 ms (`to_markdown` about 20 ms as D-130 measured, `to_text` about 5 ms); #121 measures the whole on the iPhone against D-109's 150 ms.<br>• The `*_md` fields and `search_text` default to empty, so the rules' constructors stay as they are; only `process()` fills them, and the outline snapshot shows every content block and one changed value of each other field.<br>• A converter boundary beyond D-131: a link destination holding a lone surrogate (JSON's `\ud800`, which UTF-8 cannot encode) is percent-encoded as its surrogate bytes (`%ED%A0%80`) instead of raising, since a raise would now stop `process()`. The D-131 hypothesis property found it; it has a named test. | One pass at the end keeps the rules reading the feed as Ionic did (the placeholder rule needs the raw description) and gives screens and search ready values. A fold in the model makes every search a substring test. Keeping the raw HTML costs memory but is the only source of SPON-09's role links and MAP-09's sections. |
| D-145 | *(What a good refresh publishes amended by D-224.)* **The `Conference` store** (records D-39's single flight and architecture §6.2; #70). In `state.py`:<br>• Its methods take what they use, `storage` (`Storage`) and `year` (`YearData`), not the whole `Services` bundle; P0-20's bootstrap passes `svc.storage, svc.year`.<br>• **Single flight:** `start_refresh()` returns the refresh in flight, or starts one; `refresh()` awaits that task through `asyncio.shield`, so concurrent callers share one request, and cancelling one caller (an effect cleaned up on unmount) leaves the refresh running for the others. Once it has ended, the next call starts a new one.<br>• **`fetched_at` is an aware datetime** (D-43), not epoch seconds: the cache file's modification time, the snapshot's `snapshot_captured_at` (D-135), or `clock.now()` after a refresh, so the Developer fake clock moves the data's age too. `age()` is never negative: data stamped after a fake "now" counts as just fetched.<br>• A refresh publishes nothing until the feed has parsed; any expected failure (D-146) leaves `data`, `source`, `fetched_at`, `version` and the cache as they were and sets `problem`. A good refresh clears `problem`; a cache write that fails still publishes (D-120). A good refresh whose bytes are the published feed's publishes nothing new: only `fetched_at`, `source` and `problem` change (D-224).<br>• The load order is fixed: the cache when it parses, else the snapshot (NEW-03); the cache wins even when an update installs a newer snapshot, and the next refresh replaces both. | One shared task is the smallest correct single flight; shielding it is what makes sharing safe, since awaiting a task directly cancels it with its awaiter. Narrow parameters keep the store testable with a temporary directory and a fake SharedPreferences, without building Flet services. Aware datetimes are the one time type of the data core, and `clock.now()` is the one "now". |
| D-146 | *(Status order amended by D-181; the thresholds' module by D-231.)* **Refresh failures and the data status** (records D-32's states and D-53's errors for the feed; #70).<br>• `services/api.fetch_feed(url)` is an unsigned GET through `httpx.AsyncClient` with a 10 s timeout, following redirects. Every `httpx.TransportError` (connection, DNS, timeouts, a reset or dropped connection) is a `NetworkError`; an HTTP error status is an `ApiError(status, message)`, and an answer that cannot be read (a redirect loop, an undecodable body) an `ApiError(None, message)`.<br>• `NetworkError` sets `problem = "offline"`; `ApiError` and `FeedError` (a captive portal's HTML page, a truncated or malformed feed) set `problem = "server"`.<br>• `Conference.status(info)` is what the status strip (#82) says, the first that holds: `refreshing` while ~~a refresh runs~~ a refresh the user asked for runs (`refresh()`; an automatic one is quiet, D-181); `offline` or `problem` after a failed refresh; `stale` when there is no data yet, or when it is older than 6 h on a conference day (`first_day` to `last_day` inclusive, tutorials to sprints, in conference time) or 24 h on any other day; otherwise `fresh`. The thresholds are `STALE_ON_CONFERENCE_DAYS` and `STALE_OTHERWISE` in ~~`state.py`~~ `feed_refresh.py` (D-231). | A dropped connection on venue Wi-Fi is "no answer" to the attendee, while a bad answer is not fixed by finding a better network, so the two read differently. A running refresh is the newest fact, and a failed one matters more than the data's age. The strip needs one value; computing it in the store keeps the rule in one tested place. |
| D-172 | **The `Favorites` store** (records D-48 and architecture §6.1's `Favorites`; #79). In `favorites.py`, with `FavoritesCtx` and `use_favorites()` (D-160):<br>• **`ids: frozenset[str]`**, not §6.1's sketched `list[str]`: one value is both the in-memory set for O(1) lookups and what a memoised list is given (`ids & day_ids`, equal while nothing under the list changed; D-153, F34). It is saved sorted, as `list[str]` (F12), under `storage.favorites(storage.year)` through `Storage.read` and `Storage.set`. Ids no longer in the feed are kept.<br>• **Edits:** `toggle(storage, session_id)`, `add_many(storage, ids)`, `remove_many(storage, ids)` and `undo(storage, change)` each assign `ids` once (one notification) and return a frozen `Change(added, removed, saved)`. `added` and `removed` hold only what the edit changed, so Undo never removes a session saved before and "Added 16 sessions" (SCHED-24) counts only new ones; `saved` is D-120's flag. An edit that changes nothing neither notifies nor writes; `add_many` and `remove_many` refuse a lone string.<br>• **Shown first, saved after** (unlike `Prefs.save`): an edit assigns `ids`, then writes the set as it is before its first await. Flet sends a service call before awaiting its answer (`Session.invoke_method`), so the writes arrive in the order of the edits and the last one holds them all.<br>• **A failed read is not an empty list** (refines D-120 for this key): `load()` reads through `Storage.read()`, which raises a SharedPreferences failure that `Storage.get()` turns into the default. The store is read-only on the device until a read succeeds: `loaded: bool` starts False and turns True only after `Storage.read` returns, and while it is False edits show but are not written (`saved=False`). That covers a read that failed and a start that broke before the read (D-161 opens the gate anyway). `loaded` is public, so after the gate My schedule tells "can't open" from "nothing saved" (ux.md §4.6). An absent or unreadable value still reads as none, and counts as read.<br>• **Feedback for #81:** when `Change.saved` is False, the one SnackBar says "Couldn't save your change" in place of the removal or count message, keeping Undo when there was one (ux.md §4.4).<br>• **Start:** `App` builds it with `use_memo(Favorites, [])` and provides it next to `PrefsCtx`; `bootstrap()` loads it after the theme and before the feed, so the gate opens with the stars in place.<br>• **Rows never subscribe:** only a list's owner calls `use_favorites()`. It hands memoised day lists and slots `ids & their_ids`, rows `is_favorite`, and one `on_toggle(session_id)` from `use_callback(..., [])`, which can close over the store and `Storage` because both live as long as the session. | Saving before showing would wait a SharedPreferences round trip before the star changes (D-153 wants it in the same frame) and would lose an edit: two taps during one write both start from the old set, and the second assignment drops the first (a unit test interleaves two taps). A default after a failed read is harmless for a setting, but the next star tap would write that empty set over the user's whole list; a flag that turns True only after a read also keeps a store that was never read from writing, and a public one lets My schedule say so instead of showing a false empty state. A frozenset needs no second set kept in step with a list. A `Change` per edit gives #81's SnackBar and SCHED-24's count what they need without the store knowing about the UI. A row that subscribed would re-render on every tap anywhere, undoing D-153's memoised path. |
| D-175 | **The refresh triggers and the strip's minute tick** (records D-40 for the feed and the status strip; #82).<br>• **Due rule:** an automatic refresh is due when none has started in the session, or when the last one *began* 15 min ago or more by `clock.now()` (`REFRESH_EVERY`), whatever its outcome; a clock set back before that start (the Developer fake clock) makes it due too. None is due while one is running: that one is the attempt. `Conference.refresh_due()` says so, `start_refresh_if_due()` acts on it, and `start_refresh()` records each start (`_started_at`). D-40's "older than 15 min" counts from the last attempt, not from the data's age, and the strip's 6 h / 24 h thresholds (D-146) are no refresh trigger.<br>• **Foreground:** `Services.foreground`, an `asyncio.Event` set at creation; the lifecycle handler clears it on `pause` and sets it on `resume`. `Services.run_every(seconds, action)` is the one loop for D-40's ticks: it calls `action` at once, then every `seconds` while `foreground` is set, does nothing in the background, calls once at once on `resume` when a call fell due, and returns when `closed` is set (`close()` also sets `foreground`, so a loop waiting for a resume that will never come ends).<br>• **Resume:** `app.on_resume(svc, conference, prefs)`, D-173's one place for what a resume does, has a line per consumer: the ticks, `start_refresh_if_due()`, then the accessibility flags (D-23, D-34, D-173).<br>• **The 15-min timer is the status strip's:** `StatusStrip(auto_refresh=True)` on Now and Schedule. Every strip ticks every 60 s (`TICK_SECONDS`) while it is mounted and the app is in the foreground, and re-renders when the minute has changed (its state is the epoch minute), so its age keeps up and it appears when the data turns stale; with `auto_refresh` the tick also calls `start_refresh_if_due()`. "Visible" means the tab's stack is on screen: a tab switch unmounts the root (one View key, D-142) and stops the loop, while a detail pushed over Now or Schedule keeps its root mounted, so the timer goes on for that tab.<br>• **Cleanup:** the strip's effect is an `async def` with a sync `cleanup=` that does nothing itself: its presence is what makes Flet cancel the task on unmount (F2), and a cleanup returned by a sync setup would be lost at the strip's next render (F39). | Counting from the attempt keeps an offline phone (venue Wi-Fi) from requesting on every tick while its data is days old, and retries every 15 min as D-40 intends. Putting the timer in the strip runs it on exactly the screens D-40 names, with the strip's own hooks (D-165), and keeps it out of the screens' renders. One resume function, which #84 started (D-173), lets P3's sync worker join without touching the handler. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (a debug build whose feed URL, changed only for the check, pointed at a local address that never answered): on Now, after a failed start refresh at 22:18:38 the strip's tick started the next one at 22:33:39, with no request in between; in the background from 22:36 the app made none, and the resume at 22:48:59 started one at once. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK, the public feed): with the device clock moved 16 min ahead, Now refreshed on its next tick; Map, with a refresh due, made no request in 150 s; with the clock 25 h ahead, My schedule's strip appeared on its next tick saying "Updated 1 day ago", with no refresh. |
| D-178 | *(Module and `Slot`'s key amended by D-185.)* **The schedule queries: one filtering query, schedule order and a memo per feed** (records architecture §7.5's `timeline` and `track_sessions`, and D-20 and D-31 for the data; #85, DATA-18, DATA-20, DATA-30, SCHED-17). In ~~`core/queries.py`~~ `core/timeline.py` (D-185):<br>• **Shape:** `timeline(data, day, excluded)` returns a tuple of ~~`Slot(key, start, sessions)`~~ `Slot(start, sessions)`, a frozen value, one per time slot; `key` ~~is~~, derived from the start (D-185), is `"slot-<iso>"`, the slot item's `ScrollKey` (D-155). Rows are the model's own `Session` objects, so a memoised row compares them by identity; the row view (formatted time, badges) belongs to the row family (#81), built once per feed version (D-153). A day without sessions (the sprint days) and a filter that hides every track give `()`; a screen tells the two empty states apart by asking for the unfiltered day: `timeline(data, day, ())` is `()` only when the day has no sessions at all (ux.md §4.6: the sprint-day state, or "No sessions on Thursday match your filters" with "Clear filters").<br>• **Order** (`schedule_order`): the shown start (`display_start`: the Job Fair, shown at 9:30 in the 10:00 slot, leads it), then the shown place by `slugs.room_sort_key` (natural, ignoring case and accents: D-125's room order), then name, then id; a row without a place (a notice, a generic break) comes first in its slot. Feed processing's `TimeGroup` order (the raw `location` string) stays as it is; the query sorts its own tuples.<br>• **Filter:** a session passes unless its track key is in `excluded` (SCHED-10); a slot the filter empties is left out; a lone string raises `TypeError`, as in `Favorites.add_many`. `timeline` is the only query that takes `excluded`, and only `screens/schedule.py` names `timeline` or `excluded_tracks` (besides their definitions); two tests check both, so My schedule, Now, Search and the track pages cannot filter (D-20). The filter sheet (#87) adds its module to the test's list if it gets one. "Jump to now" picks its key from the filtered slots, because a slot the filter leaves out has no item to scroll to (D-155): `current_slot_key(slots, now)` takes the shown slots, not the day (architecture §7.5).<br>• **Track pages:** `track_sessions(data, track)` returns a tuple of `DaySessions(day, sessions)`, each day in `schedule_order`, whatever the filter (open spaces show on their own page); the Posters track lists its poster details, not the Posters row (SCHED-22); until the feed publishes posters, the row itself is listed, so the page is never empty while the Schedule shows one.<br>• **Memo:** per feed, in a `WeakKeyDictionary` keyed by the `ConferenceData` (one object per `Conference.version`), then by the day and the excluded set (order-free), or the track. The same arguments return the same objects; a slot the filter leaves alone is the same object filtered or not; a filter that hides nothing on a day returns the whole day's tuple. Favourites are in no key: they reach the memoised lists as `ids & their_ids` (D-153, D-172). | D-153's memoised path skips work only when its props are the same objects or equal values (F34): identity per feed lets a filter change re-render only the slots it changed, and a remounted Schedule (a tab switch unmounts it, D-142) gets the same objects back. A weak key frees a replaced feed's results (a test checks it) without `core/` knowing about versions (D-68). The shown start first keeps a slot in reading order; natural room order is the printed grid's (D-31), which the raw string order broke on both feeds (2026's 4 PM break sat between `Room 102C` and `Room 202A`; 2025's `garden outside 315` after `Room 3xx`). One filtering query makes D-20's "My schedule, Now and Search ignore them" a property of the code that a test holds, not a review rule. Ionic sorted shared arrays in place and wrote `hide` flags onto shared sessions (DATA-30, SCHED-17). |
| D-179 | *(Module and result shapes amended by D-185.)* **Now & Next and My schedule: the phase, happening, up next, your next session and overlaps** (records D-14's seasons, architecture §7.5's `now_next`, `next_for_me` and `my_schedule`, SCHED-25 and NEW-01; #93). Pure functions in ~~`core/queries.py`~~ `core/agenda.py` (D-185) that take `now` (aware, any zone, D-43) and the saved ids (`Favorites.ids`, D-172) as arguments and never the Schedule's filters (D-20):<br>• **Phase:** `now_next(data, now)` places `now` against each day's span, from its first start to its last end over every session listed under it: `before` the conference's first start; `day` inside a span, between slots included; between spans, `after_day` ("That's a wrap for today", ux.md §5.1) while the conference-local date of `now` is earlier than that of the next day's first start, and `before_day` ("First up today at 9:00 AM") once it is the same, from midnight; `after` from the conference's last end. The sprint days, which the feed has no sessions for, are `after`; Now (#95) names them from `conference.toml`.<br>• **Happening and Up next** count the timeline only (`data.days`), so the poster details stay inside their Posters row: happening is `start <= now < end`, so a session that ends at `now` is over and a notice (no duration) is never on; Up next is the first time slot that starts after `now`, on any day, with its start (~~`next_start`~~ `up_next.start`: the Schedule's own `Slot`, D-185). Both take the Schedule's unfiltered slots, with each slot's sessions in its order (`schedule_order`, D-178), and use `start`, the grouping start, not the Job Fair's earlier label (DATA-11), to tell what is on.<br>• **Your next session:** `next_for_me(data, favorites, now)`: the first saved session that starts after `now` and within 3 h (`NEXT_SESSION_WINDOW`, inclusive); when none does, the saved session that is on and started last; else None. A session that is on never hides the next one; ties go to the agenda's order; poster details count.<br>• **My schedule:** `my_schedule(data, favorites)` lists every saved session in the feed by `Session.day` and start, in the Schedule's order within a start (`schedule_order`, D-178), whatever the filters hide; saved ids not in the feed are left out (the store keeps them). `overlaps` maps each saved session that overlaps another to those it overlaps, in agenda order, from one sweep over the sessions sorted by start. Two sessions overlap when each starts before the other ends, so back-to-back sessions do not, a chain A-B-C does not join A and C, and a notice overlaps nothing. Sessions with the same location, start and end are one visit and do not overlap: the posters of one poster session, its Posters row and the Job Fair listed with it (Expo Hall AB, 9:30 AM to 1:00 PM in 2026). Folding what has ended is the screen's (#94), since it depends on `now`.<br>• **Values:** `NowNext`, `Agenda`, `AgendaDay` and ~~`AgendaSlot`~~ `Slot` (D-185) are frozen dataclasses that compare by value and ~~name sessions by id, as `TimeGroup` does~~ hold the model's sessions (D-185); `Agenda.overlaps` stays out of the generated hash. `now_next` takes `data`, not §7.5's sketched `sessions`, since the timeline is `data.days`. | Ionic kept the last slot that had started "Happening now" until midnight, skipped groups without a start and read the Schedule's hide flags (SCHED-25). A strict end is the one rule under which a talk that ends at 10:30 is gone at 10:30 while the one starting then is on, and the same half-open interval makes back-to-back sessions free of conflict. Spans taken from the sessions hold for a day that runs past midnight and give 2025's and 2026's feeds their own phases; only the evening and the morning between them need a date, compared in the conference's zone (the next start's) in `core`, where the two-zone tests pin it (D-43), so Now (#95) only maps a phase to its copy. An Up next that crosses days gives "That's a wrap for today" its tomorrow without a second query. One order (D-178's) for the Schedule, Now and My schedule means a slot reads the same on every screen. "Your next session" answers "what should I do next?" (D-14): with "on comes first", a saved summit, tutorial or poster session (the 2026 feed has 55 sessions of 3 h or more) would hold the card for hours and hide the talk the attendee has to walk to; the session that is on stays in Happening now with its star. Saving several posters is an expected path, and flagging them against each other would bury the real conflicts that My schedule is for (ux.md §5.6). ~~Ids in value types are what D-153's memoised path compares~~ Values are what D-153's memoised path compares: an answer recomputed every 60 s equals the last one until something changes~~, and row views are keyed by id~~ (sessions, not ids: D-185). Fixed-now tests on the 2026 feed (and 2025's phase turns), under two machine zones, cover each boundary, and each comparison was checked by mutating it (each mutant failed the tests). |
| D-180 | *(`DaySessions` and the order's key amended by D-185.)* **Search: the fold, the index and the results** (records D-46 for DATA-18, DATA-19 and NEW-02; amends D-140's fold; #96). `fold` stays in `core/queries.py`, where feed processing and the Speakers sort take it; the index and the matching are a new `core/search.py` (amends section M's `core/` list; architecture §7.5):<br>• **`fold(text)`** is NFKD, `casefold`, NFKD again (the Unicode Standard's compatibility caseless match, so folding a folded text changes nothing), then: apostrophes and their look-alikes (`' ‘ ’ ‚ ‛ ′`, the modifier letters `ʼ ʻ`, and `´`, which goes before the first NFKD since NFKD makes it a space) are removed, so `don't`, `don’t` and `dont` all fold to `dont` and `O’Brien` sorts as `obrien`; typographic double quotes (`“ ” „ ‟`) and dashes (hyphen, figure dash, en and em dash, horizontal bar, minus) become `"` and `-`; the letters NFKD leaves whole become base letters (`ł ø đ ħ ı ŧ` → `l o d h i t`, `æ œ` → `ae oe`); and marks and format characters (Unicode categories Mn, Me and Cf: accents, the soft hyphen, zero-width spaces and joiners, bidi controls) are removed. `ß` already folds to `ss` and `ﬁ` to `fi`. `Session.search_text` keeps D-140's parts and takes this fold.<br>• **`search_index(data)`** pairs each session's `search_text`, each directory speaker's folded name and each room's folded name with what it finds, already in result order, and logs `Search index built in N ms: <n> sessions, <n> speakers, <n> rooms`. It is built once per feed version, never per keystroke: #97's `Search` holds it in a `use_memo` on `Conference.version`.<br>• **`search(index, query, scope)`** folds the query and splits it on whitespace; an entry matches when every word occurs in its text (AND) anywhere, as a substring, not only as a prefix or a whole word, as architecture §7.5 sketched. An empty or blank query finds nothing. `scope` is one of `SCOPES` (`all`, `sessions`, `speakers`, `rooms`); any other raises `ValueError`, so #97 reads an unknown `?scope=` as `all`.<br>• **Results** are a frozen `SearchResults`: `sessions` as `DaySessions(day, sessions)` groups (the track pages' type, D-185), oldest day first and each day in the Schedule's order (D-31, DATA-30: the start a row shows, then its place in natural order by `slugs.room_sort_key`, a row without a place first, then name and id: D-178's `schedule_order` with the day first; poster details at the Posters row's start; ~~#194 makes search use that key itself~~ search sorts by `(session.day, schedule_order(session))` itself, D-185), then `speakers` by `queries.speaker_order` (folded name, then id: the one key for D-46's Speakers order, which the Speakers screen uses too), then `rooms` in their natural order (D-125); `session_count` counts the sessions. Every session is searched, poster details and collapsed rows included, on all days and whatever the Schedule's filters (D-20); a pseudo-speaker ("Hosted by …") is found through its sessions only (D-125). Equal results hold the same objects, so a memoised list (D-153) skips a keystroke that changes nothing. | The sessions' text and the names hold `’` 166 times (2025) and 162 times (2026), `—` 41 and 72 times and, in 2026, 26 U+2010 hyphens, and iOS's smart punctuation types `’` and `—` into the field: with D-140's fold, `don't` and `don’t` never met, and people typing on a phone often leave the apostrophe out (`dont`, `oreilly`), so apostrophes go rather than becoming `'`. `Łukasz Langa` (2025) could not be found as `lukasz`, since `ł` has no decomposition. A format character is invisible but splits a word (2025 has zero-width spaces and bidi controls). Checking only the non-ASCII runs for marks made the fold faster than D-140's despite the extra steps: over the 2026 sessions' 256,000 characters, 3.7 ms against 7.2 ms (best of 30, M4 host), and `feed.process` went from 43-45 to 39 ms. A substring lets `async` find `asyncio` and `typing` find `typing.List`, and the list narrows as the user types. On the host the index takes 0.5 ms, mostly the natural room keys (2026: 345 sessions, 169 speakers, 28 rooms; 2025: 369, 164, 34) and a search 0.12 ms (median of nine queries from `p` to `python typing`, 0.5 ms at most). testing-and-quality.md gives no host-to-emulator factor; `feed.process` read about 40 ms on the host (D-140) and 66 ms on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` (D-159), so on the emulator a search should stay near 0.5 ms and the once-per-version index near 1 ms, far inside S2's 3 ms keystroke (D-159). A module of its own because search is one concern (the index, the matching and the result types, about 160 lines) and `queries.py`, which holds the schedule queries (D-178) and Now's and My schedule's (D-179), is already past D-66's 400 lines (about 600 with this fold); `fold` stays where its callers already import it. |
| D-181 | **Automatic refreshes are quiet** (settles #177 under D-101; amends D-32, D-146's status order and D-165's strip; applies to D-175's triggers).<br>• **Quiet:** a refresh the app starts by itself, the start refresh (`bootstrap()`'s `start_refresh()`, the first one over the bundled snapshot included), the resume and the strip's 15-min tick (`start_refresh_if_due()`), never changes the strip's line, icon or button. Over fresh data the strip stays hidden; over a strip that shows (stale, offline, "Couldn't update") only the 2 dp bar runs, in the slot the strip keeps for it.<br>• **Asked for:** only a refresh the user asked for, `Conference.refresh()` (the strip's Refresh, the Schedule stub's Refresh and its "Try again", later pull-to-refresh and the Developer screen's "Refresh now"), makes `status()` say `refreshing`: "Updating the schedule…", Refresh disabled. Refresh during a quiet refresh joins it (single flight, D-145) and makes it the user's at once, so the line changes on the tap, not when the request ends. Every button that calls `refresh()` takes `disabled` from `status() == "refreshing"`, as the strip's does, so none greys out during a quiet refresh.<br>• **The outcome stays honest:** a good refresh hides the strip, a failure ends in "Offline · …" or "Couldn't update · …" (D-146). A failure like the last one sets `problem` to the value it has, which notifies nothing (F5), so the strip's live region changes only when the outcome changes.<br>• **In code:** `Conference._asked`, private so setting it notifies nobody, is set by `refresh()` and cleared when the refresh ends; `status()` returns `refreshing` only while `refreshing and _asked`. When `refresh()` joins a running quiet refresh, it calls `notify()` itself, since `refreshing` is already set. `refreshing` stays "a refresh runs", which the bar reads (`visible=conference.refreshing`), and the strip takes its line, icon and button from `status()`. Nothing new subscribes to the store, so a refresh still re-renders only what shows the feed (D-153, D-165).<br>• **The first start refresh is quiet too:** it is automatic, and the strip already says the honest fact, the bundled schedule's age ("Updated 5 months ago"), while the bar shows the work; the outcome replaces it. A first-start exception would be a second rule with no gain: the user has asked for nothing yet. | Each automatic refresh changed the strip with no action from the user (#177, from the review of #175): over fresh data the strip appeared for the length of the request and pushed the content down by its height, every 15 min and on most resumes; over a showing strip the line swapped to "Updating the schedule…" and back, so TalkBack and VoiceOver announced the live region twice per attempt, and offline that was every 15 min. Delaying "Updating…" by about 1 s (#177's option 3) would hide fast refreshes but not offline ones, which last the 10 s timeout. Checked with check-only builds (never committed) whose feed URL pointed at a local server that held each request, then served the feed or dropped the connection, with the interval cut to 1 min: on the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build) and the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), light and dark, Now kept its strip hidden while the start refresh and a tick's refresh ran over fresh data; over the offline strip a tick's refresh showed the 2 dp bar under an unchanged line, icon and enabled Refresh, and ended on the same line; Refresh tapped during such a refresh changed the line to "Updating the schedule…" at once, and the server saw one request; at the largest Larger Text (iOS) the wrapped offline line kept its bar below, nothing clipped. With TalkBack on (Android), every utterance takes audio focus (`MediaFocusControl`, usage `ASSISTANCE_ACCESSIBILITY`). In a re-run against the public feed with the emulator's Wi-Fi turned off, its logcat excerpt kept in #188, the change from fresh to "Offline · …" took focus 0.19 s after the failed refresh, and the next four quiet refreshes over the offline strip took none (one held 6 s, three failed at once), so the strip is announced when its outcome changes and never for a quiet attempt. On the Schedule stub (Android), a quiet refresh over the offline strip left both Refresh buttons enabled, and a tap on the strip's Refresh during it disabled both and showed "Updating the schedule…". |
| D-224 | **A refresh that brings the published feed again publishes nothing** (settles #290 under D-101 as its option 1 recommended; amends D-39 and D-145's publish rule and D-204's button; applies to D-175's triggers and D-181's quiet refreshes; architecture §6.2).<br>• **Same bytes, same data:** `Conference._digest` is the SHA-256 of the raw feed that `data` was processed from: `load_local()` (the cache's bytes or the snapshot's), `reset()` (the snapshot's) and a refresh that publishes set it. A refresh whose fetched bytes have that digest does not process them, since `feed.process` is pure and the year's feed settings never change in a process, and publishes nothing: `data` and `version` stay. So every `use_memo` on the version keeps its view models and every `SessionView` its identity (D-153), no day list, slot or row re-renders, no `ScrollKey` box is given a new `GlobalKey` and remounted, `Shown` logs no new "Content shown" line (D-112), and the Schedule's `Landing` still holds, so "Now" stays hidden (D-204).<br>• **The freshness still updates:** `_confirm()` sets `fetched_at = clock.now()`, `problem = None` and `source = "network"`, also when the bytes were the snapshot's, since the network confirmed them. The strip and the Developer screen read the outcome of a good refresh (D-146): stale data turns fresh, "Offline · …" goes. Logged as "Feed refresh: the same feed as shown; nothing new to publish", with no "Feed processed" line.<br>• **Still cached:** the bytes are written like any good feed's (D-39), so a refresh over the snapshot creates the cache, and the cache file's mtime, the next start's `fetched_at` (D-145), is the refresh's.<br>• **A changed feed** publishes as before, a new version that brings the button back (D-204); the digest is the shown feed's, so a feed that changes and then comes again the same publishes once.<br>• **Rejected:** comparing the raw bytes (the 800 kB feed kept twice in memory, for the same answer); option 2, a `Landing` that compares a content fingerprint of today's layout instead of the version (sessions compare by identity, D-70 rule 6, so it needs a fingerprint of what each row shows, and every list would still re-render). | Every cold start with a cache published the same feed twice: `Content shown: cache feed, version 1`, then `network feed, version 2` 1.1 s later, on `flet_probe_api34` with `-cores 1` (#290's logcat), which re-rendered today's whole list a second after the jump to now and brought "Now" back over the now line it had just shown; a 15-min refresh during the day did the same to whichever screen showed. The feed changes a few times a day at most, so most refreshes bring the same bytes. A digest costs 0.3 ms for 2026's 822 kB on the host (M4) and saves `feed.process`'s 40 ms there and the 200 ms of #290's slow emulator. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34` with `-cores 1`, a release arm64 APK whose build-only patch, never committed, started the fake clock at Friday 2:31 PM): the first launch processed the snapshot once and its start refresh, the same bytes, logged the new line and no second `Feed processed`; a cold start over the cache, with the emulator's network delayed by 1.5 s (`emu network delay 1500`) so the refresh ended 1.9 s after "Jumped to now (try 1)", as in #290, logged `Content shown: cache feed, version 1` and no version 2, and 20 s later the list still showed "Now · 2:31 PM" at its top with no "Now" button (`uiautomator dump`: `schedule.now_line`, no `schedule.jump_now`). |
| D-244 | *(Which feed URL changes delete the cache, and the change log with it, amended by D-255.)* **The `Conference` store owns the Developer feed URL's cache rule; a refresh from a feed URL no longer in effect writes nothing; a URL change starts no refresh** (settles #206; amends D-187's feed URL and architecture §6.2 and §17).<br>• **The rule:** `async Conference.set_feed_url(url, storage)` deletes the feed cache when a Developer feed URL in effect gives way to the year's feed (`_feed_url` set, `url` None), ~~and keeps it from one URL to another or from the year's feed to a URL~~ and on any other change of the URL once the feed has loaded, with `changes-<year>.json` (D-255). `bootstrap()`'s `start_developer_options()` (async now), `Conference.reset()` and the Developer screen's Apply and Reset await it; `developer.leave_test_feed()` goes. At a start the store is on the year's feed, so it deletes nothing; Reset app with a URL set logs two deletions, the rule's and its own, which is harmless.<br>• **The race D-187 recorded:** `Conference._refresh` checks, after the fetch and again after the cache write, that the URL it fetched is still the one in effect (`_url(year)`), as it checks a closed session (F30). When it is not, it logs "Feed refresh ended after its feed URL was left; nothing written" and writes neither the cache nor the store, not even `problem`, which would be the old URL's. `set_feed_url` changes the field **before** it deletes, and `Storage.delete_feed()` waits for every `write_feed()` asked for before it, a cancelled caller's too (each write is a task of its own in `Storage._writes`), so a URL left during the write, or Reset app, sees the write land, then the deletion, and nothing published. The worker threads' `_write_lock` alone gave them turns, not order: a write whose thread was still queued for the lock landed after a deletion asked for later and brought the test feed's cache back (found in the D-100 review).<br>• **No refresh on a URL change**, as D-73's "from the next refresh": `bootstrap()` starts one right after, Reset app starts its own (D-198), and after Apply, Refresh now is on the same screen. Starting one in `set_feed_url` would need the year, the transport and `Services.closed` there, and while a refresh from the old URL runs, single flight would hand back that one, which now writes nothing. For the same reason a Refresh now that joins a refresh still running from the URL just left publishes nothing; tapped again, it fetches from the URL in effect. | The store already holds the URL in effect and the new one, so the rule needs no copy of `Prefs.feed_url` in the screen, and every caller (the screen, the start, Reset app) gets it. Checking the URL where the refresh writes closes the race without cancelling anything: a cancel (as `reset()` does) would also end a user's `refresh()` with `CancelledError`, for a change the user made on the same screen. Tests in `tests/test_state.py` over a real `Storage` in `tmp_path`: the rule's four transitions; a refresh held on a feed URL (`HeldFeed`), then the URL left for the year's or another, then released: no cache, version unchanged, `refreshing` off; a URL left during the cache write: the cache deleted after it, nothing published; and a URL left, or Reset app, while the write's thread waits for the lock (`tests.fakes.QueuedWrite`): the deletion waits and no cache is left, with `tests/test_storage.py`'s cancelled-caller case under it. Each check, removed, fails its test. |
| D-255 | **The change log: Schedule changes is a drill level, `changes/<year>`, pushed on the View that opens it; a refresh is diffed only against a feed from its own feed URL, and over the bundled snapshot only for saved sessions and only until the install's first network feed for the year; any feed URL change after the load deletes the cache and the log; a SnackBar per batch that changes something, Now's card for saved sessions, the Changed badge for time, room and title; feed pairs built in `tests/feeds.py`** (settles #366 under D-101; amends D-47's first-install rule and route, D-141's drill kinds, D-187's and D-244's feed URL rule, D-198's Reset app, section M's Routes, Drill, Storage and Tests, ux.md §2.5, §4.2, §5.1, §6.4 and UQ-2, architecture §5.2, §5.3, §6.2 and §7.9, testing-and-quality.md §2 and §3.4, and NEW-04's notes).<br>• **The route:** `changes` is a sixth drill kind (`DRILL_KINDS`, D-141), whose id is the conference year, as the log is kept per year (`changes-<year>.json`, D-48): `Drill` returns `ScheduleChanges` for it. Every opener pushes it on its own View with `drill_href(view_path, "changes", str(YEAR))`. Now's card pushes `/now/changes/2026`, the Schedule's overflow `/schedule/changes/2026`, Settings › Data `/more/settings/changes/2026`, and the SnackBar's Review pushes it on whatever View is on top: a root, a detail, a More page or search. Its items push `…/changes/2026/session/<id>`. Back pops one level everywhere through `parent_of()`, which already drops any drill pair, so `PAGES` and the Back code do not change, and a deep link gets the list as any drill target (D-21). An id that is not the year shows the list's not-found state on its own level (D-186). Not a fixed `/schedule/changes` (#366's option a), whose Back from Now's card or Settings lands on the Schedule; not a global modal (option c), a second modal with its own pop rules (F6, U18) for a list that is not a search; not a `changes` child of a few roots (option b as first sketched), since a SnackBar shown over a detail would then switch the tab or drop that detail.<br>• **A removed session's item** is not a link: its id would open D-186's not-found state. It shows the session's last title, time and room, kept in the batch, with "Removed from the latest schedule" inline (D-47's words), read as one sentence, and keeps its star (`FavoriteButton`, with Undo as everywhere): a saved session the feed dropped stays saved (D-172 keeps the id), the list is where the user can let it go, and if the feed lists it again it is in My schedule again.<br>• **Which refreshes are diffed:** only one that publishes (D-224: the same bytes publish nothing and record nothing), and only against a feed from the feed URL it fetched from: the cache, or an earlier network feed. The bundled snapshot counts as the year's feed URL's, and a refresh over it is diffed only when sessions are saved, the batch then holding only their changes (a time, room or title change or a removal, never an addition): someone who installed and saved offline is told that a talk moved, while a first install with nothing saved gets no log of an old snapshot's differences. That holds only until the install's first network publish for the year, from any feed URL, which saves a marker, `pycon.<year>.fetched` (a bool in section M's key table; Reset app clears it with every `pycon.` key). Once it is set, a refresh over the snapshot publishes without a diff, as after a feed URL change: a start falls back to the snapshot after Clear cached feed (D-198), a cache that does not parse (`load_local()`'s `delete_feed()`) or a feed URL change, and the snapshot's values are not what the user last saw, so a diff over them would announce again changes already in the log or already seen. After a feed URL change, the next refresh publishes without a diff, in the same session (the shown feed is the old URL's) and after a relaunch (the cache is gone, and the snapshot is diffed only by the rule above), and the ones after it are diffed again. A refresh that writes nothing records nothing: a closed session (D-147), a URL left (D-244); nor does one whose URL is left, or whose session closes, after it publishes and before its batch is written (below).<br>• **The feed URL rule (amends D-244):** any change of the feed URL once the feed has loaded (Developer options' Apply and Reset, Reset app) deletes both the cache and `changes-<year>.json`, not only a return to the year's feed; the start's restore of the stored URL runs before the feed loads (`bootstrap()`'s `start_developer_options()`, then `load_local()`) and deletes nothing. So the cache always holds the feed of the URL in effect and is a fair base for the next start's first diff, and the log never mixes two feeds. The log keeps the cache's order (D-244): its writes are tasks that `Storage` keeps with the feed writes (`Storage._writes`), and the deletion `set_feed_url` and Reset app ask for waits for every write of either file asked for before it, a cancelled caller's too. The hand-off that records a batch (`_record_changes`) checks the URL in effect (`_url(year)`) and a closed session again after its last await, just before it writes, so a refresh whose URL was left by then records nothing, though it has published. Tests: a URL left while the log write is queued (`tests.fakes.QueuedWrite`), and a URL left between the publish and the log write, each leave no `changes-<year>.json`. Reset app also deletes the log and lists Schedule changes among what is lost (D-198).<br>• **Who hears of a batch:** one floating SnackBar (D-29) whose action, **Review**, pushes the list on the current View: "2 saved sessions changed" when the batch touches saved sessions, else "Schedule updated · 3 changes", counting moves, retimes, renames and removals. Additions are listed but neither counted nor announced alone: a batch of additions only shows no SnackBar. The SnackBar follows a refresh the app started too (D-181 keeps the strip quiet about the work; this is its outcome) and the Undo SnackBar's timing: 4 s, kept for a screen reader until used or replaced (D-184), and the next SnackBar replaces it. **Now's card** shows only unseen changes to saved sessions detected in the last 24 h ("2 saved sessions changed · Review", pushing `/now/changes/<year>`), and goes once they are seen: an item is seen as ux.md §6.4 says (2 s after the list shows it), or by Mark all as seen, and the card counts only unseen items, so leaving the list sooner keeps it. **The Changed badge** (`tertiary_container`, ux.md §3.4) marks the rows whose time, room or title changed in a batch of the last 24 h, by detection time, seen or not; none for an addition or a removal.<br>• **When the Review SnackBar goes:** besides use and replacement (D-184), the page keeps its last Review SnackBar in a weak reference keyed by the page, as D-251 keeps the favourites one. The list closes it when it is shown, whatever opened it (Now's card, the Schedule's overflow, Settings › Data, Review, a deep link), and so does Mark all as seen, while Flet still reports it `open`: `open = False` and `update()`, never `page.pop_dialog()` (D-251). Review on a View whose last drill pair is already `changes/<year>` pushes nothing and only closes it. A Review SnackBar already replaced is left alone, and one shown after the list opened (a newer batch) stays until it is used or replaced, or Mark all as seen. Opening the list shows no SnackBar of its own, so D-251's rejected close-before-show race does not arise.<br>• **Feed pairs:** `tests/feeds.py` builds each change-log pair from the recorded 2026 feed, `recorded(2026)` and `changed(2026, edit)`, with named edits (move, retime, rename, add, remove; #378), and the device check's laptop server (#460) serves pairs from the same builder; nothing is committed under `tests/fixtures/feeds/pairs/`. | Ionic swaps the schedule silently (`PyCon/pycon-us-mobile#312`). The SnackBar shows wherever a refresh lands: at the start, on a resume from any screen, or on the 15-min tick while a detail is pushed over Now or the Schedule (D-175); only a drill level is one pushed View from every opener, as D-17 asks of every cross-link, with no change to `parent_of()` or `PAGES` (read at `main`: `locations.parent_of()` drops any `DRILL_KINDS` pair). The same-URL rule needs to know where the shown feed came from, and under D-244 a URL applied and then the app closed before a refresh left the old URL's cache to be read at the next start as the new URL's; deleting it on every change after the load closes that, at a Developer-only cost (after entering a test URL offline, the next offline start shows the bundled snapshot), and `app.bootstrap()` already restores the URL before it loads the feed. Open spaces are added all through the conference and are hidden by default (D-20), so a SnackBar for each batch of additions would teach people to ignore the one that matters, and a New badge would mark most of the open-space board. Five pairs of the 822,356-byte 2026 feed would commit about 8.2 MB of near-identical JSON; a named edit says exactly what differs and cannot drift from the recorded feed, and the fixtures README already builds edge cases from deep copies. The marker, not "the snapshot is the base", decides the snapshot diff, and the log keeps the cache's order: Clear cached feed, a corrupt cache and a feed URL change all leave the snapshot as the next start's base without deleting the log, and a log write that lands after a URL change's deletion would mix two feeds, as a late cache write brought the test feed back before D-244 (the D-100 review of #469 found both gaps). The list closes the Review SnackBar for the reason D-251 closes the favourites one: with a screen reader on, D-184 would otherwise keep "Schedule updated · Review" over the list it points to and every screen after it. Docs only: the code is #378 and #430 to #438. |
| D-185 | **The query modules, one slot type, one day type per list and one session order** (settles #196, which absorbed #194; amends D-178, D-179, D-180 and section M's `core/` list; architecture §7.5). Behaviour does not change: the same sessions in the same order.<br>• **Modules** (D-66's 400 lines): `core/queries.py` keeps `days()`, `fold()` and `speaker_order()`, where feed processing, the Schedule and the Speakers screen import them; `core/timeline.py` holds the schedule queries (`Slot`, `DaySessions`, `schedule_order()`, `timeline()`, `track_sessions()`, the memo) and `day_slots(data, day)`, a day's unfiltered, memoised slots, which Now reads (it was private; a private name does not cross modules, and only the Schedule may name `timeline`, D-20). The module shares the function's name, so outside the Schedule import its names directly (`from pycon_us.core.timeline import DaySessions`); `from pycon_us.core import timeline` trips D-178's source scan; `core/agenda.py` holds Now & Next and My schedule; `core/search.py` stays (D-180). D-178's filter tests cover every query module.<br>• **Sessions, not ids:** every query that returns sessions returns the model's own `Session` objects: `NowNext.happening`, `next_for_me()` (a `Session` or None), My schedule's slots and `Agenda.overlaps` (a session to the sessions it overlaps), as the timeline, the track pages and search already did. Favourites stay ids, as input (`Favorites.ids`, D-172).<br>• **One `Slot(start, sessions)`** for the Schedule's slots, Now's next slot and My schedule's slots (`AgendaSlot` goes). Its `key` (`"slot-<iso>"`) is a property derived from the start, so two slots at one start always share it. `NowNext(phase, happening, up_next)`: `up_next` is the Schedule's own unfiltered `Slot`, the same object, or None after the last one; its `start` replaces `next_start`.<br>• **One `DaySessions(day, sessions)`** for the flat lists, the track pages and search (search's copy goes). My schedule's `AgendaDay(day, slots)` stays a type of its own, with the same `day` name (it was `date`): it is a day of gutter slots (ux.md §5.6), where a `DaySessions` is a day of rows that show their own time under a day header (§4.3, §5.10). One type for both would either move My schedule's slot grouping into the screen or group search by slot, which changes its order (the Job Fair shows 9:30 but sits in the 10:00 slot).<br>• **One session order:** search sorts by `(session.day, schedule_order(session))`, as `track_sessions` does, and its private `_timeline_order` (the same key written out) goes; a test on both feeds checks that a day's matches come as the track pages list them and each slot's as the Schedule lists the slot. | The model's sessions compare by identity (`eq=False`), so equal answers cost identity checks, as D-153's memoised path needs (F34), and the screens that come next (#86, #94, #95, #97) take what they show from the answer, with no `data.sessions` lookup and no missing-id case. An answer over a refreshed feed never equals the last feed's, even where a session moved, whereas ids would compare equal and hide the change from a memo keyed on the value. Ids would only help persistence, and no query result is stored. A derived key cannot drift between the Schedule and My schedule, and Now's next slot as the Schedule's own object gives #95 its start and day. Its `key` is the Schedule's `ScrollKey` only while the filter keeps that slot (D-155, D-178): #95's "See the rest of today" falls back to `current_slot_key()` over that day's filtered slots (architecture §7.5) when the key has no item, which the default filter makes common (both feeds have slots of open spaces only: 17 in 2025, 16 in 2026), and today's list has no item for a next slot on a later day either. `core/queries.py` was 595 lines; now `queries.py` 126, `timeline.py` 230, `agenda.py` 255, `search.py` 134. |
| D-189 | *(The filter's text, Clear and count line amended by D-226: `ui/fields.py`'s, the count 8 dp over the list.)* **The Speakers list: letters from the fold, the first session, a pinned filter through search, and a memoised path** (records D-46 and ux.md §5.7 for SPKR-01, SPKR-02 and DATA-21; amends ux.md §3.9's `SpeakerRow` sketch and section M's `core/` list; #98).<br>• **Query** (`core/speakers.py`, pure): `directory(data)` gives one frozen `SpeakerEntry(speaker, letter, first_session, more)` per directory speaker, by `speaker_order` (D-46, D-180); `letter_groups()` cuts them into `LetterGroup`s; `filter_directory(entries, index, query)` keeps the entries whose name holds every word, through `search(index, query, "speakers")`, so the matching is search's own (D-180), and a filter with no word to match returns the entries themselves: a blank one, and one that folds to nothing (`fold(query).split()` is empty), such as the lone `’` iOS smart punctuation types before `O’Brien`, which search would answer with nothing and so empty the list. Entries and groups are values over the model's objects, so equal ones compare equal (F34).<br>• **Letters:** the first character of the folded name, upper-cased (`letter_of()`): `Élodie` under E, `Łukasz` under L, a letter without a Latin base under its own heading after Z (`Жанна` under Ж, `张伟` under 张), a digit under itself. Taken from the very text the list is sorted by, so every heading's speakers are together in D-46's order; no `#` bucket, which would have to move names out of that order (only a name that folds to nothing gets `#`).<br>• **First session and "+1 more":** a speaker's sessions are every session that links it (DATA-15), poster details included, in the Schedule's order with the day first (`(day, schedule_order)`, D-185); the row shows the first, at most two lines, and "+1 more" on a line of its own, so a long title never cuts it. Pseudo-speakers never reach the directory (D-125), so the list has nothing to leave out.<br>• **Row** (`ui/rows.py`'s `SpeakerRow(entry, *, screen)`): a memoised component keyed by speaker id, built like `SessionRow` (D-184) rather than as ux.md's `ListTile`, so it reads as one phrase, "Ana Lima, speaking: Title, and 1 more session", a button with the identifier `<screen>.speaker.<key>`. A 48 dp `CircleAvatar`: the feed's photo (`Speaker.photo_url`) over the initials (`initials()`: the first and last words' first letters), which show while it loads, offline and when there is none, on `primary_container` / `on_primary_container`, a pair the theme's contrast test already checks.<br>• **Screen** (`screens/speakers.py`): a controlled filter field (D-157) pinned above the list, so it stays in reach in a long list, filtering at every keystroke with no debounce; `autocorrect=False` and `enable_suggestions=False` (Android switches suggestions separately), so names are never "fixed"; a pill without a line, focused too (`border={DEFAULT: pill, FOCUSED: pill}`: a single border leaves the focused state to the theme's 2 dp primary ring); once there is text, the field merged with a "Filter speakers" label (`MergeSemantics(Semantics(label))`), since the hint, its only name, leaves the semantics tree then (Flutter's `InputDecorator`; not while empty, or both read it), with the Clear button over the field in a `Stack` rather than its suffix, which the merge would take in; while a filter finds someone, a live region under the field says how many ("5 speakers", ux.md §9), in a column with the field so the list keeps its place in the body (else Flet rebuilds it and every row); either Clear focuses the field afterwards (`TextField.focus()` through a `Ref`), since both go away when used; a lazy `ListView` whose items are memoised letter sections keyed by letter (a heading in a semantics node of its own, then the rows), as S2's slots are (D-153); entries and the search index built once per feed version. No match: `EmptyState` "No speakers match “zz”" with "Clear filter", as the field's own Clear button does; a feed with no speakers (before the programme is announced) is not a miss: "No speakers announced yet", no button, as the feed refreshes on its own (D-175). Without data, the Schedule's states (the skeleton, then "Can't load the speakers" with Try again). | The 2026 feed has photos for 161 of its 169 speakers (154 of 164 in 2025), so initials alone would throw away what the feed offers; on the iOS 26.5 simulator a filtered list first drew initials, then the photos as they arrived. A `ListTile` reads its title and subtitle as separate texts, and the phrase needs its own label, as the session rows' does. In a real Flet session (`tests/test_speakers.py`) a keystroke that narrows the list re-renders no row and only the sections it changed, and one that finds the same speakers re-renders nothing; both fail with `ft.memo` removed from either level. Python's share of a keystroke on the host (M4, busy with other builds, medians of five): 1-3 ms for one that narrows a few rows, 16-20 ms for the first letter (most sections go), 47-51 ms for clearing the filter, when all 169 rows render again, as S2's restored rows did; hiding rows instead of removing them halved the clear but made the first letters dearer, for 169 more controls, so rows are removed. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) the host was so loaded (load average 14 to 84, `Feed processed in 803 ms` against S2's 66) that its numbers are not comparable with D-159's: handler to patch mostly 15-45 ms (9-166) for a keystroke that narrows, about 200 ms for the first letter and 250-720 ms for a clear, over two runs (the first two keystrokes after the launch, while the refreshed feed arrived, took 3.7 and 5.1 s); #121's iPhone release numbers decide whether the clear needs more. On the iOS 26.5 simulator and that emulator, in light and dark: letters, rows, "+1 more", accent-insensitive matches (`mu` finds Mike Müller), the no-match state and its Clear filter, and on iOS a tap pushing `/more/speakers/speaker/<id>` and Back returning to the list; at 200 % (Android) and the largest Larger Text (iOS) every row reflowed with the title cut at two lines, and at the largest the hint wrapped instead of clipping. `uiautomator dump`: each row one clickable `Button` named by its phrase with `resource-id` `speakers.speaker.<id>`, each letter a node of its own. After the reviews (#203): with "mu" typed, `uiautomator dump` showed the field's one `EditText` node with `resource-id` `speakers.filter` (Flutter puts a text field's label in its hint text, `BaseRoleConfigurator`, which `uiautomator` does not print), the Clear button a node of its own beside it, and `speakers.count` read "5 speakers"; a labelled `Semantics` without the merge was a second node ("Filter speakers") above an unnamed `EditText`, and a merge with the Clear button inside the field took the button into the field's node (its class became `Button`), hence the `Stack`. Neither platform drew a focus ring; Android's keyboard showed no suggestion strip; after Clear filter the field kept the input focus (typing went straight into it on iOS); 200 % text kept the count on one line. |
| D-201 | **`core/html_md.py` splits along its inline layer: the HTML side stays, the Markdown it becomes is `core/markdown_inline.py`** (records D-66 for the converter; amends section M's `core/` list; #198).<br>• **The seam:** `html_md.py` keeps `to_markdown()` and `to_text()`, which every caller imports from it, and the HTML side: the tag tables, the tree (`_Element`, `_TreeBuilder`, `_parse()`, `_text_of()`), plain-text values, the blocks and lists, and `_inline()`, the walk over inline elements. `markdown_inline.py` holds what those pieces become in Markdown: escaping (`escape()`, `escape_line_start()`, with the URL, specials and destination rules of D-114 and D-115), the tokens (`Emphasis`, `Token`), `around()`, `link()`, `code()`, and `paragraph()`, which resolves emphasis by CommonMark's flanking rules (D-114, D-131) and joins a run into one paragraph. HTML's whitespace is defined once, as its `SPACE_CHARS`: `paragraph()` and `around()` strip that set and `html_md.py` builds the pattern that collapses it from the same constant, so the two can't drift apart.<br>• **One way only:** `html_md.py` imports `markdown_inline.py`, never the reverse; `_inline()` stays with the tree because it reads `_Element`, `_text_of()` and the tag tables, and moving it would make the two modules import each other. Nothing else imports `markdown_inline.py`; the names `html_md.py` uses are public in it (no leading underscore), its own helpers stay private. Two tests in `tests/test_html_md.py` hold the rule, scanning imports as D-67's guard does: only `core/html_md.py` imports the module, and it imports nothing from `pycon_us`.<br>• **The name:** `core/` modules are named after their concern, in plain words and with no leading underscore (D-185 split `queries.py` into `timeline.py` and `agenda.py`), so not `_md_inline.py`.<br>• **A move only:** the output is unchanged; `tests/test_html_md.py`, `tests/test_html_md_feeds.py` and `tests/test_feed_markup.py` test the converter through `to_markdown()` and `to_text()` and pass unchanged; only the two boundary tests above are new. | `html_md.py` had grown to 475 lines, over D-66's 400. The inline layer is the one seam the code already had: it is about Markdown's syntax, not HTML, and the dependency runs one way. Converted feed output was compared before and after on both feed fixtures, the bundled snapshot and 20,000 random tag mixes: no value differs. |

## F. Mock backend and private API

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-50 | `tools/mock_api/` implements the private API:<br>• a pure `handle(state, method, path, headers, body) -> (status, headers, body)` over an in-memory `MockState` seeded from `tests/fixtures/mock/` (fixed accounts per role, sponsors, products, attendees), which verifies signatures and the timestamp window;<br>• a stdlib `ThreadingHTTPServer` that serves it to phones on the LAN;<br>• the same `handle` behind `httpx.MockTransport` in tests (`transport.py`);<br>• a printable sheet of mock-badge QR codes (`badges.py`, P3).<br>`tools/mock_api/README.md` documents the contract, citing Ionic's `pycon-api.ts`. | One implementation and no dependencies. The Ionic client is our only specification. |
| D-51 | A structural guard: private-API features stay hidden unless a mock base URL is set in the Developer screen, and the API client refuses any `/api/v1` request to a `*.pycon.org` host (covered by a unit test). | We must never call private PSF endpoints, not even by accident. |
| D-52 | Request signing is byte-exact with Ionic: hex `sha1(secret + ts + METHOD + path_with_query + body)`. The body is serialised once (`separators=(",", ":")`, `ensure_ascii=False`) and sent as exactly those bytes; the timestamp is rounded the JavaScript way; each endpoint encodes its query as Ionic does. Test vectors covering Unicode, escaping, query order and rounding are produced once by running Ionic's own signing code, with their provenance recorded. Client and mock share `core/signing.py`. | Without vectors from outside our code, the client and mock could agree on a wrong answer. |
| D-53 | The API client is `httpx.AsyncClient` (already a Flet dependency, F19) with Ionic's per-endpoint timeouts. It has two error types: `NetworkError` (connection failure or timeout) and `ApiError(status, message)` with `.is_auth`. Clock skew is learned from the `Date` header, with one retry. | Callers only branch on "network or not" and "auth or not". |
| D-54 | Sign-in (P3) uses an `AutofillGroup` with email and password hints, an in-flight guard, and distinct errors for a wrong password, being offline and a timeout. Key and secret go in SecureStorage. Role flags come from `check_features` on start and resume. Staff entries come from a pure `staff_entries(flags)` that keeps Ionic's gating, with an exhaustive truth-table test. | Correct and accessible; Ionic said nothing on network errors. |
| D-55 | Favourites sync (P3): the first sync after sign-in takes the union of local and server lists. After that, each change sends a debounced, idempotent full-list PATCH and sets a `dirty` flag, which is retried on start and resume. On a pull, a dirty local list is pushed first; otherwise the server list is adopted. Sign-out keeps the local list. | Never overwrite an unsent edit, and no queue is needed. |
| D-56 | Staff outbox (P3):<br>• sqlite rows of kind `lead`, `note` or `door`, each with owner, sponsor snapshot, status, tries and an idempotency key (door: code + product + session);<br>• synced rows are kept, for duplicate detection and "Already checked in";<br>• one retry cap: 10 counted failures make a row `failed`, with "Retry failed". Network errors never count, and a note waits for its lead;<br>• one `SyncWorker` runs every 60 s, on connectivity change, on resume and on "Sync now".<br>Swag pickup and CoC capture are online-only, with explicit messages. Signing out with unsynced rows asks first. Door inventories and the CoC directory stay in memory only. | Never lose or misattribute a scan. The outbox is property-tested (D-77). |
| D-57 | The mock injects faults on demand: connection errors, timeouts, "server committed, response lost", expired auth and a skewed clock. It also offers both idempotent and non-idempotent capture semantics. The guarantee we test: under idempotent semantics each record is applied exactly once; under non-idempotent semantics no record is lost, and a duplicate can occur only after a lost response, where it is detected and shown. | Client-side dedupe cannot prove the server applied a record exactly once, so we test against both behaviours. |

## G. Notifications, native gaps and capabilities

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-58 | No push. The notification feature is local scheduled reminders (P2): "Remind me before sessions in My schedule" (5, 10 or 15 min) and fixed "conference moments" from `content.toml` (lightning-talk and open-space sign-ups, keynotes). | Reminders need no server and are what attendees value most; push needs the PSF's Firebase project. |
| D-59 | *(Amended by D-221 and D-222 after S6.)* A pure `plan(favorites, conference, prefs, content) -> list[Reminder]` builds reminders with deterministic ids: the numeric session id, or 1,000,000 + crc32(id) % 1,000,000 for synthetic ids; event reminders at 8000-8099; the Developer test reminder at 8999. `apply(plan)` cancels ~~everything~~ every pending reminder (D-222) and schedules the plan, at most the next 60 reminders (iOS keeps 64 pending; `plan()` applies the cap, D-222), with ~~inexact timing by default~~ exact timing on Android when the user allows it and inexact otherwise (D-221). It re-runs whenever favourites, settings or the feed change, and on every start and resume (D-222). | "Cancel all, reschedule" is the obvious correct algorithm. |
| D-60 | Until scheduled notifications (E3) are in our pinned Flet, the interim is:<br>• the reminder settings say "Reminders aren't available in this build";<br>• Now's "Your next session" and an in-app `Banner` cover the time the app is open;<br>• `.ics` files with an alarm cover calendars. | An honest interim, with no controls that do nothing. |
| D-61 | Permissions are asked in context, after a one-sentence primer: notifications on the first favourite or the first reminder switch, the camera on the first staff scan. Never at launch. After a denial, a `Banner` offers "Open settings". Notification permission is requested through the notifications extension (E3, `request_permission()`) on both platforms; `flet-permission-handler` checks status, opens the app settings and handles the camera. | Ionic prompted blindly at boot. Flet's iOS template has no notification macro for permission_handler (F17), and one request path is simpler. |
| D-62 | Calendar: a pure `core/ics.py` (UTC times, stable UID, escaping and line folding) shared with `await ft.Share().share_files([ft.ShareFile.from_bytes(ics_bytes, name="<slug>.ics", mime_type="text/calendar")], share_position_origin=...)` (a non-empty list; the origin anchors the sheet on tablets, D-09), for one session or the whole of My schedule (P2). Native add-to-calendar (E4) comes later. | Small, pure and useful. |
| D-63 | Capability gating is a set of import-time constants in `capabilities.py`: `available(module) = flet.utils.is_embedded() and importlib.util.find_spec(module) is not None`. One module per capability imports the extension and owns its fallback (`services/scanner.py`, `services/reminders.py`). There are no probes, no observable capabilities and no remote veto. | The companion app (`flet run --android/--ios`) runs Python on the laptop and is never embedded, so it always gets the interim (F14). It is the smallest mechanism that is still correct. |
| D-64 | A Flet core gap's interim is replaced only after a PR bumps the pinned Flet (D-81); the swap is its own tested PR (D-92), never runtime detection. | We pin one Flet version, and an upgrade is a tested change. |
| D-65 | Badge scanning (P3) uses one shared scan screen for all four tools:<br>• manual entry of the full `code:validator` payload and USB/Bluetooth HID scanners always work;<br>• the E1 camera preview appears when the extension is bundled;<br>• one accepted result per code every 4 s;<br>• outcomes are shown as colour, icon and word, with haptics and a live region;<br>• the screen stays awake while scanning. | It works without E1 and with hardware scanners, and it is accessible. |
