| D-220 | **E3 is the official local-notifications extension plus scheduling, and it carries its own platform setup** (spike S6, #111; amends `docs/flet-gaps-and-extensions.md` §4.1, which holds the details; F52, F53).<br>• **Nothing exists at the pin:** `.flet-src` at `15854a0ac` has no local-notifications package, so E3 stays the D-60 interim until a pin bump brings `flet-dev/flet#6948` v1 and scheduling (#19; written up for the owner in #294).<br>• **The API:** v1's `initialize`, `request_permission`, `get_permission_status`, `show`, `cancel`, `cancel_all`, `get_launch_details` and `on_notification_tap`, plus the scheduling PR's `zoned_schedule(id, title, body, at, *, payload, android_schedule_mode)` (one aware instant, sent as ISO and scheduled in UTC: no time-zone database), `get_pending()` and `cancel_all_pending()` (gaps doc §4.1). **What S6 ran** over `flutter_local_notifications` 22.3.1: initializing with the launch details, the permission request and status, the tap event, `zoned_schedule` (with a boolean `exact` for the mode), `get_pending()`, `cancel_all()` and `cancel_all_pending()`. `cancel_all_pending()` removed the pending reminder and left the one already shown, on the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator; the plugin's `cancelAll()` also removes shown ones. S6 did not run v1's `show` or `cancel(id)`: they wait for the adoption PR's checks. Exact scheduling without the permission is an error the caller sees (`exact_alarms_not_permitted`), never a silent fallback. E3 adds no exact-alarm permission API: `flet_permission_handler.Permission.SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` exists at the pin for the status and the settings page (S6 read the state through the plugin's `canScheduleExactNotifications()` instead; the adoption PR checks the two agree).<br>• **Android setup in the extension:** an Android library module (a plugin class in its pubspec, F52) whose manifest declares the plugin's `ScheduledNotificationReceiver`, `ScheduledNotificationBootReceiver` (boot, quick boot, package replaced) and `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`, and a monochrome status-bar icon with a `tools:keep` rule. The template needs no `<receiver>` hook.<br>• **iOS setup in the extension:** a Swift plugin class that makes the app delegate `UNUserNotificationCenter`'s delegate (F53); without it, iOS never reports a tap.<br>• **From Flet's template:** core library desugaring in `:app` (U7), without which no Android build succeeds; written up for the owner in #293.<br>• **R8:** the template's default rules (`-keepnames class * { *; }`) keep the plugin's GSON models; no extra rules unless an app sets `proguard_default_rules = false`. | S6 delivered and opened reminders with the app killed, locked, rebooted and moved on Android, and killed on iOS, through exactly this shape; each piece it lists was missing in a run that failed without it. |
| D-221 | **Reminders use exact alarms on Android when the user allows them, and say so when they can't** (amends D-59's inexact default, ux.md §6.9 and NATIVE-06; spike S6).<br>• **Why:** Android 14 gives an inexact alarm a window of 75 % of the time left when it is scheduled, and delivers it anywhere in that window: at its end when nothing else wakes the device. S6 measured +90 s on a 2-minute lead, +180 s on 4 minutes, +80 s on 10 minutes and +370 s on 30 minutes. Outside forced Doze, exact alarms fired within seconds (+0.3 s to +5.4 s over three runs); in forced deep Doze an exact one came +59.3 s late (inexact: +90.1 s), so exact is sooner, never guaranteed on time, and the copy promises no more. The plan is applied hours ahead, so an inexact "10 minutes before" can arrive after the session started.<br>• **How:** declare `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` (never `USE_EXACT_ALARM`, which is for alarm and calendar apps). After notification permission (D-61), when "Remind me before sessions" is on and `Permission.SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` is not granted (`flet-permission-handler`), Settings › Reminders shows a card: "For more timely reminders, allow Alarms & reminders" with `OutlinedButton("Open settings")`, which requests it (Android opens the app's "Alarms & reminders" page). `apply()` schedules `EXACT_ALLOW_WHILE_IDLE` when allowed, `INEXACT_ALLOW_WHILE_IDLE` otherwise, and the card's wording is honest: "Without this permission, reminders may arrive late, including after a session starts." (an inexact reminder planned hours ahead can come hours late) Re-checked on resume; never asked at launch.<br>• iOS has no such setting; its reminders fired on time on the simulator. | A reminder that comes after the talk started is worse than none; exact alarms cost the user one switch, asked in context. |
| D-222 | **The cap of 60 is the plan's, `apply()` cancels only pending reminders, and it runs on every start** (amends D-59; spike S6).<br>• **The cap:** iOS kept 64 of 70 reminders and dropped the six **soonest** (the first six added), so the app must never hand it more: `plan()` returns at most `MAX_PENDING = 60`, soonest first, and `apply()` schedules what it gets. Android kept all 70; one rule for both.<br>• **Cancel pending only:** `apply()` calls `cancel_all_pending()`; `cancel_all()` would also clear a delivered reminder from the shade at every favourite (seen in S6). S6 checked `cancel_all_pending()` with one reminder shown and one pending: on both platforms the pending one went (Android's `dumpsys alarm` had no alarm left) and the shown one stayed.<br>• **On every start and resume:** a force-stop (Settings › Force stop) clears an app's alarms on Android, and the OS may drop them in other ways; re-applying the plan at start and resume brings them back. Applying is idempotent, ids are deterministic. | The OS limit drops the reminders that matter most, cancelling must not touch what the user has not read yet, and only the app can restore what the OS cleared. |
| D-256 | **If E3 is not pinned when the P2 exit starts, the E3-only work moves to P4 and P2 closes on the interim** (settles #367; how PLAN §7's P2 exit criteria 1, 3 and 4 are met without E3).<br>• **Trigger:** the P2 exit run (#462) starts, every other issue it depends on is closed, and the pin bump that brings E3 and U7 (#445) is still open. If #445 has merged by then, nothing moves: the E3 issues are ordinary P2 work and the exit waits for them.<br>• **Rows:** NOTIF-06, NOTIF-07, NOTIF-10, DEV-06 and NATIVE-06 add `later` to their disposition and keep phase P2. Each row's notes start "Later (D-256): waits for E3, which was not in the pinned Flet at the P2 exit", and the matrix counts are updated. NOTIF-01, NOTIF-05, NEW-05, STORE-10 and NOTIF-03 don't change, because their interim or storage ships in P2 (D-60). NATIVE-06's camera part stays in P3 with the scan screen. The permission handler doesn't wait for E3: when #446 moves, the P3 scan epic (#37) adds the `flet-permission-handler` pin, `Services.permissions` (only with `capabilities.PERMISSIONS`) and the camera declaration in a sub-issue of its own, and #446 keeps only the E3 package, `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`, `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM` and `capabilities.REMINDERS`. #449 also carries My schedule's "Reminder settings" entry (NEW-01's P2 overflow item, ux.md §5.6) and the `.ics` alarm that follows the chosen lead; both move with it. P2's `.ics` keeps the fixed 10-minute VALARM (SESS-05, #380), which is what the lead rule gives while reminders are off.<br>• **Issues:** #445 (the swap, D-64) and the E3 issues under #27 (#446..#451) and #459 move to the **P4 Showcase** milestone. They stay open and `blocked` on #445, and each gets a comment citing this decision. The epics #27 and #19 move once only those issues are left in them, so nothing open stays in P2. #293 and #294 close on their own criteria, or move with #19. A milestone says by when the work should land; the work starts in whichever phase is active once #445 merges (D-97).<br>• **Who:** the agent running #462 does it first, in one docs PR (`Refs #462`): the rows and counts, roadmap §5 (P2-E6 and UP-E3 mark the moved issues) and §7 (listed as carried from P2). It then moves the issues, narrows #446's scope as above and comments on #37 that the handler step is now #37's, and reports in #347. Exit criterion 3 is then judged on the interim (#458), and the reminder taps of criterion 4 wait with #450.<br>• **At the P4 exit, still without E3:** the rows stay `later`, and the issues leave the milestone but stay open and `blocked`. The demo shows the interim.<br>Unchanged: D-60's interim and its checks, D-64, D-220..D-222, and nothing moves before the trigger. | None of the five rows has an interim. Each one needs E3: the primer's request is E3's (D-61), a permission card or a test reminder without reminders would be a control that does nothing (D-60), and the notification permissions have no user without E3 (D-221). Exit criterion 1 accepts a re-disposition with a reason, and the matrix already uses `later` with the phase kept (EXPO-05). P4 rather than P3 or a new milestone: P3 is the mock and the staff tools, P4 is the last phase before the demo, where reminders shine, and no P4 exit criterion depends on E3. A "Later" milestone would amend D-89 for eight issues. Keeping the issues open keeps S6's sized work, and an upstream piece we don't control never holds a phase (D-02, D-97). |

## H. Code style and project layout

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-66 | The Zen of Python is the code rule: one obvious way, explicit over implicit, flat over nested, and no speculative abstraction (no protocol with a single implementation, no registries, no code generation). Split a module at around 400 lines, not before. | Owner's direction. |
| D-67 | *(Store modules amended by D-160.)* Layout (names in section M): `src/main.py`; `src/pycon_us/` with `app.py`, `routes.py`, `state.py`, a module per further store (`prefs.py`, D-160), `config.py`, `theme.py`, `capabilities.py`, `clock.py`, then `core/` (no Flet), `services/`, `ui/` (shared components, grouped by role into a few modules), `screens/` (one module per screen, flat) and `data/<year>/`; `src/assets/`; `tools/`; `tests/`. About 40 modules at the P1 exit, most of them screens. | Flat and readable. Most of v1's ~100 modules were seams for features we cut. |
| D-68 | `core/` imports nothing from Flet (a unit test scans its imports). It holds all business rules as pure functions over frozen dataclasses. | Testable in milliseconds without Flutter. |
| D-69 | *(The boot gate also waits for the slot layout's measurement: D-216.)* Declarative Flet: `page.render_views(App)`, `ft.Router(ROUTES, manage_views=True)`, `@ft.component` screens and `@ft.observable` stores. The stores are `Conference`, `Prefs` and `Favorites` until P3, which adds `Account` and `Staff`. Each store has its own context, with the providers written out in `App`. Services are built once with `ft.use_memo(lambda: Services.create(memory), [])` (an explicit empty dependency list; without one, `use_memo` recomputes on every render). `App` shows a boot view until `Prefs.loaded`. | Follows the verified subscription rules (F3); explicit and flat; no theme flash. |
| D-70 | *(Rule 3 amended by D-147; rule 9 added by D-157 and amended by D-243.)* Flet rules, enforced in review:<br>1. Await coroutine APIs only in `async def` handlers or effects.<br>2. A `use_effect` setup is an `async def`, never a lambda that returns a coroutine.<br>3. Long-running effects pass a sync `cleanup=` (e.g. set an Event or cancel a task); never an `async def` cleanup (F2). Long-running work also stops when its session is replaced (D-147).<br>4. Hooks are unconditional.<br>5. One context per observable.<br>6. Large values are `@dataclass(frozen=True, eq=False)` without object cycles.<br>7. No store writes from other threads.<br>8. Never `adaptive=True`.<br>9. A text field in a component is controlled: `value=` from the state its `on_change` sets (D-157), or, for a field that must keep up with fast typing, from what Flutter last reported (D-243). | Verified SDK behaviour (F1-F5, F33). |
| D-71 | Errors:<br>• Expected failures (network, parsing, storage, permissions, sharing or launching) are handled at explicit operation boundaries and become visible state.<br>• Unexpected exceptions in handlers and effects are caught by one small `@report_errors` decorator. It logs the traceback, shows "Something went wrong" (with the exception text in developer mode) and re-raises `CancelledError`. It returns an `async def` wrapper for coroutine functions and a plain wrapper otherwise, both with `functools.wraps`, so Flet's `iscoroutinefunction` and parameter-count checks still see the original; a unit test covers both cases.<br>• The decorator is deleted once Flet makes handler errors non-fatal (U15). | Otherwise a handler exception crashes the session (F4), and errors should never pass silently. |
| D-72 | Logging uses stdlib `logging`. Packaged apps capture it in `console.log` (F14), which can be shared from the Developer screen. Never log secrets, emails or badge codes. No Sentry and no analytics. | We use no crash-reporting service at first. |
| D-73 | One build, no flavours. A hidden Developer screen offers: fake clock, feed URL, mock API base URL, capability flags (read-only display, from `capabilities.py`), clear cached feed, show welcome again, test reminder, share log and reset app (confirmed). It is always available when not embedded. In packaged builds it unlocks after 7 taps on the version row in About this app; a minimal About this app ships with it in P1. | Environment variables don't reach packaged apps, and demos and off-season testing need a fake clock. |
| D-74 | *(Tests amended by D-253.)* Code is documented: a docstring on every module and on every public function and class (Google style), comments that say why, and type hints throughout. `ruff` (lint and format) and `pyright` (standard mode) must be clean. | Owner's direction: "sufficiently documented". |
| D-253 | **Tests are documented like the code: every test function says in its docstring the behaviour it pins, and every fixture, test helper, test class and method has a docstring too; `ruff`'s `D` rules apply to `tests/` with no exemption** (settles #350; amends D-74).<br>• **What a test's docstring says:** one sentence in the repo's Google style stating what the test checks, concretely (`test_open_spaces.py`: "A star tap re-renders one day body and one row (D-153)."), not a restatement of its name; a comment that explained the test moves into the docstring. Fixtures and helpers say what they provide or do. Module-level private helpers (`_x`) get one too, though `ruff` skips them; nested functions inside a test don't.<br>• **How it got there:** `pyproject.toml` exempted `tests/**` from `D`, and 912 of 1,489 tests had none; eight PRs (#351 to #358, over disjoint files) added 1,214 docstrings and reworded about 118, and changed no test logic: each file's syntax tree with docstrings removed is the same as before, but for one attribute docstring under a constant in `tests/row_sessions.py`, shortened to fit the line. Each PR was reviewed against its test bodies. This PR lifts the exemption, so CI enforces it. | The owner's rule (2026-10-19): a test should say in a sentence what it pins, as Flet's own tests do (`sdk/python/packages/flet/tests/test_version.py`). A lint keeps it from drifting again, which it did while tests were exempt. |
| D-75 | Dependencies stay few and mobile-safe: `flet`, `tzdata`, `flet-permission-handler` (P2), `flet-secure-storage` (P3) and pinned extensions as they land. Dev only: the Flet host tooling from the D-81 pin (`flet-cli`, `flet-web` and `flet-desktop`, plus `flet-platform-assets` while the source pin lasts, so `flet run` never pip-installs a PyPI copy), `flet[test]` (for `flet test`; it brings `pytest-asyncio`), `pytest`, `hypothesis`, `ruff`, `pyright`, and in P3 one QR library for the mock-badge sheet (e.g. `segno`). No bs4, markdownify, pydantic or jsonschema. | Mobile wheels, app size and licence hygiene. |
| D-147 | **Page handlers re-render without `page.update()`, and a replaced session stops its own work** (amends D-70 rule 3; settles architecture §4.3; S4, F29, F30).<br>• `bootstrap()` assigns `page.on_platform_brightness_change` and `page.on_app_lifecycle_state_change` after the first render; they write stores only. The app relies on these sequences: going to the background gives `inactive`, `hide`, `pause`; returning gives `restart`, `show`, `resume`; Back on Now on Android adds `detach` and ends the session (F29). Dark mode, font scale and rotation never start a new session on Android (the template's `configChanges`).<br>• `bootstrap()` also sets `page.on_close` to `svc.close()`, which sets one `asyncio.Event` on `Services`; every long-running loop (the 60 s ticks of D-40, the P3 sync worker, a refresh in flight before it writes) checks it and returns. Only `page.on_close` sets it: an effect's own cleanup still stops just that effect (its own Event or task, rule 3), since a screen unmounts on every tab switch. Until U24 (D-149). | Seen on the Android 14 emulator (Home, relaunch, `cmd uimode night`) and the iOS 26.5 simulator (another app launched, relaunch, `simctl ui appearance`): every event reached the handler, and a component subscribed to the store showed it at once. Flet closes a replaced session (`page.on_close` fires) but does not unmount its components: in S4 an old session's ticking effect ran next to the new one's and died only at its next store write, with `RuntimeError: An attempt to fetch destroyed session.` that only asyncio saw (F4); with the stop event it ended cleanly. |
| D-157 | *(Which text the field is built from amended by D-243.)* **Text fields in components are controlled** (adds rule 9 to D-70; S2, F33). A `TextField` (and any input whose value the user changes) that a component renders gets `value=` from the state its `on_change` handler sets. | Without it, the component's next re-render creates the field with its default value, the diff sends that to Flutter and the typed text disappears (F33). |
| D-243 | **A filter field is built from what Flutter last reported, and only a Clear sends it text; the count stays a live region on iOS** (settles #307 and #250; amends D-157 and D-70 rule 9 for these fields, and D-226's hook; F63, U43, #319).<br>• **The race (#307):** Flutter's reports of the typed text update the field the last render built as soon as they arrive, while change events and re-renders run later (F63). So on a busy host, a re-render for one keystroke could run after the next keystroke's report. It sent the older text back, and Flutter put it in the field. Once the state caught up with the last report, the diff had nothing to send, so the field kept the older text under a list filtered by the newer one.<br>• **The hook:** `use_field_query()` keeps one `Ref` for the screen's life, not a fresh one per render. At each render, the field that `Ref` still holds is the one Flutter's reports updated. `FieldQuery.value` is that field's text, so the diff sends nothing at a keystroke, and the screen filters by the same text it shows: the two cannot part. Only the initial text (first render) and a Clear are sent. `clear()` empties the state and bumps a Clear count, so each Clear re-renders and is sent exactly once, even when the state is already empty (a Clear tapped before the last key's event ran). `changed` still sets state, which re-renders the screen, and returns whether it took the report. Speakers and Sprints are unchanged; search writes `ViewMemory.search_query` only for a report `changed` took, so a View rebuilt later never brings back text a Clear dropped (D-193).<br>• **A Clear races too:** Flutter puts the Clear's empty text over any key typed between the tap and the moment the empty text arrives. Before this change, the field was left empty over a list still filtered by those keys (the owner's "mmumu" on Speakers). Now `clear()` starts a landing: from the tap on, `changed` drops reports. Once the render has sent the empty text, an effect on the Clear count asks the field for the focus. Flet runs effects after the render's patch, and Flutter handles messages in order (`flet_backend.dart`, `textfield.dart:113-121`), so Flutter answers only after it has the empty text, and every report before the answer was overwritten. If one came in, the empty text is sent again (the field here still holds that report's text) and waited for the same way. The same focus request is D-182's refocus, which `clear()` used to make directly.<br>• **What stays open (U43):** a key typed in the frame between Flutter's answer and the build that applies the empty text. Only a Flet change can close that frame (#319: Flutter would drop an echo of an older edit).<br>• **#250, the count's live region on iOS:** ruled out from the pin's Flutter source, not on a device. At Flutter 3.47.7, a live region whose label changes posts a queued announcement of its label (`UIAccessibilityAnnouncementNotification` with `UIAccessibilitySpeechAttributeQueueAnnouncement`; `accessibility_bridge.mm:139-151`, `SemanticsObject.mm:357-370`). VoiceOver's focus is not moved. When nodes come or go, the bridge refocuses the node that had the focus (`FindNextFocusableIfNecessary`, `accessibility_bridge.mm:218-226`, `:331-340`). Only a `focus` semantics event posts `UIAccessibilityLayoutChangedNotification` with a node (`:366-369`), and that is the sentence F13 had misread. So `found_count()` stays a polite live region on both platforms, with no `announce_message` path. Hearing it with VoiceOver on the iPhone is the owner's check (D-102, #320). | Fixing the shared hook once fixes Search, Speakers and Sprints. Not chosen: an `announce_message` path for the iOS count (#250's proposal), which would replace a polite, queued announcement with a less polite one and add a timer, for a problem the source shows does not exist. Not chosen either: pushing the state's text only while the field is unfocused, which still races with typing that continues after a Clear. In real Flet sessions (`tests/test_fields.py`), a burst whose reports land before the re-render that the previous event asked for now sends nothing to the field, and every render filters by a prefix of the typed word. Before this change, the same burst sent the older text back. Typing sends nothing, a Clear sends the empty text once, and a Clear before the last key's event still empties the field. Keys reported between a Clear's tap and Flutter's answer to its focus request are dropped, both before and after the Clear's render, and the empty text is sent again; without the landing, both tests fail. The devices ran a throwaway build that added a 300 ms sleep to the Speakers screen's render (never committed), so Python always fell behind the keys. On the shared iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build, bundle checked against `build/python-app`), the simulator tool typed "chen" and "sweigart" in one burst. The field and the list stayed in step ("chen", 2 speakers). A build with the hook but without the Clear's landing reproduced the owner's case: a Clear tapped just before a burst left the field empty under a list filtered by "chensweigart". With the landing, the same steps ended empty over the whole list, and the next "mu" read "5 speakers". Search ("chen", "2 sessions, 2 speakers") and Sprints (two bursts of "python web", then the no-match "Clear filter" right before another burst) kept the field and the list together without the delay. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK whose `app.zip` matched `build/python-app`), `uiautomator dump` after each `adb shell input text` burst read the same text in `speakers.filter` as in the count or no-match line, four runs out of four, Clear-then-burst included, and "chen" on Search read "2 sessions, 2 speakers". |
| D-160 | *(`ViewMemory`'s module amended by D-231.)* **A module per store, and the `Prefs` store** (amends D-67 and section M's `state.py`; records architecture §6.1's `Prefs`; #72).<br>• `state.py` keeps `Conference`, its context and hook~~, and `ViewMemory`~~ (`ViewMemory` is `memory.py`'s, D-231); `prefs.py` holds `Prefs`, `PrefsCtx` and `use_prefs()`; `Favorites` (P1), `Account` and `Staff` (P3) get `favorites.py`, `account.py` and `staff.py`. `Services`, `ServicesCtx` and `use_services()` live in `services/__init__.py`.<br>• `Prefs` holds the six app-wide keys of the storage table: `excluded_tracks` as a tuple (stored as `list[str]`, F12), `reminders` and `dev` as dicts. `theme` is an `Appearance` (`system`, `light`, `dark`) and `high_contrast` a `HighContrast` (`system`, `on`, `off`); a stored value outside its set reads as `system`, logged with the key only (as D-120 does for a wrong type), and `save()` raises `ValueError` for one before writing anything.<br>• `save(storage, *, theme=None, high_contrast=None, onboarded=None, excluded_tracks=None, reminders=None, dev=None) -> bool`: None leaves a setting as it is; it writes each given key, then assigns them all even when a write failed, and returns whether every write succeeded, so the caller shows "Couldn't save your change" (D-120). `load(storage)` assigns after all its reads and leaves `loaded` to `bootstrap()`. | With `Prefs`, `state.py` passed 500 lines (D-66), and P1 and P3 add three more stores; a top-level module per store keeps each one short and the imports flat (`from pycon_us.prefs import use_prefs`), with no package. Keyword arguments let pyright check every call, which `save(svc, **changes)` (architecture's sketch) would not. Checking the two fixed-value settings in the store keeps an unknown value from ever reaching `THEME_MODES`. |
| D-161 | **The boot gate and the start sequence** (records architecture §4 for SHELL-01 and DATA-35; #72).<br>• `BootView` is a full-bleed `#680579` View with a white `ProgressRing` after 1 s (`loop.call_later` from a sync effect whose cleanup cancels it), and no logo until O-4 gives the app an icon.<br>• `main.py` runs `ft.run(main, before_main=prepare_first_page)`, which gives the page's default View the boot purple (F35) and sets `page.theme_animation_style` to zero duration in that first page; `bootstrap()` applies the stored theme before it opens the gate and resets the animation after it, so every theme change before the gate, `App`'s mount-time one included, lands at once, and `App`'s theme effect follows later changes, which crossfade. The brightness handler reads `page.platform_brightness`, not the event (F36); U25 (amends D-98, with the small ones): deliver a real `PlatformBrightnessChangeEvent`, and keep the boot screen until `main()`'s first patch (F35). An unexpected error while it loads the settings or the local feed is logged and the gate opens anyway, with whatever loaded. The refresh starts after the gate opens and is not awaited.<br>• In P0 the lifecycle handler only re-reads the accessibility flags on `resume`; the refresh on a stale resume is #82's (D-40).<br>• `bootstrap()` sets `page.on_close` before its first await; a session closed while it loads (F26, F29) stops there, without opening the gate, starting a refresh or logging the store write that fails (F30). `Conference.start_refresh()` and `refresh()` take `closed=svc.closed` (D-147): once it is set, the refresh writes nothing more, neither the store nor, after the fetch, the cache.<br>• Until #74 the Router has one stub route, `screens/stub.py`: the data source, fetch time and status, the first day's session count, Refresh, and an Appearance control that drives `Prefs.save()`; until #73 `main.py` calls `logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)`. | On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK) and the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build), an offline cold start showed the purple boot view, then the bundled schedule 0.3-0.55 s after the session started, with no network wait; online, the refresh replaced it 0.4-2.4 s later, and a new session after Back and a relaunch ran the gate again. With a stored Dark under a light OS, every recording (3 of 3 on each platform) went from the purple straight to the dark screen. Before the fixes, Android showed one white frame between Flet's boot screen and `BootView` (F35); iOS showed the first screen mid-crossfade from light to dark (mean grey 83 instead of 19), because the gate opened within Flutter's 200 ms theme animation; and a dark-mode switch crashed the iOS session (F36). Opening the gate after an unexpected error keeps a bug from leaving the user on a purple screen; the error is in the log. |
| D-163 | **The error net and logging** (records D-71 and D-72; #73). In `services/errors.py`:<br>• **`report_errors`** is D-71's decorator. Its wrapper logs `Unexpected error in <module>.<qualname>` with the traceback, returns None and shows a floating SnackBar, "Something went wrong", followed by `: <type>: <message>` when not embedded (the companion app; #88's Developer unlock adds packaged builds, D-73). Showing it is best effort: with no page in the context, or in a replaced session (F30), it logs a warning instead, so the net itself never raises. A generator or async generator function raises `TypeError` when wrapped: Flet iterates a generator handler only when the handler itself is one, so the wrapper would silently never run it.<br>• **What is wrapped:** every event handler and every effect setup that a component, `main()` or `bootstrap()` defines, sync or async, the page handlers included. `bootstrap()`'s loading steps keep their own `try`, since the gate opens anyway (D-161); callables Flet does not call as handlers or effect setups (a cleanup, `page.on_close = svc.close`) are not wrapped. The log holds an exception's message verbatim, so code that can raise keeps URLs, tokens and badge codes out of its messages (D-72): P3's API layer raises its own exceptions rather than letting httpx's `HTTPStatusError` (whose message is the full URL) escape.<br>• **`setup_logging()`** runs once, when `main.py` is imported (a new session reuses the process, F26), and a second call returns at once, without touching a level set since: one stderr handler on the root logger, lines `HH:MM:SS.mmm LEVEL logger: message`, at INFO, or at the level `FLET_LOG_LEVEL` names (`flet run -v` / `-vv`; Flet's own use of it does nothing once a handler exists). `httpx` and `httpcore` stay at WARNING. In a packaged app `sys.stderr` is the template's tee to logcat or the unified log and to `console.log`, so the one handler reaches both (F14).<br>• **No asyncio exception handler of our own** (amends the roadmap's P0-21 scope): asyncio's default handler already logs through `logging`, so the setup above sends what it reports to `console.log`, and no loop-level hook can report a failed effect sooner (F37). Background work is awaited, wrapped or given a done callback (`Conference.start_refresh`). | An exception that escapes a handler ends the session (F4), and one that escapes an async effect is reported late or never (F37), so the net has to be on the callables themselves. `console.log`, which testers share, has no time stamps of its own. httpx logs every request URL at INFO, query included, and P3's private API could put there what D-72 keeps out of logs. In packaged builds with temporary failures (never committed): on the Android 14 emulator, a button whose wrapped sync or async handler raised logged the traceback and showed the SnackBar, and the Appearance control still worked after it, while the same handler unwrapped ended the session on Flet's "Error running app" screen; a wrapped sync effect setup, which Flet calls inside its update loop rather than in an event task, raised at mount and again when the refresh changed the feed version: each time it logged the traceback and showed the SnackBar, the refresh finished and Appearance › Dark still worked; on the iOS 26.5 simulator (driven with `simctl`, so without taps), a wrapped async effect and the wrapped brightness handler raised, each logged its traceback to the unified log and `console.log` and showed the SnackBar, and the theme still followed the next brightness switch. |
| D-187 | *(Why its feedback is in place amended by D-210; who deletes the cache when a feed URL is left, and the race, by D-244; which feed URL changes delete it, with the change log, by D-255.)* **The Developer screen, its options and the minimal About this app** (records D-73 and D-43's fake clock for DEV-01, and INFO-03's P1 part; amends section M's screen-module names for these two; #88).<br>• **Screens:** `screens/about_app.py` (`AboutApp`, `/more/app`) and `screens/developer.py` (`Developer`, `/more/developer`), named after their identifiers' screen part (`about_app.version`, `developer.*`; section M), since `app.py` is the root module. The fake clock's date and time pickers are `screens/developer_clock.py` (`clock_picker()`), so that the screen, which #107 grows, stays under about 400 lines (D-66).<br>• **Where the options live:** `pycon.dev` (`Prefs.dev`), with the keys `unlocked`, `fake_now` (the fake clock's start, ISO 8601 with its offset) and `feed_url`. `Prefs` reads them: `developer_unlocked`, `developer` (`DEVELOPER_ALWAYS`, i.e. `not is_embedded()`, or unlocked), `fake_now` and `feed_url`; a value of the wrong kind (a naive time, an empty URL) reads as its default. `prefs.with_dev_options()` sets or removes the clock and the URL and keeps the rest; `clock.fake_clock_text()` and `clock.parse_fake_clock()` are the clock field's text form and `prefs.parse_feed_url()` checks the URL field; `timefmt.utc_offset_label()` writes the device's offset.<br>• **The unlock persists:** in a packaged build, 7 taps on About this app's version row set `unlocked`, so it holds after a relaunch, because the fake clock and the feed URL do too and the screen that clears them must stay reachable; #107's Reset app locks it again. The 7th tap adds a **Developer options** row under the build's rows (`about_app.developer`, a live region), which opens the screen; once it is on, and always when not embedded, the row is there and taps do nothing more. A failed save keeps the unlock for the session and the row says "On until you close the app" (D-120). Opened at `/more/developer` before the unlock, the page says how to unlock.<br>• **The fake clock is stored as its start:** `bootstrap()`'s `start_developer_options()` runs `clock.start_fake_clock(prefs.fake_now)` right after the settings load, so every start of the app, and every new session in the process (F26, F29), begins at the same simulated moment and runs forward. Apply starts it only when the start changed (a feed-only change does not set it back); Reset returns to real time.<br>• **How screens learn of a change:** Apply and Reset set the clock and the feed URL **before** `prefs.save(dev=...)`. The save assigns `Prefs.dev`, `App` subscribes to `Prefs`, so the tree re-renders as on a theme change (D-150) and every screen reads `clock.now()` again; a tab root under the Developer stack mounts afresh on the switch (D-142), and the status strip's minute tick (D-175) keeps the age current. A memoised component that shows the time (P1's Schedule, D-153) takes the minute it shows as a prop. Now's stub shows today's date and the Schedule stub marks today, by `clock.now()` in conference time.<br>• **The feed URL** is `Conference.set_feed_url()` (a private field: it notifies nothing): every refresh started after it fetches from it, None returns to `conference.toml`'s. http is accepted (a test server on the laptop, testing-and-quality §5.2). A good feed from it is cached like any other, so when Apply or Reset leaves a feed URL for the year's feed, ~~`developer.leave_test_feed()`~~ `Conference.set_feed_url()` deletes the cache (D-244): an offline start would otherwise publish the test feed as the schedule, and starts from the bundled snapshot instead ~~(a refresh still running from the URL can cache it again)~~ (a refresh still running from the URL writes nothing, D-244).<br>• **Error details:** `errors.show_details(on)`; `bootstrap()` sets it from the stored unlock and About this app when it unlocks, and `_tell_user()` shows the exception text when it is on or the app is not embedded (D-71, D-163).<br>• **About this app (P1):** the app's name, "A companion for {conference}, built with Python and Flet." and "Not an official PSF app." (D-36; More's footnote goes), then Version, Python and Flet ("source build", `FLET_VERSION`, until `flet==1.1.0`). **No build number until U4:** `flet build` passes `--build-number` only to Flutter (Android `versionCode`, iOS `CFBundleVersion`), the template puts nothing about it in the app's environment, and `importlib.metadata` sees nothing (F15); a generated constant would be code generation (D-66).<br>• **The Developer screen (P1):** titled "Developer options" (the chip already says "Developer"), with a warning-tone chip (label and 1 dp outline in `Tokens.warning`; text only, as a badge: a fixed 18 dp icon looked lost beside the label at 200 % text) at the top of the page, not in the app bar, where it pushed the title out at 200 % text, drawn as a `Container`, as `TrackBadge` is: a mark, not a control, and a `Chip` without a handler is drawn faded, as disabled (seen on the iOS simulator); the fake-clock field (controlled, D-157; ISO 8601, conference time without an offset; its helper gives the format), filled by a Material `DatePicker` and then a `TimePicker`, which open on the first day at 09:00 while the field is empty (the date picker reports the device's local midnight in UTC, read back with `astimezone()`); both fields wrap to three lines at large text (`max_lines=3` without `multiline`, which would swap the date-time and URL keyboards for the multiline one, whose Return types a line break); the time zone (the conference's, and the device's UTC offset); the feed-URL field; Reset and Apply; errors under the fields, and after Apply or Reset a start-aligned line that says what is in effect (`developer.outcome`, a live region), after a check icon in `Tokens.success`, or a warning icon in `Tokens.warning` when the save failed (icon and word, ux.md §9); the capability flags in words. Identifiers: `developer.fake_clock`, `developer.feed_url`, `developer.reset` (real time and the year's feed, unconfirmed: nothing is lost that cannot be typed again), `developer.apply` and `developer.outcome`, with `about_app.version` and `about_app.developer`; Reset app (#107) gets one of its own.<br>• **Feedback in place, not in SnackBars,** on both pages (D-29's "results the user waits for appear in place")~~: at the pin a SnackBar shown from a pushed View is drawn on the tab root under it (#197)~~; a SnackBar would show on these pages too (D-210). | A persisted unlock keeps the way to the options that persist; an unlock per session would leave a fake clock running after a relaunch with its screen hidden. Starting the clock from its stored start at each start gives a demo the same moment every time; keeping its offset would let the simulated day drift with real time. Setting the clock before the save is what makes the re-render that the save causes read the new time (Flet renders a store change after the handler yields, `session.schedule_update`). On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug build, the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", driven by taps): six taps on the version row did nothing and the seventh unlocked Developer, which then showed in More; typing `2026-05-15 10:30` and Apply made Now say "Today in Pacific Time: Friday, May 15" and the Schedule stub mark Fri, May 15 as today, with no restart; a feed URL on a closed port (`http://127.0.0.1:9/conference.json`) then made the Schedule stub's Refresh say "Offline · schedule from Fri, May 15, 10:30 AM" (the log: "Feed refreshes use a Developer feed URL", then "no answer"), and Reset cleared both. A screen recording at 2 frames/s showed Reset's SnackBar on More under the Developer page, never on it (#197), hence the in-place feedback (not seen again with this very commit at the same pin, D-210). On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK), seven taps unlocked it, the date picker and then the time picker filled `2026-10-15 21:35` (the day read back right on a device at UTC+02:00), Apply said what it put into effect, and Now said "Thursday, October 15" and refreshed, the 15-min rule being due under the clock moved ahead. At 200 % text (Android) and the largest Larger Text (iOS) the chip in the app bar pushed the title out ("Develope…"), so it moved to the top of the page. |
| D-198 | *(Why its outcome is in place amended by D-210; its rows' disabled state on screen readers by D-214; Reset app deletes the change log and lists it among what is lost, D-255.)* **The Developer screen's data actions: Refresh now, Clear cached feed, Share log, and a confirmed Reset app that resets every store without a restart** (records D-73's data actions for DEV-04, DEV-05 and STORE-13, and ux.md §5.13's P1 "Data" section; amends section M's screen modules and `ui/` (`semantics.py`) and DEV-05's route to `/`; #107).<br>• **Modules, by role:** `screens/developer_data.py` is what the actions do and say: `DataActions`, a component of its own under a **Data** `SectionHeader` between Schedule feed and Capabilities, `reset_app()`, `share_console_log()` and the words; `screens/developer_ui.py` is how the screen draws them: `action_row()`, `outcome_line()` and `padded()` (the last two moved from `developer.py`, which draws Apply's line with them too, so the screen never imports a layout piece from its Data section) and the two confirmations. So each module stays under D-66's 400 lines, as `developer_clock.py` does.<br>• **Rows:** two-line `ListTile`s, each one merged node with its identifier (`developer.refresh_now`, `developer.clear_cache`, `developer.share_log`, `developer.reset_app`), its icons and title 14 dp from the top (D-188's rule for rows with a subtitle), a disabled row saying why in its second line, which stays in `on_surface_variant` while the icon and title take the disabled colour (at the disabled 38 % the reason was too faint to read); identifiers through `ui/semantics.py`'s `named()`, D-190's merge, which Now's buttons use too; under them one outcome line (`developer.data_outcome`, a live region; a check in `success` or a warning in `warning` beside the words, the icon in a 24 dp slot 16 dp before them, so the words start in the tiles' text column, as Apply's line does), in place ~~(#197)~~ (D-29, as Apply's line; D-210). **Refresh now** is `Conference.refresh()`, a refresh the user asked for (D-181): disabled with "Updating the schedule…" and a small `ProgressRing` at its end while `status()` says `refreshing`, otherwise its second line says where the schedule on screen came from and its age ("From the cache, updated 3 hours ago"), kept current by `use_minute()` while the screen is open, as the strip's is (D-175); then "Schedule updated from the feed.", or by the failure (D-146) that the app keeps the schedule it has. **Clear cached feed** is confirmed, then `Storage.delete_feed()`, the one deletion path (a corrupt cache and ~~`leave_test_feed()`~~ `Conference.set_feed_url()` use it too; ~~#206 moves the feed URL's rule into the store~~ D-244), which now says whether the cache is gone and deletes under the write lock in a thread, so a write in progress (a cancelled refresh's, D-120) lands first instead of bringing the file back (D-244: every write asked for before the deletion, also one still queued for the lock). This session keeps the schedule it shows; the next start shows the bundled snapshot until a refresh saves a new copy. Disabled, "No cached schedule on this device", when there is none. **Share log** is `Share.share_files([ShareFile.from_path(await StoragePaths.get_console_log_filename())])` through `Services.share` and `Services.paths`, pointed from the window's centre for tablets (D-62), in a packaged build only, where the template writes `console.log` (F14); elsewhere disabled, "Not a packaged build: the log is in the terminal that runs it". No log or an empty one says "There's no log to share yet."; a failure "Couldn't open the share sheet.", logged by its class (D-72); `ShareResultStatus.SUCCESS` says "Log shared.", `DISMISSED` nothing, and `UNAVAILABLE` (the platform cannot tell, as with some Android share targets) "Log handed to the share sheet.", which is true either way. **Reset app**, its title and icon in `error`, is confirmed.<br>• **Confirmations (D-29):** an `AlertDialog(modal=True, scrollable=True)` with the row's icon, a question for a title, what is lost, then Cancel and the action in `error` (`<row>.cancel`, `<row>.confirm`), portalled with `ft.use_dialog` from the section's state; Back dismisses it as Cancel (`on_dismiss`; on Android it also pops the page, #235). "Clear the cached schedule?" says the session keeps showing it and the next start shows the bundled schedule, its capture day named (with non-breaking spaces, so it never wraps mid-date), until a refresh saves a new copy. Reset app's dialog icon is in `error`, as its row; Clear's keeps the dialog's default. "Reset the app?" lists exactly what is lost, one line each beside a decorative icon (`reset_losses()`): "Your settings: Appearance and the schedule's filters", "Your saved sessions in My schedule: 12" (the live count, or "none"), "The cached schedule: the app shows the bundled one until it refreshes" and "Developer options: the fake clock and the feed URL", with ", and Developer locks again" in a packaged build. Developer's own Reset (real time and the year's feed) stays unconfirmed (D-187): nothing it drops cannot be typed again.<br>• **What Reset app does, without a restart** (`reset_app()`): real time and no error details first, so the re-render the stores cause reads them (D-187's order); `Storage.clear_prefs()` removes every key under `storage.PREFIX` (`pycon.`): the settings, every year's favourites, the Developer options and their unlock, and no key that is not the app's; `Conference.reset()` cancels a running refresh and waits for it (one from a Developer feed URL would otherwise cache or publish the test feed afterwards; a caller awaiting `refresh()`, such as a Refresh now tapped before the reset, ends cancelled with it and says nothing), returns to the year's feed URL, deletes the cache through `delete_feed()` and publishes the bundled snapshot, so every screen shows it at once; `Prefs.reset()` puts the persisted settings back to their defaults (the device facts and `loaded` stay); `Favorites.reset()` empties the set without a write (the key is gone); `ViewMemory.reset()` puts every field back (the Schedule jumps to now again; a new session starts on Now). Then a quiet refresh starts, as at a start (D-181), and the app goes where a fresh start goes: `start_target()` over the reset `ViewMemory` (D-143), so Now, and Welcome once P2 adds it there (DEV-05's "routes to `/`"; a navigation to `/` itself left the app on the Developer View, now locked, on the iOS simulator), where a SnackBar says "App reset" or, when a key or the cache could not be deleted (logged by name, D-120), that the session is reset and some saved data returns when the app reopens. **What a restart would still differ in:** nothing a user sees. The Python process itself stays: its import-time constants (`capabilities.py`), the feed-keyed memos (which the new feed object makes miss), and `console.log`, which is diagnostics, not user data, and is kept so a tester can share what led up to the reset. From P2 and P3, the reminders plan, SecureStorage and the outbox join `reset_app()` with their features (DEV-05). | The issue asks for an app as after a fresh install with no restart where feasible, and every piece of state the app keeps is a store, `ViewMemory`, a `pycon.` key or the cache file, so resetting each explicitly in one function is enough, and a test checks every one of them on a used session. Clearing by prefix rather than `SharedPreferences.clear()` leaves keys the app does not own (Flutter's, a plugin's) alone, and needs no list kept in step with the key table. Cancelling the running refresh is what makes the reset hold: a refresh from a test feed URL that finished afterwards cached that feed again (`test_reset_cancels_a_refresh_from_a_developer_feed_before_it_caches_it` fails without the cancel). Confirming Clear cached feed too follows STORE-13 ("each confirmed with the list of what is lost"): offline, the next start falls back to an older schedule. Share log is gated on a packaged build because the companion app's Python runs on the laptop, so the device path `StoragePaths` returns is not a file it can check, and the template writes no `console.log` there. On the iOS 26.5 simulator (debug builds checked against `build/python-app`; the shared "PyCon iPhone 17", driven by taps), light and dark: Refresh now said "Schedule updated from the feed." in place; Share log opened the share sheet with the 1 KB `console.log` ("Share the app log"), the row disabled while it was up, and the log held no address; Clear cached feed asked first, then deleted `feed-2026.json` and greyed its row ("No cached schedule on this device"); Reset app's dialog listed the four losses with the live count, Cancel kept everything, and Reset app left the app's preferences plist empty, locked Developer (About this app had no Developer row until seven new taps) and, after the fix above, landed on Now with "App reset"; the next refresh cached the year's feed again, as a fresh start's does. At the largest Larger Text the rows wrapped with nothing clipped, and the dialog's text scrolled under its fixed buttons. On the Android 14 emulator (`flet_probe_api34`, release arm64 APK checked against `build/python-app`): `uiautomator dump` showed each row as one clickable `Button` node named by its two lines with its `resource-id` (`developer.refresh_now` … `developer.reset_app`) and the dialog's buttons as `developer.reset_app.cancel` and `.confirm`, 48 dp tall; Share log opened the system chooser with `console.log`; Reset app landed on Now with "App reset" and More lost its Developer row; dark at 200 % text reflowed with nothing clipped. Back with a dialog open closes it, acting on nothing, but on Android also pops the page under it, as the fake clock's date picker does (#235). |
| D-214 | *(Its guards completed by D-230.)* **A semantics identifier is one node with its control, and that node says whether the control is disabled; around a control a merge would swallow, the identifier stays bare, and never on a tile's title** (settles #239 and #236; amends D-190's pattern, D-198's rows, section M's `ui/semantics.py`, ux.md §5.13 and §9; records F50 and U34).<br>• **The disabled state:** `named()` gives its `Semantics` the control's own `disabled` (`control.disabled`) instead of a keyword its callers pass: the Developer screen's rows passed none, so Clear cached feed without a cache dumped `enabled="true"` and TalkBack never said "disabled" (F42). `ui/links.py`'s `external()`, the other merged wrapper, does the same. Both read only the control they get: a `Semantics` passed in the button's place (open spaces' Propose, a sprint's More) needs the button's `disabled` too, as their docstrings say. `named()` is the one way to merge an identifier: the Schedule sheet's Reset, Show, chips and "What do these mean?", the Schedule's Show all, Search's scope chips, the When and Room tiles, a speaker row, More's appearance rows and About's Version, which built its shape by hand (Reset and Show passing `disabled` themselves, D-195), now call it, and a scan keeps it so.<br>• **Merged** (D-190, through `named()`): `developer.reset`, `developer.apply`, `developer.feed_url` (the field reads its label, helper and error as one node), the fake clock's picker (`developer.clock_picker`, new), session detail's `session.overflow` and a contact row's Copy (`<id>.copy`). Bare, each was a node of its own (F50): an empty stop beside the button, or, for More options, the tooltip on the identifier's node and the button under it unnamed.<br>• **Bare, around the control:** where a merge would take in another control: the fake clock field (its picker inside it, D-189) and the folds (`FoldTile`, which hold rows). A fold's identifier moves from its title to around the tile (`lightning_talks.doubts`, `my_schedule.earlier_days`, `my_schedule.earlier_today`, as `session.other_rooms` already was): on the title it split the title from the tile's button, which dumped as "Collapsed" alone. Each is an empty node until U34 (the field) or an identifier on the control itself (the folds). Live regions and containers (`developer.outcome`, `more.appearance`, the empty states) stay as they are.<br>• **Guards:** unit tests for the merged shape and the disabled state, and for the folds' identifier around the tile; an `ast` scan for a merge built by hand (testing-and-quality.md §3.5). Left to the phase's screen-reader pass (#109): VoiceOver's "dimmed" on a disabled row, and what TalkBack does with a fold's empty wrapper (focusable, not clickable, the header's bounds, where the title's node was before). | Before (main at `7e7840e`, release arm64 APK, Android 14 emulator `flet_probe_api34`, `uiautomator dump`): `developer.reset`, `developer.apply`, `developer.feed_url` and `developer.fake_clock` were empty focusable `View`s over the control; Clear cached feed, disabled, `enabled="true"`; `session.overflow` a `View` "More options" over an unnamed `Button`; the doubts fold's header read "Collapsed". After (this change rebased on `5a6a7b6`, the same emulator, the APK's `app.zip` checked against `build/python-app`): Reset and Apply one `Button` each with the identifier; the feed URL the `EditText`; the picker a `Button` "Pick a date and time" that opens the date picker; Clear cached feed, disabled, `enabled="false"`, with its reason; More options one `Button` that opens its menu; the folds' headers "Think you can't do one?, Collapsed" and "Earlier days (1 session), Collapsed". A Flutter widget test (3.47.7) of the same shapes settled what is Flet's and what is Flutter's (F50). Nothing on screen changed. |
| D-230 | *(`ON_PURPOSE`'s `track.add_all` entry moved to `menu_item()` by D-233.)* **A scan keeps a bare identifier off a button, chip, field or tile; the ones that stay bare are listed, each with its reason** (completes D-214's guards; amends testing-and-quality.md §3.5; settles #279).<br>• **The rule:** an `ast` scan in `tests/test_semantics.py` flags a `Semantics` that passes `identifier` and nothing but `content` and `disabled`, not the content of a `MergeSemantics`, around a call of a `*Button` (`PopupMenuButton` too), `*Chip`, `TextField` or `*Tile` (`ListTile`, and the folds' `ExpansionTile` and `FoldTile`), or around a name its function itself assigns one to (a nested function's or class's names are its own), since screens build the control first (`fold = FoldTile(...)`). Each such identifier is a node apart from its control (F50); `named()` is the fix.<br>• **Bare on purpose** (`ON_PURPOSE`, by module and identifier as written, a comment giving each one's reason): D-214's fake clock field (`developer.fake_clock`) and folds (`lightning_talks.doubts`, `my_schedule.earlier_days` and `.earlier_today`, `session.other_rooms`); and `track.add_all`, inside the overflow's `PopupMenuItem`, whose own `MergeSemantics` (Flutter's `popup_menu.dart`) takes the identifier into the item's node, so `named()` there would merge what is merged already. The scan first checks that it finds every listed site, so a scan that matches nothing, or a stale entry, fails.<br>• **Outside the rule, so not listed:** `SearchField`'s field (merged, with a `label`), the nav icons (around an `Icon`: the destination's node takes the identifier, D-166, D-170), the day tabs' labels (D-166) and an in-app `LinkTile`'s tile (inside the `MergeSemantics` the function puts around it). | On `main` at `be97438` the scan finds those five sites and no other; a scratch `ft.Semantics(identifier="x", content=ft.TextButton("Go"))` in `screens/track.py` failed it (`track.py:215: 'x'`), reverted. `track.add_all` (main's release arm64 APK, its `app.zip` checked against `build/python-app`, Android 14 emulator `flet_probe_api34`, `uiautomator dump` with the Charlas overflow open): one `android.view.MenuItem` node, `resource-id="track.add_all"`, `content-desc="Add all to My schedule"`, clickable and enabled, with no empty node beside it. |
| D-234 | **A button's tooltip names it in its own node: each button whose tooltip is said is merged with it, through `merged()` or, with an identifier, `named()`, and a scan keeps it so** (settles #274; amends D-10's back arrow, D-27, ux.md §3.7, §5.2 and §9, section M's `ui/semantics.py` and testing-and-quality.md §3.5; records F55 and U37).<br>• **The cause is Flet's** (F55): it wraps a control in its `Tooltip` from outside, so a button's tooltip is a node of its own over the button, which has no name. TalkBack said "Button" on the app bar's Back arrow and Search. The analysis and a suggested fix for Flet are #317 (`upstream`, D-212, D-213).<br>• **`merged(button)`** (`ui/semantics.py`): a `MergeSemantics` straight around the button, which then reads its tooltip as its name, as Flutter's own `IconButton(tooltip:)` does. No `Semantics` of its own, so no enabled state to pass (F42). Used by `AppScaffold`'s Back arrow, `search_button()` (now typed `ft.Control`), `SearchField`'s Clear (placed in its `Stack` by a `Container(right=4)`, since `MergeSemantics` has no position) and a contact row's Copy without an identifier (with one it was already `named()`). The buttons with an identifier were already merged through `named()` (Share, the overflows, the clock picker; D-214), and those named by a label keep their tooltip out of the semantics (the star, Filter sessions, Propose an open space; D-184).<br>• **The "Now" button:** a View takes only a `FloatingActionButton`, so no merge can go around it. Its tooltip still shows on a long press but is left out of the semantics (`exclude_from_semantics=True`), and what it does is said from its label's `semantics_label`, inside Flutter's own merge: "Now", then "Jump to the current time", as a link's "Opens in browser" is said (ux.md §4.7). Not a `Semantics`' `tooltip`, which Flet also shows as a second tooltip (F55), nor its `hint_text`, which never reaches Flutter (#187).<br>• **Not chosen:** an `icon_button()` builder for every icon button (the merge is the one thing to add, and the buttons differ in all else); a `Semantics(label=...)` copying each tooltip (two strings to keep in step, and iOS reads a node's tooltip after its label, D-184); an identifier on every button only to reach `named()`.<br>• **Guard:** `tests/test_tooltips.py` scans every module for a `*Button` call whose tooltip is said (not `None`, not a `Tooltip(..., exclude_from_semantics=True)`) and that reaches no merge (`MergeSemantics`, `named()`, `merged()`, `links.external()`): straight, through a `Semantics` around it, a conditional, a name its function assigns it to, or a function that returns it, each of whose calls must merge it (the clock picker). On `main` it flags the four sites fixed here: the "Now" button, Clear, Back and Search. | Before (`main` at `ed7c215`, release arm64 APK, my Android 14 emulator `flet_probe_api34`, `uiautomator dump`): the Back arrow on search, the Developer screen and session detail, Search on Now, More and the Schedule, and search's Clear were each a non-focusable `View` holding the tooltip over a `Button` with `NAF="true"`; the "Now" button a `View` "Jump to the current time" over the `Button` "Now". TalkBack 14.2, moved by its own keyboard shortcuts (Alt+arrows, sent through the emulator's keyboard device; an `adb` tap reaches the app without passing through TalkBack) and read from its verbose log, said "Button" on Back and on Search. After (this change, the APK's `app.zip` checked against `build/python-app`): Back, Search and "Clear search" one `Button` each, named by the tooltip, at the same bounds (Clear at `[901,315][1028,441]` before and after); the "Now" button one `Button` "Now\nJump to the current time", whose tooltip still shows on a long press; Share, the star, More options and Filter sessions as before. TalkBack said "Back. Button", "Search. Button" and "Clear search. Button". On the iOS 26.5 simulator the screens look as before. |
| D-196 | **The log handler writes every email address as `<email>`** (amends D-163's `setup_logging()`; records D-72 for Share log; #107). `services/errors.py`'s `RedactingFormatter` formats the line as before (`LOG_FORMAT`), then replaces each match of `EMAIL` (`name@host.tld`, or with the `@` percent-encoded as in a `mailto:` URL) with `<email>`, over the whole line, traceback included; `setup_logging()` gives it to its one handler, so it covers every line the app logs through `logging`, in `console.log`, logcat and the unified log. What reaches `sys.stderr` outside `logging` is not redacted: `sys.excepthook`'s report of an exception that escapes everything and a `warnings` message; the app writes nothing there itself, and `report_errors` (D-163) keeps handlers and effects inside `logging`. The rule stays where it was: our own lines never hold a secret, a badge code or an address (D-72, D-163), and this is a net under it, not a licence. An audit of the app's log calls (#107) found keys and file names, link kinds and error classes, counts, versions, routes, scroll keys and the fake clock's start, none of them personal; the lines that quote what the app does not control are `report_errors`' exception message and traceback, `Skipped a feed record: …` (which quotes a record's value), and asyncio's and Flet's own. | D-182 found platform errors that repeat a `mailto:` address (Android's `ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND` names the intent), and the feed's records can carry contact addresses; once Share log sends `console.log`, an address in it leaves the device. Secrets and badge codes have no pattern to match, so for them the rule at the write site is the only guard; an address does, and a test writes one through the real handler, in a message and in a traceback, and finds `<email>` in both. `@ft.component`, `flet@1.1.0`, a Mastodon handle URL and `user@localhost` are kept. |
| D-200 | *(The rows' line builders moved on to `ui/row_lines.py` by D-227.)* **`ui/scaffold.py` and `ui/session_rows.py` split under D-66's 400 lines: the status strip, the time gutter, the identifier key and the rows' phrases get modules of their own or move next to their kin** (amends the modules named in D-165, D-184, D-188 and D-191, section M's `core/` and `ui/` lists, and architecture's module table and §5.5; #225).<br>• **`ui/status_strip.py`:** `StatusStrip`, `strip_line()`, `STATUS_ICONS`, `PROGRESS_HEIGHT`, and the minute tick: `TICK_SECONDS`, `current_minute()`, `use_minute()` and `stop_ticks()` (D-175, D-188). `ui/scaffold.py` keeps `AppScaffold`, `Shown`, `search_button()` and `SectionHeader`.<br>• **`ui/time_gutter.py`:** `TimeGutter` and `TimeSlot` (D-158, D-191), a slot's time column, which knows nothing of the rows beside it.<br>• **`semantics_key()`** goes to `ui/semantics.py`, beside `named()`: speaker rows, the filter sheet and session detail key their identifiers with it without importing the session rows.<br>• **The rows' spoken phrases**, `spoken_phrase()`, `notice_phrase()` and `elapsed_phrase()`, go to `core/row_views.py`: pure text over a `SessionView`, beside the `spoken_when` they compose, and under `core`'s import scan (D-68).<br>• **Moves only**, no behaviour change and no re-export: the importers name the new modules, and a test patches a name where it is looked up (`session_rows.spoken_phrase`, `status_strip.TICK_SECONDS`). The tests follow their code: the strip's and the gutter's to `tests/test_status_strip.py` and `tests/test_time_gutter.py`, the phrases' to `tests/test_row_views.py` and `semantics_key()`'s to `tests/test_semantics.py`; the `Hooks` fake, which both the strip's and the scaffold's tests use, to `tests/fakes.py`. `screens/stub.py`'s `stub_body()` drops its dead `status=`, `auto_refresh=` and `ref=`: no stub shows the strip or is a tab root since #209. | `session_rows.py` was 533 lines and `scaffold.py` 450 (D-66); the gutter alone left the rows near 470. The strip's tick has importers (the Map, room detail, Now, My schedule, the Developer screen, session detail's status line) that use nothing else of the scaffold, and each moved piece now sits with what it is about. |
| D-227 | **`ui/session_rows.py` splits again under D-66's 400 lines: the lines a row is made of go to `ui/row_lines.py`, and a name another module imports has no leading underscore** (amends D-184's and D-200's module notes, section M's `ui/` list and architecture's module table; #251).<br>• **`ui/row_lines.py`:** `meta()`, the time-and-place line (D-184's two wrapping texts); `secondary()`, a supporting text in `on_surface_variant`; `overlap_line()`, My schedule's clash warning (D-191); `elapsed_bar()` and its `TRACK`, Now's elapsed bar (D-190); and the private `_NBSP` that `meta()` keeps "AM" on its time's line with. Plain builders with no hooks, which `SessionRow` and `NoticeRow` stack under a row's title. `ui/session_rows.py` keeps `SessionRow`, `NoticeRow`, `use_session_views()`, `is_notice()` and `NOTICE_GROUP`, so D-184's and section M's description of it stays true.<br>• **The naming rule:** a function or constant that another module imports is that module's API and has no leading underscore, PEP 8's mark for "internal use"; a name only its own module uses keeps it. So `_meta()`, `_secondary()`, `_overlap_line()` and `_elapsed_bar()` became `meta()`, `secondary()`, `overlap_line()` and `elapsed_bar()`, while `row_lines._NBSP`, `session_rows._open_session()` and the memoised bodies `_SessionRow` and `_NoticeRow` stay private. A test may still reach a private name of the module it tests, as `tests/test_session_rows.py` spies on `_SessionRow`.<br>• **Moves only**, as D-200: the bodies are unchanged, nothing changes on screen or in what a row says, and nothing is re-exported: `session_rows.py` imports the builders by name, and the one test that builds a bar on its own (`test_the_elapsed_bar_shows_its_fill_and_its_end_on_every_surface`) imports `elapsed_bar` from the new module and stays in `tests/test_session_rows.py`, beside the row tests that show the bar. | `session_rows.py` was back at 419 lines after #244 and #246 gave the rows `of_speaker` and `show_time`, so the next change to the rows would have had to split it mid-PR; the builders are about a hundred lines that use no hooks and none of the rows' state. Now `session_rows.py` is 320 lines and `row_lines.py` 113. Importing underscored names across modules would have hidden an API behind the mark that says it is none; pyright and ruff accept either, so the rule is ours to state. |
| D-228 | **`config.py` splits along the track registry: `Track`, the Tracks hub's pages and the registry's checks are `year_tracks.py`** (records D-66 for the year loader; amends section M's year modules and architecture's module table and §8.2; #275).<br>• **`year_tracks.py`:** `Track`, `TrackFormat`, `TRACK_GROUPS`, `HUB_PAGES` (D-207, still beside the registry it names tracks of), `KEYNOTE_TRACKS` (D-217), and `check_tracks()`, the registry's rules that ended `_check_conference()` (unique keys and slugs, the slug pattern, `_check_hub_page()`, a legend, a group of `TRACK_GROUPS`). It imports only `year_schema.py`, so the loader stays free of Flet.<br>• **`config.py` keeps** `YEAR`, `DATA_DIR`, `SNAPSHOT_FILE`, the conference facts (`ConferenceInfo` with `track()`, `track_for_slug()` and `track_name()`, `Content`, `Snapshot`, `YearData`), `load_year()` and the conference and content rules; `_check_conference()` calls `check_tracks()` where the loop was, so the rules run in the same order with the same messages, and the loader accepts and refuses what it did.<br>• **The line:** each `year_*` module is one concern's tables with their checks (`year_pages.py`, D-205; `year_keynotes.py`, D-217), and the registry is one; the other cut #275 offered, the rules apart from the dataclasses, would split each table from the rules that give it meaning.<br>• **A move only**, with no re-export: the importers of `Track`, `TRACK_GROUPS`, `HUB_PAGES`, `KEYNOTE_TRACKS` and `TrackFormat` (`locations.py`, `screens/tracks.py`, `track.py`, `schedule_sheet.py`, `tools/update_snapshot.py` and three test modules) name `year_tracks`; the tests change only in their imports. | `config.py` had 435 lines (D-66); 333 now, and `year_tracks.py` 134. The registry is the one part of `config.py` with importers of its own (the hub, a track's page, the filter sheet, the routes' locations), which now import it without the loader. |
| D-231 | **`state.py` splits under D-66's 400 lines: it keeps the `Conference` store, its context and its hook, and nothing else; `ViewMemory` is `memory.py`, and what a refresh needs beyond the store's fields is `feed_refresh.py`** (amends D-146's and D-160's module notes, section M's top-level modules and architecture's module table and §6.1-§6.3; #296).<br>• **`memory.py`:** `ViewMemory` with its `reset()` (D-143, D-198), plain process memory with no context or hook. It imports only the standard library, so `services/__init__.py` imports it at run time: its `TYPE_CHECKING` guard was there only because `state.py` imports the services package.<br>• **`feed_refresh.py`:** the words for a refresh's outcome, `Source`, `Problem` and `Status` (D-146); its timings, `STALE_ON_CONFERENCE_DAYS` and `STALE_OTHERWISE` (D-32) and `REFRESH_EVERY` (D-175); and its network step: `fetch()`, one attempt that returns the bytes, their digest and the processed feed or the `Problem` that stopped it, `feed_digest()`, the SHA-256 that tells the published feed's bytes again (D-224), and `log_unexpected()`, the task's done callback (D-71). Under D-227's naming rule they lose their underscore, since `state.py` imports them; `_digest()` became `feed_digest()` rather than `digest()` because `fetch()` and `Conference._refresh()` name their local result `digest`.<br>• **The line:** what reads or writes the store's fields stays in `state.py`, and what needs none of them goes. Either cut #296 offered would have left `state.py` over 400 on its own: 462 lines with only `ViewMemory` out, about 458 with only the helpers and thresholds.<br>• **Moves only**, as D-200 and D-227: the bodies are unchanged and nothing is re-exported. The importers name the new modules (`main.py`, `app.py`, `services/__init__.py`, three `ui/` and four `screens/` modules, and 20 test modules); `ViewMemory`'s one test moves to `tests/test_memory.py`, and the other tests change only in their imports. The refresh's three log lines now come from the logger `pycon_us.feed_refresh` instead of `pycon_us.state`, with the same text and levels. | `state.py` was 515 lines after D-224 (D-66); it is 400 now, `memory.py` 62 and `feed_refresh.py` 92. `Conference` is about 345 of those 400 lines, so the store's next growth needs a cut inside it rather than another move around it. Importing the moved names one by one, rather than `feed_refresh.fetch(...)`, keeps the store's lines as they were. |

## I. Testing

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-76 | Unit tests (pytest) cover everything in `core/`, plus the year-data loader, capabilities, storage keys and theme contrast. Fixtures are the recorded 2025 and 2026 feeds. | Protects correctness cheaply. |
| D-77 | Feed correctness is checked three ways: the named rule tests (D-41); about ten invariants (no session lost, stable ids, placeholders filtered, Job Fair grouping, collapsed rows, speakers linked, a start on every group); and a reviewed JSON snapshot of our processed model. Ionic's own processing output is captured once in P0 (`tools/capture_ionic_reference/`) to settle ambiguous rules; it does not run in CI, and contributors need no Node. In P3, hypothesis state-machine tests cover the outbox. | Ionic is a reference, not an oracle. This reconciles the three reviews. |
| D-78 | About eight `flet test` smoke flows run on a device or emulator: start offline, switch days, search, favourite with Undo, session → speaker → session → Back, map pin, and in P3 an offline scan that syncs. They run locally before merging UI-heavy PRs and at phase exits. `flet test` always runs in device mode, so flows run on an emulator and a simulator. Spike S9 tries `flet test linux` for CI; once it has run green for two weeks, it is proposed as a separate workflow through a `needs-decision` issue, never inside `ci.yml` (D-84). A handful of goldens arrive only in P4. | These are the journeys that matter. On-device testers cannot drag (F21), and goldens churn while the UI evolves. |
| D-79 | *(Devices amended by D-102.)* Each phase exit runs a device checklist ~~on our Android phone and iPhone (plus an emulator and a simulator)~~ on an Android emulator and an iOS simulator, with the iPhone for the items that need hardware or a release build (D-102): cold start, airplane-mode start, dark and high contrast, maximum font size, TalkBack and VoiceOver on three flows, 3-button and gesture navigation (with visible navigation-bar icons in light and dark, D-12), back gestures, reminders firing (P2 on) and queued scans (P3). Results go in the phase status issue. | An API that exists in the SDK is not proof it works on a device. |
| D-80 | *(Devices amended by D-102; emulator baseline recorded by D-112; interaction latency by D-159.)* Performance targets, measured by hand at phase exits ~~on a mid-range Android phone~~ on the devices D-102 names (release builds on the iPhone against the targets; the Android emulator as a relative regression signal; absolute Android numbers once a mid-range Android phone is borrowed) and recorded:<br>• cold start to the cached schedule: 2.5 s or less;<br>• feed processing: 400 ms or less;<br>• no visible jank scrolling the busiest day.<br>App size is recorded, not gated. S1 and S2 may revise the numbers. | Honest numbers without CI machinery, and budgets from evidence. |
| D-170 | *(The title's node amended by D-211: it is also the level-1 heading.)* **Tab roots name themselves to UI flows** (adds to section M's identifiers; #78). `AppScaffold` wraps a tab root's app-bar title in an unlabelled `Semantics(identifier="<tab>.title")`: `now.title`, `schedule.title`, `my_schedule.title`, `map.title`, `more.title` (`Tab.title_identifier`; `<tab>` is `Tab.key`, the bar's own `app.nav.<tab>` key). Pushed Views get none from the scaffold: a flow knows one by its back arrow and the bar's absence (D-144), and a screen that a flow must tell apart adds its own `<screen>.<element>` identifier. | A `Finder` only counts matches (testing-and-quality.md §13), and a root's title is also its tab's label, so the text alone cannot say which root shows. The title is the page's heading, one per root, and the screen modules stay untouched, so the More list (#77) can change freely. As with the bar (D-166), the identifier merges into the title's node: the shell flow found each one on the Android 14 emulator and the iOS 26.5 simulator, and `uiautomator dump` on the Now and More roots shows one leaf node with `resource-id` `now.title` (`more.title`) and the description `Now` (`More`), so the heading still reads once. Each pin bump re-checks it with the bar's (development.md §2.2). |
| D-171 | **How the UI flows run** (records D-78's harness; #78). In `tests/ui/`:<br>• `tests/ui/conftest.py` holds the fixtures and `tests/ui/helpers.py` the helpers; the inventory is testing-and-quality.md §5.2.<br>• Flows tap only by finder: no drag, no coordinates (F21). They find by identifier, exact text or tooltip, never by icon (`RemoteTester.find_by_icon` sends Flet's packed icon value).<br>• A step that follows a push or a pop waits until the leaving View is gone, and the back arrow is tapped only when exactly one shows: a tap on the View leaving is lost.<br>• Screenshots come from `adb exec-out screencap` and `xcrun simctl io screenshot` into `build/ui/<platform>/` (git-ignored); a timeout saves `timed-out.png` when the device still answers, and its own failure never replaces the step's. `RemoteTester.take_screenshot` fails from its second call on Android.<br>• Each flow runs on an emulator or a simulator of the agent's own: the test build uses the app's bundle id and `flutter test` uninstalls it afterwards. Commands: development.md §5.7. | The shell flow (`test_shell_starts`) passed with `flet test` on `flet_probe_api34` (Android 14) and an iPhone 17 simulator (iOS 26.5). Before these rules, it failed on Android when the second back-arrow tap landed on the page being popped, and when `take_screenshot` ran a second time. Host tools give every step's picture, on both platforms, without changing how Flutter draws. |
| D-223 | **How the UI flows set the scene** (adds to D-171; #108, P1-30). In `tests/ui/`:<br>• `developer.py` drives Developer options as a person would: `open_developer()` goes More › About this app › the version row 7 times › Developer options, skipping the taps once unlocked, always through About; `apply_options()` types the fake clock, then the feed URL, and taps Apply; `reopen_developer()` leaves and reopens the screen; `refresh_now()` taps Refresh now and waits for its words; `fresh_start(now=, feed_url=)` does it all from the launch and returns to Now.<br>• `fresh_start()` first taps Refresh now only to let the launch's own refresh from the year's feed end: a Refresh now joins a running refresh, which keeps the URL it started with (`Conference.set_feed_url`). With a feed URL, it then reopens Developer (no outcome line, the keyboard gone) and waits for "Schedule updated from the feed."<br>• F2–F5 run on the recorded feed (`feed_url`) at 09:00 the day before the conference (`before_conference()`), where the Schedule opens on its first day at the top. F1 sets a feed that takes each request and hangs up unanswered (`silent_feed`) and checks the bundled snapshot's rows; it holds the strip's Refresh until the flow lets it hang up, so the flow sees that refresh run (its request counted, "Updating the schedule…") and end, not only the "Offline" line that was there before the tap; a networked device may already show the year's feed, whose sessions are the same. A cold start offline needs a relaunch and stays on the device checklist (D-79).<br>• What a flow looks for comes from `targets.py`, the feed processed by the app's own code, a day's rows filtered as a fresh install filters them (open spaces hidden, D-20); no id is written out.<br>• Waits are on identifiers and texts with deadlines. A tab switch waits for one bar and taps until the tab's title shows (`open_tab()`): both roots' bars show while one replaces the other, and the Android emulator lost a More tap while Now re-rendered. A tap that an animation can swallow, on a control that acts once (a SnackBar's Undo), is repeated until its effect shows (`tap_until()`), never after a fixed sleep. A timeout's picture is `timed-out-<flow>.png`.<br>• A flow cannot scroll or close the keyboard (`RemoteTester` has no drag and no keyboard action, U35, #288).
