So: the iOS flows run on an **iPhone 17 Pro Max** simulator (the iPhone 17 family, D-102), whose More root shows About this app, which the iPhone 17's hides under the bar; the Android AVD keeps its on-screen keyboard down (`show_ime_with_hard_keyboard 0`; its hardware keyboard types); the iOS software keyboard stays, so Developer's feed URL field keeps Apply and its outcome in sight above it (`APPLY_IN_SIGHT`, 304 dp of `scroll_padding`, a gain for people too: type, then Apply, with the keyboard up; sized for large text, since Flet does not give the app its text scale, after 168 dp left the outcome under the keyboard at 200 % on Android and Apply at iOS's largest size), and search's results are checked one scope at a time, near the top. Back to the iPhone 17 when the pin brings U35. | The five flows passed on `flet_probe_api34` (Android 14) and an iPhone 17 Pro Max simulator (iOS 26.5). Before these rules: on the iPhone 17 the run timed out on `more.app`, under the bar; the keyboard hid Apply on both platforms and search's speaker row on Android; a My schedule tap landed on the leaving root's bar on iOS. A Refresh now that may join the launch's refresh, or tell its own words from the last ones, would make a flow pass or fail by timing. |

## J. Build, CI and distribution

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-81 | *(Branch amended by D-104.)* Flet version: until 1.1.0 is on PyPI, develop on Flet 1.1 from flet-dev/flet ~~`main`~~ `flet-1.1.0` (D-104) at a pinned commit; then pin the exact release. Bumps are their own PRs. One pin serves development, CI and `flet build`. The store companion app (`flet run --android/--ios`) is built from a released client and lacks 1.1's Dart changes, so until a 1.1 companion is in the stores the on-device dev loop uses `flet debug android` or `flet debug ios` from the source checkout (a packaged debug build, so embedded) or a companion client built from `client/` at the pinned commit (not embedded, like the store companion; D-63). S5 proves the mechanics, including that the companion client and the Python protocol match, and `docs/development.md` records them. | The owner's Xcode 27 cannot build iOS with Flet 1.0.4 (F20). We use 1.1 features (`ThemeSurfaces`, `material_ui` for new extensions), and flet-cli run from a source checkout builds with the matching template and Dart package (F18). |
| D-82 | Python 3.14 (`requires-python = ">=3.14,<3.15"`), managed with uv, with `uv.lock` committed. The project has a `[build-system]` (`uv_build`), so `uv sync` installs `pycon_us` editable from `src/` and the tests, tools and mock import it (D-52's shared signing); `flet build` still copies `src/` and bundles only `[project].dependencies` (F15). | Flet embeds Python 3.14 by default (F19), so development matches the device. |
| D-83 | Versioning: SemVer `0.y.z` until the showcase `1.0.0`. `pycon_us.__version__` must equal `[project].version` (unit test). The build number is the commit count on `main`, passed as `--build-number`. We tag `vX.Y.Z` when a build goes to testers. | `importlib.metadata` cannot see the app in packaged builds (F15). |
| D-84 | *(While the repo is private, every workflow runs only by hand and the weekly feed check is dropped: amended by D-273.)* CI has three workflows:<br>• `ci.yml` on every PR and push to `main` (Linux): `uv sync --locked`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `pyright`, `pytest`; target under 2 min.<br>• `feed-check.yml` weekly: our parser and sanity checks on the public feed.<br>• `build-android.yml` on manual dispatch and `v*` tags: uploads a debug-signed APK.<br>iOS builds run locally. | Fast and enough for one or two people. macOS minutes cost 10× on private repos. |
| D-85 | **prov. O-5.** Distribution is internal only: Android by APK sideload and/or Play internal testing on the owner's account, iOS by TestFlight internal testing on the owner's team. No public store listing. | "PyCon" is a PSF trademark, so a public listing would need PSF permission. |
| D-86 | Signing keys, certificates and API keys never enter the repo or CI. `docs/development.md` says where they live and how to build signed artifacts locally. | The repo will become public. |
| D-87 | Licence: Apache-2.0, the same as the Ionic app. A `NOTICE` credits PyCon/pycon-us-mobile for ported logic and reused assets (e.g. floor plans). No CLA or DCO: GitHub's terms make contributions inbound = outbound. | Compatible with our source, and the simplest option. |
| D-176 | **A pub cache per worktree while other builds may run** (#176; supports D-102's parallel agents). serious_python 5.0.0 stages each build's Python app inside its own plugin folder in the pub cache, which every Flutter project on the Mac shares (iOS: `serious_python_darwin`'s `dist_ios/app` and its Swift package's `Resources/app`, bundled by Xcode minutes later; Android: `serious_python_android`'s `src/main/assets/app.zip`, merged later in the same Gradle run). So every `flet build`, `flet debug` and `flet test` that may overlap another build runs with `PUB_CACHE=$PWD/build/pub-cache`, an APFS clone of `~/.pub-cache` made once per worktree (`cp -cR`, only when `build/pub-cache` doesn't exist yet, since `cp -R` nests a second copy in an existing folder), and each build's Python is checked against `build/python-app` (the iOS bundle's `app`, the `app.zip` asset of an APK or AAB, the IPA's app bundle; development.md §5.8). It goes when U28 (#185, flet-dev/serious-python#255) is in the pin. | On 2026-10-12 three parallel iOS builds (#79, #80, #84) shipped another worktree's Python with no error or warning; one crashed on the other worktree's `conference.toml`. Each rebuild with a worktree-local `PUB_CACHE` bundled its own `src/`, on iOS and on Android (`assets/app.zip`). The clone took 6 s for the 1.0 GB cache and used no space beyond metadata, where an empty private cache cost a 9-10 minute first build; a check alone finds the problem only after the build. |
| D-219 | **A Flet checkout per worktree** (#208; supports D-102's parallel agents, alongside D-176). Every worktree of this repo other than the main checkout has its own `.flet-src`: an APFS clone (`cp -cR`) of the main checkout's `.flet-src`, without the clone's `sdk/python/packages/flet/build` folder, followed by `uv sync --locked`; never a link to it (development.md §5.8). The main checkout's `.flet-src` stays the read-only source of truth at the pin: nobody edits it or a clone, a clone takes no git command that writes (from the owner's `git worktree` setup its `.git` file names the same Flet worktree), a pin bump moves the main checkout and every worktree replaces its clone, and a clone's pin check also compares its files (`git --no-optional-locks diff --quiet HEAD`). It goes with the source pin, when 1.1.0 is on PyPI (D-81). | `flet build` pip-installs `flet` from `.flet-src/sdk/python/packages/flet` (`[tool.flet.dev_packages]`), and pip builds a local folder in place, so linked worktrees shared setuptools' `build/` there: overlapping builds failed with `[Errno 2]`, `[Errno 66]` and `[Errno 17]` (#94, #197, #245), and an interrupted build's leftover failed every later build. Four parallel `pip wheel` runs in one folder failed 12 of 20 times with those three errors; in four clones, 0 of 20. With clones, two worktrees' `flet build ios-simulator` started in the same second built their wheels in step and both passed, each bundle holding its own app; an APK beside an iOS build passed too. A leftover `build/lib` file also ships in the wheel. A link also pointed `uv sync`'s editable installs (so `uv run flet`) at the main checkout. The clone took 2 s for 178 MB and needs no prefix on each command; building out of tree would need a setuptools config through `DIST_EXTRA_CONFIG` on every command (pip has no out-of-tree option: 26.2 rejects `--use-deprecated=out-of-tree-build`), and a forgotten prefix races again. |

## K. Process and GitHub workflow

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-88 | One private GitHub repo, made public when the owner is satisfied (P4). Issues and PRs are the record of work. `main` stays green. Protect it with rulesets if the plan supports them for private repos; otherwise by convention until the repo is public. | GitHub Free does not enforce branch protection on private repos. |
| D-89 | Milestones are the phases: `P0 Foundations`, `P1 Attendee core`, `P2 Conference companion`, `P3 Accounts & staff tools`, `P4 Showcase`. Each milestone description carries the phase goal and exit criteria from PLAN.md. One pinned status issue per phase is updated weekly: done, next, blocked, decisions, numbers. | Progress is visible in one place. |
| D-90 | Labels form a fixed set:<br>• Type: `feature`, `bug`, `design`, `docs`, `test`, `chore`, `ci`, `refactor`, `epic`, `upstream` (a Flet gap written up here for the owner to take upstream, D-212).<br>• Area: `area:shell`, `area:theme`, `area:data`, `area:schedule`, `area:speakers`, `area:rooms-maps`, `area:sponsors`, `area:info`, `area:notifications`, `area:account`, `area:staff`, `area:build`, `area:quality`.<br>• Status: `blocked`, `needs-decision`, `good first issue`.<br>There are no phase or priority labels. | A small, stable vocabulary; milestones carry the phase. |
| D-91 | Every change starts from an issue, except trivial chores. An issue describes a user journey or a mechanism (not one feature id) and lists the feature ids, D-ids, acceptance criteria and the device evidence needed. An epic (`epic`) groups sub-issues. Spikes are issues titled `Spike: <question>`, labelled `test` and time-boxed; they close with a verdict in `docs/spikes/`. | Traceable without 263 tiny tickets. |
| D-92 | Pull requests:<br>• one issue per PR (`Closes #N`), on a short-lived branch `<type>/<issue>-<slug>`;<br>• open as a draft early, and aim for about 400 changed lines or fewer, excluding fixtures;<br>• the body says what and why; how it was tested (commands, devices, Flet pin); screenshots for UI changes (Android and iOS, light and dark); docs and decisions updated; follow-ups;<br>• dependency and Flet bumps are separate PRs.<br>~~The owner reviews and squash-merges; agents never merge their own PRs.~~ Merging: superseded by D-100. | Reviewable, traceable and easy to revert. |
| D-93 | Definition of done:<br>• acceptance criteria met;<br>• tests and linters green;<br>• UI checked on Android and iOS, with screenshots;<br>• accessibility checked (labels, contrast, 200 % text, touch targets);<br>• docs and this record updated in the same PR;<br>• follow-ups filed. | One bar for humans and agents. |
| D-94 | A GitHub Project, "PyCon US Flet", managed with `gh project`, gives a board view of issues and PRs (Todo, In progress, In review, Done). The issues stay the source of truth. | A glance view; optional. |
| D-95 | *(Trigger and process amended by D-100.)* For architecture changes and large PRs, a second opinion from another model (Claude Fable or Codex, run read-only) is summarised in a PR comment: what it flagged and what changed. | The owner asked for second opinions; summaries rather than transcripts. |
| D-96 | The documentation set:<br>• `PLAN.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`.<br>• `docs/`: `decisions.md`, `ux.md`, `architecture.md`, `feature-matrix.md`, `flet-gaps-and-extensions.md`, `development.md`, `testing-and-quality.md`, `roadmap.md` and `spikes/`. `development.md` covers the toolchain, dev loop, mock server, builds, CI, signing and releases, and replaces v1's build-and-release doc. `testing-and-quality.md` is the test strategy (D-76..D-80). `roadmap.md` breaks the phases into epics and issues and replaces v1's backlog; GitHub issues are the live task list.<br>• `docs/research/` stays as frozen input.<br>• The v1 docs are not kept in the tree: they stay in git history (the repository's first commit).<br>Docs change in the same PR as the behaviour they describe. | Fewer docs, kept current, with one place per topic. |
| D-100 | *(While the repo is private, condition 1's checks are the merge gate's: amended by D-273.)* **Agents merge their own PRs** (supersedes the merging clause of D-92 and amends D-95; owner's direction, 2026-10-11). An agent squash-merges (`gh pr merge --squash --delete-branch`) only when all hold:<br>1. checks pass on the PR's latest commit (the required checks are the `ci.yml` jobs listed in `docs/development.md`; missing, pending, skipped or cancelled checks don't count; before CI exists, the applicable local checks are run and listed in the PR);<br>2. the definition of done (D-93) is met and the agent has re-read the final diff;<br>3. a second opinion, when required, is done: required for PRs over 400 changed lines (excluding lockfiles, fixtures, generated files and data snapshots), architecture changes (module structure, state, routing, data core, signing, outbox, build or CI) and edits to this record. The reviewer is a different model from the author (Claude Fable, or Codex read-only) and reviews the final diff. Each finding is fixed or rejected with a recorded reason and evidence; a material disagreement (correctness, data loss, security) goes to the other reviewer model, a product question to `needs-decision`;<br>4. an owner request to stop or change something has been acted on, and every owner comment has a reply;<br>5. no unsettled `needs-decision` question affects the PR.<br>The owner watches, may revert at any time, and is never a blocker. Feedback after a merge becomes a new issue or a revert PR. | The project is driven by agents; waiting for a human would stall it, while these checks keep `main` green and every change traceable and reversible. |
| D-273 | **While the repo is private, local checks replace GitHub Actions: a pre-push hook runs the fast checks, and the merge gate runs the full checks on the PRs merged onto the latest `main`** (owner's direction, 2026-10-20; amends D-84, D-100 condition 1, D-103 and the feed check's schedule).<br>• **The checks.** `tools/check.sh` runs `ci.yml`'s steps in their order: `uv sync --locked`, `ruff check`, `ruff format --check`, `pyright`, `pytest --hypothesis-profile=ci`; `--fast` runs every step but pytest.<br>• **Three layers.** (1) Before every push, the pre-push hook (`.githooks/pre-push`, turned on once per clone with `git config core.hooksPath .githooks`) runs `tools/check.sh --fast`; a push that changes only Markdown skips it. (2) Before marking a PR ready, its author runs the full `tools/check.sh` and says so in the PR. (3) Before every merge, the gate `tools/gate.sh <pr> [<pr>...]` tests what will land: the latest `main` with the PR merged onto it, or a batch of PRs merged onto it in their merge order, in a scratch worktree. It runs the full checks there and, when they pass, posts on each PR the commits and the tree it checked. Its result holds for the PR's head and the `main` it names: after a new push to the PR, or once `main` has moved, it runs again. After a batch is merged, `main`'s tree must equal the gated tree; otherwise the full checks run again on `main`.<br>• **GitHub Actions run only by hand.** `ci.yml`, `feed-check.yml` and `build-android.yml` keep their jobs, and `workflow_dispatch` is their only trigger (`gh workflow run <file> --ref <branch>`). The weekly feed check is dropped: run `uv run python -m tools.check_feed` locally when needed. The quick-look APK is built locally (development.md §8.1) or by a manual run; a `v*` tag no longer builds it.<br>• **Copilot reviews are paused**: they use Actions minutes too, and their quota is used up.<br>• **No Linux VM until P4** (the owner's call). Until then the checks run only on macOS, and a failure that only Linux shows would surface when CI comes back. Spike S9 (#363, D-78), which needs GitHub's Linux runners, waits until then too.<br>• **When the repo goes public (P4)**, Actions are free for it: the PR that makes it public brings back `ci.yml`'s PR and `main` triggers (and with them D-100's `ci.yml` checks), the feed check's weekly schedule, `build-android.yml`'s `v*` tags and Copilot reviews. | By 2026-10-20 the month had used about 1,875 of the 2,000 Linux minutes that a private repo on GitHub Free includes, almost all of them `ci.yml` runs on every PR push and every push to `main`. On the owner's Mac the fast checks take about 5 s and the full ones about 1.5 minutes (3,061 tests). The gate checks the tree that lands, where a `pull_request` run checked the PR's merge with `main` as it was at the push, so `main` stays green (D-88) without a runner. |
| D-101 | *(Pre-authorised work narrowed to this repo by D-212.)* **`needs-decision` without blocking.** The issue states the options and a recommendation. **Reversible** choices (most code, UX and process details) are settled by the agent: the question is settled once the decision is posted on the issue; it is then recorded as a new D-number in a PR, noted in the phase status issue, and the label is removed; the owner can override later. **Owner-only** choices wait for the owner: app identity and bundle id (O-4); making anything public (the repo, a store listing, builds for people outside the team); accounts or anything that costs money; contacting people outside this work (the PSF, social media, blogs); anything that would lose data or need someone else's help to undo; and the open decisions marked owner-only. Routine work is pre-authorised: issues, PRs and comments in this repo, ~~and the flet-dev issues and PRs the upstream track (D-97) needs~~ and nowhere else (D-212). | Keeps work moving while the owner keeps control of what can't easily be undone. |

Absolute Android numbers wait for a borrowed mid-range Android phone, a P4 risk (PLAN §11). The targets themselves do not change. | These are the devices we have. Emulators and simulators are reproducible, cover most behaviour and can be driven by agents; the iPhone is kept for what they cannot show. An emulator's timings depend on the host machine, so they reveal regressions, not real-device speed. |

| D-112 | **Android regression baseline and how to measure it** (records for D-80 and D-102; S1). Method:<br>• `am start -W`'s `TotalTime` ends at Flutter's first frame, which is Flet's boot screen. So the app logs a line once the cached content has rendered: an `await` on a SharedPreferences call made after the render. That reply does not wait for a frame, so the line is a proxy; screen recordings in S1 showed it 0.2-0.3 s **after** the pixels, never before. Numbers held against D-80 come from screen recordings (`testing-and-quality.md` §8).<br>• Cold start = that line's timestamp minus logcat's `ActivityTaskManager: START u0` line, after `am force-stop`; ten runs on a quiet host, median and range.<br>Baseline on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` (M4 host, Flet `15854a0ac`, release arm64 APK, `.pyc` on):<br>• cold start to cached content 760 ms (741-919);<br>• `TotalTime` 426 ms (400-507);<br>• Python start → first frame 220 ms (202-277);<br>• first launch after a fresh install: `TotalTime` 441 ms, first frame 770 ms.<br>The iPhone's release numbers (#121) are held against D-80's targets; the targets stay until then. | A busy host moved the medians by about 25 % (round a in the S1 verdict), more than the 20 % regression threshold in `testing-and-quality.md` §8, so the method demands a quiet host and repeated runs. The content line measures what users wait for; `TotalTime` misses about 330 ms of it. |
| D-159 | **Interaction latency: how it is measured, and the emulator baseline** (records for D-80 and D-102; S2). Method:<br>• in the app, each handler logs its start, an effect logs when the re-render has been sent ("handler → patch", all of Python's work), then a SharedPreferences round trip (D-112's proxy);<br>• on screen, a recording: a keystroke from the frame that shows the typed character to the list's change; a day switch from the tab bar's first change; a star tap against its log line, the video's clock mapped onto the device clock by one keystroke at the start of the recording (an instant tap draws nothing before the star changes);<br>• frames from `dumpsys SurfaceFlinger --latency` for the app's `SurfaceView`: every interval within 1.5 s of the gesture's first frame, janky over 25 ms;<br>• `docs/spikes/02-day-list/drive_android.py` runs all of it.<br>Baseline on `pycon_pixel6a_api36` (M4 host, Flet `15854a0ac`, release arm64 APK), the busiest day with D-153's memoised path: median handler → patch 3 ms for 12 star taps and for 12 keystrokes (single keystrokes that bring filtered rows back reach 17-33 ms), measured on lazy lists (`tree/lazy/all`; Python's share does not depend on the list, and `tree/cache/all` read the same), the star in the same frame as a local change; on a fully built list, as today's is (D-154), a filter keystroke's list change shows 0-83 ms after the key against 0-16 ms lazy (`tree/cache/all`), so on-screen numbers for today's list are compared with that variant; day switch 2 ms of Python; flings 0-4 of about 900 frames over 25 ms; cold start to the cached content 886 ms (856-958) with D-153..D-156 (`tree/today/deferred`, D-112's method; windows differ by about ±70 ms, D-156); `feed.process` 66 ms. A **median** handler → patch over one frame (16 ms) for the star taps or the keystrokes on the busiest day is a regression to file. The iPhone's release numbers (#121) are held against D-80's targets. | The emulator is a relative signal (D-102), and Python's own work is what the app controls; the method needs no Flutter changes. During S2 the emulator twice ran 3-4.4× slower while the host's user was away, its threads at background priority, so `testing-and-quality.md` §8 checks the processing time against the last run before measuring. |

| D-114 | **HTML → Markdown converter scope** (records D-44's details and corrects architecture §7.7; S3, 2026-10-12). `core/html_md.py` parses a value with `html.parser` into a small tree and renders it:<br>• **Tags** (the 2025 and 2026 census): `p`; `br` → hard break `"  \n"`; `hr` → `---`; `h1`..`h6` → `#`..`######`; `ul`, `ol`, `li` → `- ` / `N. `, with continuation lines indented by the marker's width (three spaces under `1. `, not two); `blockquote` → `> `; `strong`/`b`, `em`/`i`, `s`/`del`/`strike`, `code` → `**x**`, `*x*`, `~~x~~`, a code span; `a` → `[text](url)`. Edge whitespace stays outside emphasis and link markup. A `br` inside a heading becomes a space (Markdown headings are one line).<br>• **Emphasis that cannot flank:** when CommonMark's flanking rules would leave a marker literal (emphasis that starts or ends with punctuation and touches a letter outside, e.g. `a<em>(x)</em>b`), the markers are dropped and the text shows unemphasised. Literal punctuation flanks the same way as a character reference, so this is a Markdown limit, not an escaping one; the 2025 and 2026 feeds have no such case.<br>• **Any other tag is unwrapped** (its children take its place, so its text stays where it was); `script` and `style` are dropped with their text.<br>• **Whitespace** follows HTML: runs of whitespace, newlines included, collapse to one space; only `br` breaks a line.<br>• **A value with no tag is plain text** (15 of the 24 2026 sprint descriptions): entities are decoded, blank lines separate paragraphs, a single newline is a hard break.<br>• **Links:** the `href` is joined to the year's site URL (`urljoin`); only `http`, `https` and `mailto` become links (anything else stays text); the destination is percent-encoded (spaces, brackets, parentheses). A bare `http(s)` URL in text becomes an explicit link labelled with the URL, without trailing sentence punctuation (`.,;:!?` and closing quotes or brackets); inside a link's text, URLs stay text. `target`, `rel`, `class` and `style` are ignored.<br>• Slot names are not converted: the name rules (DATA-08) also strip tags.<br>P0-15's tests take the 22 feed samples of `docs/spikes/03-html-markdown/census.py` (selected from the feed fixtures by year, field and a unique substring) plus its synthetic line as inputs, and an invariant over both fixtures checks that no character is lost or added (whitespace aside). That check is static: a marker the renderer would show literally is caught only by the named tests and the device check. | S3 converted all 1,389 values of the 19 HTML fields without losing or adding a character and rendered 24 samples correctly on the Android 16 emulator. The census has 17 tags; only `span` (4 uses, a font-size style) is unwrapped. Collapsing newlines matches us.pycon.org and the Ionic app and avoids ragged hard-wrapped prose; plain-text paragraphs were lost in Ionic. |
| D-115 | **Converted text is escaped with numeric character references** (S3). In text (never in code spans), `\`, `` ` ``, `*`, `_`, `[`, `]`, `(`, `<`, `>`, `\|`, `~`, `$`, `&` and `:` become `&#NN;`, and so does a block marker at the start of a line (`#`, `>`, `-`, `+`, `=`, or the `.`/`)` after digits). Code spans are emitted raw, fenced with one backtick more than the longest run inside. Bare URLs become explicit links (D-114), so no text is left raw for GFM's autolinks. Backslash escapes are not used. | Flet's `Markdown` always adds LaTeX syntax (F27): on the emulator `5$ or 10$`, `( the notes )` and `[ the FAQ ]` rendered as math, `~y~` as strike-through and `:smile:` as an emoji; with references every one rendered literally. Backslash escapes collide with the `\(`/`\[` LaTeX openers. The 2026 job listings already carry `$` amounts. |

Markdown links stay inline (no `LinkTile` list): on Android each is its own focusable, clickable node named by its text; #91 checks iOS and #109 checks what TalkBack and VoiceOver call them, and a `LinkTile` list comes back only if either fails. | Flet's default sheet colours links `Colors.blue` with no underline (about 3.1:1 on the light surface, and colour as the only cue, against D-33) and starts headings at `headlineSmall`. | D-117 | **E5 is cut; U21 and U22 are later candidates** (amends D-98; S3). The HTML control (E5) is not needed: the D-114 converter covers both feeds. U21: make `ft.Markdown`'s LaTeX syntax opt-in (an extension flag), so Markdown text and open-space descriptions need no LaTeX escaping. U22: let a partial `MarkdownStyleSheet` merge with the theme's defaults and derive link and block-quote colours from the colour scheme. Until they land, D-115 and D-116 are the interims; removing them is its own PR after a pin bump (D-64). | Two years of feeds use no tag the converter cannot express (no tables, images or preformatted blocks). Both upstream items are small changes to `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/markdown.dart` and `utils/markdown.dart` that help every Flet app. |
| D-152 | **The Markdown style sheet names fonts and decoration colours** (amends D-116; #71). `MARKDOWN_STYLE` in `theme.py` is one constant for every scheme: its colours are roles (`PRIMARY`, `ON_SURFACE`, `SURFACE_CONTAINER_HIGHEST`, `OUTLINE`), which Flet resolves against the theme on screen. Besides its colour (F28), every text style it gives names `font_family="packages/roboto_fonts/Roboto"`, except code spans, which use `"monospace"` with `font_family_fallback=["Menlo"]`; links and strike-through name `decoration_color` equal to their text colour. h5, h6 and the other styles keep Flet's theme-derived defaults. `tests/test_theme.py` checks all three rules. | On the iOS 26.5 simulator, the S3 sheet rendered Markdown text in SF Pro, because a style given in the sheet does not inherit the theme's font (Android hid this: its system font is Roboto), and `"monospace"` is no iOS family, so code spans were proportional; with the families named, both platforms showed Roboto and a monospace code font (Menlo on iOS). On the dark surface the link underline and the strike-through were drawn in `#1D1B20` (about 1.1:1) until their colour was named. |
| D-212 | **Agents act only in this repository** (the owner's direction, 2026-10-13; amends D-97 and D-101). Agents, and every agent they start, create and edit issues, PRs, comments, reviews, reactions or anything else only in `ndonkoHenri/pycon-us-flet`. They never open or comment on an issue, nor review or send a PR, nor make any other change, in `flet-dev/flet`, `flet-dev/serious-python`, an extension's repository or any other repository. Reading flet-dev (the source at the pin, its issues and PRs) stays allowed. An upstream gap is an `upstream` issue here with the full analysis (what happens, the Flet source at the pin with files and lines, a minimal repro, the suggested fix, the interim); the owner, who maintains Flet, decides whether and how to take it to Flet, and the issue links the flet-dev issue or PR once the owner has opened one. Every brief an agent gives another agent (a subagent, a reviewer, Codex) repeats this rule. | Before this rule, agents opened flet-dev/flet#6963, flet-dev/serious-python#255 and flet-dev/flet#6964 from the owner's account, relying on D-101's former "flet-dev issues the upstream track needs"; the owner had meant from the start that the project manages one repository. Those three stay as the owner decides. |
| D-213 | **What an `upstream` issue may hold** (the owner's decision on #267, 2026-10-13; completes D-212). By default the analysis: what happens, the Flet source at the pin with files and lines, a minimal repro, the suggested fix in words or a short snippet, and the interim. When a fix is small and its diff makes the analysis clearer, the issue may also hold a draft diff in a fenced block. Nothing for Flet lives in the app's source tree, nothing is applied to `.flet-src`, and nothing leaves this repository; any code for Flet beyond that is the owner's. | The owner takes upstream work to Flet; a short diff in the issue saves them time without putting Flet code in the app or anywhere outside this repo. |

## L. Upstream Flet work

| # | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| D-97 | *(Filing amended by D-212.)* Upstream work is a parallel track. Each item is an `upstream` issue here ~~that links the flet-dev issue or PR~~ with the full analysis, linking the flet-dev issue or PR once the owner has opened one (D-212). App issues that wait on it are `blocked`, and the documented interim ships meanwhile. | The owner contributes the missing pieces to Flet; the app never forks Flet or its build template. |
| D-98 | *(E5, U21 and U22 amended by D-117; U23 and U24 added by D-149; U25 added by D-161.)* Upstream priorities:<br>1. E3, scheduled local notifications in the official flet-dev/flet#6948 extension (after S6).<br>2. U1, `RefreshIndicator`.<br>3. U15, non-fatal handler and effect errors with a Python error hook.<br>4. E1, a barcode-scanner extension (after S8, before P3).<br>5. U4, `page.get_app_info()`.<br>6. The small ones: U16 `PageMediaData.text_scale_factor`, U17 an accessibility-features change event, U10 `AppBar.bottom`, U2 sticky headers, U3 an absolute `InteractiveViewer` transform, U9 `use_route_search`, U18 Router Back inside a modal sub-chain (pop to the previous view entry unless the popped view is the modal itself; D-19), U25 a typed `PlatformBrightnessChangeEvent` and the boot screen kept until `main()`'s first patch (F35, F36; D-161).<br>Later or low: E4 native calendar, ~~E5 HTML,~~ E2 Firebase; U19 pinning platform adaptations (a `Theme.platform` or scroll-behaviour option; D-10), U20 several weighted files per family in `page.fonts` (D-26), U21 LaTeX syntax opt-in in `ft.Markdown` (F27), U22 a Markdown style sheet that merges with the theme (F28); U6, U7, U12 and U13 only when a feature or a spike needs them. Cut: E5 HTML (D-117), E6 slidable, U5, U8, U11, U14. | Attendee value first. Small core items remove interims for every Flet app. |
| D-99 | New extensions are written clean-room (never reading GPL code such as `AyberkA0/flet-barcode-scanner`), import Material and Cupertino from `material_ui`/`cupertino_ui`, and live where the Flet team decides (likely next to the existing `flet-*` packages in the monorepo). | Licence safety, and Flet 1.1 moved Material and Cupertino to those packages. |
| D-149 | **U23 and U24 are later candidates** (amends D-98; S4). U23: a shell that keeps one `NavigationBar` outside the `Navigator` while `manage_views` Views push and pop (go_router's `ShellRoute`), which would let the bar return to pushed Views (D-144). U24: closing a replaced session unmounts its component tree, so effect cleanups run and effect tasks are cancelled, and nothing renders after `detach` (F30), which would retire D-147's `page.on_close`. | Both are small, generic Router and session changes that every Flet app with tabs or long-running effects needs; until then D-144 and D-147 are the interims. |

## M. Naming (binding)

- **Project, package, entry point.** The project is `pycon-us`, the import package `pycon_us`. The entry point is `src/main.py`, which renders `App` with `page.render_views`. `__version__` lives in `pycon_us/__init__.py`.
- **Top-level modules** in `pycon_us/`:
  - `app.py`: `App`, providers, boot gate.
  - `routes.py`: `ROUTES`, `Drill`, ~~`drill_href()`, `parent_of()`, `use_query()`~~ `Start`, `NotFound` (D-164).
  - `locations.py`: `drill_href()`, `parent_of()`, `start_target()`, `query_of()`, `is_top_view()`, `use_query()` and the route constants (D-164).
  - `state.py`: ~~stores and `use_*` hooks~~ `Conference`, `ConferenceCtx`, `use_conference()` ~~and `ViewMemory`~~ (D-160; D-231).
  - `memory.py`: `ViewMemory`, the plain process memory (D-143; split from `state.py` at D-66's ~400 lines in #296, D-231).
  - `feed_refresh.py`: the feed refresh without its store: `Source`, `Problem` and `Status`, the stale limits `STALE_ON_CONFERENCE_DAYS` and `STALE_OTHERWISE`, `REFRESH_EVERY`, and `fetch()`, `feed_digest()` and `log_unexpected()` (D-231; split from `state.py` in #296).
  - `prefs.py`: `Prefs`, `PrefsCtx`, `use_prefs()` (D-160); P1 adds `favorites.py`, P3 `account.py` and `staff.py`, one store each with its context and hook.
  - `config.py`: `YEAR` and the year-data loader, `load_year()`, with the conference facts it builds (`ConferenceInfo`, `Content`, `YearData`; D-228).
  - `year_schema.py`: the loader's schema half, `read_table()`, `build()` and `YearDataError`, which `config.py` imports (#102); `build()` takes a table of its own as its dataclass (D-205).
  - `year_pages.py`: the Open spaces and Lightning talks pages' year data, `OpenSpacesInfo`, `Doubt`, `LightningTalksInfo`, and their checks, `check_pages()`, with `check_https()` and `check_text()`, which `config.py` imports (D-205; split from `config.py` at D-66's ~400 lines in #248).
  - `year_keynotes.py`: the keynotes' year data, `Keynote` and `KeynoteSpeaker`, their checks, `check_keynotes()`, `check_keynote_sessions()` and `check_snapshot_sessions()`, and `keynote_of()`, a session's entry (D-217).
  - `year_tracks.py`: the track registry, `Track`, `TrackFormat` and `TRACK_GROUPS`, the Tracks hub's pages of their own, `HUB_PAGES` (D-207), the keynotes' tracks, `KEYNOTE_TRACKS` (D-217), and the registry's checks, `check_tracks()`, which `config.py` imports (D-228; split from `config.py` at D-66's ~400 lines in #275).
  - `theme.py`: `build_theme(*, dark, high_contrast)`, `overlay_style()`, `Tokens`, `BadgeColors` (D-174), `build_tokens(*, dark, high_contrast)`, `TokensCtx`, `use_tokens()` (D-150), `MARKDOWN_STYLE` (D-116).
  - `capabilities.py` and `clock.py`.
- **Packages** in `pycon_us/`:
  - `core/`: `feed.py` (`process()`, the pipeline), `records.py` (decoding), `rules.py` (Ionic's processing rules), `names.py` (title cleaning), `models.py`, `queries.py` (`days()`, `fold()`, `speaker_order()`), `timeline.py` (the schedule queries, D-185; `keynote_sessions()`, the Keynotes page's sessions, D-217; `posters()`, a Posters row's posters, D-206), `agenda.py` (Now & Next and My schedule, D-185), `search.py` (D-180; `first_results()`, a result list's first rows, D-218), `speakers.py` (`SpeakerEntry`, `LetterGroup`, `directory()`, `letter_groups()`, `filter_directory()`; D-189; `speaker_sessions()`, `has_bio()`, D-203), `sprints.py` (`projects()`, `filter_projects()`, `cut_short()`; D-202), `row_views.py` (`SessionView`, `session_view()`, `session_views()`, `by_day()`; D-184; `by_slot()`, D-205; `Places`, `places()`; D-186; `people()`; D-197; the rows' phrases `spoken_phrase()`, `notice_phrase()`, `elapsed_phrase()`; D-200; `share_text()`, what session detail's Share sends; D-206), `rooms.py` (`RoomEntry`, `RoomList`, `room_list()`, `session_count()`; D-188; `room_now()`, D-194), `timefmt.py`, `slugs.py`, `html_md.py` (`to_markdown()`, `to_text()`; its inline layer is `markdown_inline.py`, which only it imports; D-201), `expo.py` (each booth with every exhibitor of its number, or its `map.toml` label without one, and the sponsors with no rectangle; the job fair tables in natural order with each sponsor's role count; D-254), `diff.py`, `ics.py`, `signing.py`, `badges.py`, `admission.py`, `outbox.py`.

Staff screens are `staff_<tool>.py`.
- **Shared components** (`ui/`, grouped by role; split a module at about 400 lines, D-66):
  - `scaffold.py`: `AppScaffold`, `SectionHeader` and `search_button()` (D-165).
  - `status_strip.py`: `StatusStrip` with `strip_line()` (D-165), and the minute tick: the hook `use_minute()` (D-188), `TICK_SECONDS`, `current_minute()` and `stop_ticks()` (D-175; D-200).
  - `navigation.py`: `TABS`, `navigation_bar()`, `use_scroll_to_top()` (D-166); P2's tablet rail joins it.
  - `rows.py`: `SpeakerRow`, `SpeakerTile`, `speaker_avatar()` (D-197; a keynote's people too, D-217), `LinkTile`, `LocationCard` and its plan-only form `job_fair_card()` (D-254).
  - `session_rows.py`: `SessionRow`, `NoticeRow`, with `use_session_views()` and `is_notice()`; their views and phrases are `core/row_views.py`'s (`SessionView`, D-184; `spoken_phrase()`, `notice_phrase()`, `elapsed_phrase()`, D-190, D-200), their lines `row_lines.py`'s (D-227).
  - `row_lines.py`: the lines a session row is made of, plain builders with no hooks: `meta()` (the time and place), `secondary()` (a supporting text), `overlap_line()` (D-191) and `elapsed_bar()` with its `TRACK` (D-190; D-227).
  - `time_gutter.py`: `TimeGutter` (D-158) and `TimeSlot` (D-191), a slot's time column beside its rows (D-200); at large text `TimeAbove`, through `slot_layout()`, `time_above()`, `MAX_GUTTER_SHARE` and the `GutterProbe` that `AppScaffold` and `BootView` hold (D-216).
  - `links.py`: the link rule (`LinkKind`, `link_kind()`, `open_link()`, `copy_contact()`; D-182), for `LinkTile` and `Markdown`'s `on_tap_link`; feed texts' `feed_link()`, `open_feed_link()` and `feed_markdown()` (D-197); `external()`, a button that opens a web page, as a named link (D-205).

  - `states.py`: `EmptyState`, `SkeletonList` (D-182), `NO_DATA_BODY` (D-188) and `NoDataState` (D-191).
  - `when_where.py`: `WhenAndWhere`, `StatusLine`, `room_href()` (D-186), session detail's "When and where" card.
  - `fold.py`: `FoldTile`, `expand_style()` and `EXPAND_MS`, the controlled fold of session detail's other rooms and My schedule's past (D-191).
  - `fields.py`: `SearchField`, the pill of search and the Speakers and Sprints filters (D-193), with the hook `use_field_query()` (its `FieldQuery`: the text Flutter last reported, the screen's one `Ref`, the refocusing `clear()` and `focus()`; D-243) and `found_count()`, the live count line under the field (D-226).
  - `scrolling.py`: `jump_to_key()` and its `Jump`, `FULL_LIST_EXTENT`, `JUMP_MS`, `SCROLL_EVENT_MS` (D-154, D-192).
  - `day_groups.py`: `DayGroup`, a memoised day of session rows under its heading, for search, the track pages (D-199) and speaker detail (D-203).
  - `semantics.py`: `named()`, a semantics identifier merged into its control's node (D-190's pattern; D-198), its `Semantics` taking the control's `disabled` (D-214); `merged()`, a button merged into one node with its tooltip, which names it (D-234); and `semantics_key()`, an id as an identifier's key (D-184, D-200).
  - `menus.py`: `overflow_menu()`, the app bar's "More options" button, and `menu_item()`, its items, whose labels wrap at 200 % text (D-233).
  - `share.py`: the platform's share sheet, `centre()` (where it points from on a tablet, moved from the Developer screen) and `share_text()` (D-62, D-206).
- **Stores and hooks:** `Conference` / `use_conference()`, `Prefs` / `use_prefs()`, `Favorites` / `use_favorites()`, `Account` / `use_account()` (P3), `Staff` / `use_staff()` (P3), plus `use_services()` (`services/__init__.py`, with `Services` and `ServicesCtx`) and `use_tokens()`.
- **Routes.** S4 confirmed the shape (D-141), and `docs/architecture.md` §5 holds the full table; keep it and this list in sync.
  - Roots: `/now`, `/schedule`, `/my-schedule`, `/map`, `/more`. `/` goes to `/welcome` on first run (P2) and to `/now` otherwise.
  - Schedule changes: ~~`/schedule/changes`~~ the drill pair `changes/<year>`, pushed on the View that opens it (D-255).
  - More pages are `/more/<page>`: `speakers`, `tracks`, `tracks/<slug>` (and, ahead of it, the hub's pages of their own: `tracks/sprints`, `tracks/lightning-talks`, `tracks/open-spaces`, D-207, and `tracks/keynotes`, D-217), `keynotes`, `open-spaces`, `lightning-talks`, `sprints`, `sponsors`, `jobs`, `venue`, `wifi`, `help`, `about`, `psf`, `social`, `settings`, `account`, `sign-in`, `app` (About this app), `developer`, and `staff/<tool>`, where the tool is `lead`, `door`, `swag` or `coc`.
  - Search: `/search?scope=<scope>`, a global modal route. Back from a pushed result returns to search through our `page.on_view_pop` and `parent_of()` (D-19); Back from search returns to `ViewMemory.search_base`.
  - Drill: a recursive child of every root, More page and search, with the segment pairs `session/<id>`, `speaker/<id>`, `room/<slug>`, `sponsor/<slug>`, `plan/<plan>` (P2: a floor plan, `expo` or `job-fair` from `map.toml`) and `changes/<year>` (P2: Schedule changes, the year's change log; D-255); e.g. `/schedule/session/42/speaker/17/session/99`, `/more/sponsors/sponsor/acme/plan/expo?booth=613`.
  - Query parameters carry view options only (`?scope=`, `?pin=`, `?highlight=`, `?booth=`), read through `use_query()` until U9. The Router's Back navigates to pathnames only, so view options in the query are lost on Back: any option that must survive Back (map pin or booth, search query and scope) lives in `ViewMemory` (D-18), and the query only seeds it on entry.
- **Ids.**
  - In this plan: decisions `D-nn`, open decisions `O-n`, Flet facts `F1`..`F53`, spikes `S1`..`S9`, phases `P0`..`P4`.
  - Upstream items: `E1`..`E6` and `U1`..`U15` keep their v1 meanings; U16..U36 are new.
  - Features: catalog ids (`SCHED-05`) and our additions `NEW-01`..`NEW-09`.
- **Storage.**
  - Prefs keys are prefixed `pycon.`. App-wide: `theme`, `high_contrast`, `onboarded`, `excluded_tracks`, `reminders`, `dev`; in P3 `account` (JSON with email, nickname and role flags), `staff_sponsor` (JSON) and `start_screen` (D-16). Per year: `pycon.<year>.favorites`, `pycon.<year>.fetched` (P2; bool: a network feed for the year has been published on this install, so a refresh over the bundled snapshot is not diffed, D-255) and `pycon.<year>.favorites_dirty` (P3; bool, D-55).
  - Secure keys: `pycon.api_key`, `pycon.api_secret`.
  - Files in the data directory: `feed-<year>.json`, `changes-<year>.json`, `outbox.db`.
- **Year data:** `src/pycon_us/data/<year>/` holds `conference.toml`, `content.toml`, `map.toml` and `conference.json` (the snapshot). Assets live in `src/assets/` (~~`fonts/`,~~ `icons/`, `maps/<year>/`); the Roboto files live in the Flutter package `flutter_packages/roboto_fonts/` (D-105).
- **Tools:** `tools/mock_api/` (`handlers.py`, `server.py`, `transport.py`, `badges.py`, `README.md`), `tools/update_snapshot.py`, `tools/check_feed.py` with `tools/feed_invariants.py` (the invariants it shares with the tests), `tools/capture_ionic_reference/`, `tools/check_matrix.py`; the local checks `tools/check.sh`, the merge gate `tools/gate.sh` and the hook `.githooks/pre-push` (D-273).
- **Flet pin (D-81):** `.flet-ref` (committed; the pinned flet-dev/flet commit SHA) and `.flet-src` (git-ignored checkout at that SHA), used through `[tool.uv.sources]` and `[tool.flet.dev_packages]` until 1.1.0 is on PyPI.
- **Tests.**
  - Unit tests: `tests/test_<module>.py`; UI flows: `tests/ui/`.
  - Helpers and goldens: `tests/ui/helpers.py`, `tests/ui/golden/` (P4).
  - Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/feeds/conference-<year>.json`, ~~`tests/fixtures/feeds/pairs/<name>/` (change-log cases)~~ (change-log pairs are built in `tests/feeds.py` from the recorded feed by named edits, D-255), `tests/fixtures/snapshots/`, `tests/fixtures/ionic/<year>/`, `tests/fixtures/ionic/signing.json`, `tests/fixtures/mock/`.
  - Test names are descriptive; feature ids may appear in docstrings.
- **Semantics identifiers:** `<screen>.<element>[.<key>]`, e.g. `schedule.day_tab.2`, `session.favorite`, `now.next_session`; shell controls use `app.<element>[.<key>]`, e.g. `app.nav.schedule`, `app.nav.my_schedule`; a tab root's app-bar title is `<tab>.title`, e.g. `now.title` (D-170).
- **GitHub:**
  - Branches: `<type>/<issue>-<slug>`.
  - Workflows: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `feed-check.yml`, `build-android.yml`.
  - Spike write-ups: `docs/spikes/NN-<slug>.md`.
  - Milestones, labels and the Project: D-89, D-90, D-94.

## N. Verified Flet SDK facts

Facts F25..F39 were added later and name the spike and commit that verified them. Each other fact below was re-verified on 2026-10-11 in the local monorepo (flet-dev/flet at `9fbe52a61`, unreleased 1.1.0 in `CHANGELOG.md`). PyPI's latest release is 1.0.4 (2026-10-08). Paths are relative to `sdk/python/packages/flet/src/flet/` unless stated. This is **API evidence**: behaviour on devices is confirmed by the spikes.

| # | Fact |
|---|---|
| F1 | These are coroutines, so they must be awaited from `async def`:<br>• `Page`: `show_drawer`, `close_drawer`, `push_route`, `pop_views_until`.<br>• Controls: `View.confirm_pop(should_pop)`, `ListView.scroll_to`, `InteractiveViewer.zoom`/`pan`/`reset`/`save_state`/`restore_state`, `Dismissible.confirm_dismiss`.<br>• Services, all the methods we use: `Clipboard`, `SharedPreferences`, `UrlLauncher`, `Share`, `SemanticsService`, `StoragePaths`, `HapticFeedback`, `Wakelock`, `Connectivity.get_connectivity`.<br>These are sync: `page.navigate`, `page.show_dialog`, `page.pop_dialog`, `page.run_task`. |
| F2 | `use_effect(setup, dependencies, cleanup)` (`components/hooks/use_effect.py`; runner in `messaging/session.py`, `__updates_scheduler`):<br>• a coroutine function runs as a task; any other callable is simply called, so a lambda that returns a coroutine never runs;<br>• the previous setup task is cancelled whenever the effect re-runs; on unmount it is cancelled only when a `cleanup` exists;<br>• a sync setup may return a callable, which becomes the cleanup;<br>• on a dependency change the cleanup is queued before the new setup, whose `hook.cancel()` cancels an `async def` cleanup before it starts, so cleanups must be plain sync callables. |
| F3 | `use_context` subscribes the component only when the provided value is an `Observable`, and nested observables do not propagate (`components/hooks/use_context.py`). |
| F4 | Error handling in `messaging/session.py`:<br>• an exception in an event handler is logged, then `session.error()` sends `SESSION_CRASHED` (`dispatch_event`); a sync effect setup does the same;<br>• exceptions in async effect tasks reach only asyncio;<br>• `page.on_error` is fed by Flutter-side errors (`packages/flet/lib/src/flet_backend.dart`). |
| F5 | Assigning an observable field notifies only when `value_equal(old, new)` is false: identity first, then `==` (`components/observable.py`, `components/utils.py`). |
| F6 | Router (`components/router.py`):<br>• Signatures: `Router(routes, not_found=None, manage_views=False)`; `Route` fields are `path`, `index`, `component`, `children`, `loader`, `outlet`, `modal` and `recursive`.<br>• With `manage_views=True`, each view level is its own `View`, and `not_found` is wrapped in `View(route=pathname, ...)`.<br>• A recursive route consumes one match per level, trying non-recursive children first. Each view level sees the params accumulated up to its own level, so same-named params hold that level's values.<br>• A top-level `modal=True` route is global: its base is the last non-modal location. A child modal is local.<br>• While a modal is anywhere in the chain, every pop (including from a child view pushed over the modal) navigates to the modal's base location. A non-modal pop navigates to the previous view entry's resolved pathname. Both drop the query string. An app's own `page.on_view_pop`, set before the Router mounts, receives every pop instead (`e.route` is the popped view's route).<br>• Hooks: `use_route_params`, `use_route_location` (pathname only), `use_view_path`, `use_route_outlet`, `use_route_loader_data`, and `is_route_active`, which is itself a hook. `LocationInfo.search` exists internally, but there is no `use_route_search`. |
| F7 | `ListView.scroll_to(offset, delta, scroll_key, duration, curve)` does nothing when the keyed control has not been built (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/scrollable_control.dart`). `ListView.build_controls_on_demand` defaults to `True`. |
| F8 | Absent from the SDK: `RefreshIndicator`; slivers, sticky headers and `SliverAppBar`; `AppBar.bottom`; an absolute `InteractiveViewer` transform (`zoom` and `pan` are relative); `Page.get_app_info`; `CarouselView`; an HTML control; `Theme.platform`; `TextStyle.font_features`; `PageMediaData.text_scale_factor`; an accessibility-features change event; a local-notifications package; a barcode-scanner package. |
| F9 | Theme:<br>• `Theme` has `color_scheme_seed`, `color_scheme`, `surfaces` (`ThemeSurfaces.NEUTRAL`/`TONAL`, new in 1.1; seeded themes default to neutral surfaces), `font_family` and `page_transitions`.<br>• The Dart side builds `ColorScheme.fromSeed`, neutralises surfaces unless `TONAL`, then applies the explicit `color_scheme` overrides (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/theme.dart`).<br>• `PageTransitionsTheme` has `android` and `ios`; `PageTransitionTheme` includes `CUPERTINO`, `PREDICTIVE`, `FADE_FORWARDS` and `ZOOM`.<br>• The build template sets `android:enableOnBackInvokedCallback="true"`. |
| F10 | Navigation controls:<br>• `NavigationBar.selected_index` is an `int` (default 0), `adaptive` defaults to `None`, and `label_behavior` includes `ALWAYS_SHOW`. `NavigationRail` exists.<br>• `View` has `can_pop`, `on_confirm_pop` and `fullscreen_dialog`, and renders either `navigation_bar` or `bottom_appbar`.<br>• `IconButton` has `selected`, `selected_icon` and `badge` (`str` or `Badge`). |
| F11 | Dialogs and overlays:<br>• `SnackBar` and `Banner` are `DialogControl`s: show them with `page.show_dialog` or `use_dialog`.<br>• `SnackBar` has no top position.<br>• `Banner` requires `content` and at least one visible action, and has no duration.<br>• `BottomSheet` has no detents; it offers `show_drag_handle`, `draggable`, `scrollable`, `use_safe_area` and `size_constraints`. |

| F13 | *(Its iOS live-region sentence corrected by D-243.)* `SemanticsService.get_accessibility_features()` returns a snapshot, with no change event. Its fields: `accessible_navigation`, `bold_text`, `disable_animations`, `high_contrast` (iOS and Android 34+), `invert_colors`, `reduce_motion` (iOS only), `on_off_switch_labels` and `supports_announcements`. `announce_message(message, rtl, assertiveness)` exists, but Android discourages announcements; prefer `Semantics(live_region=True)` (how TalkBack takes one: F61). `supports_announcements` is never true on Android: the engine sets `NO_ANNOUNCE` on every version (`AccessibilityBridge.java`), though `announce` still calls `announceForAccessibility` (with a deprecation warning from API 36). On iOS a live region whose label changes posts a queued announcement of its label and leaves VoiceOver's focus where it is. Only a `focus` semantics event moves the focus to a node (`accessibility_bridge.mm:139-151`, `:366-369`, Flutter 3.47.7; corrected by D-243). |
| F14 | A packaged app's environment (build template `lib/main.dart`):<br>• `FLET_PLATFORM` is set, so `flet.utils.is_embedded()` is True.<br>• `FLET_APP_STORAGE_DATA` is durable, app-private and under application support; it is also the working directory and survives updates.<br>• `FLET_APP_STORAGE_CACHE` may be purged by the OS; `FLET_APP_STORAGE_TEMP` is volatile.<br>• `FLET_APP_CONSOLE` is `<cache>/console.log` (also `StoragePaths.get_console_log_filename()`).<br>`flet run` mirrors the storage part: it sets the three `FLET_APP_STORAGE_*` variables to `<project>/.flet/storage/{data,cache,temp}` (git-ignored; the working directory is `data`), but not `FLET_PLATFORM` or `FLET_APP_CONSOLE` (flet-cli `commands/run.py`). |

| F16 | For Android and iOS builds, flet-cli reads deep links only from `[tool.flet.android.deep_linking]` and `[tool.flet.ios.deep_linking]`; the global table applies to other targets until flet-dev/flet#6953 merges (`build_base.py`, deep-linking resolution). The template registers custom schemes; there is no App Links `autoVerify` and no universal links. |

There is no `PERMISSION_NOTIFICATIONS` entry, so request iOS notification permission through the notifications plugin. | F19 | `flet build` embeds Python 3.14 by default (3.14.7: python-build manifest `20260921`, the release flet-cli pins at `utils/python_versions.py:31`), and `flet` depends on `httpx` on non-Emscripten platforms. |

| F21 | `flet test` targets `android`, `ios`, `macos`, `linux` and `windows`, with `flet.testing.FletTestApp`. The host `Tester` has `tap`, `tap_at`, `drag`, `drag_from`, `long_press`, `enter_text` and `find_by_semantics_identifier`. The on-device `RemoteTester` has no `drag` or `tap_at`. |

| F26 | On Android, when the activity is recreated (seen in S1 when the navigation mode changed), Flet reuses the running Python process and starts a **new session**: `main()` runs again and the component tree and its state start over, while module-level state survives. A dark-mode switch does not recreate the activity. |
| F27 | `ft.Markdown` always adds `LatexBlockSyntax` and `LatexInlineSyntax` (flutter_markdown_plus_latex 1.0.5) to the chosen extension set (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/markdown.dart`, Flet `15854a0ac`). Inline math opens on `$`, `$$`, `\(`, `\[`, `\pu{`, `\ce{`, `( ` and `[ `, whenever a matching closer comes later on the same line and is followed by whitespace, one of `?!.,:` (or their full-width forms) or the end of the text. These custom syntaxes are tried before Markdown's own at every position, so a backslash escape of `(` or `[` produces a LaTeX opener; numeric character references (`&#36;`) are decoded to text and never match (S3, D-115). |
| F28 | When `ft.Markdown.md_style_sheet` is set, each text style given replaces the theme-derived default instead of merging with it (`parseMarkdownStyleSheet` in `packages/flet/lib/src/utils/markdown.dart`): a style without `color` renders black in dark mode. The defaults themselves use `Colors.blue` for links (no underline) and `Colors.blue.shade100` for the block-quote box when a sheet is given; without a sheet, h1 is `headlineSmall` and h2 `titleLarge` (S3, D-116). |
| F29 | On Android, Back on a root View with nothing to pop finishes the activity (lifecycle `inactive`, `hide`, `pause`, `detach`); the Python process stays, and a relaunch starts a **new session** in it at `route=/`, the build template's "process reuse" path (`lib/main.dart`, `runPythonApp`). So F26's new session also follows an ordinary Back exit (S4, `15854a0ac`). |
| F30 | When a new session replaces an old one in the same process, `FletDartBridgeServer` closes the old `Session` (`page.on_close` fires) without unmounting its components: `use_effect` cleanups don't run, effect tasks keep running, and the first store write from one raises `RuntimeError: An attempt to fetch destroyed session.` A render after `detach` fails to send (`dart_bridge.send_bytes failed`, logged) (S4, `15854a0ac`; `messaging/flet_dart_bridge_server.py`, `app.py` `_DartBridgeServerHandle`). |
| F31 | A packaged app's initial route comes from the platform: on Android the `route` intent extra (`am start --es route <path>`, what `flet debug --route` passes) or a deep-link intent; on the iOS simulator the engine's `--route=<path>` launch argument (`xcrun simctl launch <udid> <bundle> --route=<path>`). `simctl openurl` with a custom scheme stops at a system "Open in …?" prompt that needs a tap. Route strings round-trip percent-encoded (S4). |
