A `ScrollKey`'s `GlobalKey` is registered in `ControlWidget.build` (`control_widget.dart`), so `scroll_to(scroll_key=...)` to a far item does nothing in either mode. It works in `Column(scroll=...)` and in a `ListView` whose `cache_extent` covers the list (S2, Flet `15854a0ac`; Android 16 emulator and iOS 26.5 simulator). |
| F33 | A component that renders `TextField(on_change=...)` without `value=` loses the typed text on its next re-render: the new control's default `value` differs from the old control's (which the change event updated), so the diff sends the default to Flutter (`Component.update` in `components/component.py` diffs the new tree against the old; S2, Flet `15854a0ac`). |
| F34 | `ft.memo` skips only the memoised component's own body when its props are shallow-equal (`is`, then `==`; `components/utils.py`, `shallow_compare_args_and_kwargs`). The parent that renders it still runs and its own controls are rebuilt and diffed, so memoised rows under a parent that re-renders on every change keep the parent's cost; a frozen `eq=False` prop compares by identity, so it must be the same object across renders (S2). |
| F35 | Flet sends the page, with its default empty `View`, in the `REGISTER_CLIENT` response, before `main()` runs (`messaging/flet_dart_bridge_server.py`), and the build template removes its boot screen as soon as that page arrives (`FletBackend.isLoading`). So Flutter draws one frame of the default View (white in light mode, about 19 ms) before `main()`'s first patch. Changes made in `ft.run(main, before_main=...)` are part of that first page (#72, Flet `15854a0ac`; Android 14 emulator). |
| F36 | `Page.on_platform_brightness_change` is typed `PlatformBrightnessChangeEvent`, but `flet_backend.dart` sends the brightness name as a plain string, so the handler gets a `ControlEvent` (`data="dark"`) without `.brightness`, and reading it raises `AttributeError` (the session crashed on the iOS 26.5 simulator). `page.platform_brightness` is already updated when the handler runs (#72, Flet `15854a0ac`). |
| F37 | asyncio reports a task's unretrieved exception only when the task is released (`Task exception was never retrieved`, from `Task.__del__`), and never once `cancel()` has been called on it, even after it has finished. `use_effect` keeps an async setup's task on its hook (`hook._setup_task`) and cancels it only while it is pending (`components/hooks/use_effect.py`), so a failed async effect is reported only when its next run replaces the task or its hook is dropped: for an effect with `[]` dependencies, not while its component is mounted, and in a replaced session (F30), never (#73, Flet `15854a0ac`; a host script on Python 3.14: a held task logged nothing, a dropped one logged at once, a cancelled one never). |
| F38 | A re-rendered control takes over its predecessor's place: `_compare_dataclasses` (`controls/object_patch.py`) calls `dst._migrate_state(src)` (`_i`, `controls/base_control.py`), sets the new instance's `_parent` and re-indexes the session from the old instance to the new one, without unmount or mount. A control built with `ref=` sets `ref.current` to itself when it is created (`BaseControl.__post_init__`), and `Ref.current` is a weak reference (`controls/ref.py`). So a ref passed to the control on every render points at the live control after a re-render, and its methods (`scroll_to`) reach the mounted widget (#75, D-166, Flet `15854a0ac`). |
| F39 | `use_effect` assigns its `cleanup` argument to the hook on every render (`hook.cleanup = cleanup`, `components/hooks/use_effect.py`), so a cleanup that a **sync** setup returned (F2) is replaced by `None` at the component's next render and does not run on unmount; with no cleanup left, an async setup's task is not cancelled either (`_run_unmount_effects` in `components/component.py`). An effect whose component re-renders passes `cleanup=` itself (#82, Flet `15854a0ac`). Upstream: [flet-dev/flet#6963](https://github.com/flet-dev/flet/issues/6963), tracked in #182 (U27). |
| F40 | *(Sources at the pin's Flutter amended by F49.)* Screen-reader headings come from the heading **level**: on Android (API 28+) Flutter's `AccessibilityBridge.java` calls `setHeading(semanticsNode.headingLevel > 0)`, and the IS_HEADER flag alone does not make a heading; Flet's `Semantics.heading_level` docs say the same of VoiceOver. So `Semantics(header=True)` without `heading_level` is not a heading for TalkBack or VoiceOver (#205). A `Text(selectable=True)` (Flutter `SelectableText`) inside `Semantics(header=True, heading_level=1, identifier=...)` is one Android node of class `View` (not `EditText`) holding the text and the identifier, so the heading sits on the labelled node (`uiautomator dump`, Android 14, Flet `15854a0ac`, #90). |
| F41 | Flutter marks a tappable text span as a link (`isLink`, `paragraph.dart` and `editable.dart`, so in `ft.Markdown` with or without `selectable`), but on Android `AccessibilityBridge.java` treats a link without a URL as a button (`shouldBeTreatedAsButton`; only a `linkUrl` becomes a `URLSpan`), and no text span carries a URL; Flet's `Semantics` has `link` but no `link_url`. So TalkBack calls every link in the app a button (`android.widget.Button` in `uiautomator dump`), while iOS gets `UIAccessibilityTraitLink` (`SemanticsObject.mm`) (#91, Flutter 3.47.7, Flet `15854a0ac`; U31). |
| F42 | Flet's `Semantics` always sets Flutter's `enabled`, to `!control.disabled` (`controls/semantics.dart`), and `Control.disabled` is the control's own `disabled` or an ancestor's (`models/control.dart`), never a descendant's. So a `Semantics` around a disabled button says it is enabled, and wrapped in `MergeSemantics` the merged node read as enabled on Android (`enabled="true"` in `uiautomator dump`, Android 14). A wrapper around a control that can be disabled sets `disabled` itself too (#87, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-195); `named()` takes it from the control (D-214). Flutter's `Tristate.merge` lets `isTrue` win, which is why the merge read as enabled (`dart:ui` `semantics.dart:1136`, Flutter 3.47.7; #239, U34). |
| F43 | Given a `style`, `color`, `bgcolor` or an `elevation` other than 1, Flet's `Button` (so `FilledButton` and `FilledTonalButton`, not `TextButton` or `OutlinedButton`) sends `style.copy(color=..., bgcolor=..., elevation=self.elevation)` (`before_update`, `controls/material/button.py`). `Button.elevation` defaults to 1 and is never `None`, so it replaces the style's own `elevation`, and as a plain number it becomes every state's (`getWidgetStateProperty`, `utils/widget_state.dart`): a styled `FilledButton` has elevation 1 even when disabled, where M3 has 0. Such a button passes its elevation on the button (`elevation=0`, or a per-state value), not in the style (#87, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-195). |
| F44 | `ft.Image`'s `placeholder_src` takes the image's own size: `ImageControl` builds the placeholder with the `Image`'s `width` and `height`, and `ImageFadeConfig.wrapFrame` puts it in a `SizedBox(width: width, height: height)` inside an `AnimatedSwitcher`, whose default layout is a centred `Stack` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/image.dart`, `utils/images.dart`). So an image laid out without a size, scaled into a frame by its constraints, gets a placeholder at the placeholder's own intrinsic size, centred, which cannot fill the frame. `fade_in_animation` alone turns the same fade on, with an invisible copy of the image keeping the layout while it loads (#92, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-206). |
| F45 | A fading `ft.Image` is laid out loose: with `fade_in_animation` (or a placeholder) set, `ImageFadeConfig.wrapFrame` puts the image in an `AnimatedSwitcher` whose layout is a `Stack(alignment: center)` with the default loose fit (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/images.dart`), so the image gets loose constraints even where its parent's are tight. An image meant to fill a box at `COVER` (a `Stack` child at `left`…`bottom` 0) then keeps its own proportions, centred: seen on the iOS 26.5 simulator as a copy no wider than the image. Such an image takes no fade (#92, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-206). |
| F46 | Flet handles a system pop before Flutter's Navigator (the Android Back key or committed gesture, and VoiceOver's escape on iOS, the two-finger Z, which Flutter's iOS embedder sends as `popRoute` when the focused node has no dismiss action: `accessibilityPerformEscape` in `FlutterViewController.mm` and `SemanticsObject.mm`, Flutter 3.47.7): `SimpleRouterDelegate.popRoute()` calls `PageControl._handleSystemPopRoute()` first (`packages/flet/lib/src/routing/router_delegate.dart`, `controls/page.dart`, added by flet-dev/flet#5915). With two or more Views, when the top View has `can_pop` true and no `on_confirm_pop` handler, it marks that View popped and sends `view_pop` itself and returns true, so `Navigator.maybePop()` never runs. It does not look for pageless routes above the View's page: a dialog, a date or time picker, a bottom sheet or a popup menu, all pushed on the same Navigator (`useRootNavigator: false` in `alert_dialog.dart`, `date_picker.dart`, `time_picker.dart`); removing the View's page takes them along, so one Back closes the dialog and pops the View (#235). `_handleSystemPopRoute()` has no platform check, so the escape does the same on iOS (read from the source; VoiceOver does not run on the Simulator). A committed Back gesture ends there too: with a dialog on top the page route is not current, so its predictive-back detector declines (`predictive_back_page_transitions_builder.dart`, Flutter 3.47.7) and the commit falls back to `popRoute`. When the top View has `can_pop` false or an `on_confirm_pop` handler, or is the only View, the handler returns null and `Navigator.maybePop()` pops the topmost route; a View that can pop is then popped by the Navigator, which calls `onDidRemovePage` and so sends `view_pop`, as a committed gesture does, and its `on_confirm_pop` runs only for a pop that `can_pop` refuses (`PopScope` in `controls/view.dart`) (#235, `15854a0ac`; on flet-dev `main` too). |
| F47 | `PopupMenuItem.icon` and a text `content` are laid out as `Row([icon, SizedBox(width: 8), content])` with no `Expanded` (`buildPopupMenuEntries()`, `packages/flet/lib/src/utils/popup_menu.dart` at `15854a0ac`), inside Flutter's menu, which is at most 280 dp wide. So a label longer than the menu's width does not wrap: at 200 % text on the Android emulator "Add all to My schedule" ran off the menu's edge (#103). An item whose label may be long makes its `content` a `ListTile(leading=Icon(...), title=Text(label))` instead, which Flutter documents as a `PopupMenuItem` child (the item merges a zero `contentPadding` into its `ListTileTheme`): the title wraps, and the theme's `TITLE_HEIGHT` keeps the icon beside its first line (D-188), where a `Row` would centre it on the block; `min_height=48` keeps the menu's row height, which a tile would make 56 (D-209). Every overflow item is now `ui/menus.py`'s `menu_item()`, with that tile (D-233); the layout gap is U36 (#308). |
| F48 | A `SnackBar` shown with `page.show_dialog` is drawn on every View, the top one included. Flet builds the page's dialogs only into the last View's body (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/view.dart`, `control.id == pageViews.last.id`), where `SnackBarControl` calls `ScaffoldMessenger.of(context).showSnackBar` after the frame (`controls/snack_bar.dart`). That messenger is the page's one, above the `Navigator` (`MaterialApp.router`'s own; `scaffoldMessengerBuilder` adds one to a `CupertinoApp`, `controls/page.dart`), and it hands the bar to every registered `Scaffold` with no `Scaffold` of the same messenger above it (`_isRoot`: `parent == null || !_scaffolds.contains(parent)`, and `_updateScaffolds`, in material_ui 1.6.0's `scaffold.dart`; a `Scaffold` under a nested `ScaffoldMessenger` leaves the ones below it roots of this one). Each View is a route of its own with its own `Scaffold`, so each is such a root: the top View shows the bar, a covered one draws it offstage, a `Scaffold` that mounts while it shows (a View pushed then) gets it when it registers (`_register`), and one timer, the messenger's, runs its duration (its `ModalRoute.of` is null above the `Navigator`) (#197, Flet `15854a0ac`; iOS 26.5 simulator, Android 14 emulator, a Flutter widget test of the same shape). |
| F49 | *(Amends F40's sources to the pin's Flutter.)* At Flutter 3.47.7 (the pin's `.fvmrc`) both bridges make a heading of the level alone: Android's `BaseRoleConfigurator.java:144` calls `result.setHeading(node.headingLevel > 0)` (the code F40 cites moved there from `AccessibilityBridge.java`), and iOS's `SemanticsObject.mm:840` adds `UIAccessibilityTraitHeader` only when `headingLevel > 0`; IS_HEADER is left to Android's collection item info and to iOS's scroll on focus. (Flutter 3.44.8's iOS bridge still took the trait from IS_HEADER.) The framework keeps the flag and the level apart (`header` never sets a level), and a merged node keeps a child's level when its own is 0 (`_mergeHeadingLevels`, `semantics.dart`). So Flutter's `AppBar` title, `Semantics(header: true)` without a level (`app_bar.dart`), is no heading for TalkBack or VoiceOver, and an app's `Semantics(heading_level=1)` around the title's text merges into that title's node with its identifier (on the Android 14 emulator, `isHeading()` true on "More" with `more.title`, #205). |
| F50 | A bare `Semantics(identifier=...)` around a button, a field or a tile, or on a tile's title, is not that control's node but one of its own. In `uiautomator dump` (Android 14, release APK): around a button an empty, focusable `View` over the `Button`; around an `IconButton` or a `PopupMenuButton` with a tooltip a non-clickable `View` that holds the tooltip over an unnamed `Button` (`NAF="true"`); around a `TextField` an empty `View` over the `EditText`; on an `ExpansionTile`'s title a node of the title's own, beside a header button read "Collapsed" alone. `MergeSemantics` around the wrapper makes one node that carries the identifier: the `Button` named by its label or tooltip, the `EditText` with its label, helper and error in its text (a Flutter widget test); a merge takes in every descendant, so it cannot go around a field with a button inside or a fold that holds rows. For the field the cause is Flet's: it always gives its `Semantics` an enabled state (F42), and Flutter keeps two configurations apart when both have one (`SemanticsConfiguration.isCompatibleWith`, `Tristate.hasConflict`), so a Flutter widget test with `enabled: null` (U34's fix) puts the bare identifier on the `TextField`'s node; around a button and on a tile's title it stays a node of its own even then, which is Flutter's (#239, #236, Flet `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7; D-214). |
| F51 | `on_size_change` (on any `LayoutControl`) reports the control's laid-out size at mount and after every change, sampled every `size_change_interval` ms (10 by default; `SizeChangeObserver` in `packages/flet/lib/src/controls/base_controls.dart`), so a text-size change or a rotation while the app runs sends the new width. A `Text` in a 0 dp high box keeps its own width: Flutter clamps only its height. It cannot be combined with absolute placement in a `Stack`: `LayoutControl` builds the `Positioned` (`_positionedControl`) and then wraps it in the size observer (`_sizeChangeObserver`), so the `Positioned` is no longer the stack's direct child, and a release build draws Flutter's grey error box over the stack. Seen at `15854a0ac`: the widths at mount and after a font-scale change on the Android 14 emulator, and at mount at each content size on the iOS 26.5 simulator; the grey box in a release APK on the Android 14 emulator (#224, D-216). |
| F52 | A Flet extension can bring native Android and iOS code with it. `flet build` copies the extension's whole `flutter/<name>/` folder from its wheel (`[tool.setuptools.package-data]` must include it) into `build/flutter-packages/` and adds it as a path dependency (`register_flutter_extensions()`, `sdk/python/packages/flet-cli/src/flet_cli/commands/build_base.py`). A `flutter: plugin: platforms:` entry in its pubspec then makes it a Flutter plugin: `android: package + pluginClass` builds its `android/` as a Gradle library module, whose `AndroidManifest.xml` (receivers, permissions) and `res/` Gradle merges into the app; `ios: pluginClass` with `ios/<name>/Package.swift` builds a Swift package (the pin's builds use Swift Package Manager), and `GeneratedPluginRegistrant` calls the class's `register(with:)`. A Java no-op class is enough on Android (no Kotlin plugin). Seen at `15854a0ac` in S6: the merged manifest of a release APK (`aapt2 dump xmltree`) held the extension's two receivers and `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`, and the iOS registrant held `S6RemindersPlugin`. |
| F53 | At the pin's Flutter 3.47.7, `flet build ios-simulator` migrates the generated project to the UIScene life cycle ("Finished migration to UIScene lifecycle"): `AppDelegate` is a `FlutterAppDelegate, FlutterImplicitEngineDelegate` that registers plugins in `didInitializeImplicitFlutterEngine(_:)`, and nothing sets `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().delegate`. `flutter_local_notifications` 22.3.1 does not set it either (it only calls `addApplicationDelegate:`, `FlutterLocalNotificationsPlugin.m:155`), so iOS reports no notification response: on the iOS 26.5 simulator a cold-start tap gave empty launch details and a tap while running no event. A plugin whose `register(with:)` sets `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().delegate = UIApplication.shared.delegate as? UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate` makes `FlutterAppDelegate` forward the responses, and it runs early enough: the cold-start tap then carried its payload (S6). |
| F54 | Flutter's `Navigator` gives the screen reader's focus back when a route closes (Flutter 3.47.7, the pin's): `NavigatorState` listens to `ServicesBinding.accessibilityFocus` (`widgets/navigator.dart:3851`), which the engine sets on its `didGainFocus` message each time TalkBack or VoiceOver focuses a node (`AccessibilityBridge.java`'s `ACTION_ACCESSIBILITY_FOCUS`; `accessibility_bridge.mm`), and records the node on the topmost present route entry (`_recordLastFocus`, `:3856`). When a route above an entry pops, `handleDidPopNext` (`:3312`) waits for the popped route's disposal and sends a `FocusSemanticEvent` for that node: on Android 300 ms later (`_kAndroidRefocusingDelayDuration`), which the engine turns into `TYPE_VIEW_FOCUSED` (`AccessibilityBridge.java:342`, `onFocus`), and TalkBack moves its focus there; on iOS at once, as `UIAccessibilityLayoutChangedNotification` with the node (`accessibility_bridge.mm`, `HandleEvent`). Only a pop runs it; a route taken away with `removeRoute`, which Flet's `closeModalRoute()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/utils/misc.dart:249`) uses for a route that is not on top, does not. Flet's `AlertDialog` (`useRootNavigator: false`), `DatePicker`, `TimePicker`, `BottomSheet` and `PopupMenuButton` are routes on the Views' `Navigator`. Another activity on top (a browser, the share sheet) is no route; on the return `FocusManager._appLifecycleChange` (`widgets/focus_manager.dart:1868`) restores the input focus it suspended, not the screen reader's. Flet has `focus()` on `IconButton`, `Button`, `TextButton` and `OutlinedButton`, not on `ListTile` or `PopupMenuButton` (#262, Flet `15854a0ac`; D-225). |
| F55 | Flet applies a control's `tooltip` around the whole control: `BaseControl` and `LayoutControl` call `_tooltip()` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/base_controls.dart:27`, `:46`, `:64`), which wraps it in Flutter's `Tooltip`, whose `RawTooltip` adds a `Semantics(tooltip: ...)` that is no boundary (`widgets/raw_tooltip.dart:870`). Flutter's own `IconButton(tooltip:)` puts that `Tooltip` inside the button's `Semantics(container: true, button: true)` (`ButtonStyleButton`, material_ui 1.6.0 `button_style_button.dart:581`, `:597`), and its `FloatingActionButton(tooltip:)` inside its `MergeSemantics` (`floating_action_button.dart:623`, `:630`), so the tooltip names the button. Flet's `IconButton`, `FloatingActionButton` and `Button` pass Flutter no tooltip and hand the built button to `LayoutControl` (`icon_button.dart:250`, `floating_action_button.dart:111`, `button.dart:225`), and its `PopupMenuButton` passes `tooltip: null` and hides Flutter's own (`popup_menu_button.dart:29`, `:57-60`). So the tooltip is a node of its own over the button: in `uiautomator dump` (Android 14, release APK) a non-focusable `View` with the tooltip as its `content-desc` over a `Button` with `NAF="true"`, which TalkBack reads as "Button" alone; over a FAB with a label, a `View` with the tooltip over the labelled `Button`. A `MergeSemantics` straight around the button makes one node named by the tooltip ("Back, Button"); a `Tooltip(exclude_from_semantics=True)` adds no node. A `Semantics`' own `tooltip` is shown as a tooltip too, since `Semantics` is a `LayoutControl` (`semantics.dart:47`, `:113`) (#274, Flet `15854a0ac`, Flutter 3.47.7; D-234, U37). |
| F60 | The packaged app's embedded Python reads the device's time zone, so `datetime.now().astimezone()` (which CPython computes with the C library's `localtime()`) gives the device's offset (checked 2026-10-14 on packaged builds at `15854a0ac`, #218). **Android** (API 34 emulator, release APK): no `TZ` in the app process's environment (`/proc/<pid>/environ` has only `ANDROID_TZDATA_ROOT`), so bionic's `localtime` follows the system zone; with the zone set through the system's time-zone state (`cmd time_zone_detector set_time_zone_state_for_tests`, which moves the status bar's clock too, as Settings does), Developer options read "This device: UTC+02:00" in Europe/Berlin and "UTC−07:00" in America/Los_Angeles after a relaunch, and a change made while the app ran reached the Schedule at its next minute tick, without a restart. Setting only the `persist.sys.timezone` property (`service call alarm 3 s16 <zone>`) moved `adb shell date` but neither the status bar nor the app, so it is no way to test this. **iOS** (26.5 simulator, `flet build ios-simulator`): the simulator follows the Mac's zone (Europe/Berlin here), and the app's Python saw it: Developer options read "This device: UTC+02:00" and the Schedule showed the note; launched with `SIMCTL_CHILD_TZ=America/Los_Angeles xcrun simctl launch ...`, its Python followed `TZ` and the note was gone. The bundled `tzdata` (D-43) serves `zoneinfo` only; `astimezone()` with no argument uses the C library's local time. |
| F61 | A Flutter live region on Android (Flutter 3.47.7, TalkBack 14.2, API 34): the engine sends `TYPE_WINDOW_CONTENT_CHANGED` (`CONTENT_CHANGE_TYPE_SUBTREE`) for a node with `IS_LIVE_REGION` whose label changed (`didChangeLabel()`, `AccessibilityBridge.java:1705`), only for a node that was in its previous update and is not hidden (`updateSemantics()`, from line 1555); TalkBack reads it as `nodeLiveRegion=1` and speaks the label without moving its focus. A node whose rect is empty is dropped from the tree (`SemanticsNode.isInvisible`, `semantics.dart:2912`; `shouldDrop`, `object.dart:5765`), so a live region needs a size. A node with no label, value, hint, actions or focusable flags is not focusable (`isFocusable()`, line 2698), so linear navigation skips it. TalkBack drops a second announced change of the same node within 30 s (`WindowContentChangeAnnouncementFilter`, `CONTENT_CHANGE_MINIMUM_CYCLE = 30000` while "limit text change rate" is on, unless the node sets `minDurationBetweenContentChanges`, which Flutter does not); a change with empty output is not counted, and the start of a touch interaction resets it (`invalidateRecordNode()`), so a change after a double tap is always spoken. Seen in D-241's checks. |
| F62 | A control whose `key` is an `ft.ScrollKey` gets a new Flutter `GlobalKey` at each build of its `ControlWidget` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/control_widget.dart:34-38`: `key = GlobalKey()`, registered in `FletBackend.globalKeys` for `scroll_to`), so Flutter unmounts it and mounts it again, with everything under it, whenever an ancestor rebuilds: new `State`s, render objects and semantics nodes with new ids. A push or a pop of a View, a bottom sheet and a re-render of one row all rebuild the covered list's items, so a row under a keyed box lost its node id each time, and the `Navigator`'s refocus after Back (F54) found no node. Python keeps every control's `_i` (#303, Flet `15854a0ac`; Android 14 emulator, TalkBack 14.2; D-242, U42). |
| F63 | A controlled `TextField` can be rewritten by an older keystroke's re-render. Flutter reports each edit as a property update, then a `change` event (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/textfield.dart:356-360`). It applies any `value` from Python that differs from the last text it knows, and moves the cursor to the end (`:195-203`). Python applies a property update to the indexed control as soon as it arrives (`UPDATE_CONTROL_PROPS`, `messaging/flet_dart_bridge_server.py:204-206`, `Session.apply_patch`). It runs each event in a task of its own (`:198-202`), and the re-render that a state change asks for later still, from `Session.__updates_scheduler` (`messaging/session.py:702-720`). The re-render diffs against the indexed control (F38). So a keystroke's re-render that runs after the next keystroke's update sends the older text, and Flutter shows it. Once the later events' state matches the last report, the diff sends nothing, and the field and the state stay apart. Nothing tells an echo apart from a change the program made. Reproduced in a real session (`tests/test_fields.py`, the reports of "c", "ch", "che" and "chen" interleaved with their events) and seen on the iOS simulator and Android emulator under load (#307; U43, #319, D-243). Flet `15854a0ac`. |
| F69 | A `Chip`'s string `label` reaches Flutter as a plain `Text` (`packages/flet/lib/src/controls/chip.dart:55`, `buildTextOrWidget()` in `packages/flet/lib/src/extensions/control.dart:97-98`), and the `InputChip` it builds wraps its label in a `DefaultTextStyle` with `maxLines: 1`, `softWrap: false` and `TextOverflow.fade` (`material_ui` 1.6.0, `lib/src/chip.dart:1483-1489`), so a long name fades at the chip's end. A `Text` control label wraps when it sets `max_lines` (Flet's `Text` always passes `softWrap: !no_wrap`, `controls/text.dart:118-126`, but leaves `maxLines` to the `DefaultTextStyle` when unset), yet the chip still cuts it: `_RenderChip._computeSizes()` measures the label's dry layout at the chip's whole width (`chip.dart:1987`), then lays it out in that width less the check mark or avatar, `padding` and `label_padding` (`_labelConstraintsFrom()`, `:1893-1911`), with the measured height as its maximum. A label that fits one line at the whole width but not in the narrower one wraps into a second line that the chip clips (and fades). The chip also hit-tests only the label, at its centre (`hitTest()`, `:1942-1965`). Seen at `15854a0ac` on the iOS 26.5 simulator at the largest Larger Text: with `ft.Text(name, max_lines=3)`, "Sponsor presentation" (wider than the chip either way) wrapped whole, while "Lightning talks" showed "Lightning" over a cut-off "talks" (#330, D-249). With `show_checkmark=False` and no horizontal `padding` or `label_padding`, the two widths are the same and the label wraps in full. |

## Changes from v1

- **Purpose.** v1 aimed to ship as 27.0.0 of the PSF's store listings. v2 is a community showcase, so everything that existed only for the PSF is dropped: the PSF deliverables P1-P13, gates G1-G6 and the Track decision, Badge Days with PSF staff, Linear, outreach dates, PSF-held secrets and on-call.
- **Platforms.** Web and PWA are dropped (Pyodide transport, Firebase Hosting, offline service worker). Only Android and iOS remain.
- **Fidelity.** v1 kept Ionic's URLs, aria strings, exact colours, four-mode theme picker, Cupertino pickers and platform dark split. v2 redesigns in Material 3 (sections C and D).
- **Scope enforcement.** Gone: the 263 named tests, the `feature_trace` gate, the Node differential harness in CI, 168 goldens, the fallback-parity suite and contract tests against PSF staging. In their place: named rule tests, invariants and a one-off capture of Ionic's output (D-77).
- **Server-driven content.** `app-config.json`/`app-content.json`, kill switches, update gates and banner polling become bundled TOML year data (D-45). A content fix now needs a new build.
- **Push, analytics and Sentry** are cut. Local reminders are prioritised instead (D-58), with local logs (D-72).
- **Capabilities.** The observable `Caps`, CI manifest, API probes and remote veto become import-time constants (D-63).
- **Architecture.** 12 stores and 13 contexts become 3 stores (5 from P3). The service locator and provider families are gone. `@safe` plus a lint rule and a top-toast `Banner` become explicit error boundaries plus one small decorator until U15 lands (D-71).
- **Navigation.** The `/app/tabs` URLs, drawer, global floor-plan modal and short links become five fixed tabs, a flat More list and one recursive drill route (D-13..D-17).
- **Favourites.** Server-authoritative sync through the outbox becomes local-first sync with a dirty flag (D-55). The outbox goes from three retry caps to one (D-56).
- **Releases.** v1 had `27.x` versions, `YY*1000` build numbers, a `builds.txt` ledger, release/nightly/content workflows and a release train. v2 has SemVer `0.y`, commit-count build numbers, one CI workflow plus a weekly feed check and a manual APK build, and internal distribution (D-83..D-85).
- **Flet version.** v1 said "never ship on a prerelease; pin a released 1.1.x by 2027-03-05". v2 develops on a pinned 1.1 commit until 1.1.0 is released, because the owner's Xcode 27 needs it (D-81).
- **Planning.** The capacity model, dated milestones and cut order become phases with exit criteria and soft markers (PLAN §7).
- **DEMO build flavour** becomes the hidden Developer screen (D-73).
- **Docs.** v1's seven large documents become a smaller set (D-96), and v1 stays in git history.

## Open decisions

Each open decision has a recommendation. Where a provisional decision applies (marked **prov. O-n** above), it holds until it is settled. Under D-101, decisions marked **owner-only** wait for the owner; agents settle the others when the work needs them, recording a new D-number.

| # | Decision needed | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|
| O-1 | Bottom-bar destinations and the start screen. | **Now · Schedule · My schedule · Map · More, opening on Now** (D-13, D-14). Revisit after a week of dogfooding at the P1 exit. The alternatives were a Speakers tab instead of My schedule, or opening on Schedule. |
| O-2 | ~~Per-platform page transitions, the one exception to "identical on both platforms".~~ **Settled by D-148 (S4).** | **Yes**: iOS swipe-back and Android predictive back, with identical visuals (D-11). |
| O-3 | Font. | **Bundle Roboto** (D-26). Consider a display face for headings only if a 2027 brand appears. |
| O-4 | **Owner-only.** App identity: bundle id, display name, icon, URL scheme and deep-link host (D-21). | Use a bundle id under a reverse domain the owner controls (e.g. `io.github.<handle>.pyconus`). For anything that leaves our devices, pick a neutral working name and our own icon, with no PSF marks; "PyCon US" appears inside the app only as the event's name, next to "Not an official PSF app". Decide before the first TestFlight upload of the real app; S5 can use a throwaway id. |
| O-5 | **Owner-only.** Distribution. | **Internal only**: APK sideload plus Play internal testing, and TestFlight internal (D-85). Consider a public listing only with PSF permission. |
| O-6 | **Owner-only.** Submit a PyCon US 2027 CFP proposal? | Decide around **2026-11-20**, the expected mentorship deadline (UNCONFIRMED), based on P1 progress. If yes, submit one talk (optionally a poster with phones for a live demo) framed as a community showcase, never as the official app, by about **2026-12-18** (expected close, UNCONFIRMED). |
| O-7 | How deep the staff tools go (P3). | **All four** on the shared scan screen, in this order: lead → door → swag → CoC. Swag and CoC may move to `later` without hurting the showcase. Staff-UX defaults from the UX study: show attendee names at the door, pre-check available swag items, hide attendee emails in CoC capture until revealed. |
| O-8 | A backend for stage demos. | Decide at the P3 exit. If the demo must run without a laptop, ship the pure mock `handle()` behind the Developer screen as an in-app backend. Otherwise keep the LAN mock (D-50). |
